You got 15 Essence.") and a Watch replay button. The full after-action cards (the Results section) appear only once the finale has a result. Phase 1 and 2 after-action cards are therefore not shown during the week any more; replays of those phases stay reachable from the mark.
- **Boss card (R2, R5):** the art keeps its size (24x24 at scale 4). On wide screens the name, Rank, readiness meter, element, HP and the icons sit beside it; on phones they stack below it. The turn order and each trick are icons with a caption that open a popover: the glossary's panel, but with the boss's own sentence ("Every 3 rounds: hits every creature in the front zone for 80% of its attack") above the glossary's general line. That replaces the "Tricks" list and the "How the boss fights" text. The popover is a small local component in `BossCard.tsx` rather than a new glossary reference type, so the shared glossary code is untouched. The readiness word became a four-step meter next to the Rank (still a word, never a number; its glossary popover is unchanged).
- **One placement step (R3):** "My team" and "The party row" are one card: a short hint, a tray of the three creatures (still dragged, or tapped then tapped, or moved with the keyboard, and dropping a placed creature on the tray unplaces it), then the row. The role legend and the long paragraphs went; the keyboard instructions sit in a small "Keyboard controls" disclosure. The placement logic (`raid-slots`, `raid-dnd`, the drag hook) and the lock during phases are untouched. I did not extract a shared slot component: the Dungeon team picker is built separately.
- **Avatar strip (R3):** "Who is in this week" is up to eight initials in each player's party-row colour with "N players this week"; a tap opens the names as text (with party numbers when there are several). Sitting out and joining again stay on the tab: the button is in the opened list, and a player who sat out always sees "Join this week's raid".
- **Abilities chip (R4):** "Abilities: Auto" when nothing is customised, "Abilities: Shield Wall · 1 manual" otherwise (a picked basic ability, then how many unlocked abilities have Auto fire off). Tapping it opens the same basic-ability pick and Auto fire switches as before, with shorter text.

## 2026-10-13: UX pass 1, Dungeon (less text, no ability picks) and glossary copy

Spec: `design/ux-pass-1.md`, Dungeon items 1 to 5 and "Glossary copy".

- **Pre-run screen.** It leads with the three-room comic strip (room 1, room 2, boss), each panel a "?" until the run (the foes are rolled from the run's seed, so nothing about them is known before). The day's modifier is an icon on its panel with a tap popover (the glossary entry the modifier already had); the icon comes from what the modifier does (`dungeon-rooms.ts`: an element boost shows that element, crits the reticle, a trick its own icon). The explanatory paragraphs, the row notes and the landmark sentence are gone. Streak is a tiny pip row beside the depth; the full streak line and the reset time moved to the end report ("Next run"), which keeps its strip, rewards and team as before.
- **Landmark depths** use the shared `ProgressTrack`: the previous landmark (when there is one), the depth the player is at, and the next landmark, with what a landmark gives in words (a sure gear drop, bonus Essence). The ladder has no end, so there is always a next step.
- **Team picker.** Three small slots in the look of the raid placement rectangles (the same `.party-slot`, slot number, sprite, name, level) and no text about rows. No raid file was touched.
- **Abilities.** The basic-ability pick and the per-ability fire rounds are gone from the dungeon UI, the request, the worker and the simulator. The request is `{ team, idempotencyKey }`, strict: an old body with `abilities` (or `formation`) is `invalid_body`, and the `invalid_abilities` error is removed. Every unlocked creature ability of the team fires at its default round. The idempotency fingerprint is now the team alone (an old key reused with a new body is a `key_conflict`, as before for a changed body). Dungeon fixtures were re-captured from the worker.
- **Balance.** The full simulator (`pnpm sim`, seed `leet`) passes all 47 targets with no basic ability and **no change to `RULES.dungeon`**: first-week depth 1 clear rate 81.0% (target at least 80%), median depth growth 1.5 per week (1 to 2), dungeon Essence 3.9% of a daily player's income, gear share at week 8 12.8%. The first-week clear rate has little room (1.0 points), so a later change that weakens the dungeon team should re-check it.
- **Glossary copy (Misa).** Reactions "deal much more damage", a Payoff "hits even harder when it sets off a reaction", and "Each Rank makes the boss stronger", in the glossary and How to play, with no 4.5x, +10% or +40%. Crit, Guard and Support numbers stay. The tests that pinned those numbers to `RULES` now pin that the text has no digit for them. The mechanics are unchanged; whether Payoff should depend on stats is left open for a later design pass.

## 2026-10-13: UX pass 1, Expeditions: rank-up gifts, no chances on screen

Misa's review asked for every explorer rank to unlock something, and for the Expeditions screens to stop showing chances. What changed, and the numbers.

**One-time rank-up gift** (`RULES.expeditions.rank.gifts`, index 0 is rank 2). It is granted on the claim that raises the rank, inside the claim transaction, once per (creature, rank): the Bait goes through the ledger and the parts and supplies through the grant ledger, both under the key `rank:<creature>:<rank>`, so a repeat claim, a retry or a replay pays nothing. A trip that jumps two ranks pays both gifts. It is shown in the claim reveal and listed on the rank `ProgressTrack`. Players already above rank 1 get nothing back-dated; gifts start with their next rank-up.

| Rank | Bait | Scrap | Glimmer | Supplies                     |
| ---- | ---- | ----- | ------- | ---------------------------- |
| 2    | 2    | 1     | 0       | none                         |
| 3    | 3    | 1     | 0       | none                         |
| 4    | 4    | 2     | 0       | none                         |
| 5    | 5    | 2     | 0       | none                         |
| 6    | 6    | 3     | 1       | none                         |
| 7    | 50   | 5     | 2       | Glow Lantern, Sweet Lure     |
| 8    | 75   | 8     | 3       | Inked Map, Sturdy Satchel    |
| 9    | 100  | 10    | 4       | Golden Lure                  |
| 10   | 150  | 15    | 6       | Trail Atlas, Explorer's Pack |

Totals per creature over all nine ranks: 395 Bait, 47 scrap, 16 glimmer, 7 supplies (14 Bait and 6 scrap up to rank 5).

**Retuned the same day (less Bait at ranks 2 to 6).** With the dungeon's basic ability removed, the first table (Bait 5, 10, 15, 20, 30 at ranks 2 to 6) and the dungeon change each passed 47/47 alone but failed together on `leet`: typical finale 61.1% (target 40 to 60%) and casual species discovery 0.32 (target at most 0.3). Extra Bait at the low ranks fed training and discovery. Only the gift numbers changed (no targets, boss, gear or dungeon numbers): Bait at ranks 2 to 6 is now 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, and the scrap, glimmer, supplies and ranks 7 to 10 are as before. Scrap is not the lever here: raising it at ranks 2 to 6 pushed the finale up again on seed `s2`. The table above is the shipped one. It passes 47/47 on `leet` (typical finale 53.8%, casual species 0.21). On seed `s2` it passes 46/47 and only `econ-hard-pity` (16.4%, at most 15%) fails, which also fails there with almost no gift at all, so it does not come from the gifts; the first table failed three targets on `s2` (phase 2 clear, typical finale, casual species).

**How the numbers were found (seed `leet`, full run).** Baseline 47/47. The gift is per creature, so it multiplies with the number of creatures that explore. A first draft with Bait 15 to 150, scrap, glimmer and a basic or good supply at every rank failed 5 targets (phase 2 clear 93.1% against at most 85%, typical finale 80.7% against at most 60%, 15-player finale 83.3%, boss spread 20.0%, casual species 0.4 against at most 0.3). Splitting the gift by kind: parts alone 47/47, Bait alone 47/47, supplies alone 43/47 (phase 2 87.5%, typical finale 65.5%, 15-player finale 67.4%, casual species 0.3 with rounding). Supplies feed discovery, and discovery feeds raid readiness, so **supplies only come at ranks 7 to 10**, which a sim player hardly reaches inside its horizon. The shipped table passes 47/47 on `leet`. The sim now pays the gifts too (`packages/sim/src/trips.ts`), so this stays checked.

**Chances are words, not numbers (item 7).**

- Push-on cards say Safe, Risky or Very risky with a mark (check, warning, skull) and keep the failure line ("May fail and bring nothing back."). The word comes from the odds the server rolled: a risky goal that works less than half the time (below 5000 basis points) is Very risky (`VERY_RISKY_BELOW_BP` in `push-on-view.ts`). Reward lines no longer say "50% more XP". A running push-on and the claim reveal also drop the percentages.
- Glossary entries for explorer rank, familiarity, the deeper area, push-on goals and milestones lost their percentages, and the tests now pin that they have none. Familiarity's three stars are on their own bullets and its short line has no "3 stars".
- The deeper area says only "Deeper area open: its rarer creatures can turn up." once the explorer has a star there. The mechanics are unchanged.
- Kept as they are: the supplies' own effect lines (Misa likes them), the weekly special's description, the combat numbers, the profile's milestone bonuses.

**Layout decisions.** Familiarity (stars and trips) moved from the place to the creature's row in "Who goes". The "Expected haul" is `haul-view.ts`: the same icons for every length plus a "Deeper area" icon when a lantern is carried; pips compare with the best any length does with the best supplies; a "+" marks what a chosen supply raises; chances are words ("rarely", "often"). The destination glossary entries (their "?" popup is gone) still exist because the content coverage test requires them; they are no longer reachable on screen and can be deleted with that test if Misa wants.

## 2026-10-13: Compendium contradictions resolved

The compendium author listed twelve places where content, glossary and design docs disagree (`docs/compendium/README.md`, "Known contradictions"). Fixed now, with the code as the judge:

- **Ember burrow page** (`journal.json` `ember-ash-burrow`) said "the next day". The mechanic, Warm Glow, is an in-fight boost for a few rounds, so the page now says that, with no numbers.
- **Daily dungeon design** said "Thorns: guards reflect" and "3 and 7 days in a row". Thorns makes the foe turn the first reaction of each round back on the team (as `room-modifiers.json` has it), and the streak pays on the 3rd and 7th day of each 7-day cycle (`RULES.dungeon.streak`). The doc now says both.
- **Gear design** said about 12 bases and 8 enchants (shipped: 19 and 9), and listed raids, the dungeon and Festivals as blueprint sources. The code (`gear-grants.ts`, `rollBlueprint`) grants blueprints only from expedition claims, expedition push-on rewards and the welcome kit. The doc now says so. Side effect found: the glossary says Festival gear is "crafted only from Festival blueprints", but nothing grants one yet. Left for Misa to decide.
- **Storm Ridge odds:** the schema falls back to `RULES.expeditions.defaultOddsBp` when a place has no block, so behaviour was always defined and the sim was tuned with it. Nothing records an intent to omit it, so the place now has an explicit block with the default values. No behaviour change.
- "Row" against "zone" in raids is not a contradiction: zones are rows.
- Still open: 3 (Festival residents text), 4 (night hours), 6 ("Very risky" wording), 12 (enchant costs).

A new test, `packages/game/test/compendium.test.ts`, fails when shipped content has no compendium entry.

## 2026-10-13: Festival gear stays a Festival-only prize (Misa)

Festival gear is won only by clearing a Festival finale. There are no Festival blueprints, so it is never craftable. `rollBlueprint` keeps skipping Festival bases, and the glossary says "It cannot be crafted."

## 2026-10-13: Scalars registry and patch notes draft (balance loop, parts 1 and 3)

The scalars registry and the patch notes generator from `docs/design/balance-loop.md` are in. Misa wanted a list of the knobs and patch notes once balance patches roll out.

- **Where the code lives.** `packages/sim` (`src/scalars.ts`, `src/patch-notes.ts`, run through `scripts/*.mjs` the same way as `pnpm sim`). The sim already depends on the game package and on `typescript`, so no new dependency was needed. `pnpm scalars` writes `docs/balance/scalars.md` and `docs/balance/content-ids.md`. A test in the sim package fails with "run pnpm scalars" when either is stale, and `pnpm scalars --check` does the same from the shell.
- **What is a scalar.** Every number under `RULES`, plus every numeric field of the content data that tunes the game (creature stats and growth, bosses, reactions, dungeon foes, room modifiers, enchants, destinations, supplies, push-on goals, weekly twists). Journal pages, lore and art briefs are left out. Values come from the validated runtime objects, so they cannot drift from the code.
- **Paths and rows.** Arrays of objects that all have unique ids are keyed by id (`RULES.raid.phases.finale.rounds`, `content.bosses.tidal-colossus.baseHp`), so a reordered list is not a change. Other arrays use `[i]`. An array of at most 8 numbers is one row with the array inline; a longer one has a row per element.
- **Meaning.** The first sentence of the comment on the field (read from the TypeScript source with the compiler API, not a regex), joined with the next sentence when the first is very short. A field without a comment of its own takes the nearest parent's comment below the system level; a system's own comment is not copied onto every number in it. Content fields take the comment of the same field name in the schema files, only when the name has one meaning everywhere. Units come from the key name (`Bp` basis points, `ms`, `Hours`, `Days`, else count).
- **Sim targets** come from a small hand-kept map (`packages/sim/scalar-targets.json`, path prefix to target ids; `raid-*` means every target with that prefix). A test fails when an id matches no target or a prefix matches no scalar.
- **Patch notes.** `pnpm patch-notes [from..to]` reads the two generated files at both refs with `git show` (git is run with argument arrays, never a shell, and refs must match a strict pattern), lists the pull requests named `(#NN)` in the range, the balance log entries whose Action names them, content ids added, removed or renamed, and changed numbers. With no range it starts at the latest `balance-*` tag; with no tag yet it starts at `origin/main` (or the root commit if that is the end of the range), and says so. A start with no registry says "no baseline" and lists the current values. Drafts go to stdout, or with `--out <folder>` to `draft.md` and `draft.json`, which are gitignored.
- **The skill** (`.claude/skills/patch-notes/SKILL.md`) writes the player-facing notes into `docs/patch-notes/YYYY-MM-DD.md`. It never posts to Discord without Misa approving the exact text, and tags `balance-YYYY-MM-DD` only after merge and only when asked.

## 2026-10-13: Analytics phase 2, server side: rollups, guild directory, ops report

Built per `design/analytics.md` (steps 2 to 5, phase 2 items 1 to 3 and 6). Decisions:

- **Rollup day is the game day.** The guild alarm rolls up each finished game day (13:37 to 13:37 guild-local, `gameDay`, `gameDayEnd` and `RULES.daily.reset`, with the timezone passed in) into `analytics_daily (day, metric, dims_json, value)` (migration 20). A row's `day` is the date the game day started on. The rollup runs at reset plus 3 minutes (13:40), the same slot as the leet sync, so one alarm serves both. Recomputing a day deletes and rewrites its rows in one transaction. A `rolled` row marks a finished day (also an empty one); the last rolled day is the newest `rolled` row, and a run catches up from the day after it, at most 14 days back. Raw events still prune at 90 days; rollup rows are kept 400 days. Events of kind `dev_action` never count.
- **Where each flow comes from.** Bait and Essence flows come from the `ledger` table (every row has a source label). Parts and supplies come from events. To get per-kind parts, `gear_drop`, `gear_dismantle` and `job_start` now carry a `parts` prop (kind to amount, only kinds that are present), and a supply `job_claim` carries `yield`. Events from before that fall back to the old totals (`shard-any` for shards). Finale abilities (hand-fired, held, Auto) are read from the `raid_live` table, not from events. Rollups hold counts only; distinct players (DAU, new players, watchers, users per feature) are counted from the hashed actors and only the count is stored. Every dim value is re-checked on write (id-like string or integer, never a hash-like run).
- **One guild directory object.** `GuildDirectory` (SQLite Durable Object, one instance named `guild-directory`) stores `guild_id, first_seen, last_seen` (UTC days). It is the only cross-guild index. `GuildWorld.createSession` tells it at most once per UTC day per guild object (in memory), without awaiting: a failure is logged by type only and never delays or fails a login. It has no HTTP surface. Both Durable Object classes are bound in every environment; the `v2` migration tag (`new_sqlite_classes: GuildDirectory`) is at the top level only because wrangler environments inherit `migrations`.
- **The ops endpoint.** `GET /api/ops/report` is authorised only by `Authorization: Bearer <OPS_TOKEN>`. `OPS_TOKEN` is an optional Worker secret: unset or empty, a wrong token (including a session token), or any method but GET all answer the same plain 404 (corrected 2026-10-13 below: not hidden, since unknown `/api/*` routes answer 401). Tokens are compared as SHA-256 digests with `crypto.subtle.timingSafeEqual`. A 429 rate limit (burst 5, then one request per 10 seconds) is keyed by a hash of the connecting IP and comes after the "unset" check and before the token check, so guesses cost tokens. Query parameters are strict (`from`, `to`, `format`, `weekly`, `compareFrom`, `compareTo`; unknown or repeated parameters are refused; at most 92 days). `weekly=1` compares with the previous period of equal length; `compareFrom` and `compareTo` give an explicit one.
- **Aliases, not guild ids.** A guild shows up as `g-` plus the first 6 hex characters of an HMAC-SHA256 of its id, under a key HKDF-derived from `DISCORD_CLIENT_SECRET` with its own info label (`analytics-guild-alias-v1`). The prefix grows (8, 10, ... characters) only when two guilds in the directory would collide. It is stable while the secret is the same, and not reversible without it. The report holds no user ids, actor hashes, guild ids, names or free text; every rollup row is validated again in the Worker before it can appear. Privacy tests seed distinctive ids, nicknames and hashes and check both formats.
- **Report workflow.** `.github/workflows/report.yml` runs daily at 06:17 UTC (and on demand) in the `reports` environment. It has no checkout and uses no third-party action: only `node` on the runner and a script inside the workflow, so it needs no permissions and no pinned SHAs. Mondays add `weekly=1`. A 404 (token not set yet) is a warning, not a failure. The deploy job passes `OPS_TOKEN` to the Worker like the other secrets, but optionally: an empty value is not uploaded and does not fail the missing-secrets check. Keeping the files as an artifact needs `actions/upload-artifact` pinned to a SHA, which no workflow here uses yet, so for now the Markdown and (collapsed) JSON go into the job summary.

## 2026-10-13: Dev tools: the gate, the dev clock and where forced state lives

Server side of `docs/design/dev-tools.md`, staging only.

- **Gate.** A Worker var `DEV_TOOLS` ("on" or "off"), set like `RAID_PRACTICE`: `"off"` at the top level and in production, `"on"` only in the staging env of `wrangler.jsonc`. A test parses the file and pins this. Every route under `/api/dev/*` answers a plain 404 (`{ "error": "not_found" }`; see the correction of 2026-10-13 below) unless the var is "on". The check is the first thing the Worker does for `/api/*` (before the session check) and the first thing the Durable Object does, so it holds even if one layer is bypassed. `GuildCore` only builds the dev tools when the var is on (`GuildCoreOptions.devTools`), so with it off none of that code runs. When it is on, `/api/config` and `/api/me` carry `devTools: true` (the key is absent otherwise) so the client panel can be loaded lazily. Dev routes still need a verified session, take the identity from it (no body carries a user id) and share the per-player rate limit.
- **One template route.** `GET /api/dev/state`, `GET /api/dev/events` and `POST /api/dev/<tool>` live under one template (`/api/dev/:tool`), with the tool ids in `dev-schemas.ts` (`DEV_ACTION_TOOLS`). Each body is a strict schema; each tool runs in one transaction and is logged as an analytics event `dev_action` with the tool id as its only prop (reports must exclude the kind). A refused tool rolls back and logs nothing.
- **The dev clock.** A per-guild offset in ms (an integer from 0, at most ten years) stored in the guild's Durable Object. `GuildCore` builds one `now` and gives it to every game module (collection and expeditions, gear, the dungeon, the raid, Discord posts, the leet sync, the alarm): it is exactly the real clock when the var is off, even if an offset is stored, and the real clock plus the offset when on. Sessions (creation, expiry), the rate limit and the "may manage the server" cache keep real time, and so does analytics (its events and retention are about the real world). The clock only moves forward (`advanceMs` up to 30 days a call, "to the next 13:37", "to the next raid event", or a phase of the raid week) and "reset" puts it back to zero; moving it back is not offered because stored game state (finished trips, resolved phases) cannot move back with it. Alarms are real time: the target is computed on the shifted clock and the offset is subtracted before `setAlarm`, and `ensureAlarm` compares in real time; moving the clock re-arms the alarm at once.
- **Where forced state lives.** A small key/value table `dev_state`, created with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` when a Durable Object starts with the var on. It holds the offset and the force flags (the next push-on offer and result, forced night and Festival per player, the dungeon day's picked modifiers). It is not a numbered migration, on purpose: production databases never get the table, and no migration number is spent on a staging tool. The game modules read flags only through an optional `dev` dependency that is `undefined` when the var is off, so a flag that is stored anyway is never read. A test pins that for the clock, night and the modifiers.
- **Tools write the tables directly** (they set a state for testing; they do not play the game), but still refuse what a rule forbids outright: a level above the species' cap, a rarity a gear base cannot have, a full bag, a roster after Phase 1 locked it.
- **Simulated players** for the raid have ids like `dev-guest-3` (never a Discord snowflake), three creatures of three lines near the caller's level (drawn from the live crypto RNG) and the automatic team; the row auto-places them when a phase resolves. "Run a full simulated week" resolves Phase 1, Phase 2 and the finale (as an instant finale, abilities at their default round) with the real resolver, so payouts, the ladder and the after-action are the real ones.
- **Live finale now** is the existing practice fight with a shorter countdown and without the finale block and the cooldown (`Raid.startPractice` options, passed only by the dev tool, which does not need `RAID_PRACTICE`).
- **Analytics rollup** is a hook (`DevToolsDeps.rollupNow`, marked TODO) that the rollup work wires; until then the tool answers 501.

## 2026-10-13: Live analysis and live-calibrated sim (`pnpm analyze`, `pnpm sim:live`)

Phase 2 items 4 to 6 of `design/analytics.md`, in `packages/sim` (`live-report.ts`, `live-analyze.ts`, `live-render.ts`, `live-calibrate.ts`, `live-projection.ts`, the two CLIs under `scripts/`). No new dependency. Decisions:

- **The report is parsed again, not imported.** The sim package must not import from the Worker, so `live-report.ts` holds a strict valibot schema of `Report` (unknown keys refused; metric, dim keys and dim values match the same patterns the Worker enforces; bounded sizes). A report that does not match is refused whole. Every id printed in the output is in a code span, so report text cannot become Markdown.
- **Flags and the small-sample rule.** A rate raises a **flag** from `MIN_N = 20` samples (`MIN_PLAYER_DAYS = 30` active player-days for resources), a **watch** from `WATCH_FLOOR_N = 5`, and nothing below that. Thresholds are named constants in `live-analyze.ts`: nobody picks (a known option at 0, flag from 30 picks or offers, watch from 10), everybody picks (above 60% in a group of at least 4 options), depth wall (clear rate drops by more than 40 points between consecutive depths, both with at least 10 runs; a flag needs 20), finale band 40 to 60% per rank (Festival weeks excluded, they are eased), a currency piling up (in at least 3 times out, and a net per active player per day at or above `PILE_NET_PER_PLAYER_DAY`: bait 25, essence 2, scrap 5, shards 2, glimmer 1, supplies 3) or running dry (out above in), and an expected sink with no use while its currency has sources (`EXPECTED_SINKS`). The pile amounts were chosen from the sim's per-player income (a daily player earns about 50 Bait and 3 Essence a day) and are meant to be tuned from real reports.
- **Which sim targets are checked against live values.** Only those with a clean live equivalent (`LIVE_TARGETS`): Phase 1 and Phase 2 clear rate and finale win rate (as "typical group" targets, compared only when the average group is 5 to 10 players, else "not comparable"), the Festival finale win rate, dungeon depth 1 clear rate, the dungeon's share of Essence, and the element share of raid team slots. Everything else needs per-player data, levels or timings that rollups do not carry.
- **Calibration heuristic.** Sessions per active player-day `s` gives the heavy share `h = s - 1` (a casual has 1 session on an active day, everyone else 2, the sim's own check-in convention), and the casual share of players follows from casuals being active 2 days in 7. The server size is the average daily active players per server divided by the active fraction (3 to 30 players). A bounded coordinate search then fits `tripPercent` (share of check-ins that send trips) to trips sent per active player-day and `dungeonPercent` to dungeon runs per active player-day, and may move the casual share by at most 20 points. The fit error is the mean relative gap. Daily against top is fixed at 2 to 1 (they differ by one leet day; aggregates cannot tell them apart). New players are not modelled. Supplies per trip are compared but not fitted.
- **Two new sim knobs.** `EconomyOptions.behaviour` (leet days per archetype, `tripPercent`, `dungeonPercent`). Left at their defaults a run draws the same numbers as before (a test checks it); the extra random stream is only read when a percent is below 100.
- **Projection.** Seeded and reproducible (`--seed`, default `leet`; `--quick` for a small server and few sims). The sim starts from an empty server, because reports carry no holdings; `--age-weeks N` shifts the table to start at week N+1 of the server's life. It reports the first week a stockpile passes 4 weeks of its own income (and 50), the week dungeon depth growth falls under the band, and the week-bound sim targets (casual level at week 4, daily species at week 6, power ratio at week 8) as this server meets them. Outputs go next to the live report: `live-YYYY-MM-DD.md` and `.json` from `pnpm analyze`, `live-YYYY-MM-DD.calibration.json` and `.projection.md` from `pnpm sim:live`; an earlier calibration in the same folder is shown as parameter deltas.
- **Skill.** `.claude/skills/balance-review/SKILL.md` describes the loop for agents: report from the workflow log, analyse, project, log entry, scalar proposal from the registry, Misa's OK, patch notes.

## 2026-10-13: Dev tools: the client panel

The client half of `docs/design/dev-tools.md`. Decisions:

- **Where it lives.** `apps/activity/src/dev/` (panel, forms, content lists, `dev.css`, the calls to `/api/dev/*`). The only code outside it is `src/dev-gate.ts` (one function with the dynamic `import()`), `DevPanelHost.tsx` (loads it and renders it), the `devTools` flag read from `/api/config` and `/api/me`, and `Game.tsx` handing the panel the current tab, the player's creatures and a refresh callback.
- **Two gates in the client.** The server's `devTools: true` (staging Worker only) decides whether `loadDevPanel` imports the panel. A Vite `define`, `__DEV_TOOLS_BUILD__`, decides whether the import exists at all. It is false in every build except `TARGET_ENV=staging` (fail closed): `vite build` is `production` mode for staging too, so the build `mode` cannot tell them apart, but `deploy.yml` already sets `TARGET_ENV` for the whole deploy job (`production` on `main`, `staging` otherwise) and `pnpm build` inherits it, so no workflow change was needed. A plain `pnpm build` (local, CI) has no panel. `pnpm dev` and the tests always have it.
- **A build test** (`src/dev-build.test.ts`) builds in memory with the Vite API for production, for an unset target and for staging, and searches every emitted file for the panel's marker string, its class names and the `/api/dev/` path: absent in the first two, present only in a separate lazy chunk (and its CSS) in the third, so the search is known to work. It takes about 3 seconds.
- **Controls are data.** `specs.ts` lists one `ToolSpec` per control (tool id, fields, fixed body keys); `forms.ts` turns the typed values into the request body and checks ranges first. The dropdowns come from `@creatures/game` content, creature pickers from the game's state. A test pins that every server tool id has a control and that the bodies are the literals the strict schemas expect (the bodies were also checked against the Worker's schemas once, by a throwaway test that was not kept, so the client does not import Worker code).
- **After a tool succeeds** the panel calls `Game`'s `devRefresh`: reload the state and the bag, and re-key the tab panel and the slot-wipe notice so views that load their own data (Raid, Dungeon, Expeditions' profile) start afresh.
- **Local flags.** The app keeps two "seen it" flags in `localStorage` (the Raid tag hint, the slot-wipe notice); "Reset local flags" removes them and anything else under the app's own prefixes (`lc.`, `creatures:`). The welcome kit is server state, not a local flag; "Reset my account" brings it back. The reset needs `RESET` typed in the panel (the server checks it again).

## 2026-10-13: Analytics security review fixes

Fixes from the security review of the analytics work. Decisions:

- **One report token per target.** The `reports` environment holds `OPS_TOKEN_PRODUCTION` and `OPS_TOKEN_STAGING` (different values). The workflow sends the first only to production and the second only to staging, each read from `env:` and never interpolated into `run:`. An empty one skips that target with a warning. The Worker secret is still `OPS_TOKEN` in each deploy environment (`deploy.yml` is unchanged). The `reports` environment is limited to the `main` branch (Misa's checklist in `analytics.md`), so a branch cannot edit `report.yml` to send the tokens elsewhere.
- **Public output is cross-server totals only.** The repo is public, so workflow logs and job summaries are public; the earlier "private" wording was wrong. The ops endpoint now leaves out `byGuild` unless the query has `byGuild=1` (strict: allow-listed key, value only `1`, not repeated); the workflow never asks, the Markdown renderer never prints per-server data, and `byGuild` is optional in the sim's report schema (whose alias pattern now allows the `-` of `g-xxxxxx`, which it wrongly refused).
- **Fan-out.** One RPC per guild returns both the current and the compare range (`analyticsDaily(from, to, compare)`). At most 400 guilds are asked (`MAX_GUILDS_ASKED`), most recently seen first so active guilds are covered; `GuildDirectory.list(limit)` returns `{ ids, total }`, `guilds.known` is the true directory count and `guilds.reporting` the number that answered.
- **Privacy filter.** Dim values that are 15 or more digits (Discord snowflakes) or UUID-shaped (with or without dashes) are refused in the rollup (stored as `other`) and again in the report (the row is dropped), next to the existing hash-like check.
- **Rollups skip dev ledger rows** (`source = 'dev'`, the staging set-balances). On staging with the dev clock moved, ledger and `raid_live` times are on the game clock while events are real time, so day attribution can be off there (accepted, staging only).
- **`dungeon-reset` keeps the ledger append-only.** It no longer deletes the day's dungeon credits. It appends one negative `dev` entry per currency for the amount actually removed (credits not already dealt with by an earlier reset, capped at the balance, so never below zero). Consequence: the credits keep their idempotency keys, so a run of the same day and attempt after a reset is not paid Bait or Essence again; gear grants are still deleted, so gear can be granted again. This is a staging-only test-tool limit.
- **Correction on 404s.** Earlier entries said the ops endpoint and the dev routes answer "the answer of an unknown route". Not so: unknown `/api/*` routes answer 401 (the session check comes first), while `/api/ops/report` and `/api/dev/*` (when off) answer a plain 404. The 404 is therefore visible; that is accepted, because the routes are protected by the token and the var, not by hiding. Docs and comments now say 404 plainly.
- **Dev tools scope and limits.** The clock, raid-phase, raid-resolve, raid-simulate, raid-boss, raid-festival, dungeon-modifiers and dungeon-seed act on the whole staging guild; `GET /api/dev/events` shows the guild's recent events without actors; dev POSTs are not idempotent (a retry grants twice), accepted for a staging test tool.
- **Timezone change.** A guild timezone change can drop or double-count a few hours in one rollup day (noted in `analytics.md`).

## 2026-10-13: Cleanup 1: one keyed-action helper and the key namespace rule

Worker half of cleanup pass 1 (`cleanup-1-audit.md` section 10). No behaviour change; every key already stored stays valid. Decisions:

- **Client keys.** A client idempotency key (`IdempotencyKeySchema`, 8-64 characters of `A-Za-z0-9_-`, so never a `:`) is one namespace per player. It may sit in the ledger (a spend), in `gear_actions` (gear, workshop and expedition-send actions) or in `dungeon_runs`. A repeat of the same action returns the stored result; the same key for a different action (another kind, another fingerprint, or another table) is a `key_conflict`, and nothing runs or is charged.
- **Server keys.** Keys the server builds are `<source>:<scope...>` and always contain a `:`, so they can never collide with a client key. When one event pays both a ledger credit and a grant, the grant keeps the key and each credit gets a `:<currency>` suffix (`dungeon:<day>:<attempt>` and `dungeon:<day>:<attempt>:bait`). Existing strings are unchanged: the dungeon keys above, the raid ones (`raid:<week>:<party>:<phase>` for credits, `raid:<week>:<phase>` for gear grants), `festival:<week>` and `rank:<creature>:<rank>` (the last two use the same string in the ledger and in `gear_grants`, as before). No migration.
- **Code.** `apps/worker/src/keyed-action.ts` holds `runKeyed` (gear and expedition-send actions), `keyTaken` (the cross-table check) and `settleSpend` (the answer of a ledger `spend`). `payOnce` in `gear-grants.ts` pays credits and grant of one server event under the rule above (the dungeon run and streak use it). One `GameError` and one `atomically` (`outcome.ts`) replace the four copies; `parseBody` is in `http.ts` and `IdempotencyKeySchema` in `schemas.ts`.
- **Coverage is deliberately not widened yet.** Today a gear or expedition key is checked against the ledger only, a dungeon key against the ledger and `gear_actions`, and a collection key (train, evolve) only by the ledger itself. Making every table check every other would be stricter, a behaviour change, and is harmless to skip while client keys are per-action UUIDs; it is a one-line change per caller (`keyTaken` takes the list of tables) if it is ever wanted.

### 2026-10-14: Stylesheet split and colour tokens (cleanup pass 1, slices 10–11)

`apps/activity/src/styles.css` is now `apps/activity/src/styles/index.css` plus 66 files under `styles/{base,components,views,stage}/`. The import order in `index.css` is the cascade order, so a rule moves only together with its neighbours; a component whose rules were scattered has several files rather than one reordered one. The split produced a byte-identical CSS bundle. Translucent colours use channel-triple tokens (`--bad-rgb` and friends, written `rgb(var(--bad-rgb) / 14%)`) instead of `color-mix()`, so the values stay exact and work in older iOS WebViews. The old alias tokens (`--bg`, `--panel`, `--text`, `--muted`, `--accent`, `--danger`) are gone; use the real palette tokens. Screens and computed styles were compared before and after across 26 scenarios at three widths, with no differences.

### 2026-10-14: One popover shell (cleanup pass 1, slice 17)

Every popover (glossary tags, the small-creature popover, boss icons, and the stage's ability, note and help popovers) now uses one shell: `ui/Popover.tsx` with `usePopoverNav`, `PopoverCard` and `PopoverBack`, placed by `ui/popover-place.ts`. Page-level popovers share one slot in `PopoverHost`, so only one is open at a time; stage popovers keep their own slot inside the stage, and a tap inside any popover layer never counts as "outside" for another, so a tag can open from inside a stage popover. The options that differ on purpose stay options: `layer` (stage or page), `size` (compact or wide) and `closeWhenAnchorGone`. Boss icon popovers joined the host, which unified three things: they now close when another page popover opens, close when their anchor leaves the page, and sit in the host layer in Tab order. `StagePopover.tsx` and `creature-popover.ts` are gone; older entries that name them describe the code of their time.

### 2026-10-14: One slot row (cleanup pass 1, slice 18)

The raid Placement and the dungeon team now draw their slots, tray, drag ghost and keyboard from shared pieces in `apps/activity/src/ui/`: `SlotRow` (with `SlotButton`, the one slot markup, in one or several zone groups), `CreatureTray`, `DragGhost`, and `use-slot-keyboard.ts` (the key handler and the screen reader announcer). The key map is the pure `slotKeyTarget` in `dnd-core.ts`. `slot-outcome.ts` holds what both rule modules share: `Tap` (`{kind:'creature', id}` or `{kind:'slot', slot}`, one shape for both screens), `Selection`, `DropTarget`, `DropOutcome`, `dropOutcome()` and the spoken sentences (the dungeon now uses `CANCEL_ANNOUNCEMENT` instead of its own copy of the text). The rules stay where they were (`raid-dnd.ts` and `raid-slots.ts` for the raid, `dungeon-team.ts` for the dungeon); only the shapes are shared. The CSS is one file, `styles/components/slot-row.css` (it replaces `party-slots.css` and `placement-dnd.css` and takes the slot and tray rules that sat in `raid.css`, `raid-placement.css`, `app.css` and `dungeon.css`). The dungeon's unused `dungeon-slot` class and three rules that repeated the base slot are gone. Server request bodies are unchanged and pinned by tests. Left on purpose, as options of the shared parts: the empty label ("empty" in the raid, "Empty" in the dungeon) and the remove button ("Unplace" and "Take out"), which wait for Misa's answer; the raid's arrow keys also start from a tray chip while a creature is picked up and the dungeon's do not.

## 2026-10-14: A home-grown component workbench on staging

**Decision.** `apps/activity/workbench.html` (code in `apps/activity/src/workbench/`) is a gallery of the game's reusable components: a list by kind, and a page per component with a live preview in an iframe of phone width, controls for its props, presets, width and background switchers, the view kept in the address, and a "Give feedback" button that opens a prefilled `ui-feedback` GitHub issue. It is built only when `__DEV_TOOLS_BUILD__` is true (dev serve and `TARGET_ENV=staging`), the same gate as the dev panel, and a build test checks that production has no `workbench.html`, script or styles. The dev panel links to it. The `ui-feedback` skill lets an agent turn those issues into changes. Details: `docs/design/workbench.md`.

**Why not Storybook or Ladle.** Both bring large dependency trees (many transitive packages, a second build tool) and each package is supply-chain surface under our rules; neither would be gated by the existing staging switch without extra work. The home-grown version has zero new dependencies, uses the same Vite build, the real components and the real stylesheet (so a component looks the same as in the game), reuses the staging gate, and is small enough for agents to read and change. A registry test makes it impossible to add a `src/ui` component without a page.

**Tradeoffs.** Fewer features than Storybook: no addon ecosystem, no automatic prop tables (each entry lists its controls by hand), no visual regression tooling, no docs pages. Props are limited to numbers, choices, toggles and text; component inputs that are whole game objects are built from fixture data in the entry. Screens that need a full game state are not covered. Feedback is a plain link to a GitHub issue (no token, no API call), so Misa must be signed in to GitHub to send it.

## 2026-10-14: The daily dungeon always acts left to right, and the order is shown

**Decision (Misa, 2026-10-14).** "Dungeons could be the more simple fighting experience. Left to right by default ... The raid can have these mechanics because it's a single fight people can prepare for, not three changing rooms. The other modifiers are what make dungeons interesting." Any change to the normal rules must be shown before the run, or, for a mystery modifier, announced when it happens. Players optimise their loadout and order, and the dungeon must reward that planning.

**What changed.**

- **One rule, not content.** `RULES.dungeon.turnOrder = 'left_to_right'`: your creatures act left to right (slot 1, 2, 3), then the foe. `buildRoomInput` gives every room's fight that order. Dungeon foes lost their `turnOrder` field (schema, content and the stored-run type): the schema is strict, so a foe with one is refused, and content cannot lie about it. A stored run from before the rule can still carry `turnOrder` on its foe; it is ignored (a test covers it, and replays read stored events, so they are unaffected). The raid's bosses keep their own turn orders, and the engine's generic `turnOrderOverride` stays (the raid tests and the golden fight use it); no dungeon code sets it any more.
- **Fog is retired, Echoing replaces it.** Fog's only effect was the `turn_order` modifier op, which now has nothing to do, so the op is gone from the schema. The pool stays at 8 (3 are rolled a day, all different): Echoing, "Guards and supports hit 50% harder", is two existing `team_stat` Attack effects (one per role, no engine change) instead of an element bonus, so it rewards a team with a Guard or a Support in it and fits the planning Misa wants. It first shipped as "Payoff creatures hit 20% harder" and was changed in review the same day, because payoffs already dominate and a modifier that boosts them makes the setter and payoff spike stronger. It takes Fog's slot in the content, so the same day seed rolls the same positions and only `fog` becomes `echoing`. Content ids and scalars were regenerated (`pnpm scalars`). Old runs that rolled Fog keep its name and text in their stored rooms, so replays and summaries still show it (the client icon falls back to the plain diamond and adds no gimmick; a test builds a run with Fog). Analytics keeps only shipped content ids, so a new `dungeon_run` event can never carry `fog`; old rollups that did are untouched. One side effect: on the day this ships, a player who already ran with Fog in a room sees Echoing in that room on the pre-run screen of the same day (the stored run still says Fog).
- **The order is visible.** The pre-run screen says "Your creatures act left to right, then the foe." under the team, as a tag that opens the "Your row" glossary entry (it was not linked from anywhere before). The playback writes the acting order under each creature in the stage's row ("1st", "2nd", "3rd", from `ordinal` in `stage.ts`), and the creature acting now is outlined in gold with its ordinal lit. A first version had a turn strip under the stage ("then the foe" at its end); Misa asked for the ordinals instead, so the dungeon no longer draws the strip. The strip stays for the raid replay: it moved to `ui/TurnStrip.tsx` (with `turnChips` in `replay.ts`, a workbench page and its own stylesheet) and the raid replay draws through it.
- **Playback layout (Misa, 2026-10-14).** The stage's head reads "Room 1 / 3 · Round 2 / 2" (the room's place before the round counter; the "?" link to the glossary entry was dropped from the playback and stays on the setup line). The foe's name is under its sprite, as a creature's is, and its HP is written on the bar ("62 / 337") through a new `text` prop of the shared `SegmentBar` (the `XpBar` words-over-the-bar base, ink outline), so the red segmented look stays and the workbench page shows it. The gap between the foe and the team is much smaller (a `single` class on the stage). Pause, 2x and Skip are hidden: `DungeonPlayback` takes `showControls`, off by default, and the controls and their state still work. Progress counts use a slash with spaces, never "of" (Misa); only the strings touched here were converted, and a separate sweep converts the rest. The room banner needs no turn-order text, since it never changes; it still states each modifier's rule in the modifier's own words.
- **Glossary and telegraph fixes.** "Your row" said the foes act left to right, right to left or fastest first, which is false now and was never about your team: it now says your creatures act left to right, then the foe acts, every room. "Room modifiers" no longer says some change who acts first. The stage's "Next round" forecast read only the foe's own gimmicks, so Thorns' added reflect never showed in it; the stage now takes the room modifier's gimmicks too (`addedGimmicks`, from the content by the stored modifier id).
- **The order decides whether reactions happen, so the suggested order is element-aware (lead's addition, same day).** Under left to right a mark stays on the foe until a Setter replaces it or a creature of the cashing element sets it off, so a row can go round after round with no reaction at all. The three starters in the old role order (Splashet, Sproutle, Cindle: a Tide Setter first, and nobody has Spark) can never react, while Cindle, Sproutle, Splashet reacts every round; in the study the same trio swung 30 to 55 clear points by order alone (about 47% against about 3% at depth 5, level 8). New pure rules in `packages/game` (`rules/dungeon.ts`): `rowReactions` plays the marks over six rounds of left-to-right hits exactly as the engine does (first hit marks, the cashing element sets the reaction off, only a Setter replaces a mark, from the reaction table in content) and `rowScore` counts the reactions; `dungeonRowOrder` now needs each creature's element too, tries the six orders, keeps the ones that score highest and breaks ties by the old role order (setters and supports, Guard, payoffs), then by the order given. "Use my strongest three", the default team and the simulator's players all use it. It does not look at stats, the foe's element or abilities: it is a rule of thumb about marks, not a solver. The team setup shows one line under the slot row from the same `rowReactions` (`orderHint` in `dungeon-team.ts`): "Cindle marks Scorched → Splashet sets off Steam." for each different pair, or "In this order nobody can set off a reaction." Tests pin the starters case, that the picked order never scores below another order of the same team (every triple of the first 14 species), and that the hint names a reaction exactly when the row scores.
- **"Use my strongest three"** (`dungeonRowOrder`) already assumed left to right; its comments changed, and it now picks the order as described above.

**Balance (full run; before this change / after the left-to-right rule alone / final, with the element-aware order).** Seed `leet`: depth 1 cleared in a player's first 7 days 80.1% / 81.3% / 80.5% (limit 80%); depth a median daily player gains a week 1.67 / 1.67 / 1.67 (1 to 2); dungeon Essence share 3.9% / 3.9% / 4.0% (limit 40%); gear share at week 8 with the dungeon 12.8% / 13.0% / 12.9% (limit 20%). Clear rates by depth moved by noise in both steps (daily players at depth 5: 63% / 68% / 62%; depth 15: 20% / 19% / 21%; the median depth after 12 weeks 21 / 21 / 22), and a daily player clears 1.67 / 1.70 / 1.75 runs a week. No dungeon scalar was touched. The other seeds on the final code: `leet2` 84.0% depth 1 and `leet3` 82.2%, growth 1.67 on both, and all 47 targets pass on all three seeds (47 of 47). **Watch the raid targets, which the dungeon's income feeds.** Under seed `leet` "finale win rate, typical group" went 53.8% / 60.8% / 59.8% (limit 60%) and "Phase 2 clear rate, typical group" 79.5% / 83.1% / 84.5% (limit 85%), 15 players 42.4% / 47.4% / 48.5%; the middle step failed the typical-group limit on seed `leet` only (60.8%, while `leet2` and `leet3` gave 52.7% and 54.4%), so most of that is the thin-target noise noted on 2026-10-13 (the dungeon's dice changed, so the loot and economy streams of the whole sim diverged). On the final code the other seeds give 51.9% and 55.3% (Phase 2 78.1% and 79.5%), so only seed `leet` sits at the edge. The dungeon's clear rate did not get much easier, but better clears do pay a little more Bait, Essence and gear, so if a later change adds to dungeon income the raid's typical win rate has about 0.2 points of room on seed `leet`. The raid was left alone as asked.

**After the Echoing swap (Guards and supports +50%, instead of Payoff +20%).** Seed `leet`: depth 1 cleared in the first 7 days 80.5%, depth gain per week 1.67, dungeon Essence share 4.0%, gear share 12.9%: the same as before the swap. The raid targets at the edge: typical-group finale win 59.7% (limit 60%) and Phase 2 clear 82.6% (limit 85%), 15 players 46.0%. The three seeds were not clean this time, each missing one thin target that is not a dungeon target: `leet` misses the expedition casual-discovery target (0.34 of 0.3), `leet2` misses it too (0.32; it already missed it at 0.39 with the left-to-right rule alone), and `leet3` misses the boss spread of the raid finale (17.7% of 15%). The dungeon's income is the same as with the Payoff version (a daily player clears 1.75 runs a week in both), so the swap is not what moves these; they are the seed-sensitive thin targets noted on 2026-10-13, and nothing was tuned. If the sweep wants them clean on seed `leet`, the expedition casual-discovery threshold and the raid boss spread are the ones to look at, not the dungeon.

**Pre-run screen feedback (Misa, 2026-10-14).**

- **Modifier popups are just the modifier's text.** Every modifier glossary entry carried two filler bullets ("Only for the room that has it, and shown before you enter." and "Plan your team and the order it stands in around it."; the four element ones had "Works for every X creature on your team, in this room only." and "Plan your team around it: it is shown before you enter."). They were written per entry, not from a shared template, so all eight entries now have no lines; the popup is the title, the description and the "More in How to play" link. A glossary test pins it.
- **Depth progress.** `depthTrack` now goes from the depth the player is at to the next landmark (the dimmed previous landmark is gone) and says how many depths lie between. `ProgressTrack` got an `edge` mode (one dashed line across the whole width, the last step right-aligned at the right edge), `between` (that many small unlabelled pips on the line, "3, 4, 5, 6" between 2 and 7) and `labelEntry` (the labels become dotted-underline glossary tags). `DepthProgress` is the one use of it for the dungeon. "Sure gear drop" is now "Gear drop" (the landmark chest is still certain; the how-to text still says gear for sure). The Dungeon depth entry lost its numbers line and "It is yours alone" line, so it says only what depth is.
- **Header and after the run.** The pre-run header is the title only: the depth badge, the landmark chip and the streak pips are gone from it (the depth is in the progress below the rooms, where the landmark rewards show on a landmark depth). The end report's "Next run" section now has the depth progress with the depth after the run (`today.depth` is already the new depth) and the streak: its "Streak" label (a glossary tag) and line, then `ui/StreakTrack`, a larger row of the seven days lit up to today with a small gift (a new `gift` pixel glyph) under each reward day, the 3rd and 7th, which are the streak bonuses in `RULES.dungeon.streak.bonuses`. `StreakPips` (the small tag and plain row) was removed with its workbench page; `StreakTrack` and the new `ProgressTrack` modes have workbench pages.

**Correction (no history edited).** The 2026-10-13 entry "Daily dungeon v1" says the sim's players stand the team in the default row (`autoPlace`) and use Rally as the basic ability. That was already outdated when the single row landed: the simulator stands its 3 strongest creatures by `dungeonRowOrder` (setters and supports left, a Guard in the middle, payoffs right) and there is no basic ability; every unlocked creature ability fires at its default round.

## 2026-10-14: Counts use a spaced slash ("1 / 3"), never "of"

**Decision (Misa, 2026-10-14).** Counts use a spaced slash ("1 / 3"). Misa wants "round 1 / 3", never "round 1 of 3". The unit stays after the count: "200 / 2500 XP", "Room 1 / 3", "62 / 337 HP".

**What changed.** Every player-visible counter against a total was converted: the Activity (round, room, week, page, slot, fusion, star, level, HP and XP counts, the aria labels that read them, and the workbench samples), and the Discord finale post ("2 / 3 parties won"). The game content (glossary, how-to-play, descriptions) had none. The unspaced "(2/4 used)" of the live other-abilities header is now spaced too. Ordinary prose that only uses the word "of" is left as written ("3 of your creatures", "the 3rd and 7th day of every 7"). Two guard tests (`apps/activity/src/count-wording.test.ts` and `packages/game/test/count-wording.test.ts`) fail on any `<n> of <m>` or `${a} of ${b}` in string literals, templates and JSX text (and in the content JSON), with a pointer to this rule and a tiny allowlist for justified prose (empty today). The operator pages (`ops-render.ts`) and the simulator's reports are not player-facing and were not changed.

## 2026-10-14: The living room's lounger and `/api/room`

**Decision.** Each player has one **lounger**, the creature that sits in the living room, stored on the player (`players.lounger_id`, a nullable column from migration 21, no backfill). It is picked lazily: whenever `GET /api/state` or `POST /api/room` reads it and finds it missing, gone (fused away) or busy, the server picks a new idle creature of the player at random (`crypto.getRandomValues` through `pickLounger` in `@creatures/game`) and stores it, or stores NULL when every creature is busy. A player with no creatures gets null and nothing is written. The choice is not remembered for later: whoever is on the couch is the lounger. `PUT /api/lounger` lets the player choose (404 `creature_not_found` unless theirs, 409 `creature_busy` on an expedition or a workshop job).

**Creature permanence.** A creature does one thing at a time, so one on an expedition or an open workshop job cannot lounge. The raid is the one exception: raiders can lounge, so a raid team never changes who is idle. The rule reuses the "busy" meaning of the state view (`away_expedition_id` or an open `workshop_jobs` row).

**`POST /api/room` trusts the ids the client sends.** The body is up to 25 distinct snowflakes (duplicates are a 400, not merged); they only say who to look up, never who is asking (that comes from the session). The answer covers players of this guild who chose a starter; everything else is left out silently, so the endpoint does not reveal which ids exist. It shows one creature of a guild-mate (species, level, nickname, quirk, Crafting level, Explorer rank) plus the sanitised display name: the same data class the raid roster already shows, scoped to the guild's own Durable Object and capped at 25 ids a request. It goes through the normal session auth and per-player rate limit. It reads in bounded queries (one for players, one per looked-up player to resolve the lounger, three for the creature details) and may write, so it runs in one transaction.

## 2026-10-14: Living room presence comes from the Discord SDK, not the server

**Decision.** Who is "here" is the activity instance's participant list from the Embedded App SDK (`getInstanceConnectedParticipants` plus `ACTIVITY_INSTANCE_PARTICIPANTS_UPDATE`). The server keeps no presence: no heartbeat, no WebSocket, no polling. A client asks `POST /api/room` once at start, then only for newcomers (debounced 2 s); leaves cost nothing; a tab switch refetches only when the data is over 60 s old.

**Why.** Misa wants idle games to cost nothing. Discord already pushes joins and leaves to every client.

**Tradeoff.** Presence is per activity instance: players who opened the game from different channels do not see each other. Misa accepted this. A guild-wide room would need a server heartbeat.

**Also.** Spot assignment is client-side and deterministic (FNV-1a of instance id and user id), so everyone in an instance sees the same room. Speech bubbles and the idle pan are local cosmetics using browser crypto randomness; they are not game state, so the server-RNG rule does not apply to them.

## 2026-10-14: Raid phases end at a floor, and bosses enrage when time runs out

**Decision (Misa, 2026-10-14).** Every week has a finale. Each raid phase has an HP floor; when the boss reaches it, it turns immune and the phase ends. Damage past the floor counts for nothing (no overkill), because counting it would be unintuitive; instead the replay and the phase card say clearly why the boss is gone and what happens next, in a per-boss line with a shared fallback. The finale carries 15% of the boss's HP (the Phase 2 floor), which Misa finds "lots to do and it's still exciting". When the finale's rounds run out, the boss enrages: multi-target hits at high damage that wipe a typical row in 2–3 rounds, with a slim chance of a win. Dungeon boss rooms enrage too instead of simply timing out, so players learn the mechanic daily; Misa may walk this back if it plays badly. An enrage loss is a normal loss.

**How.** New scalars `RULES.raid.phases[].floorBp`, `RULES.raid.enrage` and `RULES.dungeon.enrage` (the last can be switched off). Built with the attack patterns as one raid retune, because both change raid lethality and the win band, and enrage uses the multi-target moves. Details: `raid-ux-2.md`, "Raid structure".

## 2026-10-14: Raid neighbours stop at row edges

**Decision.** In the raid, "beside" means directly left or right **in the same zone** (Front, Middle or Back, the rows of the board). The last slot of Front is no longer beside the first slot of Middle. The engine has a new `zoneNeighbours(index, length)`; the plain `neighbours()` is kept for the dungeon only. The Support buff uses the zone-aware version in the raid. The client's "who works with this creature" highlight (`adjacentSlots`) uses the same engine helper, so the board cannot drift from the fight.

**Why.** The raid board is now a grid of three rows (Front, Middle, Back) by one lane per player. A grid can only show adjacency honestly if it means "next to me in my row": a tile at the end of one row cannot be drawn beside the first tile of the next. The attack patterns that come next (cover, Slam splash) also depend on "beside" being the same thing the player sees.

**What it touches.** Only the Support buff in the raid. The raid's Guard intercept already worked by zone (`guardReach: 'zone'`), so it is unchanged. The dungeon is a row of three with one slot per zone, so zone-aware adjacency would leave everyone with no neighbours; dungeon fights set `guardReach: 'adjacent'`, which keeps the plain line for both the Support buff and the Guard. A dungeon test (a middle Support still buffs both sides) and the unchanged dungeon fight tests prove it; the dungeon sim section is identical on seed leet2 and differs on leet and leet3 only by run counts shifted by the raid results upstream of it (the dungeon fights themselves are untouched).

**Sim result** (full `pnpm sim`, before and after, seeds leet, leet2, leet3). The raid targets barely move:

- Typical-group finale win: leet 59.7% to 60.3% (limit 40% to 60%, now **fails** by 0.25 points), leet2 51.9% to 52.2%, leet3 52.7% to 52.6%.
- Phase 2 win: leet 82.6% to 82.9%, leet2 78.9% to 79.2%, leet3 81.6% to 81.7%.
- Boss spread: leet 12.5% to 9.8%, leet2 12.2% to 13.2%, leet3 17.7% to 18.1% (already failing before this change on leet3, limit 15%).
- Finale win with 15 players, leet3: 43.1% to 42.6%.

No retune was made; the numbers are handed to the lead. `exp-casual-discovery` also failed before this change on leet (0.34 to 0.32) and leet2 (0.32 to 0.32), so it is unrelated. `pnpm scalars` output is unchanged.

## 2026-10-14: Raid UX pass 2, slices 4, 6 and 10 (stage head, glossary, finale row, coach mark)

**Stage head (decided 3).** `ui/StageHead` is the one head of the fight stage, for the dungeon and the raid: the room or phase at the top left ("Room 1 / 3", "Phase 1 / 3") and the round at the top right ("Round 2 / 12"). It replaces the joined "Room 1 / 3 · Round 2 / 2" of PR #18. The finale is the third phase but goes by its own name, "Finale" (`phaseLine` in `raid-text.ts`), as it does on every other screen. The raid stage names the boss under its sprite and writes its HP on the bar (the shared `SegmentBar` `text`), so the head no longer carries "Boss HP x / y".

**Ordinals follow the engine's order in the raid.** Under each creature the stage writes its place in the round on show (`actingSlots` in `stage.ts`). The dungeon always acts left to right, so it is the row order. The raid's orders vary by boss, so it reads the `turn` and `skip` events of that round: the numbers are exactly what the engine did, with no copy of the order rules in the client. A creature with no turn that round (knocked out earlier) has no ordinal. The boss card's turn-order icon stays.

**Raid replay plays by itself.** Pause, 2x and Skip are hidden unless `showControls` is set, as in the dungeon, and the replay starts playing at once (it used to wait for Play). `TurnStrip` is no longer used by any screen; it is kept with its workbench page.

**Glossary popups (slice 6).** Every `raid-week` and `finale` entry has no lines (title, description, the "More" link), and a finale ability's popup is only its own description from the content. Not touched: `crit` (also used by the dungeon, keeps its odds), the marks, reactions, roles and elements (core to every mode) and the Boss Rank, Festival and Season entries (their lines are the rules). The Counter-move's one line now says what it does ("After Phase 1 the boss resists what worked best.") since its numbers left with its lines. A glossary test pins it.

**Boss Rank and season row after a finale.** Under the finale result, `FinaleProgress` draws the Rank as the dungeon's edge-to-edge `ProgressTrack` (the Rank for next week, lit, and the next one with "A tougher boss" and "More Essence", in words) and the season as a `PipRow` with the Festival as the gold-ringed last pip. The Rank shows once the ladder has moved (`rankChange`, null until every party has finished). The season week is known when the finale was a Festival's, or when the raid page is on the same week; otherwise the pips are left out rather than guessed.

**Coach mark (decided 5).** `SHOW_COACH_MARK` in `coach.ts` is off. The Raid tab checks it; nothing else draws the component, and a test pins both. The workbench page still draws it.

## 2026-10-14: Raid board tiles, sit-out removal

**Decisions.**

- **Tile look.** The element colour is the tile's border (the "element ring"). The owner is a one-letter badge in the corner, in the owner's colour (the existing roster-based `owner-0` to `owner-7` palette, with a text colour chosen per owner for contrast). Raid tiles show no slot number; places are written as "Middle, lane 1" everywhere (team cards, screen reader labels, announcements). Names are one line with an ellipsis, never broken mid-word; the full name is in the popover and the aria label.
- **Other players' creatures.** Tapping one (with nothing picked up) opens the creature popover, which carries the owner's full name, instead of only showing "That slot belongs to X".
- **Dragging.** Dragging near the left or right edge of the board scrolls it sideways, so off-screen lanes can be reached; near the top or bottom the page scrolls as before.
- **Sit-out.** Opt-outs are no longer honoured: the roster ignores `raid_opt_outs`, and the client has no button or status line. The route `PUT /api/raid/opt-out` stays for one release as a validating no-op (always answers not opted out) so no client breaks mid-week; the table and route go in a later migration.

**Why.** Misa's review of the raid page on a phone: a grid where lanes line up, compact tiles, and no weekly button that can hurt the party by accident. Dropping the server check at once means nobody who sat a week out with the old button stays stuck out after the button is gone.

## 2026-10-14: The raid stage is a lane grid, like the placement board

**Decision.** The raid fight stage (replay and live finale) draws its party the way the placement board does: Front, Middle and Back are three lines that never wrap, a lane is a column, the row names are a fixed column at the left, and the three lines scroll sideways together in one scroller. Tiles are small (sprite scale 2, 3.25 rem wide, one-line name with an ellipsis) so about four lanes fit on a 360 px phone. Before the fight starts the stage scrolls to the viewer's own creatures; as it plays it follows the creature that acts, and only moves when that creature is not on screen with a tile to spare. The scroll is instant under `prefers-reduced-motion`, and it waits four seconds after the player scrolls by hand. A lane-number row sits above the grid; the lane pips are not used on the stage, because the stage is already tall and the numbers give the same orientation. The dungeon's single row is unchanged (checked pixel for pixel at 360 and 390 px).

**How.** The rows and lanes come from `boardRows`, `placeOf` and `laneStates` in `raid-board.ts` (which use the engine's `ZONES` and `zoneIndices`), plus one new pure helper, `keepSpanInView`. The grid CSS the board and the stage share moved to `components/lane-grid.css`; the stage's own rules are in `stage/stage-grid.css`. The measured tier picking (`STAGE_LAYOUT`) is gone, since nothing wraps any more.

**Known limits.** A reaction burst or a strike beam aimed at a creature outside the window is clipped by the scroller (the beam is drawn to where the creature is). Floating numbers rise less than before, so the scroller does not cut them off. Adjacent lanes owned by the viewer touch.

**Why.** Misa's phone review: with 11 players a zone has 11 creatures, and wrapped boxes hid which creature stands beside which. The same grid on the board and the stage means a lane looks the same in both places.

## 2026-10-14: Raid UX pass 2, slices 8 and 9 (placement cues, influence, the shared `affects` helper)

**The shared helper.** `affects(row, index, mode)` in `packages/engine/src/affects.ts` says which slots the creature at `index` affects: `buff` (a Support's neighbours), `cover` (the slots a Guard takes hits for) and `reaction` (partners that set off, or are set off by, a reaction with it). It is built from the engine's own rules: `neighbours` for the dungeon, `zoneNeighbours` for the raid, the Guard's reach as `guardFor` plays it (the dungeon: right beside it; the raid: every other slot of its zone, never another Guard), and `rowReactions` played in the row's acting order (left to right in the dungeon, the boss's turn order in the raid, so `slotReactions` returns slot numbers for a row with other players' creatures and empty slots). Tests compare it with seeded fights (the `support_buff`, `boss_hit` `from` and `reaction` events), so the screen cannot drift from the fight. Speed-order bosses need each creature's speed; the client only knows species and level, so for those bosses the raid cue is a close guess (gear and fusions are not in the view).

**Cues instead of a text line (decided 4).** The reaction hint line under the dungeon slot row (PR #18) is gone. A reaction pair is drawn on the tiles (a ring in the element colours of the mark and of the hit, a badge with the reaction's name from the content) while a creature is dragged over a slot, picked up (tap or key), hovered or focused. A carried creature is previewed where it would stand. "No reaction" is a small mark under the row (and on the row name in the raid). The old sentence is kept as the screen reader's live announcement. In the dungeon the cue shows every reaction of the order; on the raid board it shows those of the creature in focus only (a whole raid row would light up everything).

**Influence (decided 6).** The tiles a Support buffs (dots) and a Guard covers (bars) are shaded for the creature in focus, and a one-line effect with the real numbers (`RULES.combat.supportBuffBp`, `guardHpBp`, the reaction's multiplier) shows in the creature popover and in the strip under the row. It replaces the raid board's old neighbour highlight (`adjacentSlots`).

**Precedence.** (1) Whose cues show, one creature at a time: the carried creature (dragged or picked up), then the creature whose popover is open, then the one under the mouse or the keyboard focus. (2) Where they draw: a reaction cue is a ring and a badge, influence is a shade of the fill, so a tile can carry both and neither hides the other; a drag's valid or refused outline and the gold ring of a selected tile stay as they are.

## 2026-10-14: Raid Roster: you bring the creatures you place

**Decision (Misa, 2026-10-14).** The raid placement section is redesigned. A player's raid creatures for the week are **the creatures they placed on the board, at most three** (`RULES.raid.teamSize`). The chosen-team feature is gone: no "Choose team", no "Use automatic team", no tray. At every lock (a phase resolving, and the finale's start) each player's empty places up to three are filled with their strongest unplaced owned creatures (`autoTeam` order: power, then the older creature, then the smaller id) and placed with the existing `autoPlace`. A player who placed nothing gets the full automatic team, as before. Misa explicitly wants the auto-fill with the strongest.

**How.**

- `raidTeam(sql, week, user)` in `raid-teams.ts` replaces `effectiveTeam`: the player's placements (only creatures they still own, at most three by `capPlacements`), then the strongest others, sorted strongest first. The fill is **not stored**; it is worked out again whenever a fight is built (`buildPhaseInput`, so also the live finale and practice fights), so the three are always the strongest at that moment and a level-up between phases is picked up. Placed creatures always fight, wherever they stand.
- Placing: the creature must be owned and the week not locked (the finale has not resolved, as before). A creature already on the board moves; a new one needs a free place among the three, otherwise **409 `board_full`**. This includes dropping a new creature onto the slot of one of your own: it is refused too, for a simple rule (remove one first). The rules for occupied slots and swaps stay.
- `PUT /api/raid/team` stays as a validated no-op (old clients mid-week), as `PUT /api/raid/opt-out` did. The `raid_teams` table is kept, no longer read or written, for a later migration. The `team_set` analytics kind stays in the schema (old rows, the rollup) but is no longer logged. The view keeps `you.team.mode`, always `auto`, for older clients.
- **Party balancing** (the snake draft) keeps using the **automatic** team's power (`playerPower`). Parties are made at week start (and for a late joiner before phase 1), when nobody has placed anything, so the automatic team is also what the player brings then; it does not move with what someone happens to place, and the split stays stable and explainable.
- The client has no tray. The count line says "1 / 3 placed · the rest join automatically when Phase 1 locks", from the view's next event.

**Why.** Misa wants players to place their creatures on the shared board themselves, while the balance still relies on everyone's creatures being placed, including those of less active players. "Place what you like, we fill the rest with your strongest" gives both.

## 2026-10-14: A player could have four creatures on the board (bug and repair)

**What happened.** In production one player had four creatures on the raid board; the most is three. **Cause:** `place` only checked that the creature was in the player's _current effective team_ (the three strongest, or the chosen three). The team changes whenever levels, gear or catches change, but a placement made earlier stayed in `raid_placements` (only `setTeam` cleaned up). So after a creature dropped out of the strongest three while still placed, a creature that had just come in could be placed too: three placed rows plus one stale row made four. The view showed every row of the party without filtering, so all four appeared; the fight builder ignored rows of creatures outside the team, so it never fought with four. Nothing counted placements.

**Fix.** `place` now needs a free place among three for a creature that is not on the board (`board_full`). Placements are capped per player by one deterministic rule, `capPlacements`: first the creatures in the player's team as it stood, then the earliest placed (`rowid`; a move places again), then the lowest slot. The view and the fight builder cap with it, and read only creatures their placing player still owns (defence in depth), and every placement write first deletes a player's stale rows beyond three. Migration 22 (`raid-placement-cap-migration.ts`, no schema change) repairs existing weeks with the same rule, using the player's team as it stood (the chosen team if it still belongs to them, else the automatic one). With the team feature gone (previous entry) there is no team preference left, so the read and write guards cap by earliest placed, then lowest slot; only the migration still prefers the old team, because it runs once on data from before.

**Tests.** A regression test reproduces the level-change sequence and failed before the fix (the fourth was accepted); more cover the cap rule, the capped view and fight, the write-time repair and the migration (including running it twice).

## 2026-10-14: The creature picker's effect preview is behind `SHOW_PICKER_EFFECTS`

**Decision.** Each option of the Raid Roster's creature picker shows one quiet line (an ellipsis cuts it) about what the creature would do in the slot being filled, built from the engine's `affects` helper: the first reaction it takes part in ("Steam with Splashet", plus "+N" for more), then "Buffs 2 neighbours" (a Support) and "Covers its row" (a Guard); nothing when it does nothing. The flag is the constant `SHOW_PICKER_EFFECTS` in `apps/activity/src/roster-picker.ts` (on). Misa: "If it crowds the picker I think it's a bad idea, but I do like it and would like to see a version where it's tried."

**How to compare.** The workbench page `RosterPicker` has presets with the effects on and off. Removing the feature is one line (the constant to `false`); the picker keeps working and the lines are not built.

**Limits.** The line uses the same approximations as the board cues: speed is worked out from species and level only (no gear or fusions), so for a boss that acts by speed the reaction shown can differ slightly from the fight. The picker only fills an empty slot, so the line judges the creature standing there with its old slot (if it was on the board) free.

**Touch drag.** A finger picks a creature up after resting on it for 250 ms (`TOUCH_HOLD_MS`); a move before that is a swipe and scrolls the board. The shared drag hook takes it as an option (`touchHoldMs`), so the dungeon team keeps its immediate drag.

## 2026-10-15: Raid page feedback (phone test): boss card holds only the boss

**Decision.** From Misa's phone test of the Raid page: (1) an open creature popover closes the moment a drag lifts on the Raid Roster (mouse, and a finger after the hold), the carried creature's own popover included. (2) The picker's effect line leads with a bold "In this slot:" (only when there is an effect, still behind `SHOW_PICKER_EFFECTS`); it now wraps to a second line instead of being cut with an ellipsis, since the lead takes room at 360 px. (3) The boss card keeps only boss information: the player count line is gone (the Roster's board total covers it), this week's HP is the shared `SegmentBar` with compact numbers written on it (`compactCount`; the exact numbers stay in the aria label), and the season pips with "Week N / 5 this season" moved into the "This week" phase timeline at the top of the page. "Best rank cleared" stays on the boss card (it is about the boss's rank ladder; Misa did not ask to move it). (4) At 3 / 3 placed the Roster line shows a green check (the pixel tick, with spoken "Done:") and drops "the rest join automatically"; with fewer it keeps it. (5) A second line under the count reads "7 / 18 on the board": every creature on the party board out of the party's row length.

**Note.** The picker's own status line ("2 / 3 on the board") counts only the player's creatures; the Roster's board total counts everyone's. Both use the same words as Misa asked, so they may need a clearer wording if players mix them up.

## 2026-10-15: Dungeon team board and Recommended

**Decision (Misa, 2026-10-15).** (1) The dungeon team setup uses the Raid Roster's board and interactions: `ui/RaidBoard` has a `single` mode (one row of three places, no row labels, no lane header, no scroller, larger tiles) and `DungeonTeam` is built on it.
