Tapping an empty place or pressing Enter opens the same creature picker, with its "In this slot:" line read in the dungeon's left-to-right order; tapping a creature opens the standard popover with "Remove from team"; creatures move by drag (press and hold on touch, a drop on another creature swaps, the popover closes when a drag starts); keyboard users use the picker. The count line is "3 / 3 placed" with the green check, no board total. Removed: the "Your creatures" tray list, "Use my strongest three" (as in the raid), "Take out", tap-then-tap-a-slot, and their code (`CreatureTray`, the `SlotRow` component, the selection state, the keyboard pick-up mode, the tray drop target, the geometric key fallback of `dnd-core`), styles, tests and workbench pages. `ui/SlotRow.tsx` became `ui/SlotButton.tsx` because the shared tile is all that is left in it. (2) The creature picker, for both raid and dungeon, shows a small "Recommended" tag on the player's three strongest creatures: no reason or explanation (Misa: "Let's not offer any context into why it's recommended"), no sorting, the same three whatever the slot.

**Strength measure.** The server's `autoTeam` order (power with gear, then the older creature, then the smaller id) moved to `compareStrength` and `strongest` in `packages/game`; `autoTeam` and the client both use them. The client creature now carries `caughtAt` (the server already sent it) and the picker computes power from `statsWithGear`, which is the stats a fight uses, as the server's `TeamCreature.power` does. Tests cover the ranking, the tie-breaks and that the picker order is unchanged.

**The team and the API.** The dungeon team was never stored: the setup screen started with the strongest three (by level and HP) and kept the choice only in the open screen, and the server needs a full team of three for a run. Both stay: the screen starts with the strongest three and keeps the choice while it is open; nothing is stored, and Enter is disabled with fewer than three placed, with the line "Place 3 creatures to enter (N / 3 placed). Tap an empty slot to choose one." **No API change:** `POST /api/dungeon/run` already takes the whole ordered team. One consequence for the starting team: it now uses the same strength measure as Recommended (power) instead of level and HP, so the three tagged Recommended are the three already placed at the start.

**Why.** One board, one picker and one set of gestures for both team screens, so what Misa learns on the Raid page works in the dungeon, and less code to keep (the tray, the selection mode and the second slot row are gone).

**Limits.** With three places a full team has no empty place to open the picker from, so the picker's "3 / 3 placed, remove one first" reason cannot show in the dungeon (it is kept so both pickers share one rule). The team resets to the strongest three when the setup screen is opened again (after a run, or the day's reset), as before.

## 2026-10-15: the dungeon setup opens with the player's last team

Misa's call: the dungeon team setup opens with the player's last team, each creature in the slot it stood in, with no explanatory text. The server already keeps the ordered team of every run in the run's `summary_json` (which, unlike the replay input, is kept after the retention period), so no new table or migration: `GET /api/dungeon/today` (and the `today` inside a run response) now carries `lastTeam`, the creature ids of the caller's newest run on any day, or `null` before the first run. It is read from the verified session's own runs only, through a schema that checks the ids and the team size; a damaged summary gives `null`. The client (`startingTeam` in `dungeon-team.ts`) keeps each id in its slot if the player still owns the creature and the picker offers it, leaves other slots empty without shifting anyone, and falls back to the strongest three on the first run (and if none of the last team can be placed any more, so the row is never empty). Edits made in the setup without starting a run are not stored.

## 2026-10-15: the dungeon's pre-run screen is one card

Misa's feedback: the dungeon is like the raid boss, and the team assembles below it. So "Daily dungeon" and "Your team" are one card, in this order: a header (a big "Daily Dungeon" title with a "?" for its glossary entry at the left, the streak row with its gift days at the right, or under the title on a phone), the three rooms, the depth track, the team board centred with roomy padding, the order line (quiet), the "3 / 3 placed" count with its check, and Enter (with its disabled state and the "Place 3 creatures to enter" line). The title is the shared `PixelHeading` at the boss name's size, not a copy of its CSS. The streak, which PR #18 moved to the end report, is back in the header as `StreakTrack` inside a streak glossary tag. The board is the Raid Roster's `RaidBoard` in its single-row mode, now at the raid's own compact tile size (the larger single-row tiles are gone) and centred; it takes any number of slots in one row. When a run starts, the page scrolls to the fight (smooth, instant under `prefers-reduced-motion`). Nothing changes on the server. The blocked ("Not today") state keeps its separate message under the card.

## 2026-10-15: A player's living-room spot is stored per player; the client settles conflicts

**Decision.** A player can drag their lounging creature to another spot of the living room, and everyone sees it there. The spot belongs to the **player**, not to the creature: it is stored on the player (`players.lounge_spot`, an id from `ROOM_SPOT_IDS` in `packages/game`, and `players.lounge_spot_at`, the server time of the claim; migration 23, both nullable) and stays when the lounger changes. `PUT /api/lounge-spot` takes `{ spotId: string | null }` (a string must be one of the 16 ids; null clears both columns) and answers `{ spot: { id, at } | null }`. `POST /api/room` residents carry the same `spot`, and the player's own state carries `loungeSpot`. A stored id that is no longer a known spot reads as null.

**Conflicts.** The server does not check whether a spot is taken: presence is per Discord activity instance (see `living-room.md`) and the server does not know who is in which instance, so it cannot tell who competes for a spot. Instead every client uses the same rule, so everyone with the same participant list sees the same room. It seats the present players in `(at, userId)` order (a player with no claim sorts last): each keeps their claimed spot unless an earlier claim already holds it, in which case they, like everyone who never chose, get the hash-based spot (`instanceId + userId`, then the next free one). The older claim keeps the spot. Nobody is ever shown twice on one spot, and nothing is lost: the stored claim stays and takes effect again when the earlier claimer is not in the instance.

**Why.** It is one small write per drag and no presence tracking, heartbeat or WebSocket, which keeps the idle game silent. The ids are duplicated in the Activity's `ROOM_SPOTS`; a test in `apps/activity` checks that both lists match in order. The spot ids are also the data of the schema, so removing a spot later only makes old claims read as null.

## 2026-10-15: A 45 s living-room refresh while others are present

**Decision.** While at least one other participant is in the activity instance and the page is visible, each client refetches `POST /api/room` every 45 s (one `setTimeout` chain in `room-loader.ts`). Alone or hidden, no timer waits. Misa approved low-frequency polling so lounger and spot changes reach others without a join.

**Cost.** Five players for two hours a day is about 800 requests a day, about 24,000 a month: well inside the free plan's daily limits and a fraction of a percent of the paid plan's included requests.

**Alternative kept for later.** Durable Object WebSockets with the Hibernation API would make updates instant and allow guild-wide presence at near-zero cost, but they need reconnect handling, a heartbeat through Discord's proxy, careful upgrade auth and a guard that nothing keeps the object awake. Not done until planned.

**Also.** `useDragDrop` is generic over the target type: the raid board and the dungeon keep their slot targets by default, and the living room passes its own `locate` (spot ids).

## 2026-10-15: Dungeon fight feedback: bigger team tiles, a longer room intro, no spoiled round count, traits on the stage

Misa's feedback after trying the dungeon, five changes (Activity only, no server change):

- **Bigger team tiles.** `RaidBoard`'s single-row mode takes `size="large"`: sprites at 3x (sprites only scale by whole pixels, so there is no 2.5x) in a 5.25 x 6.75 rem tile, about halfway between the raid's tile (3.25 x 6 rem at 2x) and the larger one the dungeon had before it shared the raid's. This partly reverses "the larger single-row tiles are gone" above: the same component and the same tile, in two sizes. The raid's size stays the default, and the dungeon team asks for `large`.
- **The count line** ("3 / 3 placed") sits right under the tiles, above the order line; Enter stays last.
- **The room intro** stays 5.2 s (was 2.2 s). A yellow outline just inside the stage's edge starts whole and drains toward the bottom over exactly that time (a CSS animation on a class, with the duration set from code as `--fx-ms`, no inline `style=`). It moves in 48 steps to match the pixel look, and in 6 big steps under `prefers-reduced-motion`; the wait is the same either way. The intro has never been skippable (it lets taps through), so nothing changed there. The banner is remounted on resume so a paused outline starts again with the wait.
- **No spoiled round count.** The stage head, the round intro banner and the forecast no longer use the stored fight's own round count (`room.rounds`, `data.rounds`), which gave the result away ("Round 2 / 3"). They use the fight's limit: the room foe's `rounds` from the content (`roomMaxRounds`), the finale boss's `finaleRounds`, or the phase's `rounds` in `RULES.raid.phases` (`raidMaxRounds`, the same limits the server plays with). Without a limit the head says just "Round 2". The live finale already used `finaleRounds`. The line above the raid replay ("cleared after N rounds") and the after-action report keep the real length: they are results, not part of the playing fight.
- **Traits during the fight.** The head shows each room modifier as a small glossary tag after "Room 2 / 3". The foe's element chip (the shared `ElementChip`) sits right after its name. Tapping the foe or boss picture opens a popover from the popover host (`openFoe`, built like the creature popover): name, HP, element chip (opens its glossary entry in the same popover), its basic attack and tricks as sentences, and its line, all read from the content by id (`foe-info.ts`). In the dungeon a zone hit reads "hits the right creature", as the forecast does. The raid boss gets the chip and the popover too, through the same stage.

## 2026-10-15: boss and foe abilities only ever target living creatures

**Decision.** Every targeting rule in the engine picks among living creatures only, in the raid and the dungeon (one fight engine). Misa saw boss abilities aimed at "the leftmost creature of a row" still land on a creature that was already knocked out, which made the boss easier (the fallen creature acted as a flesh wall). The rules, and what each does when its whole target set is down:

- **Row ends** (`silence_ends`: Bramble Bind, Sea Mist, Thorn Snap, Tangle): the leftmost and the rightmost _living_ creature, not slots 0 and n-1. One creature standing is silenced once. (Before, a fallen end creature made the silence miss.)
- **Zone attack** (`hit_zone`: Undertow, Thorn Rain): hits every living creature of the aimed zone. If nobody in that zone is standing, it falls to the nearest zone that has someone (one step away first; on a tie Front before Back), so a wiped-out zone does not make the attack miss. On the dungeon's row of three (one creature per zone) a fallen creature at that place passes the attack on to the next one. Nothing happens only when the whole row is down (the fight is over then). Misa confirmed the fallback over "do nothing" (2026-10-15). The client forecast uses the same `nearestLivingZone`, so its warning names the zone really hit.
- **Strongest** (`strike_highest_atk`), the **plain attack** (front-most living, or the Guard of its zone), a **random target** (luck): already chose living creatures; now pinned by tests.
- **Guard cover**: only a living Guard intercepts (already true; tested for zone and adjacent reach, and for a Guard knocked out mid-fight).
- **Support buffs**: a living Support buffs its living neighbours; beside a fallen creature it buffs nobody there (no skipping over the fallen, that would change the board's adjacency).
- **Re-mark's owner default** (`own`, no chosen element): the element of the player's first _living_ creature.
- **Left as they were:** a creature ability's own `strike` still lands if its creature is knocked out (an explicit design rule, the source is not a target), and Shield Wall `own` covers the zone of its creature even when it fell (a zone, not a creature).

**Result.** No golden or hand-checked fight changed; one test did (`hit_zone` skips knocked-out creatures: it used to expect no strike once the back zone was wiped out, now it expects the strike on the living Middle). New tests in `packages/engine/test/targets-alive.test.ts`, each with the would-be target already down. Full sim, before (79584b2) to after, seeds leet / leet2 / leet3: typical finale win rate 60.25% / 52.2% / 52.6% to 56.1% / 50.6% / 49.3%; typical Phase 2 clear 82.9% / 79.2% / 81.7% to 79.8% / 75.7% / 77.2%; boss spread 9.8% / 13.2% / 18.1% to 7.0% / 17.2% / 12.2%; `exp-casual-discovery` 0.32 / 0.32 / 0.25 to 0.18 / 0.14 / 0.302. Raids got a little harder, as expected, since bosses lose their flesh wall. No retune: see B-006. **Cost to know:** the Activity's boss warning for a zone attack names the aimed zone; when that zone is empty the hit now lands on another one, so the text is then slightly off (not changed here).

## 2026-10-15: the raid retune: attack patterns, phase floors and enrage (how it is built)

Misa decided the game feel on 2026-10-14 (`attack-patterns.md` section 7, `raid-ux-2.md` "Raid structure"). These are the technical choices the lead made while building it:

- **The phase floor is the clear threshold.** No new `floorBp` scalar: `RULES.raid.phases[].clearAtLossBp` (now 3000 and 8500, so 70% and 15% of the HP are left) is also the floor. The worker passes it to the fight as `FightInput.bossHpFloor` (`phaseFloorHp`, rounded the same way as the clear threshold so reaching the floor always clears the phase). A hit that reaches the floor counts only down to it, the engine emits `retreat` and the fight ends at once. One number, so "cleared" and "drove it to its floor" can never disagree.
- **Close calls.** A cleared phase can no longer overshoot, so its "spare" is the rounds left over, as the finale's already was.
- **Enrage is one generic move**, not the boss's own patterns at a multiplier: in each enrage round the boss makes its plain attack and then hits every living creature for `powerBp` of its Attack (area hits, so Guard cover and Shield Wall soften it, and the single-hit cap holds). Its gimmicks rest, and a charge is never announced to land in an enrage round. This works for every boss (the Storm Sovereign has no multi-target move), reads in one line ("hits every creature"), and is the same rule in the dungeon's boss room. `RULES.raid.enrage` (3 rounds) and `RULES.dungeon.enrage` (2 rounds, `enabled` switch); the powers are tuned by the simulator.
- **Stored fights keep their rules.** `FightInput.rules` (`coverBp`, `maxHitBp`, `supportFx`), `bossHpFloor` and `enrage` are copied in when a live fight is built (`live-rules.ts`), as `luck` is; absent means the rules before this change, so every stored fight replays unchanged. Pattern dice (40 a round) are drawn after every older die and only for a boss with a pattern op.
- **A slam can aim `left`** (the left-most living creature), for the Slag Tyrant's Anvil Fall in the dungeon ("drops on the left creature"); the design's three aims did not cover it.
- **Landmark bosses** go through the room bosses in content order (depth 7 the first, 14 the second, and round again); other days draw their rooms exactly as before.
- **The retune re-based the raid's sim bands** (B-007 in `docs/balance/log.md`). With floors at 70% / 15% left, a team that reaches the Phase 2 floor wins its finale almost always, so the old bands (Phase 2 cleared 70% to 85% of weeks, finale won 40% to 60%) cannot both hold. The bosses are fitted to a typical finale won about 92% of the time at placement rank (the Boss Rank ladder still pulls a long-running server back to about 55%), Phase 1 cleared about 86% and Phase 2 rarely. Three targets are new: knock-outs 10% to 22% of the row, a Guard worth at least 2 win points, no unfair barrage repeats. Pattern powers and the single-hit cap are as designed.
- **Enrage resolves in the normal order**: creatures act first, then the boss's attack and the enrage hit. So the first enrage round is a free round for the team, and about one Sunday in twelve is won in overtime. Raid enrage `powerBp` 50000 and dungeon 10000; above about 400% the single-hit cap sets the hit, so a wipe takes at least two rounds.

## 2026-10-15: Misa's answers on the retune: floors, overtime, the Wyrm, and the Guard reworked

- **Floors stay at 70% / 15% HP left.** Saturday's fight rarely drives the boss to its 15% floor; that is fine (its damage still counts). The floors decide how much HP the boss brings to Sunday, not how often the finale is won: that is mostly the Boss Rank ladder.
- **Overtime stays as built.** The team acts before the first enrage hit; about one finale in twelve is won in overtime, a clutch moment. Same order as every other round, no special rule.
- **Backdraft stays.** The Cinder Wyrm keeps knocking out the most creatures; that is its character.
- **A Guard takes reflected hits.** A reflected reaction hit on a creature a Guard protects goes to the Guard, like any other hit on one creature (zone in the raid, beside it in the dungeon). Misa: "a guard sitting next to a reaction-triggering creature would take the reflected hit onto itself. I think that is what players would expect." It stays uncapped (the cap is for the boss's own attacks).
- **Two Guards: a roll.** When more than one living Guard could take a hit, a die decides which (drawn at round start, so replays stay exact).
- **Guard armor instead of +30% HP.** With the new rules a Guard has no extra HP and takes less damage from every hit it takes (start 25%, tuned by the sim). Armor is a trait meant to grow with level or gear later; it is read through one function so that is a local change. The lead chose "every hit" over "only hits taken for others" so a Guard stays tough against sweeps it cannot step in front of; the damage armor saves on a hit taken for another creature is credited to the Guard. Stored fights without `rules` keep the +30% HP.
- **Rules written like a card game's.** Misa wants the combat rules defined as precisely as Magic: The Gathering's or Arkham Horror LCG's: what an attack, a hit and damage are, the order one hit resolves in, what triggers what. A rules reference (`docs/design/combat-rules.md`) is written from the engine, and the glossary's role and move entries follow its wording.
- **No single-hit cap (Misa, 2026-10-15).** The cap (a boss hit takes at most 50% of a creature's max HP) is switched off: `RULES.combat.maxHitBp` 0. It was a guard rail against high Boss Rank, where boss Attack is about three times today's at rank 9, but it is invisible to players ("a hit for a million does half your HP") and the Boss Rank ladder already stops a server that keeps losing from climbing. Big hits stay big; a newcomer's level-1 creatures on a veteran server can be flattened, and a Guard visibly saves a friend. The number stays in the fight rules (0 = off) for a possible mutator. If high ranks collapse, the next lever is separate curves for boss HP and boss Attack by rank (Misa), and raid tiers for newcomers later.
- **What a raid should feel like (Misa).** The finale is kill or wipe: the boss dies, the row is wiped, or overtime runs out; creatures that fall stop dealing damage. A greedy row (setters and payoffs packed together, no Guards) should hit harder but die more and swing more; a protected row should survive more. Neither should simply be best: the sim measures both, week by week, and the bands catch broken numbers without flattening the swings.

## 2026-10-15: Guard follow-ups: one pick rule, healing credit, the client reads the fight's Guard

- **One pick rule for several Guards.** With `rules`, every hit that goes to "the Guard" uses one rule (`pickGuardWith` in `patterns.ts`): the die of the slot the hit is aimed at picks among the living candidates. That covers the plain attack, a random-target plain attack, a slam aimed `front` (the front-most creature's slot, so it picks the same Guard as the plain attack that round), `strongest`, `left` and `random`, a chain's first hit, and a reflected hit. A lance has no pick (the first Guard in its lane stops it, by geometry), and chain jumps, splash, sweeps and enrage are area hits that no Guard steps in for. Cover is not a hit taken: its credit still goes to the left-most Guard beside the creature. Without `rules` nothing changed (replay fixture green).
- **Healing credit is new credit, not a move.** The property test's "credit is zero-sum" failure with the live Support effects was Mend (Tide, Grove): the HP a Support really restores is credited as `supportCredit` by design (`attack-patterns.md` section 5), like what a Guard absorbs, so it was never part of the damage dealt. Spark's Overcharge and the buffs were zero-sum all along (a focused test and a 1500-fight stress run with a phase floor confirm it). The invariant now subtracts the healed HP (from the `heal` events), and the property test runs with the live rules again.
- **The client reads the fight's Guard.** The replay and live views carry `rules: { guardHpBp, guardArmorBp }` (null for a fight stored without rules). The stage's HP estimate uses them (the content's old +30% only without rules), and Guard text on the boards (before any fight) uses `RULES.combat.guard`: "takes 25% less damage from every hit". A boss hit that a Guard's armor reduced shows a small shield pip on the Guard's left (`armored` on `boss_hit`).
- **Rules fixes from the rules reference** (`docs/design/combat-rules.md`, written from the engine). With fight rules: a Shield Wall protects the creature that **takes** the hit, after a Guard steps in (the research's "the final recipient's modifiers apply"; it only changes the dungeon, where a Guard stands in another row); a chain never hits the same creature twice, including a Guard that took its first hit; the killing blow on the boss counts only the HP it had left (no overkill, as at a floor). A hit on a creature still **shows its full damage** (a 300 hit on a creature with 130 HP shows 300, as in card games damage dealt can exceed what is left), while HP lost and credit stop at the HP left. Terms: **row** is Front, Middle or Back (as the forecasts say), **line** is the whole formation; code still says zone.

## 2026-10-15: Staging deploys only on request; main locked against agents

**Decision.** Pushing a branch no longer deploys staging. Staging changes only with a manual run of the `deploy` workflow (`workflow_dispatch`) on a chosen branch; a manual run always targets staging, even on `main`. Production still deploys from every push to `main`, and only from that (a guard step fails the job otherwise). The deployed branch shows in the footer (`BRANCH` Worker var, set by CI, validated before `/api/config` shows it).

**Why.** With two sessions working at once, every push redeployed staging, so the branches took turns and it was unclear what Misa was testing. Misa wants control over what is on staging.

**Agents.** An agent starts the staging run only when Misa asks in that session. The `main` ruleset (PR required, no bypass) stops direct pushes from everyone, agents included, so no agent-side push guard is kept (a hook was written and dropped as redundant). Merges are different: the GitHub connection acts as Misa's account, so GitHub cannot tell an agent's merge from Misa's, and `.claude/settings.json` denies the merge and auto-merge tools and `gh pr merge`.

**Considered.** A pinned staging branch in a repo variable (still automatic, but easy to forget to switch) and a second staging slot with its own Discord app (true parallel testing, but much more setup). Either can be added later.

## 2026-10-15: a player journey simulator, and the planned Bait allowance

- **Journey simulator (Misa: "sounds good").** The balance sim gets player profiles as dials (login days, check-in times including night, leet rate, how many creatures share the Bait, expedition uptime, dungeon and raid skill, crafting), four presets (min-maxer, engaged, regular, drop-in), and a per-player journey log: milestones, power, the one limit holding the player back at each check-in, and waits. The raid and dungeon sims then draw rosters from journeys. Design: `docs/design/player-journey-sim.md`. Built after PR #24.
- **Planned Bait income (Misa, numbers by the lead).** A daily allowance of 30 Bait for opening the game, plus a leet bonus of 15 to 25 Bait by how many players leeted (15 for 1 or 2, then 17, 19, 20, 21, 22, 23, 24, 25 at 10 or more), keeping the leet table's rules (more leeters never pays less, solo equals double, each extra adds less). A day pays 30 to 55. Not shipped yet: the sims model it as a second income rule first, and it ships with its own balance pass.

## 2026-10-15: an open raid week is declared again when the difficulty rules change

**Decision (Misa: "fine with basically redeclaring the entire raid, assuming the boss stays the same and the roster is unchanged").** The worker has a `RAID_DIFFICULTY_VERSION` (`raid-difficulty.ts`; bump it whenever the rules behind a week's boss numbers change). Each raid week stores the version its Boss Rank was declared with. If a week is still open, no stage of it has resolved and nothing real is live, and its stored version is older than the current one, the guild object declares its numbers again on its next normal path (the alarm or any authenticated request; also right before Phase 1 resolves): rank (the server's placement for its first week, the ladder's rank after) and effective rank, with the current rules. Same boss, week id, sign-ups, board, abilities and choices. It is logged as `raid_week_redeclared`. A week with a resolved stage and every past week are never touched.

**Why.** The boss's HP pool and Attack are not stored until Phase 1 resolves (scaled from the week's rank and the head count at that moment), so they already follow the current rules; only the stored rank could go stale. Week opening and re-declaration share one function (`declareWeekRank`), so they cannot diverge. Weeks from before the column have version 0, so the first deploy with version 1 re-declares the running week.

## 2026-10-15: the Bait allowance and leet bonus ship

- **Numbers are the `RULES.income` contract**, not the earlier planning numbers in the journey design (30 and 15 to 25): a daily allowance of 25 Bait and a leet bonus of 15 to 32 (`leetBonusForDay`, by how many players leeted that day). A solo leet day pays 40, a day with 10 or more leeters 57. The balance pass still follows with the sim.
- **A leet credits `leetBonusForDay(n)`**, not `baitForDay(n)`. The sync only sees finalised days, so `n` is the day's final count and there is no top-up; the per-player key `leet:<day id>` still keeps a re-delivered day from paying anyone twice. Leet points are still computed and kept in the ledger meta (`points`, with the new `bonus`). `baitForDay` stays exported for the simulator's "current" income model and is unused by the game.
- **The allowance is granted in the guild object's `handle()`**, on a player's first authenticated request of a game day (13:37 guild-local, DST-safe through `gameDay`). The Bait is a ledger credit with source `allowance` and key `allowance:<game day>` (unique per player and day), written in the same transaction as the `daily_allowance` row (migration 25) and a `daily_allowance` analytics event, so retries and parallel requests never pay twice. Missed days are never paid later. The grant is idempotent and never fails a request.
- **The client is told once.** `GET /api/me` returns `allowance: { day, amount }` on the first call after a grant and `null` afterwards (the row's `announced` flag is set in the same step); the Activity shows "Daily allowance: +25 Bait" on its notice line. The history list shows the credit as "daily allowance".
- **Tests only:** `GuildCoreOptions.dailyAllowance: false` switches the grant off; the shared test helper does so, so other features keep exact Bait balances.

## 2026-10-15: rank-gated boss moves, more boss attacks, and two targeting options that punish setups

- **Why (Misa).** "Let's not get locked down with a one dimensional difficulty scale. Later bosses can vary attack patterns, crits, how many times the boss attacks, which moves the boss uses (moves that punish certain player setups should absolutely be introduced to the move pool as ranks go higher)." The setup study (B-008) found a Guard beside each payoff costs almost nothing, so greedy rows had little downside. This change builds the mechanisms and the first content; the per-rank curve (how many of them apply at each rank) comes next.
- **`fromRank` on boss gimmicks** (integer from 2, absent is always). `bossAtRank(boss, rank)` in `@creatures/game` keeps the moves whose `fromRank` is reached, in content order, so a move keeps its place and its pattern dice window. The worker builds the week's boss with it wherever a boss is built for a week: the fight input (so the readiness forecast too), the raid view and boss card, the live snapshot and the Discord boss reveal; the sim's raid path calls it in `openRaidWeek`. A stored fight keeps `input.boss` as it was fought, so replays are untouched by later rank or content changes (the worker test replays stored fights after moving the rank). The client's forecast reads the moves the fight was played with from `EventNames.gimmicks` (built from the stored boss), so it never warns of a move that is not in the fight. Dungeon foes, signatures and room modifier moves may not carry `fromRank` (checked at load). The boss move limit went from 4 to 6.
- **`FightInput.bossAttacks`** (1 to 3, absent is 1): the plain attack is made that many times a round, each picking its target when it is made and having its own crit die. The extra crit dice (only with boss crits on) are drawn last in the round and only when the number is above 1, so older fights replay (the replay fixture is untouched). `RULES.raid.scaling.bossAttacksByRank` (read by `bossAttacksAtRank`) is the per-rank table the curve sets; `[1]` today, and the worker puts the field on the input only when it is above 1.
- **Two new targeting options.** (1) A slam can aim `payoff`: the living Payoff with the highest Attack (the strongest creature if none stands). It is an aimed hit, so a Guard in the row takes it: a protected row shrugs it off, a greedy one loses its carry. (2) A sweep row can be `payoffs`: the row with the most living Payoffs (ties front first). The brief suggested `fullest` (the row with the most creatures) for the second, but every row has the same number of slots, so "fullest" would only ever mean "the row with the fewest knock-outs" and would punish nothing a player chose; the Payoff count is what tells a stacked row from a spread one.
- **Eight new moves** (two for each raid boss, from rank 4 and rank 7; the curve may move the ranks). Tidal Colossus: Deepwater Spear (rank 4, a lance down the lane of the strongest creature: a single carry) and Spring Tide (rank 7, a charged sweep of the Payoff row). Cinder Wyrm: Magma Spit (rank 4, a slam on the strongest Payoff: exposed Payoffs) and Eruption (rank 7, a charged sweep of every row: nobody is out of reach, so Guards' cover is what counts). Thornmother: Choking Vines (rank 4, silences both ends for two turns) and Thorn Lash (rank 7, a chain on the strongest creature that jumps three times: a single carry). Storm Sovereign: Static Field (rank 4, a sweep of the Payoff row) and Judgement Bolt (rank 7, a slam on the strongest Payoff). Powers sit in the range of the existing moves; the periods (7, 9, 5, 7, 6, 7, 5, 7 rounds) were chosen so that no boss has more than two damaging moves in any round at ranks 1, 4, 7 and 10 (a content test). Because the existing periods are fixed, a new move fires only once or twice in a 12-round finale; the curve can change that.
- **Open for the curve.** The sim's `fightFor` does not set `bossAttacks` yet (the sim edit here was one call site); when the table goes above 1 it needs `bossAttacksAtRank(week.rank)` there as the worker has.

## 2026-10-15: the raid finale pays a reward slot: gear (pre-upgraded by rank) or a blueprint

**Decision (Misa: "promise a gear drop for everyone in a successful raid ... another option in the same slot could be a blueprint ... if we drop gear, tie its power to boss rank like the Essence").** Every participant of a won finale gets exactly one reward. A quarter of the time (`RULES.gear.drops.finale.blueprintBp` 2,500) it is a blueprint the player does not know yet; otherwise, or when they know every blueprint, it is a gear piece. Rarity follows the same Boss Rank odds for both. Gear arrives upgraded by `min(5, floor((rank - 1) / 2))` levels (`ranksPerUpgrade` 2): +0 at ranks 1-2, +1 at 3-4, up to +5 from rank 11. The boss card says what a win pays and the reveal shows the item with its rarity and "+N".

**Why.** Gear drops are rare elsewhere, so a promised piece makes the finale exciting; blueprints share the slot because scrap-starved players gain more from crafting options than from another common item; and upgrades by rank give high-rank wins power that grows like the Essence does. Pre-upgrading uses the existing level column, so stats, upgrade prices, dismantle value and display needed no new rules; dismantling a pre-upgraded piece refunds part of the scrap its levels would have cost, a small intended bonus that also eases the scrap shortage.

**Details.** The grant key stays `raid:<week>:finale`, so retries pay nothing and old rows (one gear item, no level) still read as +0. If the rolled rarity's blueprints are all known it steps down, then up, before falling back to gear. Festival finales keep the Festival piece next to the slot. The balance sim still rolls a gear piece (it calls `rollFinaleDrop`, which now carries the level); it does not model the blueprint branch yet.

## 2026-10-16: the raid curve replaces 1.4^rank; every server starts at rank 1

**Decision (Misa's brief).** No rank skipping: a win is income and an achievement. Instead of "each rank is 40% tougher", the raid has a custom curve that we control and do not explain to players: "HP and damage on the raid boss are just some variables we control. Later bosses can vary attack patterns, crits, how many times the boss attacks, which moves the boss uses ... Everything should be based on what we expect from players and their progress." Every week must be winnable ("a full raid team, optimized loadouts, positioning and maybe just good luck"); a server that loses should rearrange and improve; and the first raid week should be clearable fairly easily even by mostly casual players.

**What shipped.**

- **`RULES.raid.curve`**: a table by Boss Rank (1 to 15) with `hpBp` and `atkBp` (independent; they are shares of the boss's own `baseHp` and `baseAtk`, so rank 1 is not 10000), `bossAttacks` (1 to 3) and `critBonusBp` (extra boss crit chance). Above the last row HP grows 5% and Attack 3% a rank (`beyond`). Integer basis points everywhere. `rankFactorBp`, `rankAtkFactorBp`, `bossAttacksAtRank` and the new `bossCritBonusAtRank` / `luckAtRank` / `rankRow` read it; `scaleBoss`, the worker's fight input (so the readiness forecast), the sim and the Boss Rank view all go through them. `rankStepBp`, `atkRankShareBp` and `bossAttacksByRank` are gone. `maxRank` (30) stays as a safety cap. The boss moves that join at ranks 4 and 7 (`fromRank`) are unchanged: measured with them off, the rank-7 set moves a committed server's win rate by about 3 points, so the table, not the moves, carries the difficulty today, and the moves are a content lever for later.
- **Placement is rank 1.** `placementRank` returns `RULES.progression.placement.startRank` (1) whatever the creatures; the level-based rule (`legacyHpPerLevelBp`) is gone. Servers that were placed under the old rule keep their stored rank; the Festival ease (rank - 3) and the season reset still work. The open week is re-declared by `RAID_DIFFICULTY_VERSION` (already 1 on this branch; production has no stored version, so it counts as 0): a server in its first week, not yet resolved, goes to rank 1 and keeps its sign-ups.
- **Shape (docs/balance/log.md B-010).** Rank 1 is a tutorial boss (35% of the calibrated pool, 65% Attack). Ranks 2 to 5 climb steeply while rosters grow fast. From rank 6 the pool is almost flat (power stops growing quickly after week 6) and Attack, boss crit chance (from rank 9) and a second plain attack (from rank 14) carry the difficulty; the rank-gated moves punish setups.
- **Targets** (`curve-*` in `packages/sim/targets.json`, measured on whole 12-week journey servers by `pnpm sim` and `pnpm sim:curve`): first week 85%+ for a casual-heavy server and 90%+ for the default one; ranks 2 to 5 70% to 85% (default) and 95%+ (committed); ranks 6 and up 45% to 65% (default) and 80%+ (committed); the worst rank a committed server reaches in 12 weeks 60%+; Phase 1 cleared 75%+ of default weeks, Phase 2 sometimes (5% to 50%).
- **Old population targets re-based.** The long-run and pity sims now play the default journey server (min-maxer, engaged, regular, drop-in), the population the curve is tuned for; the legacy archetype servers are stronger (everyone opens the game daily). `longrun-win-*` 40% to 60% became 60% to 85%, `longrun-rankup-gap` 2 to 4 weeks became 1 to 2 (a winnable curve raises the rank most weeks), `econ-hard-pity` 15% became 25% (it counts finale wins: at a 75% win rate more players reach ten without a jackpot; the all-win ceiling is about 22%).

**Why not the old step.** At +40% a rank the old ladder was a walkover in weeks 2 to 6 (94% to 100% won, B-009) and then a wall (52% by week 12, 3% at rank 7 for a casual server), and week 1 was a wipe. It had one lever. The curve lets each rank ask for what players can bring that week.

**Open.** Ranks 10 and up are extrapolated from 24 and 36 week simulations, not live data; the presets are guesses until the live report fits them (journey slice 6). Fully casual servers (no engaged player) lose most weeks from rank 2 on (about 45% at ranks 2 to 3), by design: they can still clear week 1, and a server that rearranges, trains and gears climbs.

**Sim.** The balance sim now models the finale reward slot of the merged raid-reward change: a won finale pays each participant a gear piece pre-upgraded by rank or, a quarter of the time, a blueprint they do not know yet (`grantFinaleLoot` calls `rollFinaleReward`).

- **Raid difficulty version 2 (2026-10-16).** Bumped after the custom curve and placement at rank 1, as its rule says, so a week declared under the first re-declare build (version 1, staging only) is re-declared too; production weeks are at 0 and re-declare either way. Accepted from the overnight review: a pre-upgraded raid item refunds some scrap for levels nobody paid for when dismantled (at most about 15 scrap times the rarity multiplier per finale win; scrap is not what limits gear, B-009/progression study); the readiness band can lag one refresh interval after a re-declare (display only).

## 2026-10-15: Tab pips, a saved dungeon team, and the page follows a claim

**Pips.** A small orange square sits on the top right corner of a main tab when something there needs the player, and goes away when nothing does (Misa's request). The reason is also read out after the tab's name ("Expeditions, a trip is home").

- **Expeditions:** an active trip has ended (home, or a push-on choice waiting). **Workshop:** a job is ready to claim. Both are worked out on the client from the trips and jobs it already holds, by the server's clock, with one timer for the next trip or job to run out (`tab-alerts.ts`).
- **Dungeon:** a run can start now (the first-day retry included). **Raid:** the Raid tab would ask the player to set their team (in a party, the finale not started, a team creature without a slot: the same rule as `needsPlacement`). These come from the server, as `attention` on `GET /api/state` only (the answers of actions leave it out, and the client keeps the last one, like `timezone`). The Dungeon and Raid screens also report their own news at once, so a pip clears as soon as the run is made or the last creature is placed.
- **No polling.** `attention.recheckAt` is the soonest time either answer can change by itself (the 13:37 reset, the next raid event); the client asks once then. Otherwise the existing state refresh on every tab switch keeps it current. GET /api/state now also does the raid week lookup the Raid tab does (`currentWeek`, `syncRoster`; idempotent), so a recently active player joins the week's party on their first state read instead of their first Raid tab visit. That matches the no-sign-up roster. If working out `attention` fails, the state is still sent, with `attention: null`.

**Dungeon team draft.** The setup row now survives a tab switch, a reload and another device. Every change is kept in memory at once and sent to `PUT /api/dungeon/team` 800 ms after the last change, and at once when the screen goes away or the page is hidden. `GET /api/dungeon/today` gives it back as `draftTeam` (ids the player no longer owns come back as empty places; an all-empty row stays empty). A run deletes it in the same transaction, so the next setup starts from the team that ran. New table `dungeon_drafts` (migration 26), pruned with the dungeon's other old rows. Not a spend, so no idempotency key; an upsert is harmless to repeat.

**After a claim.** Claiming a trip or a job puts its result card at the top of the tab while the pressed button disappears, and the page used to stay wherever it was. The card now scrolls itself to the top of the screen when its top is out of view (smoothly, at once under `prefers-reduced-motion`) and takes focus when the pressed button is gone (`RevealShell`'s `arrive`). A card already in view is left alone, so coming back to the tab does not move the page.

## 2026-10-16: Misa's direction after the raid retune: supplies now, a systems review, then gear and creature revamps

Misa's answers on the progression overview (the forecast page for PR #24), recorded as decisions:

1. **The week-2 wall for casual-heavy servers stays** (about 39% finale wins in week 2, about 50% in weeks 2–5, then 60–70%).
2. **Ranks 10 and up are checked only in long simulations**; that is enough, because the game will change a lot in the coming weeks.
3. **A dungeon depth picker** (choose the depth, with a likelihood-of-success hint like the raid's) comes later, after the reworks.
4. **Supplies:** no more buying them with Bait. They come as rewards instead (a guaranteed one from the daily dungeon, better tiers deeper), and crafting them in the workshop should be a real, interesting option, with more scrap to make it possible.
5. **Gear rework, genuinely exciting:** gear levels that raise stat caps; modifiers, more of them on higher rarities; every piece rolls differently, so there is a reason to chase the perfect roll; "gear identities", where the pool of possible rolls is limited by something such as the gear's element, slot or the role that can wear it.
6. **Currency sinks belong in the crafting and gear rework:** spending spare currency on crafting should feel like a lottery worth joining because the rewards are exciting.
7. **Collection pacing gets a plan:** are new catches too fast now; what design space new creatures have; what makes a player with a working max-level trio collect and level more.
8. **Step back before the revamps:** review every system, its currencies, rewards and interactions, what each system is for and whether that identity is good, and learn from games with similar systems. "Let's not be afraid of tearing down what we've built in order to make something better." This is `docs/design/systems-review.md`; Misa reviews it before anything is built.
9. **Priority:** the gear and creature revamps come first, after the systems review.

## 2026-10-16: supplies cannot be bought; the dungeon drops one on every run; crafting is how you choose

**Decision (Misa, after the progression study).** Buying supplies with Bait was a trap (a min-maxer was 13% weaker at week 6 for buying them) and nobody crafted one. Supplies now come from three places: the daily dungeon, finds on trips, and the Workshop. Supplies a player already owns keep working. Numbers and the sim comparison: `docs/balance/log.md` B-011.

- **No Bait for supplies.** `POST /api/expeditions` still parses `source: "buy"` (the strict schema keeps the old shape) so an old client gets a clear answer instead of a validation error: `400 supplies_not_for_sale`, raised before anything is written (the idempotency key is not used up, so the same key can send a valid trip). A trip's `cost` is always 0 now (the column stays for trips sent earlier; no migration). The Bait `spend` path is gone from the send, so a send is one transaction of "supplies taken from stock, trip created". The `expedition_send` analytics event lost `bought` and `cost`; the daily rollup still reads `bought` of older events so history stays right. The client sends `owned` only and the picker lists the supplies in stock (an empty stock says where to get them and links to the Workshop).
- **Supply `price` became `worth`.** The content field was the Bait price; it is now only the simulator's estimate of a supply's value (found-supply worth, the sim's choice of kit), never shown and never charged.
- **The dungeon drops the supply.** `RULES.dungeon.perRoom[].supply` is the number of supplies a room drops when it is cleared, and `RULES.dungeon.supplyTiers` the tier odds by depth (good from depth 4 and great from depth 12, rising with depth up to a cap, the rest basic; the kind is drawn evenly). `dungeonPayout` counts the supplies of the cleared rooms and `rollDungeonLoot` rolls them last, after the shard and gear dice, so those are drawn exactly as before. The server rolls them with the live gear RNG inside `grantDungeonRun` (key `dungeon:<day>:<attempt>`), the same grant that pays the run's parts and gear: a retry or a replayed grant draws nothing and adds nothing. The supplies are in the grant row (`gear_grants.result_json.supplies`), in the `gear_drop` analytics event (source `dungeon`, `supplies`, which the rollup already counts as a supply source) and in `dungeon_reward.supplies`; the run's rewards carry `supplies` (absent on runs stored before this change, read as none). Shipped: the first room drops one (a run that clears its first room always takes a supply home, even if lost later).
- **Why the first room.** One per full clear dried up: a min-maxer's dungeon supplies fell from 4.6 a week at week 1 to 1.3 at week 6 and 0.1 from week 12 (the depth wall: full clears are rare there), a regular's from 1.8 to 0.3, so a "guaranteed supply" would not have been true. One per room gave a regular 6 to 8 a week and a stock of 8 to 10 it could not carry, a drop-in a stock of 22 by week 30, and cut the engaged crafted share to 23% (target 25%). One per run (the first room cleared) is 6 to 7 a week for a min-maxer or an engaged player, 4 for a regular and about 1 for a drop-in, in every week.
- **Scrap and recipes.** The sim found scrap is not what limits crafting for engaged, regular and drop-in players (their scrap piles grow whatever is added) and that supply crafting competes with gear for a min-maxer's scrap. So the lever is cheaper recipes (scrap 3, 6, 10 became 2, 4, 7; the shard costs are unchanged) and 1 scrap in the first dungeon room next to the supply (the 1 scrap of the boss room stays). The rank-gift and trip scrap are unchanged.
- **Workshop UI.** The Workshop says supplies cannot be bought and has a button to the supply section; the send flow's supply step says where supplies come from and links to the Workshop. The pre-run dungeon card carries the promise ("Clear the first room and you take home a supply, guaranteed. Better ones start at depth 4"), built from `RULES`, and the result screen shows what dropped as a count badge with the supply's name.
- **Sim.** The `supplyPercent` dial and the Bait budget are gone; a trip carries the supplies in stock. The workshop makes a supply when fewer than 4 are in stock or being made (before it crafts gear; a new player's first craft still comes first, for the first-craft target) and leaves alone the scrap of the next two upgrades of every worn item (the study's `q1-keep`). `pnpm sim:supplies` runs and compares variants. The legacy digest was re-pinned on purpose (no Bait goes to supplies, the dungeon drops them).
- **Open.** With nothing to buy, a min-maxer's Bait piles up from about week 13 when every creature is at its level cap (3,481 at week 20 and 14,814 at week 30; before, supplies took 250 to 480 a week): the progression study's levels 31 to 40 would use it, not done here. Scrap piles for engaged and regular players are larger than before (regular 367 at week 20 against 190; engaged 604 against 39) because their workshop turns now make cheap supplies instead of gear that was salvaged back into scrap; scrap has no other use for a player who does not craft (B-011).
- **Raid curve left alone (open).** Players are 5% to 12% stronger at weeks 4 to 12 now that Bait does not go to supplies, so on the full `pnpm sim` (seeds leet, leet2, leet3) `curve-r2to5-default` is 85.3% to 87.0% (limit 85%), `curve-r6up-default` 69.3% on one seed (limit 65%), and the sweep's `raid-finale-typical`, `raid-ko-share` and `choice-creature-min` are just outside theirs. The curve shipped this morning, so it was not retuned here; a tested retune fixes those but breaks `longrun-loss-streak` (B-011). Misa's call.

## 2026-10-16: Misa's answers to the systems review (they replace parts of its proposals)

Answers to the ten questions in `docs/design/systems-review.md`. Where they differ from the review's proposals, these win.

1. **Three gear slots**, each with its own job and roll pool (not three generic slots).
2. **Gear's share of power depends on the stage of the journey:** early power comes mostly from creatures; as creature growth slows or caps, gear becomes the way to grow. No single target share.
3. **Fight-triggered modifiers come later.** Ideas for that design space: modifiers that depend on or are boosted by the creatures next to the wearer, its row, its distance from the edge; set bonuses; chance-based effects whose chances grow with item level and rarity (Path of Exile and ARPGs as references).
4. **No rerolls, and nothing that only improves an item without risk, for now** (future design space). The crafting loop: find or craft an item with rolled modifiers; a bad one is dismantled in the Workshop for a fraction of its cost, shown up front as a range (for example 2–4 scrap). Dismantling gives the creature Crafting XP and a small chance to learn the item's blueprint; extra finds of the same blueprint stack a small chance of crafting a higher-rarity version. Higher-rarity crafting is gated by Crafting level. **Enchanting is dropped** until the crafting loop is settled.
5. **Existing gear is converted, not necessarily 100% fairly;** refunding it as scrap and other materials is fine.
6. **Duplicates become a choice:** teach a talent (or a meaningful skill), fuse, or release. Release pays something other than Bait if it fits better (lead's call).
7. **Evolution:** no de-evolve or re-evolve; evolved forms are rare or absent in the wild; two sequential evolutions (base, first, final) are welcome for depth. Training should become more than "spend Bait, level up at once", and fusion must stay meaningful.
8. **Weekly boss rules: yes**, as weather, omens or boons, with plenty of variety, allowed to be extreme at high raid ranks, and possibly affecting dungeons and expeditions too.
9. **Spare Essence goes into advanced features**, not training: gear crafting, and later dungeon choice rooms ("spend 20 Essence to enter this room").
10. **No levels past 30** for now.

Also asked: whether using a supply is loosely net positive (not every time; randomness is fine).

## 2026-10-16: Misa's second round on the revamps: weighted slot pools, training by activity, Boons and Omens

1. **Slots: Hat, Scarf, Charm** (Cape kept as a possible future slot). Slots are **not** locked to one kind of stat: each slot's roll pool is **weighted** toward its theme (Hats lean to attack, and so on) and may roll other lines too. A few lines are **exclusive** to one or two slots (Path of Exile style: only boots, rings and amulets roll movement speed), so players know where to hunt for them. Charms are **not** role-locked as a rule; role-locked items may exist, but the interesting choice is finding the item with the right lines for a particular creature.
2. **Gear upgrades (+1 to +5) go away.** An item's level is fixed when it drops or is crafted.
3. **Items remember who crafted them and when** (creature and date), for the "this item is mine" feeling and any future gifting.
4. **Dismantling** may have a small random range, improved by the creature's Crafting level, and may take time. Workshop odds need not be fully shown; showing them is fine.
5. **Training by activity:** creatures gain experience from expeditions, the dungeon and raids, with limited activity slots making the player choose who goes where; Bait becomes treats that speed it up. Every 10 levels there is a gate, but not one that demands a deep dungeon from a low-level creature: materials at least.
6. **Both branches of a line can be owned:** keeping a duplicate is a fourth duplicate choice when the player does not yet have that line's other branch.
7. **Boons and Omens:** positive effects are Boons, negative ones Omens. They change at the **daily reset**, so a well-planned raid can hit bad weather part way through the weekend and players adapt. They may also touch expeditions and the dungeon, and grow stronger at high raid ranks.
8. **Process:** Misa reviews the gear modifier list and creature talents and choices (all modifiers, example talents, and why each choice is meaningful) as an artifact before they are built. Gear and creatures are the foundation; raids and dungeons are rebalanced around them afterwards with the curve scalars.
9. **A reference-games log** records what we draw inspiration from, what we looked into, and what works for this game and what does not (`docs/research/reference-games.md`). The Bazaar is added as a key reference.

## 2026-10-16: converted gear matches a fresh drop, and an item needs its level to be worn

Misa asked whether converted items could get too strong and whether creatures need a level to wear gear. They could: the first draft converted a +5 item to item level 19 (about week-20 power on day one), and nothing limited who wore what. Two rules, recorded in `gear-and-creatures-v2.md`:

- **Conversion:** an old item converts to the item level a fresh drop would have for that player today (the higher of `3 + Boss Rank` and their best dungeon depth's drop level, at most 12), not to its old upgrade count. Players are testers, so fairness is not exact; the aim is that nobody starts the new system ahead of what it would give them.
- **Wearing:** an item's level may not exceed the wearer's level. A high drop waits in the bag; levelling gains a purpose; a new catch cannot skip the journey in top gear.

## 2026-10-16: Misa agrees with the podcast's points; Market Day boosts only the day's first crafts

Misa listened to the review episodes and agrees with the changes they proposed unless he says otherwise. One change: **Market Day** must not reward hoarding items until it rolls. Its Workshop bonus now applies only to the day's first craft (+5 pt one-step-up chance) and the first 2 dismantle jobs (top of the refund range). The inventory cap of 60 also limits hoarding. The same rule applies to any future Boon that boosts a spend: limit it to the first few actions of the day.

## 2026-10-16: roll out fast, balance at the end; slot ladder, golden talents, the weather calendar

- **Rollout:** the revamp ships in quick succession (several PRs within about a day), not as long-lived releases. Interim states that feel off between two steps are acceptable; balance passes for raids and dungeons happen once the systems are in place, aimed at the final state.
- **Gear beyond percentages:** yes. The first gear release includes one position-based line (for example "stronger when a Guard stands beside it") as well as the characterful lines (Hunter, Unbothered, Lingering).
- **Weather forecast:** today's and tomorrow's Boon and Omen are shown. In the living room they live on a clickable wall calendar or weather window that pops out of the background, animates and reacts to hover or tap, and now and then shows a speech bubble like the creatures do, as a quick glance at the game's state.
- **Golden talents (1 in 20):** a golden talent is about 1.5 times a normal one (roughly a tier III gear line, a few percent of power). Every later duplicate of the line rolls a new talent and can Teach it, so any creature can still reach a golden talent; nobody restarts a creature for it.
- **Unlock ladder:** Scarf at creature level 7, Charm at 13 (evolution stays at 15), talent slot 3 at 25, so a creature has something to look forward to every few levels (`gear-and-creatures-v2.md` 9.3a).

## 2026-10-16: top-tier rolls stay at about 1 line in 7

Misa agrees: at the highest item level about 1 line in 7 rolls tier V. A perfect item needs that on every line, so it stays rare while great items still arrive regularly.

## 2026-10-16: groundwork built: the modifier sheet in the engine, the Guard's shadow line, fight-scored power

Step 1 of the build order in `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` (section 14). Nothing is player-facing yet: no gear or talent data fills the sheet. Technical decisions, made by the lead and implementer:

- **The sheet is `FightSlot.mods`** (`SlotMods`, `packages/engine/src/types.ts`, checked by `resolveMods`). Plain integers in basis points, optional, strict (unknown fields, fractions and out-of-range numbers throw). A slot without `mods`, with `{}` or with all zeros replays byte for byte as before (the replay fixture is untouched and a new test checks the three cases on random fights). The full field list and what each field changes is `combat-rules.md` section 10.
- **Conditions are resolved before the fight.** The two that cannot be are keyed tables: `vsElementBp` and `wardBp` by the boss's element (so one sheet serves a dungeon's three rooms), `reactionBp` by reaction id, `besideRoleBp` by role.
- **The hit's bonuses add to the hit's one combined bonus** (with boost, Rally, crit and mutators), not multiply, to stay consistent with 2.4.3. A reaction's extras (`reactionBp`, `ownerMarkBp`, Spark's share and `shareBp`) add into the one factor on the reaction's bonus; the pounce is added to the multiplier's percentage next to a Payoff's own 110%.
- **The taker's order is fixed:** Shield Wall, armor, cover, then the sheet (element ward, charged, shape, every hit), then the single-hit cap, then HP. Each step rounds down. The cap comes after so that it still means "at most this much of max HP". Armor and cover credit is what they really saved given the sheet's later steps, so the sheet never moves credit.
- **The position line for Gear A is "Guard's shadow":** Attack +4/7/10/13/17% (tiers I to V) while a living Guard stands beside the wearer, resolved by the engine from the board (`besideRoleBp: { guard }`, checked at every hit). It uses the same adjacency as Support boosts and cover (same row in the raid, the plain line in the dungeon). It is Misa's own example ("stronger when a Guard stands beside it") and the adjacency sibling of "Lane anchor" in 6.1; the field takes any role, so the other adjacency lines in 6.1 need no engine work.
- **Silence ignored writes no event.** The client rejects event types it does not know, and a `silence` event makes it draw the creature silenced, so an Unbothered creature simply gets no event. "Ignores the first silence in each fight" (the Light Sleeper and Gentle but Stubborn talents) needs `ignoreSilence` to become a count; left for the talents step.
- **Fight-scored power** (`packages/sim/src/fight-power.ts`) plays the raid team (the three strongest creatures) in one line, each beside the next, through a full finale against each of the four bosses at the server's Boss Rank, 3 fixed seeds each, with an unbeatable pool, live rules, live luck, the creature abilities and enrage. The score is the damage dealt before the team falls, per finale round, with the share of HP kept shown beside it. The boss's Attack is divided by the typical server size, because three creatures alone would take every hit a whole raid spreads out (without that, the team is wiped by rank 2 and the score says nothing). It is computed on the last day of each sim week (12 fights per score, about 15 ms) and shown in the journey report and its Markdown next to the old Attack + HP score, which stays so the existing targets still pass.
- **Not done yet, on purpose:** a gear or talent share of fight-scored power (a with-and-without comparison) for the section 4 targets; that is the first thing the Gear A step needs once items exist. The 6.1 "while it stands" lines other than the adjacency ones, sets, chance and trigger lines stay later.

## 2026-10-16: Misa's answers to the review page's last questions

- **Dismantling takes a little time** (Commons in bundles of up to 5 in 15 minutes, a Rare 1 hour, an Epic 2 hours; may grow longer later) and gives both Crafting XP and creature experience, like any Workshop job: downtime work for idle creatures. The **refund does not depend on the dismantler's Crafting level**, so one master crafter is not the only creature worth using; the "Tinker's eye" gear line is dropped for the same reason.
- **Treats stay simple:** no treat item to stockpile or spoil. When sending a creature on a trip, into the dungeon team or onto a Workshop job, one toggle "Pack a treat (N Bait)" doubles the experience that activity gives that creature. Raids take no treats. Nothing to hoard, nothing to expire, one line of UI.
- **Wild Omens: yes**, from Boss Rank 10, one a week, shown a day ahead, never in a Festival week.
- **Workshop odds: numbers up front** (they depend on the blueprint, its well-thumbed marks, the crafter's Crafting level and the Reach chosen; the server sends the final numbers).

## 2026-10-16: fewer organic catches and Bait-crafted lures; treats as a checkbox; no catch-up bonus; lean UI

- **Expeditions bring fewer creatures** and a wider mix of other finds; new and rarer creatures come less often on their own. **Lures are crafted from Bait** in the Workshop and raise a trip's chance of a creature and of a species the player lacks. Bait's two jobs: treats grow creatures, lures find them. Built with the talents step; numbers from the journey sim (`gear-and-creatures-v2.md` 9.5).
- **Treats stay**, as a small per-creature "Pack a treat" checkbox on the screens where creatures are sent or assigned.
- **No catch-up experience bonus:** a bonus tied to the player's highest creature rewards pushing one creature. Low levels stay quick because level costs rise while activity experience is flat. Wider rosters are encouraged by content instead (Boons and Omens favouring elements, bosses and dungeons that want different teams), not by level caps.
- **UI stays lean by default:** show only what the decision on that screen needs, group related information, keep secondary details one tap away. Misa tests on staging and trims; agents should start smaller rather than larger.

## 2026-10-16: Gear A built: Hat, Scarf and Charm, item levels, tiered lines, Equip best, conversion (migration 27)

Step 2 of the build order in `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` (section 14). Technical decisions made by the lead and implementer; the numbers are starting values in `RULES.gear` and the content files, and the sim reports where they land (`docs/balance/log.md` B-012).

- **The item.** `GearItem` is a base, a rarity, an item level (1 to 20, fixed when it drops or is crafted) and the lines it rolled (`{ id, tier }`, tier 0 to 4). A base is a slot (Hat, Scarf, Charm) and a tag (an element, a role for the role-locked Charms, `explorer` for the Crossroads Compass, or neutral); it brings one fixed base line whose value follows the item level's tier band. Rolled lines: Common 1, Rare 2, Epic 3, Festival 2 plus its glow. Everything is schema-validated data (`gear.json`, `gear-lines.json`); per-element lines (Attunement, Hunter, Ward, Reaction) are written once and expanded per element at load. Only the rolled lines are stored (`gear_items.lines_json`); text, numbers and "does nothing for this wearer" are worked out on the server for the gear view.
- **Tiers.** The best tier an item level allows is I from level 1, II from 5, III from 10, IV from 15, V at 20. A roll counts down from the best allowed tier with weights 15, 30, 30 and 25 percent (the best tier first; tiers further down cannot roll), so at item level 20 it is V 15%, IV 30%, III 30% and II 25%: the top tier is about 1 line in 7 (Misa's answer). The conversion rolls at tier II or better.
- **Line pools.** An element-tagged base weighs its own element's lines 3 times; a role-locked Charm weighs its role's lines 3 times and never rolls another role's; the Crossroads Compass weighs explorer lines 3 times and combat lines 0.3 times (weights are multiplied by 10 so 0.3 stays an integer).
- **Item level by source.** Finale `3 + Boss Rank`, Festival `5 + Boss Rank`, dungeon by depth with a landmark bonus, an expedition find twice the explorer rank (the Old Crossroads gives the Compass), a craft twice the crafter's Crafting level, the welcome Hat 1; at most 20. The finale gear takes the boss's element as its tag (1 Charm in 20 is role-locked instead), the dungeon the room boss's element, a trip the place's element.
- **The wear rule** is one pure function (`wearBlock`) used by the server, the sim and the client: a role-locked Charm only on its role, a slot opens at a creature level (Hat 1, Scarf 7, Charm 13) and the item's level may not exceed the creature's. The server answers `wrong_role`, `slot_locked` or `item_level_too_high` (409, with the level or role as the reason).
- **Equip best** is `planEquipBest` (pure, `packages/game`): for one creature or a team it considers the bag and what the planned creatures wear (never what other creatures wear), best pair first by `itemScore`, ties to the item already worn and then to the smaller id, and changes a slot only for a better item. `itemScore` is a deterministic stand-in in hundredths of power (Attack and HP counted exactly, every other line a share of power by its content `worth`, a quarter for a creature it is not meant for), not a fight; the player's own swaps beat it where position or this week's boss matter. The server takes at most 24 creatures; the Workshop's button plans for the three strongest, a creature card's for itself.
- **What lines do.** Stat and Attunement lines fold into the creature's stats and crit (`applyGear`, still capped by the luck room). The sheet lines fill `FightSlot.mods` with their conditions resolved to keyed tables (Hunter by the element it beats, Ward by the element that beats it, Reaction by reaction id, Guard's shadow by role). The trip and Workshop lines (Trail XP, Quick feet, Keen nose, Lucky find, Packer, Busy paws, Apprentice) are read by the server when a trip is sent or claimed and when a job starts or is claimed: Quick feet and Busy paws are capped at 50%. **Between-room rest** (a Scarf line) is a new dungeon input, `DungeonRunInput.rest`: a share of max HP recovered by a creature still standing when a room is won (`combat-rules.md` 2.1.2a). The "Tinker's eye" line is dropped (see the review answers above).
- **Removed, with clear answers.** Upgrades (`POST /api/gear/:id/upgrade` answers 410 `gear_upgrade_removed`), Improve and Enchant jobs (410 `job_kind_removed`), enchants, the speed and crit-as-roll stats, the old caps and the blueprint rarity. A job of those kinds that was running at the switch still finishes: it pays its Crafting XP, returns its parts and shows as an old job.
- **The conversion** runs once per player, inside the guild's Durable Object right after the migration (`convertPendingGear`), and is idempotent (`gear_conversion`; the old rows stay in `gear_legacy`). A worn item becomes the new item of its old slot kind and tint at the item level a fresh drop would have for that player today (`convertedItemLevel`: the higher of `3 + Boss Rank` and their best dungeon depth's drop level, at most 12), with its lines rolled at tier II or better, whatever its item level; it is taken off, with a reason, if its creature may not wear it. Unworn items are refunded as parts; upgrade Essence and scrap and enchant parts come back in full; blueprints map to the new bases. One card in the Workshop says what happened until the player dismisses it (`POST /api/gear/conversion/dismiss`). The welcome kit's blueprint is a neutral piece so a new player's first craft costs scrap only.
- **Migration 27** adds `gear_legacy`, the new `gear_items` (with a unique index on the creature and slot, so a slot holds one item), `blueprints.marks` (not used until Gear B) and `gear_conversion`.
- **Sim.** The journey sim wears the same items with the same pure functions, and a new study (`packages/sim/src/gear-study.ts`) measures gear's share of the raid team's fight-scored power (`1 - bare / full`) per profile at weeks 2, 8, 20 and 30: 16 range targets from section 4 of the design. They are reported against the shipped numbers, not tuned to fit. The targets of the old stat-power share at week 8, the two Essence sink checks and the supplies check on upgrade levels are retired, because gear no longer has upgrades.
- **UI.** Lean by default (the decision above): an item card shows its name, slot, item level and its lines with tier pips; the tier is also spoken, and a line that does nothing for the wearer says so. Where an item came from and who made it are under "Details", one tap away. A creature card shows three small slots with their item level; a locked slot says the level it opens at, and the picker gives the reason in words for an item a creature cannot wear. "Equip best" is one button on each card and one in the Workshop.

## 2026-10-16: Gear A review fixes

A review of Gear A found six problems, fixed in the first commits of the Gear B branch (each with a test):

- **Old job results no longer break the Workshop.** `jobView` and a repeat claim now map results claimed before gear v2 into the new shapes: an old craft names the new base of its slot and tint at item level 1 (the same mapping as old grants), an old Improve or Enchant becomes the display-only `retired` shape with an empty refund. The client's strict parser used to throw on them, which broke the whole Workshop for most active crafters.
- **A craft running at the switch keeps its promise.** It rolls at least the rarity its blueprint had (a Festival blueprint counts as Epic) and pays the XP of its old hours (`RULES.gear.legacy.craftHours`: 4, 8, 12, 12).
- **A creature's gear is frozen while it is away or on a job.** Trip and Workshop lines are read at the claim, so one Charm could be moved onto each explorer just before its claim. Equip, unequip and the item leaving a busy wearer answer 409 `creature_away` or `creature_on_job`; Equip best skips busy creatures, leaves their gear out of the plan and says so (`skipped` in its result, with a line in the Workshop). Expeditions are untouched.
- **A failed conversion no longer bricks the guild.** Each player's conversion is wrapped in try/catch (their transaction rolls back whole), logged without player data, and that player stays pending: every gear and Workshop route answers 503 `gear_conversion_pending` for them, and the next start tries again.
- **`gear_legacy` rows stay** (as the Gear A entry said): the player's `gear_conversion` stamp marks them converted, so nothing reads them again. The best dungeon depth for a conversion counts cleared runs only. Blueprint marks from the conversion stop at the most.
- **Equip best never takes a better item away.** A creature's slot changes only for a better item: when the plan takes a creature's item for someone else and leaves it nothing or something worse, that slot is pinned and the plan is made again (it ends: each round pins one more slot). The sim's dungeon runs now carry between-room rest from the same folded gear rules as live play.

## 2026-10-16: Gear B built: Reach, dismantle jobs, well-thumbed marks (migration 29)

Step 3 of the build order in `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` (section 14), per section 3 and the 2026-10-16 decisions (the review page's last questions, the lean UI, Market Day). Technical decisions by the lead and implementer; the numbers are starting values in `RULES.gear` and the sim reports where they land (`docs/balance/log.md` B-013).

- **Reach.** A craft is aimed at `none` (a Common), `rare` (Crafting level 3, +25 Essence, +1 shard, 6 hours: Rare 60%, Common 40%) or `epic` (level 6, +70 Essence, +2 shards, +1 glimmer, 10 hours: Epic 30%, Rare 55%, Common 15%). After that rarity is drawn the **one-step-up chance** (1 point per Crafting level above 1, 1 point per well-thumbed mark, at most 10 marks) can raise it one rarity (Common to Rare, Rare to Epic). The craft always draws twice (the Reach, then the step up), so the result is exactly the shown odds. Every input is a whole percent, so the odds are exact in basis points (a test checks it for every Reach, level and mark count). The craft's XP follows its nominal hours (6 h: 72, 10 h: 120).
- **The server sends the odds; the client never computes them.** `POST /api/workshop/preview` (strict body, no key, changes and draws nothing) answers for a creature and a blueprint with all three ways to start: exact parts and Essence, time with that creature's Crafting level and Busy paws, XP, whether it is unlocked and the odds in basis points. It is a POST so the body is validated like every other request. The craft card shows the numbers up front (Misa's answer to the review page); "Details" holds the item level and the step-up explanation.
- **What a job stores.** `input_json` of a craft keeps its `reach`, the blueprint's `marks` and the Crafting `level` at the start (and the day's `mods`, below), and the claim rolls with exactly those, so a mark that arrives while the job runs changes nothing and what was shown is what is rolled. Jobs from before Gear B have none of them and read as a plain craft at the claim-time level.
- **Essence is spent through the ledger.** A Reach with Essence pays it with the keyed `spend` inside the keyed action (balance check, Essence debit, parts debit and job in one transaction; the client's key serves both the action row and the ledger, as the old upgrade did). Parts are checked first, so a short purchase takes nothing; short Essence answers 409 `insufficient_essence`; too low a level 409 `skill_too_low`; a retry with the same key returns the stored job.
- **Shards of bases without an element.** The spec says "any shard you choose" for neutral, explorer and role bases. To keep the screen lean the extra shards of a Reach on such a base are taken from the player's biggest piles (ties in element order), and the preview shows the exact bundle that will be taken. The plain craft of those bases stays scrap only (Gear A: the first craft must work on day 1). A choice of element can be added if Misa wants it.
- **Dismantle is a Workshop job.** `POST /api/workshop/jobs` with `kind: 'dismantle'`, a creature and 1 to 5 item ids. Commons bundle (up to 5, 15 minutes whatever the count); a Rare takes 1 hour, an Epic or a Festival piece 2 hours, each alone (a mixed or repeated list is 400 `invalid_bundle`). The items leave the bag **when the job starts** (a worn one comes off with them; an item worn by a creature that is away or on a job is refused, see above), and a snapshot (base, rarity, item level) is kept in the job, so nothing can be lost or used twice and the inventory frees at once. Time uses the worker's Crafting level and Busy paws like any job; XP is 12 per nominal hour, at least 6 (a Common bundle pays 6), more with Apprentice.
- **The refund is a range, rolled at the claim.** `dismantleRange` (per item: the rarity's range, plus 1 at each end of the scrap range for every full 5 item levels; summed over a bundle) is what the screens show first; `rollDismantle` draws each item's scrap, shards (the item's element, or a random element for neutral, explorer and role pieces), glimmer and Essence with the live RNG at the claim. Neither takes the worker's Crafting level (Misa's answer). Essence goes to the ledger once (`dismantle:<job id>`). The old fixed `dismantleYield` is now the bottom of the range, used only where nothing can be rolled (a drop that does not fit a full bag turns into it; no Essence, no shard for pieces without an element).
- **Learning blueprints.** Each item of a job that can have a blueprint (not Festival pieces, not role Charms) gets one draw at `6% + 1 point per Crafting level` of the worker (read literally: level 1 is 7%, level 10 16%): a new blueprint is learned, a known one gets a well-thumbed mark (nothing at 10). The chance is shown in the preview. No "Tinker's eye".
- **Well-thumbed marks.** Every repeat find of a known blueprint (trip loot, a finale's blueprint slot, a dismantle) adds a mark (`blueprints.marks`, from migration 27) up to 10; only at 10 does a repeat find still turn into the old 5 scrap. Grants carry `marks` on the blueprint entry, so the loot reveals say "well-thumbed 4 / 10". The Workshop's blueprint card shows "Well-thumbed 4 / 10".
- **Migration 29** rebuilds `workshop_jobs` (SQLite cannot widen a CHECK) so a job may be `dismantle`, and adds `work_minutes`. Rows keep their order and values.
- **Creature experience is not built yet; the work is recorded.** Every claim writes the nominal minutes the job worked into `workshop_jobs.work_minutes` (craft 180, 360 or 600; supply by its hours; dismantle 15, 60 or 120; old jobs and retired ones 0). The creatures step credits 4 experience per hour of a Workshop job from it (`gear-and-creatures-v2.md` 9.1) without reconstructing anything.
- **The old instant dismantle is gone.** `POST /api/gear/:id/dismantle` answers 410 `dismantle_is_a_job` and takes nothing. Items show the range they can give (`dismantle`: ranges, not fixed parts), computed on the server.
