- **Market Day hook.** `WorkshopMods` (`stepUpBonusBp`, `dismantleTopOfRange`) is what the day's Boon will change (only the first craft and the first 2 dismantles, `decisions.md` "Misa agrees with the podcast's points"). `craftOdds`, `stepUpBp`, `rollCraftItem` and `rollDismantle` take it, the Workshop asks an injected `GearDeps.workshopMods(userId, job)` when a job is priced and stores the answer with the job. Nothing sets it; the Boons step does, including the "first of the day" counting.
- **Bulk dismantle.** The Workshop's "Dismantle several" picks by rarity and highest item level: the lowest levels first, unworn only, one bundle of up to 5 Commons (or one piece of anything else) per job, with the number that match shown and a creature to do the work, exactly the single-item rules. It starts one job at a time (not several at once), so the two workshop slots stay the player's choice.
- **Crafted items remember their maker.** Gear A already filled `made_by`, `made_by_name` and `made_at` at the claim; a test now checks the Reach craft too.
- **No rerolls, no risk-free upgrades, no enchanting:** nothing in this step changes an item after it is rolled.
- **Analytics.** `job_start` and `job_claim` carry the Reach (and the Essence it cost); a dismantle logs `job_start`, `job_claim` and one `gear_dismantle` at the claim with the rolled totals, the items, Essence, blueprints learned and marks added. Content ids and numbers only.
- **UI.** Lean by default: a blueprint card shows its name, slot, element and marks; the craft panel shows who does it, then the three ways to start with odds, time and cost, and "Details" for the rest; the dismantle panel shows the range, the time and the blueprint chance before it is confirmed. Reuses the creature picker, cost list, reveal shell and glossary tags. The reveals say what a dismantle gave and which blueprints it taught.
- **Sim.** The journey sim's workshop aims crafts with the biggest Reach it can pay (keeping 40 Essence for evolutions) and takes spare items apart in dismantle jobs (a slot, a creature, time, the refund range with Essence, the blueprint chance) instead of instantly; repeat blueprints add marks. Results and the gear share table: `docs/balance/log.md` B-013.

## 2026-10-16: Gear B: no free lottery (a craft's scrap follows the item level; a dismantle returns under half; a Reach needs no extra shards)

The lead's decision on B-013 (`docs/balance/log.md`), from Misa's rule that a dismantle returns "a fraction of its costs". Numbers are starting values; B-015 has the study.

- **A craft's scrap grows with its item level.** `RULES.gear.craft.scrapPerItemLevels` is 4: an element base costs `6 + floor(itemLevel / 4)` scrap and one shard, every other base `8 + floor(itemLevel / 4)` and no shard (`craftScrapCost`, `craftCost(baseId, itemLevel)`). The crafter's item level is twice the Crafting level, so a level 10 crafter pays 11 or 13. The preview and the job use the exact price for the picked creature; the blueprint card shows the price for the best crafter. The welcome scrap (10) still pays the first craft (8).
- **A dismantle's scrap is a share of that price, never half.** By rarity: Common 25 to 40%, Rare 35 to 45%, Epic and Festival 40 to 50% (`RULES.gear.dismantle.scrapShareBp`; rounded down, at least 1). Crafted and dropped items use the same table by base and item level (a role-locked or Festival base prices like any base without an element). The "+1 scrap per 5 item levels at both ends" is removed; shards, glimmer and Essence ranges are unchanged. A test checks that for every base, rarity and item level the best refund is at most half of the craft price, and that a neutral Common craft and dismantle loop always loses at least half its scrap. The fallback for a drop that does not fit a full bag is the bottom of this range.
- **A Reach costs the plain craft's parts plus Essence (and 1 glimmer for an Epic), no extra shard.** The shards were what limited the Reach (a min-maxer reached 17 times in 30 weeks and held 3,388 Essence); with the extra shard gone it is the better use of every craft. `anyShards` stays in the rules for a future cost with a shard on a base without an element.
- **Migration:** none. Job inputs store the cost that was paid.
- **Sim:** the workshop prices crafts and Reaches at the crafter's item level; a test of the journey recorder allows the old Attack + HP score to dip by a point (Equip best chases the item score). The legacy digest was re-pinned on purpose.

## 2026-10-16: Creatures A built: talents, the duplicate choice, the Talent Book, lures and fewer catches (migration 28)

Step 4 of the build order in `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` (section 14) with the expedition change of 9.5. Technical decisions made by the lead and implementer; the numbers are starting values in `RULES.talents`, `RULES.expeditions` and the content files, and the sim reports where they land (`docs/balance/log.md` B-014).

- **Talents are content.** `packages/game/src/content/data/talents.json` holds a pool for every one of the 14 lines (7 or 8 talents each, branch forms share the line's pool), the fixed signature talent of each epic and Festival line, and a `deferred` list. `talent-schema.ts` checks it when it loads: the pool size, unique ids, real lines and gear lines, once-only talents with the value 1, signatures for epic species and for nobody else, and no deferred talent in a pool. A talent is `{ id, name, effect, value }`; the effect is a gear line (the same vocabulary, so no engine work) or one of two talent-only rules (`speed`, `glimmerFind`). Names and what each is about are in `docs/compendium/talents.md`.
- **Held back, on purpose (listed under `deferred`, in no pool):** Shade Napper (needs training by activity, Creatures B), Light Sleeper and Gentle but Stubborn (need the engine to count silences: "ignore the first one in each fight"). Their pools carry stand-ins of the same line (Cindle two, Sproutle and Ashmole one: Pounces When Perfect, Fiercely Loyal, Steady Under Pressure and Tunnels Through Anything), so every pool still has 7 or 8. Coralynx's Patrols at Dusk is the plain Explorer XP talent for now: its "on Moonlit Reef trips" condition needs a Rules hook that is not built.
- **Rolling.** One talent per creature, drawn with live randomness (`crypto.getRandomValues` through a separate `talentRng`, so a talent never moves a catch, a quirk or a fight seed) at the moment the creature is made (`insertCreature`: a new catch, the starter, a Festival grant, a Kept duplicate keeps the one it rolled), and for a duplicate at the claim, stored on its card so it is never rolled twice. One roll in 20 is golden: 1.5 times the value, rounded down to a whole percent or point (+14% becomes +21%, +25% becomes +37%; a once-only talent has no golden value). The draw order is the pick, then the golden draw, so seeded tests can script both.
- **Existing players.** Migration 28 only creates the tables. Right after the migrations the guild object rolls every creature that has no talent yet, once, for each slot its level has opened (a level 20 Splashet line creature gets 2), never the same talent twice on a creature, with live randomness; a second call changes and draws nothing.
- **Slots.** Slot 1 at catch, slot 2 at creature level 20, slot 3 at 25 for rare and epic species (`RULES.talents.slotLevels`, `slotsByRarity`); a common stage-1 line reaches level 20 only after it evolves. A slot that opens is empty until a Teach or a book slotting fills it. An epic's signature talent is active from the start, in no slot. A talent in a slot the level has not opened does nothing.
- **The fold.** One pure function, `applyGear(stats, crit, wearer, items, talents)`, folds the worn items and the active talents into the stats, the crit bonus, the modifier sheet and the trip, Workshop and dungeon rules; the worker (raid, dungeon, the collection view, trips, the Workshop) and the sim call the same one. A talent that does nothing for the creature (a Guard's cover on a Setter) is flagged `idle` and says so on its card.
- **The Talent Book.** `creature_talents` holds every talent a creature has held, golden or not (a golden Bigger Splash and a plain one are two entries), with the slot it sits in or none. A slot holds one talent; the one a slotting or a Teach replaces goes back to being a book entry. Slotting from the book costs `RULES.talents.bookSlotBait` (10) Bait, is instant and idempotent: a ledger spend with the player's key, the slotting applied inside it, checked first (`not_found`, `slot_closed` with `needs_level_N` or `no_such_slot`, `not_in_book`, `already_slotted`).
- **A duplicate is a card.** A catch of a line the player owns is stored as a pending card (`pending_catches`) with the species, whether it is shiny, the talent it rolled and where it came from (a trip or a Festival win). Up to `RULES.talents.maxPending` (10) wait; the 11th settles the oldest with Recommended before anything else, and the claim says so (`autoSettled`). The answers are `POST /api/catches/:id/choose` with `teach` (creature and slot), `fuse` (creature), `release`, `keep` or `recommended`, and `POST /api/catches/recommended` for all of them. Every one carries the client's key and runs inside one `transactionSync` (`catch-choices.ts`, keyed in `gear_actions` like the Workshop): a retry returns the stored result, a second answer to a settled card is `not_found` (the same answer for a card that does not exist or is someone else's), and nothing is drawn when the player answers. A shiny duplicate still makes the creature it joins shiny, at the catch.
- **Recommended** is `recommend()` in `packages/game/src/rules/duplicates.ts`: Teach when the talent is new to the book (or golden over a plain one) and scores better than what an open slot holds (`talentScore`: Attack and HP counted exactly, other effects by their line's worth, a quarter when they are not meant for the creature, 0 when idle), else Keep when a branch is missing, else Fuse until five, else Release. Deterministic; ties go to the earlier creature and slot.
- **Release** pays parts by rarity and the creature's element and never Bait: Common 3 scrap, Rare 6 scrap and 1 shard, Epic 12 scrap, 2 shards and 1 glimmer (`RULES.talents.release`).
- **Keep is built and tested but not offered yet.** A line can have a second branch (`altEvolvesTo` in the species content, validated: same line, element and rarity, a stage-2 target of exactly one species, branches differ), and `creatures` now allows two creatures of a line (`UNIQUE (user_id, line, line_slot)`, `line_slot` 1 or 2), at most `maxPerLine` (2). Keep is offered only while the line has a branch the player does not own; no shipped line has a second branch, so the button is not drawn and the server answers `keep_not_offered`. The tests inject a branching line (`branchesOf`).
- **The Festival grant** uses the same rules: a new Festival creature (with its signature), or a duplicate card (`outcome: duplicate`, with the talent and the card's id in the grant view).
- **Fewer catches, a mix of finds (9.5).** Each catch roll of a trip brings a creature only with the chance of its length, `RULES.expeditions.lengths.<length>.catchBp` (Short 3%, Medium 6%, Long 18%, Day 18%, plus the lure's); a roll that brings none brings `findScrap` scrap (finished trips only), and the first draw of a roll is that chance, so scripted tests list it first. The first expedition still guarantees the Zipmouse. The dupe shield now starts at a streak of 3 (it was 4; fewer catches make a long streak rarer).
- **Lures are made from Bait.** A lure's supply content now says `catchBp` (added to every roll's chance: 5%, 9%, 15% for Plain, Sweet and Golden) and `newBp` (the chance, drawn only when a lure is carried, that the creature is of a species the player lacks: 25%, 45%, 70%); its blueprint costs a little scrap and `bait` (12, 30, 60), and only lures cost Bait. The Workshop takes the Bait when the job starts (a ledger spend, purpose `lure`, with the client's key); the job view and the analytics event carry `bait`. Lures are crafted only: trips and the daily dungeon no longer find them (`FOUND_SUPPLY_KINDS`), and the rank gifts that gave lures give a satchel or a lantern instead. The old rare-up and epic-up fields of a lure are gone.
- **Removed:** the duplicate-to-Bait conversion (`RULES.fusion.convertBait`, `RULES.expeditions.convertBp`, `expeditionConvertBait`). Claims stored before this still read: their catches show `fused` or `converted` as they were.
- **Migration 28** creates `creature_talents`, `pending_catches` and a rebuilt `creatures` (same ids and order, `line_slot`, the new unique key), and rebuilds `raid_festival_grants` (its outcome check now allows `duplicate`; it gains `talent_json`). Nothing else refers to a creature by anything but its id.
- **Analytics.** `talent_roll` (species, talent, golden, and `backfill` for the one-time roll of an existing creature), `duplicate_choice` (choice, whether the server chose, species, rarity, talent, golden) and `talent_slot`; the catch event carries the talent and golden flag and the claim result a `duplicate` outcome. Ids and numbers only; the ops report has a Duplicate column.
- **Sim.** The journey sim gives every creature its first talent, answers every duplicate with Recommended straight away, fills open slots from the book for Bait (strongest creatures first), and crafts a lure only with Bait in the purse and within a quarter of the week's Bait earned (`GEAR_POLICY.lureBaitShareBp`; training runs first). A new study (`creatures-study.ts`, `pnpm sim:creatures`, part of `pnpm sim`) reports catches, duplicates and their answers, new species, the week the base catalogue (every species a trip can find, 12) was complete, the Bait split and the talents' share of fight-scored power (`1 - without / with`, the same three creatures with their talents taken away). Targets `creatures-*` are reported against the shipped numbers.
- **UI.** Lean by default. A catch card shows its talent (a gold frame, and the word Golden, for a golden one); a duplicate shows four buttons with the recommended one marked; Teach and Fuse go straight through when there is one place for them and open a short list when there are several. The Collection tab lists waiting cards with "Apply Recommended to all"; a creature card shows its slots (a closed one says the level it opens at) and a Talent Book one tap away with a button per open slot (10 Bait). The send screen has one compact lure row; the Workshop shows a lure's Bait cost and whether the player has it. Keep is drawn only when the server offers it.

## 2026-10-16: Player feedback through `/feedback`, public issues, private screenshots

**Decision (Misa, 2026-10-16).** Players send bug reports and ideas from Discord with `/feedback`. Each becomes a public GitHub issue labelled `player-report`. Misa marks the ones worth fixing with the `fix-this` label. Nothing is fixed automatically: a triage session (the `triage` skill) comments on new reports and fixes the marked ones in one PR. Screenshots stay private, because a player could upload anything and the repository is public; agents still read them. Plan and details: `docs/design/feedback.md`.

- **A Discord modal, not slash command options.** The modal has a String Select for the kind, a paragraph Text Input and an optional File Upload, each in a Label component. That is easier to fill in than long slash command options. The Label's description tells players their words may appear on the public GitHub page, without their name.
- **HTTP interactions on the same Worker.** `POST /api/discord/interactions` checks Discord's Ed25519 signature with Web Crypto, and refuses timestamps more than 5 minutes off. The public key is not a secret: the deploy job reads `verify_key` from `GET /applications/@me` with the bot token and passes it as the `DISCORD_PUBLIC_KEY` var, so nobody copies it by hand. With an empty key, the route is a 404.
- **Registering the command.** `POST /applications/{id}/commands` replaces a command of the same name, and the deploy job runs it after each deploy. A bulk `PUT` would have to carry the Activity's entry point command, and is reported to fail without it. A failure is a warning, never a failed deploy.
- **Storage: one `FeedbackInbox` Durable Object, screenshots in 1 MiB SQLite chunks**, not R2. R2 would need a payment method on the Cloudflare account, a bucket per environment and a new binding. The volume is tiny (a few reports a week; limits of 3 per player and 20 per server per day, 200 waiting at most), and everything is deleted after 180 days.
- **The screenshot is copied right after the reply** (`ctx.waitUntil`), because Discord's attachment links are signed and expire. The type comes from the file's first bytes; it is fetched only from Discord's two CDN hosts, with no redirects and an 8 MiB cap.
- **Issues come from a GitHub workflow, not from the Worker.** `feedback.yml` (hourly, `reports` environment, `issues: write`) reads new reports with the existing `OPS_TOKEN` and opens the issues as `github-actions[bot]`. So the Worker holds no GitHub credential, and a player's report can never look like one of Misa's issues (the `ui-feedback` skill trusts issues by Misa). The player's text is in a code block whose fence is longer than any backtick run in it, so nothing in it renders. The title is fixed. A hidden marker on the body's last line prevents duplicates when a run fails half way.
- **A separate read-only token for agents.** `FEEDBACK_READ_TOKEN` (a Worker secret, set by the deploy job when present) can read only a report and its screenshot. Misa stores the same value in the Claude Code cloud environment as `FEEDBACK_TOKEN_PRODUCTION`, and `apps/worker/scripts/feedback-fetch.mjs` sends it only to the fixed game host. This is the first secret an agent session holds. It is read-only and limited to player feedback. The `OPS_TOKEN` stays with GitHub, and the analytics report needs no agent token.
- **Only Misa's labels count.** On GitHub only people with triage access can add labels, so `fix-this` is his decision. The skill checks that the collaborator list is still just Misa.

## 2026-10-17: Creatures B built: training by activity, treats, ribbons (migration 30)

Step 5 of the build order in `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` (section 14), per 9.1 to 9.3a, 12 and the 2026-10-16 decisions ("no catch-up bonus", "treats as a checkbox", "lean UI"). Technical decisions by the lead and implementer; the numbers are starting values in `RULES.experience` and the sim reports where they land (`docs/balance/log.md` B-016).

- **Levels come from activity, never from Bait.** Experience from level L to L + 1 is `25 L + 2 L^2` (27 at level 1, 450 at 10, 1,300 at 20, about 28,000 from 1 to 30). Paid per creature that takes part: a finished trip by length (Short 20, Medium 60, Long 120, Day 240; a recall or a failed trip pays none), the daily dungeon per room cleared (60) plus a full clear (60; depth adds nothing), a raid stage (Phase 1 200, Phase 2 200, Finale 400; times 1.5 when the stage is cleared) and a Workshop job at 8 an hour of its nominal work time (`workshop_jobs.work_minutes`). **Deviation from the spec's starting values** (10 / 30 / 60 / 120 for a trip, 20 and 20 for the dungeon, 50 / 50 / 100 for the raid, 4 an hour for a job): the journey sim needed about twice as much from trips and jobs, three times from the dungeon and four times from the raid to meet the pace of 9.2 (about 20 tuning runs; B-016). There is no catch-up bonus. Everything is in `packages/game/src/rules/experience.ts` (pure, shared by the worker and the sim).
- **Bait no longer buys levels.** `POST /api/creatures/:id/train` answers 410 `training_is_by_activity` (old clients get a clear error); the Bait balance stays and is spent on treats, lures and talent slots. `RULES.training` is gone; the `train` analytics kind stays readable for old rows.
- **Treats.** `treat: true` on a trip, a dungeon run (a set of creature ids) or a Workshop job doubles that creature's experience from that activity. It costs `3 + ceil(level / 2)` Bait, paid when the activity starts, in the same transaction as the start and idempotent with it: the spend key is server-derived (`treat:<kind>:<id>`, `SERVER_SPEND_KEY_RE`; a client key cannot contain a colon, so the two never collide) and a retry of the start returns the stored result without a second charge. Nothing is stored as an item. A raid takes no treats. Flags are kept on the trip row (`expeditions.treat`), in the job's stored input and in the dungeon run's stored rewards. A short purse is an error (`insufficient_funds`) before anything starts.
- **Ribbons.** A creature stops at level 10 until it earns the Bronze Ribbon (6 scrap and 2 shards of its element) and at 20 until the Silver Ribbon (15 scrap, 5 shards, 1 glimmer and 5 finished trips of its own). Materials only, no Bait, no Essence. Experience earned while it waits is banked, up to the next level's need; earning the ribbon spends the bank. A stage-1 creature with an evolution also waits at 15 until it evolves (`evolve` settles the bank the same way). The Gold Ribbon is the reward at level 30 and costs nothing. `POST /api/creatures/:id/ribbon` is keyed (`gear_actions`, `runKeyed`), and parts are debited in one transaction.
- **Unlock ladder** (9.3a, all in `unlockLadder`): Hat 1, Scarf 7, Charm 13 (gear already enforced these), the finale ability at 10, evolution at 15, talent slot 2 at 20, slot 3 at 25, Gold at 30; the creature card shows "Scarf at 7" as the next unlock. The widest gap is Hat to Scarf (6 levels); every later gap is 5 or fewer.
- **Existing players.** Migration 30 only adds columns (`creatures.exp`, `creatures.ribbons`, `expeditions.treat`, `player_progress.pity_new` and `pity_creature`). Levels, fusions, quirks, nicknames and talents stay; creatures above level 10 get the Bronze Ribbon, above 20 the Silver and at 30 the Gold, free. Banked Bait stays. The ribbon numbers are written into the migration on purpose (a released migration must not move when rules are tuned).
- **Where experience is paid.** `awardExperience` in `creature-exp.ts`, synchronous and inside the transaction of the thing that earned it: the trip claim, the dungeon run, the raid stage resolve (`settleParty`) and the job claim, so each pays exactly once. Fights read the level the creature had when the stage started (stored inputs are unchanged by the experience they pay). Analytics events: `creature_exp`, `treat`, `ribbon`, `catch_pity` (content ids and numbers only; the rollup takes the treat Bait flow from the ledger, not from the event).
- **UI.** Lean. The creature card has the experience bar, one line ("40 / 200 experience, Scarf at 7") and, when it waits, the ribbon button with its cost and what is missing. "Pack a treat for Cindle (5 Bait)" is one compact line (`TreatBox`) on the trip screen, under each creature of the dungeon team and in the Workshop job panel; it cannot be ticked without the Bait. Result screens say what each creature gained. Workbench entries and 390 px layouts for both.
- **Not built (deferred, on purpose).** The Shade Napper talent and the "Growing" gear line (they were waiting on this step; they need their own number pass), raid experience in the raid result views beyond the creature card, and a treat for dismantle jobs in the UI (the server accepts it).
- **Sim.** The journey sim plays the same pure functions: experience by activity, treats (a per-profile dial: committed players pack them), ribbons (crafts leave ribbon parts alone, and trips prefer places with wanted shards), the stop at 10, 15 and 20. The legacy digest was re-pinned on purpose; `RULES.training` in `scalar-targets.json` became `RULES.experience`.

## 2026-10-17: new-species pity: after 60 dry rolls a species the player lacks; a creature at least every 10 rolls

The lead's decision (B-014 follow-up): the catalogue pace had no friction that was not a lower chance. Server-side counters per player (`player_progress.pity_new`, `pity_creature`) count catch rolls since the player's last new species and since their last creature of any kind; both move with the trip claim in one transaction, so a retry or a concurrent claim cannot count twice or skip.

- **Rule (`RULES.catching.pity`, `rollTripCatch`).** After `newAfter` (60) rolls without a new species, the next roll brings a creature and it is a species the player lacks (searching the whole place including its deep species: by the drawn rarity first, then any); when the place has none, it is an ordinary catch. Whatever the species, the `creatureEvery`-th (10th) roll in a row without any creature brings one. A forced roll draws no randomness; species picks use the live rng as before (`crypto.getRandomValues`, drawn when needed). A run of misses of any length ends within 10 rolls with a creature.
- **Numbers from the sim** (2 seeds x 6 servers x 30 weeks; catalogue week for min-maxer / engaged / regular, share of regulars who finish, creatures a week for a min-maxer): no pity 7 / 12 / 28 (19%), 13.5; floor only (10) 7 / 12 / 28; N = 20: 3 / 6 / 17.5; N = 30: 4 / 7 / 20.5; N = 40: 4 / 8 / 22 (97%); N = 50: 5 / 9 / 23 (94%); **N = 60: 5 / 9 / 24 (81%), 14.9**; N = 70: 5.5 / 9 / 27 (92%). The floor of 10 adds about 1.4 creatures a week to a min-maxer (13.5 to 14.9, inside the 10 to 15 asked for) and about 1.6 to an engaged player (9.3 to 10.9).
- **Why N = 60 and not lower.** The regular's review target of 24 weeks is the only one pity can still reach: committed players roll 50 to 70 times a week, so any N below about 60 completes their catalogue in 3 to 5 weeks. Pity can only speed players up, so the review targets of 9 and 14 weeks for the min-maxer and the engaged player cannot be met with it; the `creatures-catalogue-*` targets now say 4 to 8 and 7 to 12 weeks (B-016). The floor of 10 is what limits the flood: with it the committed profiles stay at 10 to 15 creatures a week.
- **Not a flood.** Duplicates are still answered by choice (Teach, Fuse, Release, Keep); a forced creature that is a duplicate is an ordinary duplicate.
- **Analytics:** `catch_pity` (`new` or `creature`, species) when a roll was forced. The send and claim screens show nothing about it; the how-to-play says a long dry spell always ends with a creature.

## 2026-10-17: creature levels halved at the switch to training by activity, Bait refunded

Misa's decision, for the testers who already hold levels bought with Bait. Done once per player, inside the guild's Durable Object, right after the migrations (`level-reset.ts`, `convertPendingLevels`); no new migration number (it follows Creatures B, migration 30, in the same release).

- **Rule.** Every creature's new level is `max(1, floor(old / 2))`, with no experience banked. Fusions, quirks, nicknames and species stay (an evolved creature stays evolved, even below level 15).
- **Refund.** The old training price from level L to L + 1 was `3 + 2 x L` Bait. The refund is that price, at full price (the catch-up discount ignored), for every L from the new level up to the old level minus one, summed over the player's creatures. Level 30 becomes 15 and gives back 705 Bait (L = 15 to 29: 3 x 15 + 2 x 330). Level 12 becomes 6 and gives back 120 (L = 6 to 11: 3 x 6 + 2 x 51). Level 1 gives back nothing. The numbers are written out in the module on purpose: `RULES.training` is gone and a one-time conversion must not move when rules are tuned. Levels that came from fusion or other means are refunded by the same formula; this is for testers, not exact fairness.
- **One transaction per player.** The level change, the ribbons, the talents, the gear check, the stamp and the Bait credit (ledger source `level_reset`, key `level-reset:<user>`) commit together; a failure leaves that player pending whole (the others convert, the next start retries) and a second run changes and credits nothing. A per-player try/catch like the gear conversion.
- **Who.** Migration 30 itself only adds columns (and still grants ribbons by the old level, which the reset then overrides). The list of players is made by the conversion the first time this code runs on a guild (the table `level_reset` does not exist yet; it is created and filled with every player who owns creatures in one transaction). A table made on the spot, rather than in migration 30, so it also works on a database that already ran migration 30 on staging, and so a player who arrives later is never halved.
- **Ribbons** follow the new level: Bronze above level 10, Silver above 20 (so after the reset nobody holds more than Bronze, and a creature at exactly 10 waits for it).
- **Gear.** Worn items the creature may no longer wear (item level or slot opening level above its new level) go back to the bag. Worn items already count against the bag cap of 60, so unequipping can never overflow it and no item becomes parts here. It runs after the gear conversion (migration 27): if that ran first its items are fixed up by the check; if it failed for a player and runs later it reads the halved level itself.
- **Talents.** A talent in a slot the new level does not open (slot 2 at level 20, slot 3 at 25, so every creature that had them) goes back to the creature's Talent Book (`slot` NULL), not lost. The reset runs before the talent rolls of migration 28, so a creature that has none yet only rolls the slots its new level opens.
- **Nothing else caches levels.** Raid teams, dungeon drafts and trips hold creature ids, and fights read the level live.
- **Notice.** `GET /api/state` carries `levelReset` (counts only: creatures, Bait, items unequipped, talents booked) until the player dismisses it with `POST /api/level-reset/dismiss` (the server remembers it); a player whose creatures were all level 1 gets no card. The card says: "Creatures now grow by doing things. Every creature's level was halved and you got back the Bait those levels cost: N Bait." plus a line for unequipped items or booked talents when there were some.
- **Analytics:** `level_reset` once per converted player (creatures, levels removed, Bait, unequipped, talents booked; no names). The Bait reaches the daily flows from the ledger (label `level_reset`).
- **Sim.** The journey sim starts every player fresh (no "existing player" scenario), so it has nothing to convert.

## 2026-10-17: items carry base stats; local and global lines; Festival gear is special, not stronger

Misa's direction (2026-10-17), built on the gear v2 branch before it merges (so migration 27 and the conversion changed, no new migration). The design is `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` section 2.5; the numbers and their tuning are `docs/balance/log.md` B-018. Technical decisions by the lead and the implementer:

- **Base stats are fixed per base type.** A Hat carries Attack and HP (Attack-heavy), a Scarf HP and Attack (HP-heavy), and the finer bases a third stat the engine already has (crit chance or crit damage). They are never rolled and not scaled by item level: every Trail Cap is the same. Charms stay the mechanics slot and keep their implicit line. The kinds are one list in the content schema (`BASE_STAT_KINDS`), so a new kind is a data and fold change, not a redesign.
- **Growth comes from the local line tiers (still gated by item level) and from better bases that unlock at higher item levels.** Each slot and tag has three makes unlocked at item levels 1, 5 and 10 (`minItemLevel` in `gear.json`; 15 Hats and 15 Scarves). A drop picks evenly among the bases its item level unlocks; a craft needs a known blueprint whose base the crafter's item level (2 x Crafting level) unlocks (Crafting level 3 and 5), checked on the server (`base_locked`) and shown in the Workshop. The levels 5 and 10 follow the tier bands of the lines; the study preferred them to 8 and 15 (regular and drop-in players reach item level 5 and 10 in weeks, 8 and 15 left them on plain bases until week 25).
- **Local lines** ("+60% Attack (this item)") change one of the item's own stats: `(base + flat local) x (1 + percent local)`, percentages of one stat added. They roll only where the base has that stat, Hats favour the Attack ones and Scarves the HP ones, Charms never roll them. They replace the old percentage Attack and HP lines, Sturdy blow and the Hat and Scarf implicits. Global lines (crit, element, reaction, ward, Hunter, role, Guard's shadow, utility) are unchanged.
- **The plain percentage Attack and HP lines survive only as Creature Attack and Creature HP**, rare (weight 6) and on Charms only: creature talents of the same name use them, and a Charm that raises the whole creature is a real, if rare, choice. They never multiply an item's own stat (the fold adds the items' stats after the global shares). Thick coat stays as a talent-only line; Sturdy blow is gone.
- **Festival gear is special, not stronger.** No higher item level (the finale's `3 + Boss Rank`), no rarity floor (the finale's odds), no extra power. Festival is a **base**, not a rarity (`GearRarity` is Common, Rare, Epic; a Festival piece has one of them): it has one fixed unique line (Hearth warmth, Lantern luck: small utility effects, no Attack or HP), a narrow pool of five lines to roll from, base stats of a plain first-make piece, and its own look (a lantern-gold outline, a star, a Festival tag). Conversion turns an old Festival piece into a Festival base at the converted item level with a rarity drawn from the finale's odds; a database that already ran migration 27 has its Festival-rarity rows read as Epic (a one-line update at start, `convertPendingGear`).
- **Migration 27 changed in place** (its `rarity` CHECK no longer allows `festival`), as the lead allowed while PR #31 is unmerged. The glossary, the compendium, fixtures (captured from real server output), the Workshop and the workbench follow.
- **Sim.** The fold and every policy use the same pure functions (`itemStats`, `wornEffects`, `applyGear`, `itemScore`, `dropBase`); the crafting policy only picks blueprints the crafter's level can make. Base stats and local tiers were tuned toward the section 4 gear share targets (B-018); the raid was not tuned.

## 2026-10-17: staging held the first gear v2; rows of that shape are normalised at start, views skip what they cannot read

**What broke.** Staging ran b9cb38d (the first gear v2: migration 27 with `festival` as a rarity, percentage implicits, the old lines) and then 20b9c42, which changed migration 27 and the item shape in place. A migration that has run anywhere (staging counts) is released and must not be edited; the "PR is unmerged, so migration 27 may change" rule above was wrong for that reason. Misa's Workshop said "The Workshop could not be loaded": `GET /api/gear` answered 500 because one of his items held the line `sturdy-blow`, which the base-stats work removed, and the item view threw on the unknown line (the same row also kept his level reset pending, and would have thrown in the Creatures view and in fights once he wore it).

**Fix, in three layers.**

- **Normalisation at every Durable Object start** (`gear-normalise.ts`, `normaliseGearRows`, before the gear conversion and the level reset; idempotent, no new migration number). Items keep id, wearer, level and tiers. A removed or moved line becomes its nearest current one at the same tier: `sturdy-blow` the item's own flat Attack (`attack-local-flat`), `attack` its own percent Attack, `hp` its own percent HP, `thick-coat` its own flat HP (on a Charm, `sturdy-blow` becomes Creature Attack and `thick-coat` Creature HP; a Charm's own `attack` and `hp` stay). A line with no nearest one is dropped; repeats keep the higher tier. An unknown base becomes the best base of the same slot and tag that the item level unlocks; the rarity `festival` becomes `epic` in items, in dismantle snapshots of jobs (inputs and results) and in loot records. Item stats are computed, not stored, so nothing else is recomputed.
- **Views and rules never throw for one row.** A line the content does not know is left out when an item is read; an item, job, blueprint or loot record that still cannot be read is skipped and logged (kind and error name only) instead of failing the Workshop view; a worn item that cannot be read adds nothing to its wearer (so a fight or the Creatures view never fails for it); the level reset takes such an item off.
- **The client degrades per entry.** `parseGearState` leaves out an item, job, blueprint or loot record that does not match (and logs its number and id) and shows the rest; the shape of the view as a whole is still checked.

**Rule going on.** After a branch has been deployed to staging, its migrations are frozen; later changes to the shape of stored data are a new migration or a start-time normalisation like this one, and a test replays the earlier shape (`apps/worker/test/staging-b9cb38d.ts`, `staging-upgrade.test.ts`).

## 2026-10-17: the creature card is small, "More" holds the rest, and the Creatures tab has find and filter

**What.** Misa chose "Option B, Focused" for the card and "Find and filter" for jumping to a creature (from the card options mock). A card shows only what the daily decision needs: sprite, name (nickname), element icon, level, the XP bar with what comes next ("Next: Scarf at 7", "Full: earn the Bronze Ribbon to keep growing"), the three gear slots as icons, the talents as chips, and **one gold button only when something is ready**. About 250 px tall at 390 px. Away, On a job and Lounging show on the card (they change what you can do); everything else moved under **More**.

**The gold button** is the first of: the ribbon (server `ribbon.ready`), evolution (`evolution.available`), a waiting catch this creature can take (it is in the card's `offered.teach` or `offered.fuse`; the button scrolls to and focuses the waiting catches at the top, which stay as they were), a talent slot to fill (an open empty slot, a Talent Book talent for it and the Bait for it). Further needs are counted on the button ("1 more in More"). "Needs you" is the same list (`creature-needs.ts`), so the card and the filter cannot disagree. "A better item in the bag" is not a signal: it needs a server field and was left out on purpose.

**More is an expansion under the card, not an overlay.** The glossary popovers live in a page layer that a modal dialog (top layer) would cover, and an overlay needs a focus trap; an expansion under the card needs neither, scrolls like the page and works with a keyboard and a screen reader as a plain disclosure (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`). An open card spans the whole row on a wide screen. It holds every action and fact the old card had: Growth (experience line, ribbon button with cost and what is missing, evolve or its locked line), Stats with the gear's share, Gear (all three slots, picker with Take off and equip, Equip best, the item's lines), Talents (slots, Talent Book, slotting for Bait), Finale ability and evolution teaser, About (description, traits, personality), Fusion, ribbons held, explorer rank, XP and trips, Lounge, Rename. The card's gear icons open More on that slot's picker; the talent chips open More on the talents. Place familiarity is not shown: it comes with the expedition view, which the Creatures tab does not load (the old card did not show it either).

**Find and filter** sits at the top from four creatures up (a short roster needs none). A combobox with live suggestions on nickname and species (folded for case and accents, best match first, at most six; Arrow keys, Enter, Escape; the count is spoken), and one row of chips that scrolls sideways: Needs you (with a count), the four elements, the four roles. Within a kind chips mean "either", across kinds "and". Choosing a suggestion clears chips that hide the creature, scrolls to its card (smooth, or a jump with prefers-reduced-motion), focuses it and marks it with a gold edge for four seconds. The element and role chips are remembered per viewer in `localStorage` (guarded; "Needs you" is not, since a stale one would hide the roster).

**Tests.** The activity package has no DOM library and gets none. `test-dom.ts` is a small fake DOM (about 300 lines, test support only) that lets the render tests draw the real components, click, type and read the tree.

## 2026-10-17: Boons and Omens built: the valley's weather (migration 31)

Step 6 of the build order in `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` (section 14), per section 10 and the 2026-10-16 decisions (the wall calendar, "lean UI", Market Day's limits). Technical decisions by the lead and implementer. The numbers are starting values in `RULES.weather`; the sim reports what they do to the raid (`docs/balance/log.md` B-017) and the raid was not tuned.

- **The draw.** `drawWeather` in `@creatures/game` is pure: it takes the injected RNG and `{ rank, festival, previousOmen, wildAllowed }` and returns the Boon, the Omen (or none), whether it is wild, and the strengths. The Worker draws a day ahead with crypto randomness at the moment it is needed (never a seed): `Weather.ensure()` makes sure today's and tomorrow's days are stored, today's first, so tomorrow's Omen knows today's. It runs on every authenticated request, on the alarm (a weather candidate at the 13:37 reset joins the guild's alarm computation) and after a dev clock change, and is a string comparison when the day is known. Table `weather_days` (day, boon, omen, wild, boon_bp, omen_bp, rank, week_id, festival, drawn_at) keeps 30 days (`RULES.weather.keepDays`). The rank is the Boss Rank at the moment of the draw, so a day drawn on Thursday keeps Thursday's strength.
- **Rules of the draw (section 10.1).** One Boon a day, never one that cancels the day's Omen (cancels are symmetric and checked when the content loads). An Omen from Boss Rank 3, never the same one two days running, none in a Festival week (Boons only). A wild Omen (Stormfront) from rank 10, with chance `wildChanceBp` (20%), at most one a raid week, only for a day that has not started yet (so it is always known a day ahead), never in a Festival week. The strengths are the `RULES.weather.tiers` table (x1 from rank 1, Omens from 3, x1.25 and x1.5 from 6, x1.5 and x2 from 10, x1.5 and x2.5 from 14). A rank 1 or 2 server never meets an Omen.
- **Content is data.** `weather.json` (10 Boons, 10 Omens), schema-validated at load (`loadWeather`): every effect is one of a closed set of operations (a sheet field, linger, a reaction bonus, crit, foe crit, foe attack, extra boss attacks, trip time, parts, shards, supplies, extra catch rolls, rarity, journal chance, Moonlit Reef open, experience, Workshop time, Market Day, dungeon scrap) with its words as a template (`{n}` is the figure at the day's strength). The words on the calendar come from the same data and the same scaling, so the numbers shown are the numbers applied.
- **Where it touches.** Only through the existing hooks, no engine rewrite. The raid stage and every dungeon run: the engine's `SlotMods` sheet (merged into each creature's, guests too, clamped to the engine's ranges by `mergeMods`), `luck` (crit chance, foe crit chance) and `bossAttacks` (a new optional field of the dungeon run input, as the fight already had it). Trips: time, parts, shard and supply chances, extra catch rolls, rarity, journal chance and the Reef. Workshop jobs: time and experience. Creature experience: a percentage on top and the treat's multiplier. Dungeon payout: scrap a room.
- **Stored with the thing, so claims are deterministic.** A trip stores the day's effects when it is sent (`expeditions.weather_json`, folded into plain numbers); a Workshop job stores its time, experience and Market Day mods in its input; a dungeon run stores the day in its input and summary; a raid stage's fight input carries the sheet, luck and attacks it was played with. A claim or a replay never reads the weather, so a day that changes (or leaves the content, or is forgotten) cannot change an old result. A planned or unknown id does nothing.
- **Market Day is limited.** It boosts the player's first craft of the game day (+5 pt one-step-up chance) and first 2 dismantle jobs (top of the range). Every job of that kind started that game day counts toward the limit, boosted or not (counted from `workshop_jobs.started_at` inside the day's bounds), so saving jobs for the day pays nothing.
- **Shipped (live): 9 of 10 Boons and 8 of 10 Omens.** Boons: Fair Winds, Warm Hearth, Clear Skies, High Water, Bonfire Night, Hearty Breakfast, Lantern Light, Market Day, Quiet Night. Omens: Heatwave, Static Sky, Heavy Rain, Long Shadows, Restless Wind, Cold Snap, Muddy Roads and the wild Stormfront.
- **Skipped, marked `planned` in the data, because the engine needs work first:** **Kind Rain** (every creature heals 2% of max HP at round start: there is no per-round heal on the sheet yet), **Morning Haze** (the first creature of each row skips its first turn: no skip-turn hook) and **Bramble Season** (the first reaction each round is reflected at 30%: a new reflect share in the reaction step). They are in the content with their words and a `planned` note, are never drawn, and the compendium lists them as not yet in the valley.
- **Forecast UI.** `GET /api/weather` answers today's and tomorrow's days (names, words, the numbers at the day's strength, start and end, wild). A wall calendar in the living room is the main place: a paper page that sways a little, comes off the wall on hover or keyboard focus, opens the forecast card on tap or Enter, and now and then says a short line in a speech bubble (a gap of 10 to 30 seconds, drawn on the client). The raid card and the dungeon pre-run card each get one compact line ("Sky today: Fair Winds and Heavy Rain.", or "Sky when Phase 2 runs" when the next stage is tomorrow). It is all text, prefers-reduced-motion is respected (the calendar keeps still), and the workbench has fixtures for the card, the line and the calendar.
- **Discord.** The raid reveal and stage posts get one sky line (escaped like every other name; `allowed_mentions: { parse: [] }` was already sent).
- **Analytics.** `weather_draw` (boon, omen, wild, rank, festival, strengths) when a day is drawn and `weather_use` (where: trip, job, dungeon, raid; boon, omen) when something is started or resolved under a day that changes it.
- **Staging tool.** The dev panel's `set-weather` sets a day's Boon and Omen by hand (staging only, like the other dev tools), so every card and effect can be looked at; it can set an Omen the draw would not.
- **Existing servers.** Migration 31 adds the `weather_days` table and `expeditions.weather_json`; nothing else changes. Trips sent before it have no weather and behave as before.
- **Sim.** The economy and the journey sims draw weather from a seeded stream of their own (`${seed}:weather`, drawn a day ahead at the rank of the moment) and apply the same pure functions; the raid sweep draws three days in a row for each week it plays (`weekSky`). It is on by default (`weather: false` or `--no-weather` for the run without it). A new study (`weather-study.ts`, `pnpm sim:weather`) plays raid weeks under each Boon and Omen alone against no weather on the same seeds, and the default journey server with and without weather. The legacy digest was re-pinned on purpose, and `RULES.weather` was added to `scalar-targets.json`.

## 2026-10-17: the creature page replaces "More"; text pass on Creatures, gear and Workshop

**What.** Misa: tapping a creature card opens a **creature page** in the list's place, and the card's "More" expansion is gone. The page holds everything "More" held (gear per slot with the picker and Equip best, talents and the Talent Book, stats, finale ability, About, fusion, ribbons, explorer facts, Lounge, Rename) plus the ribbon, evolve and "answer a waiting catch" buttons. It opens at the top (a gear icon on the card opens it on that slot's picker, the talent chips on the talents).

**Navigation is app state only.** `Collection` holds which creature is open. There is no `history.pushState`: inside Discord's iframe we cannot be sure a history entry will not make the back gesture leave the Activity, and app state needs no such guess. Back is a button at the top left ("‹ Creatures") and Escape (not from the Rename field, and the gear picker keeps its own Escape). The list stays drawn but hidden while a page is open, so the search text, chips and card state survive; going back restores the scroll position and focuses the card that was opened.

**The card is one tap target.** The card handles the click (anywhere except its own buttons: the gold button, gear icons and talent chips keep their actions; the last two now open the page on their part). The name inside the card is a real button for the keyboard and screen readers. The card lights up under a pointer and dips when pressed, and has a small "›" at the right edge. A stretched-link overlay was tried and dropped: a press that lands on the bobbing sprite and releases elsewhere sent the click to a common ancestor and lost the tap.

**Text pass.** Cut explanations the player can infer; keep requirements ("Needs Crafting level 5") and short refusal reasons. The page drops the experience line and the "Growth" heading (the bar and the "Next:" line already say it).

## 2026-10-17: one Accordion for every expand and collapse

Misa: the open and close pattern (Workshop, creature card "More", Talent Book, "Recent Bait activity", Rename, item Details) looked different in each place, so players could not tell what opens what. `ui/Accordion.tsx` is the one component for all of them (plus `AccordionGroup`, optionally one open at a time). Built and shown in the workbench first; existing views move to it in later changes.

- **Header** is a real `<button>` (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, full width, at least 2.5 rem tall, Enter and Space from the native button) with a pixel chevron that turns when open, the title, an optional short note on the right ("3", "2 new" in gold, "Ready") and an optional icon. The body is `role="region"` labelled by the header. Open: lit header with a gold underline, body inside the same ink edge.
- **Looks:** default (a page section) and compact (inside a card); an accordion inside another is drawn lighter automatically, one level deep.
- **State:** controlled (`open` and `onToggle`) or uncontrolled (`defaultOpen`). `persistKey` keeps the state in `localStorage` (guarded; callers put the viewer id in the key; it is ignored for controlled accordions). In an exclusive group the group holds the open name (and can remember it).
- **Motion:** a 150 ms height and fade (grid rows 0fr to 1fr) when `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`; with reduced motion the body is removed at once and no timers run. A closed body is not rendered unless `keepMounted`.

## 2026-10-17: Accordion rolled out; golden talents shimmer; Talent Book shows states

Misa's feedback on the first Accordion and the creature page, and the rollout the earlier entry promised.

- **Accordion look.** No gold underline under the open header: open is the lit header background and the turned (gold) chevron. No divider at the top of a body: dividers sit between items (`li + li`), never above the first (the Talent Book rows follow this).
- **Golden talents.** The yellow "Golden" badge is gone. A golden talent's title and the number golden raised ("+21%") are gold text with a slow shine (an animated gradient clipped to the text); under `prefers-reduced-motion` the gold is still. "(golden)" is read by screen readers after the title. The number is found by cutting the server's phrase around its signed number (`talentTextParts`); the words are never changed, and a phrase with no number just has a shimmering title.
- **Talent Book states.** The "Book only" and "In slot N" texts are gone. Rows are ordered in use (lit, a "Slot N" mark with a check), available (a compact "Use · 10 Bait" button, or "Slot 2 · 10 Bait" when two slots are open) and blocked (muted "No open slot"; in practice only when no slot is open). Short Bait gives one muted line under the list and disabled buttons. A slotted talent can still move to another open slot ("To slot 2 · 10 Bait"). The glossary link "What is the Talent Book?" moved into the body, because a header is one button and cannot hold a link.
- **Rollout.** Every `<details>` is now the Accordion: creature page About (open to begin with) and Rename (`keepMounted`), Talent Book, Recent Bait activity (loads on every open), item Details and "Who cannot wear it", the Workshop notice, CraftStarter Details, Journal places (count in `meta`, open when something is found), Live "others" and "Earlier rounds", Raid "Testing tools", After action "Show all N" and the dev panel sections. The old `summary` and `details` CSS is deleted. Popovers, toggle buttons and panel switchers were left alone. A test fails if a `<details>` or `<summary>` appears outside the workbench.

## 2026-10-17: creature page v3 (design round, workbench only): slots, bottom drawer, no forward-looking text

Misa's feedback on the creature page, built in the workbench as "Creature page v3" (`CreaturePageV3`) with labelled scenes; **the live Creatures tab is unchanged** until Misa picks options from the screenshots.

- **New reusable parts.** `ui/Slot.tsx` (`Slot`, `SlotRow`): one box for gear and talents, side by side across the full width. Filled shows the item icon (item level tiny in the corner) or the talent's name and number (golden ones keep the shimmer); empty shows its own name ("Scarf", "Talent"); locked is muted and shows "Lv 7". A tappable slot is a button, a locked one plain content. `ui/Drawer.tsx`: a bottom sheet over a dimming backdrop, a modal dialog (focus moves in, Tab stays inside, focus returns to the slot), closed by backdrop, drag handle (tap or pull down), Escape (stopped, so the page does not go back) and the close button; optional toolbar and pinned footer around a scrolling body. The slot and drawer pair replaces the picker under the row and the Talent Book accordion.
- **Header.** Name, element icon and level share one centred row. A name too long for that takes the whole row (two lines at most, then an ellipsis) and the icon and level move to a line under it. The "Next: ..." line is gone.
- **Text removed.** "Next: Scarf at 7" and "Next: the Bronze Ribbon" lines; "Evolve into X: needs level N and N Essence" (only the gold button when ready remains); "Finale ability: unlocks at level N" and the "Evolves into: ..." teaser (the ability shows once unlocked); "Scarf opens at level 7" and "Slot 2 opens at level 20" (now inside the locked slot); "Hat empty" and the item name and lines under each slot; "Festival prize, not found on expeditions."; "What is the Talent Book?" and the glossary "?" next to Equip best (the Gear heading is the glossary link); the Equip best button (the server route stays). Kept on purpose: the ribbon button's cost and "missing" line (a reason a button is off), "Not enough Bait.", "Does nothing for this creature." on an idle talent.
- **Item picker options (Misa picks one).** P-A list (rows with icon, name, item level, rarity, numbers and lines, Equip; worn item pinned with Take off), P-B icon grid with a detail panel pinned at the bottom of the drawer, P-C either of them with the find bar built from `CreatureFinder`'s classes (search, Can wear, rarity and element chips; its `Magnifier` is now exported). Items this creature cannot wear show a compact reason ("Needs Lv 16", "Guards only").
- **Talent Book in the drawer** for the tapped slot: in use (lit, "Slot N"), usable ("Use · 10 Bait", "To slot 2 · 10 Bait" for one sitting in another slot) and, short of Bait, muted rows with off buttons and "Not enough Bait."

## 2026-10-17: creature page v3 is the live page; rarity colours and creature rarity borders

Misa approved v3 (with changes) from the screenshots. It replaces the live `CreaturePage`; the workbench keeps scenes for it (the `CreaturePage` entry) and the sample data. Nothing of the round's other options (item picker list P-A, find and filter P-C) is in the game bundle (a test searches the production build).

**Design rule (Misa).** Rarer creatures are not stronger by default; rarity buys options and cooler things, not raw power (individual creatures may still be stronger or weaker).

- **Page.** Back at the top left and Lounge at the top right of the same row. Order: head (name, element, level, bar; tags; ready buttons), Stats, Finale ability (once unlocked), Gear, Talents, About, Rename. The level badge is a hidden interaction (a tap opens its explanation; no "?" drawn) in the pixel font (`.gtag` resets the font, so the badge sets it back). A nicknamed creature's species name is a tag in the tags row (not in the name row), only when it differs from the nickname. The "Common/Rare/Epic" tag is gone. The ribbon shows only when the server sends one (the creature stands at the level that asks for it), with its cost and what is missing; the button is gold when affordable. The "Next:" line, the forward-looking texts and the Equip best button are gone (the server route stays; the Workshop still uses it).
- **Rarity colours, one set of tokens** (`gear-tokens.css`): Common grey, Rare blue, Epic pink; `--rarity-legendary` (gold) and `--rarity-mythic` (crimson) are defined and reserved, nothing uses them yet. Every rarity shown (slots, picker cells, item frames on cards, loot reveals, the Workshop) reads `--rar` from these. A creature's portrait gets one border in its rarity colour (page header and the cards in the list), and its rarity is in the portrait's spoken label ("Cindle, common").
- **Gear slots.** One border only, in the rarity colour, no inner frame; the item sprite is shown bare and large with the item level as a small pixel number in the corner (no "Lv", so it is never taken for a lock level). A locked slot shows a pixel padlock (SVG, no emoji) over the slot's name and "Lv 13" small under it (talent slots too); an open empty slot shows only its name.
- **Item picker (drawer).** The grid only. Cells have one rarity border each (the worn item first, marked "Worn"; the selected cell is lit from inside, not outlined twice). The detail panel is all pixel font: name, level and rarity, then a white base-stat block with a row per stat ("Attack 26 +15": the base number, then what its local lines add; "HP 32"), a divider, then the modifiers with values and tier pips. The same size for every row. "Loading..." shows while the gear view loads. Base and bonus come from the server's `base` and `value`; a stat in points (crit) keeps the server's text.
- **Talent Book (drawer).** The talent in the tapped slot is lit ("Slot N"), free ones have "Use · 10 Bait", and a talent already in another slot is greyed with its "Slot N" mark and no button (slots are equivalent, so there is no move).
- **Removed.** `CardGear`, `CreatureTalents` (the Talent Book accordion), their styles and workbench pages, and `onEquipBest` on the page's actions.

## 2026-10-17: creature rarity by name colour; gear rarity by one border (polish v5)

Misa's changes after trying v3 live.

- **Creatures show rarity by the colour of their name**, not a portrait border (page header and list cards): common grey, rare blue, epic pink-purple, from the rarity tokens. Because colour is never alone, a visually hidden word follows the name ("Cindle, rare"), and the card's name button reads "Open Cindle, rare". The portrait's spoken label is just the name (and "(shiny)").
- **Contrast.** Names use a lighter `--rarity-*-text` variant (`--rar-text` on `.rarity-*`): common `#c2c2d0`, rare `#7ebcff`, epic `#f397ff` (leans purple), reserved mythic `#ff9db0`. Each is at least 4.5:1 (WCAG AA) on the page ground, the card, a hovered card and a pressed card; the plain tokens failed on the pressed card (3.5 to 3.9). `dev-build.test.ts` reads the colours from the built stylesheet and checks it.
- **Gear rarity is one single border** in the rarity colour everywhere (item frames on cards, loot, the Workshop, like the slots and picker grid). The old double and triple edges are gone; the pips still say rarity without colour.
- **Smaller changes.** The card's level badge has no "?" and uses the pixel font (a tap still opens the explanation). Gear slots show "Lv 2" in the corner again (pixel font). The Lounge button and the "Lounging" badge have a small pixel armchair (`ui/ArmchairIcon.tsx`, an SVG in the text colour).

## 2026-10-17: one item-level indicator; item card and compact rows (polish v6)

Misa's changes after polish v5, with the lead's decision on item levels.

- **Rule.** An item's level is always shown one way: the small pixel "Lv 15" in the bottom-right corner of the item's image (`ItemLevel`, the `.item-lv` class), the same on the creature page slots, the picker grid, item cards, the Workshop bag, loot lists, craft and dismantle panels and reveals. The rarity pips and dots in item image corners are gone: rarity is the border colour and, where there is room, a rarity badge. The creature's level keeps its yellow badge, so the two never look alike. The level is never repeated as text beside an image that carries the label (the spoken name still says "item level 15" for screen readers). Blueprints and the how-to-play pictures are not real items and have no level.
- **Item card (`ItemCard`).** The one layout for an item's full details (the Workshop's item and the picker's detail panel): the image at the top left, to its right the name, a small pixel rarity badge and the base stats one row each in the same size ("Attack 41 +22"), then a full-width divider, then the modifiers with values and tier pips (line tiers, not rarity). All in the pixel font. The wrong font came from `.gtag` (`all: unset`) dropping the font of a tag that only had `.pix`; `.gtag.pix` now restores it. A Charm has no stat rows: name, badge, divider, modifiers.
- **Compact rows (`ItemRow`).** Loot lists (expedition claim, dungeon results and streak bonus, the finale reward and Festival gear) and the creature popover's worn items: image with the corner label on the left, name and rarity badge on the right, no stats.
- **Creature cards** lose the "Next: ..." line altogether (ribbon, evolve, "Waiting", "Top level" too; the gold button and the XP bar say what is needed). The Lounge button and the Lounging badge have a clear gap after the armchair.

## 2026-10-17: Workshop bag polish (Misa's feedback on the v6 screenshots)

- **The item-level label may cover a corner of the item art**, also on small pictures (Misa: "perfectly fine and expected").
- **Workshop bag.** "Bag: 3 / 60" sits on the Gear heading's line, at the right. The "Equip best on your top 3" and "Dismantle several" buttons are gone: choosing the best gear is the player's puzzle, and the extra buttons cluttered the view. The server's equip-best endpoint and the client's API call stay for now (unused by the UI); bulk dismantling is still possible in the server's job API (one job, several items) for a later, leaner UI if wanted.

Its heading is spoken only (`titleHidden`); the card's own name is the visible title and the close button sits in the handle's row.
- **Item card.** The name is in its rarity's text colour (as creature names), the image keeps the rarity border (players glance at gear pictures for rarity; creatures' art is too expressive for a border). Under the name, one row of same-looking badges: slot (a small pixel silhouette plus "Hat"), rarity, element (its icon plus "Ember"), and Festival when it is one. None shows a "?", all are tappable. The old dotted "Hat" tag and the element chip under the modifiers are gone.
- **Element on gear has its own glossary entry** (`gear-tag-<element>`, "Ember gear: its lines lean Ember"): the creature entry ("hits Grove bosses harder") made no sense on an item. The coverage check requires one per element.

## 2026-10-17: Item corners everywhere, and tags that explain themselves by context

- **Every item image that shows its level also shows its slot** (Misa). One component, `ItemCorners` (`GearArt.tsx`), draws both: the slot icon top left, "Lv N" bottom right; the slot comes from the item's base. `ItemFrame` uses it, and so do the creature page slots and the picker cells; a test fails if `ItemLevel` is drawn on its own anywhere else. The picker's "Worn" flag moves to the top right to make room. Loot-list names take their rarity colour too, like the item card.
- **An element explains itself by whose it is** (Misa: "the Ember help on items is super good, do the same elsewhere"). `ElementChip` takes a `context`:
  - **creature** (default): the existing entry (who it beats, its mark).
  - **boss** (raid boss card, the stage's foe line, the foe popover; dungeon room bosses too): "Tide creatures deal it 50% more damage, Grove creatures 25% less." One line only, keeping the rule that raid popups show only a description (2026-10-14).
  - **place** (expedition places): most creatures there are that element, shards found there are its shards, gear found there leans to it.
  - **gear** (blueprints, and the item card's badge): its lines lean that element.

  The coverage check needs all four per element, so a new element cannot ship without them.

- **Role Charms and the Compass get their badge too** (`GearTagBadge`): "Guard" (only a Guard can wear it; Guard lines roll three times as often, no other role's at all) and "Explorer" (trip and Workshop lines three times as often, fight lines much less). Neutral bases show none.
- **Tags that already differ by context stay as they are:** creature rarity and gear rarity, creature level and item level, Boss Rank and explorer rank, the finale's Festival and Festival gear each have their own entries. Roles, marks, reactions, gimmicks and room modifiers appear in one context only.
- The glossary's sanity cap goes from 200 to 400 entries (it is now just over 200, counting one generated entry per gear line).

## 2026-10-17: How to play pass for the revamp

- **Misa:** How to play is the player's last resort for how things work: as little text as possible, no exact numbers or niche detail, but nothing new left out. A pass over the new content, Boons and Omens included.
- **A new Gear section** (between Rewards and the Workshop; eleven sections now) says what an item is: the three slots and when they open, own stats on Hats and Scarves and a fixed line on Charms, lines by rarity and "(this item)", tiers capped by item level, element tags leaning lines. Its picture is a real item card (a Rare Ember Forge Hood at item level 8 with two tier II lines), built from the content by the same rules as any item, nothing rolled. Every `gear-*` glossary entry's "More in How to play" now opens it; the Workshop section keeps jobs, crafting, dismantling and where gear comes from.
- **Creatures** says how a creature grows (experience by doing, treats, ribbons, evolution, talent slots, the duplicate choice) and its picture adds the level ladder from the rules (`unlockLadder`, plus the finale ability at 10). "Tap any tag" moves to the first week.
- **Ranks** says a Boon helps and an Omen hinders, with tomorrow's on the wall calendar; its picture gets Boon and Omen tags. **Rewards** says Essence also pays for Reach crafts.
- **Glossary fixes found on the way:** gear stats ("An item's own Attack and HP", not "a Hat's Attack or a Scarf's HP"), golden talents shimmer (no gold frame), the duplicate choice has no Keep for now, Essence pays for Reach crafts and comes a little from dismantling, and the "Bait no longer buys levels" note is gone.
- The workbench gets a HowToPlay entry (pick a section).

## 2026-10-18: The daily dungeon becomes a path on a daily map (camps, keepsakes and a pack)

- **Misa (2026-10-17):** a Slay the Spire style loop in the existing room format: players push on and make choices on where to go, with push-your-luck mechanics, built on top of the gear and creature revamp. Design: `dungeon-paths.md`; research summary at its end (Slay the Spire's visible map and optional elites, Hades' rewards shown on the door, Loop Hero's camp retreat and partial loss, Quacks' safety net, daily seeds shared by everyone).
- **The map:** seven floors, 2 or 3 rooms on most floors, the camp on floor 3 and the room boss on floor 6; fights, elites, mystery rooms and one cache, with Slay the Spire's placement rules (floor 0 all fights, no elites before floor 2, different kinds on sibling exits). **One map per server per day**, drawn from the day's seed (already a crypto draw) and stored, so everyone sees the same map and can compare routes; every fight's seed is still a fresh crypto draw when the player steps in. Foe numbers follow each player's depth.
- **Played room by room:** `POST /api/dungeon/start` opens a run, `POST /api/dungeon/act` moves, answers a mystery room or the camp, or heads home. Each act carries the run's step (stale ones are refused) and its own idempotency key; the open run's state (HP, pack, keepsakes, the camp's offers) is stored, so a reload never re-rolls anything. `POST /api/dungeon/run` answers 410. Migration 34 (32 and 33 went to the raid's `placed_by` and the lounge on main): run status, state and outcome columns, the day's map, and one `dungeon_steps` row per act.
- **Push your luck is one visible checkpoint:** loot goes into a **pack**. At the camp the player picks Rest, a keepsake (one of two) or a swap, then pushes on or heads home with the whole pack. A defeat keeps half the pack, rounded up; experience and the supply are always safe; beating the boss keeps everything, opens the old full-clear chest and goes one depth deeper. Loss is partial on purpose (total loss splits players; a defeat used to cost nothing extra, so half keeps a bad day worth something), and rounding up keeps an early defeat almost free.
- **A run left open** when the day ends closes as home only if it is resting at the camp, otherwise as a defeat. Closing as home everywhere (the first build) made quitting the app a free safe exit at every step and the camp choice meaningless.
- **Keepsakes** (8) are run-only buffs that fold into later fights exactly like room modifiers (no engine feature). **Mystery rooms** (8) have two options with their exact outcome and odds written on the button, one always free, risky ones about even in value with safe ones.
- **Server review fixes:** a creature swapped in at the camp is loaded without gear already frozen into the run (otherwise moving an item from a row creature to a bench creature counted it twice); a creature that left the row cannot return; closing a late run never moves depth, streak or last day backwards; the prune also empties step answers and the state of finished runs.
- **Client:** the pre-run card shows today's map (tap a room for its foe, trick, modifier and HP); during a run a strip shows the row's HP, the keepsakes and the pack "at risk"; a fight plays its one room on the existing stage; the camp card has the three choices, a free reorder and "Head home with your pack" (with one confirm). Results say how the run ended and what the pack kept; Watch again plays every room in its own row.
- **Balance (B-019, `docs/balance/report-2026-10-18-dungeon-paths.md`):** the simulator walks the day's map with a simple policy (match foes the team beats, elites only when healthy, swap a knocked-out creature at the camp, head home when a depth beat the team before and someone is down). Runs now have 4 to 5 fights, so foe HP at depth 1 drops (`baseHpBp` 11 750 → 6 500) and the ladder steepens (`hpStepBp` 3 000 → 4 500); experience per fight 60 → 35 (a daily player's weekly dungeon experience stays about the same); an elite pays scrap and a keepsake but no Bait. Full run: first-week clear 89.4%, depth growth 1.83 a week, home rate 19.3%, 4.13 fights a run, dungeon Essence 4.1% of income. A full clear pays about Bait 3.8, Essence 1.06, scrap 2.3 (before: 3, 1, 2). Still failing: the week-8 gear share (22.8% against 20%; it was 21.7% before, and only dropping the dungeon's supply brings it under), and the raid got a few points easier (`curve-r6up-default` 72.7%); both go to the final balance pass agreed for the revamp.

## 2026-10-18: The trip result screen, and Teach goes to the Talent Book

- **One trip result card** (`TripResult.tsx`, replacing `TripReveal`, `CatchList` and `DuplicateCard`):
  - the place's banner with the explorer standing on it, then one sentence: "Sproutle spent 12 hours at Tidepools and found…";
  - one card per creature found (`FindCard`), with the element and the role;
  - what came home as count badges and item rows;
  - one progress block (explorer rank and XP, level with the treat, familiarity);
  - the slot, rank gifts and journal pages (icon on the title's line);
  - one full-width Claim that confirms every duplicate's pick, in order, and closes the card.

  Misa approved the draft as shown: the banner as a strip (not a faded backdrop), journal pages open, no "Decide later" (Recommended is picked from the start, so Claim is one tap; an unanswered card still waits on the Creatures tab if the app closes).

- **A duplicate chooses with radio rows**, not three buttons:
  - Fuse and Teach (one row per creature of the line, named only when there are several), then Release (its parts as badges), then Keep when offered.
  - The recommended row is picked first and carries a small gold star; no yellow button.
  - A row that cannot be picked is greyed with the reason: "Already in its Talent Book", "Fully fused".
  - Fuse shows the five fusion stars, the one it adds green and pulsing (with reduced motion: green with a glow, still).

  The Collection tab's waiting catches use the same cards and one full-width Confirm; the "apply Recommended to all" button is gone (Recommended is already picked).

- **The talent a creature rolled sits in a box headed "Discovered talent"**, with a green New when no creature of its line holds it yet (a golden one is new unless a golden one is held). A new species' talent is new by definition.
- **Teach puts the talent into the creature's Talent Book, never into a slot** (Misa). The player slots it later from the book for the usual `RULES.talents.bookSlotBait` (10 Bait). The server ignores a `slot` an old client still sends. Teach stays within the creature's own line, as before: each line has its own pool of 7 to 8 talents, so a talent can only ever come from a catch of that line, and teaching within the line does not shrink anyone's variety.

## 2026-10-18: The Undercroft (a deeper, optional level) and the carved door

- **Why:** runs should grow longer as players progress, by the player's choice (Misa). Design in `docs/design/dungeon-paths.md`, "The Undercroft".
- **Server shape:** the main boss at depth 8 or more does not end the run. Depth +1 is written to `dungeon_progress` at once (`MAX`), and the chest opens: its gear, shard, glimmer and supply are paid right then under the grant key `dungeon:<day>:<attempt>:chest` (an Undercroft keeper's under `...:u<level>`), and its Essence and scrap go into the pack. A defeat below or a run left open can therefore never undo the depth or the banked gear. The run's end pays the pack through the usual ledger keys and adds what the chests paid to the summary's rewards, without paying it again.
- **Outcome names stay `cleared | home | defeat`.** A run that beat the main boss ends `cleared` when it heads home or is closed at a safe point (the descent card, an Undercroft camp), and `defeat` when it is lost below; its summary still says `cleared: true` and the run row `cleared = 1`, so the first-day retry and the clear counts treat it as a win. `deepest` counts the levels cleared. A run that is closed at a camp before the boss stays `home`.
- **The carved door** (Misa's refinement, same day): the player chooses nothing. Stepping through adds ONE room of a random kind (fight, elite, mystery, cache, camp or epic, never a boss; odds in `RULES.dungeon.undercroft.markOdds`) drawn with live randomness, the player is only told what it could be, and the history says only that a room was added. The room is a floor of one room before the keeper (camps last). With at most one door a map, the cap of 2 marks (`maxMarks`) is reachable only if a second source appears; it is enforced anyway.
- **The door is drawn last** in `generateDungeonMap` and only when asked (`{ doors: true }`; the worker asks, the simulator does not until it plays the Undercroft, which keeps its pinned numbers), so a map without a door is exactly what the same stream drew before doors. Below depth 8 its view is a mystery room named "Quiet room" (so the client needs no special case) and it plays as nothing, so the shared map shows nothing a player cannot use.
- **Undercroft maps** have floors of two rooms in lanes 0 and 2 that lead straight up their lane (a real choice of path, no crossing), so the player's first tap chooses a lane until the next single-room floor.
- **Floors:** the first level has `min(5, 2 + (landmarks passed - 1))` floors of two rooms, where a run at depth d has passed `floor((d - 1) / 7)` landmarks, plus the marked floors and the keeper; a deeper level (a cracked floor) has 2 plus the keeper.
- **Starting values for the simulator to tune:** epic fight HP +60% and Attack +30% of the depth-scaled foe (an elite is +40% and +20%), door on 35% of maps.

## 2026-10-18: The fight uses the board's rows (issue #29)

**Problem.** The raid board is a line of `rowLength` slots (three per player, or nine with guests) cut into Front, Middle and Back on that full length. The fight got only the creatures, with empty slots dropped, and the engine cut that shorter line again by `floor(n / 3)`. A player with fewer than three creatures, or a hole in the board, moved creatures across row boundaries, so pincers, sweeps, lances, "beside", Guard cover, the forecast and the replay stage used rows that differed from the board.

**Decision.** The fight input carries the board's positions: `FightInput.board` is `{ length, slots }`, where `length` is the board row's full length and `slots[i]` the board slot of `row[i]` (strictly increasing, each below `length`). `buildPhaseInput` reads it off the board after auto-placement and guests (`sparseBoard`), and the stored input keeps it, so replays read the same positions. The engine builds one shape per fight (`fightRowShape`) and every helper derives from it: zone of creature i is the board formula on the full length applied to its slot (`zoneOfIndex`), its lane is the slot minus its zone's first slot (`laneOfIndex`), "beside" is the creatures whose slot is exactly one left or right in the same zone (a gap breaks adjacency, `zoneNeighbours`), and the lane count is the board's row width (`laneCount`). All accept a plain length (a dense row) or the board (`RowShape`). An invalid board (length below the row, wrong slot count, slots not strictly increasing integers below the length) throws a `RangeError` when the fight starts. The first version of this change carried only row sizes (`zones`, never merged); it could not keep columns, so lances and "beside" still differed from the board. Misa wants the fight to be exactly the board, so positions replaced sizes. The dungeon (a row of three, `neighbours`) is unchanged.

**Old inputs.** An input without `board` is the dense row (creature i in slot i, the `floor(n / 3)` formula), so stored fights and replays replay identically (a test compares an input without a board to the same row with the dense board written out, and the sim's legacy digest is unchanged). The views send `board` to the client when the stored input has it. The stage, the replay and the forecast use it, and the stage grid draws an empty cell where the board has a gap, so it looks like the placement board (rows by lanes).
