**Silence by row.** `silence_ends` now has a required `scope`, like the pincer: `rows` silences the leftmost and rightmost living creature of each board row (a row with one creature once, an empty row skipped), `row` is the whole line. Raid bosses (Bramble Bind, Choking Vines) use `rows`; dungeon foes use `row`, so dungeon fights are unchanged. Powers, turns and periods are not retuned (no balance pass). The forecast, the move text, the Discord post, the glossary, the Thornmother lore and the journal page say "each row"; the forecast marks the ends of each row as for the pincer.

## 2026-10-18: Auto-joining creatures are on the board; teammates can move an away player's creatures (issue #29)

> **Superseded 2026-10-26** ("Raids: you join by placing; no auto teams"): there are no auto-joining creatures and no away-player assist any more. The board shows only what players placed.

**Problem.** Everyone in the raid fights, but the placement board drew only stored placements. A player who placed fewer than three creatures saw "random" creatures in the fight that were never on the board, and an auto-placed creature could sit where they had meant to put their own.

**Decision 1: the board shows what the fight will build.** One worker function, `buildPartyBoard` (in `raid-resolve.ts`), builds the sparse board: the placements (at most three each), each player's strongest others topped up (`raidTeam`), `autoPlace`, then guests. `buildPhaseInput` makes the fight from it and the raid view draws it, so the two cannot disagree (a test compares them). Each tile carries `auto` (nobody placed it). The client draws auto tiles with a dashed outline and a small "auto" tag, with the owner's colour and initial like any tile. Tapping your own auto tile offers "Place here", which makes it a real placement in the same slot (the normal placement request). Guests are not drawn (their slots look empty, as before). The "n / 3 placed" count counts real placements only.

**Decision 2: away players' creatures can be moved by their party.**

- **Away** means `players.last_seen_at` is before the week's start. The start is `eventTimes(weekId, tz).weekStart`, the same place the schedule comes from, with the guild timezone passed in. `last_seen_at` changes only when a session is created or touched, so a leet credit never counts as being online.
- While a member is away, any other member of the same party may move their joining creatures (auto ones, or ones a teammate placed) to free slots, and swap one joining creature for any other creature they own (the same eligibility as placing your own). It uses the same `PUT /api/raid/placement { creatureId, slot }`; when the creature belongs to another player the server reads the owner from the creature, never from the body.
- The first such move pins that member's whole team (their auto creatures at their current slots) as real placements, then applies the move or swap, so their team is exactly their placements afterwards (the cap of three stays). `raid_placements.placed_by` (migration 32, nullable) records who placed each row: NULL is the owner, a user id is the teammate. Pinned rows count as the teammate's.
- Creatures the owner placed themselves (`placed_by` NULL) can never be moved or swapped by teammates. Once the owner opens the game they are no longer away: teammates cannot touch their creatures, and the teammates' placements stay until the owner changes them (an owner's own move writes NULL). Same lock and phase rules as normal placement.
- Refusals use the existing style: `not_away`, `not_in_party`, `not_movable`, `slot_taken`, `invalid_slot`, `board_full`, `locked`, `not_your_creature` (unknown creature). Another player's creature used to answer `not_your_creature` and now answers `not_away` when its owner is in the party and here.
- The view (`party.away`, tile `movable`, `movedBy`, `creatureId` for movable tiles) tells the client who is away and which tiles the viewer may move. The client shows "<name> is away — teammates can move these." and "<creature> (<owner>) moved by <name>.", lets those tiles be dragged, and offers "Swap for another" in the tile's popover (a picker of the away member's creatures). Names are rendered as text.

**Why.** Misa's call: a player who is not around should not leave the party with a badly placed row, and the party can fix it, but only until the owner is back, and never what the owner chose themselves. Placing onto a slot where a teammate's auto creature is drawn still works server-side as before (the autos are placed again around it); the client refuses that drop.

## 2026-10-18: Auto tiles are soft and muted (issue #29)

**Decision.** A player may drop their own creature onto a slot that shows another player's auto tile: their creature takes the slot, and the auto creature moves to a free slot by the normal auto rules. Nothing is stored for an auto creature (the board is built at read time), so the server's `place` already allowed this; tests now pin it (auto creature ends in a free slot, nobody is lost, board and fight agree, and with three members of three creatures each the board is exactly full, so a free slot always exists). A creature that was placed stays hard (`slot_taken`). Teammate moves for an away player (`raid-assist.ts`) follow the same rule: another member's auto tile makes room, another member's placed creature is `slot_taken`, and the owner's own placed creature is still `not_movable`. No invariant breaks: the owner's team is pinned first, other members' autos are never stored, and the cap of three per player holds.

**Client.** The drop is no longer refused. While a creature is carried over another player's auto tile the page says "<creature> makes room." (text only), and the drop announcement says the same. Auto tiles are muted so they read as suggestions: the dashed outline and "auto" tag stay, the sprite is faded and half desaturated, and the text uses the quiet text colour (about 6.5:1 on the tile, above AA); hover, focus and a drag over the tile bring the full look back so it never looks disabled. The workbench's "Others join by themselves" toggle shows the same look.

## 2026-10-18: The lounge message replaces Discord's launch card

**Problem.** Discord's default Launch command posts a "Game invitation" card on every launch, which later reads "Game ended. Start a new one?". In an active server the channel filled with these cards and people got notified each time someone started playing.

**Decision.** Launch uses the app handler and is answered with LAUNCH_ACTIVITY, which posts nothing. The game keeps one message per server in the announcement channel, edited in place, showing who is playing, with a Play/Join button that launches the same way (design in `docs/design/lounge-message.md`). Presence comes from Discord's activity instance API, already used at login, polled once a minute while a session is tracked: no client heartbeat and nothing a client could fake. A buried message is reposted silently when a new session starts; Misa chose silent reposting over pinning, and the raid post channel over the last launch channel.

**Why.** It keeps joining one click away (the lounge) without a new message per launch. Edits never notify, and a repost is `@silent`. The deploy job switches the handler only while the Interactions Endpoint URL is ours, and going back is one constant.

## 2026-10-18: Branch evolutions built: Sunfawn and Ohmouse, Keep is live

Step 7 of `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` (section 8.4). Sproutle grows into Thornbuck or Sunfawn, Zipmouse into Voltaro or Ohmouse; the choice is permanent; a Kept duplicate (a second creature of the line) can grow the other way. Technical decisions by the lead and implementer; Misa kept the names Sunfawn and Ohmouse (2026-10-18).

- **The stats follow the role budget** (systems review: finished forms within about 5% of each other inside a role). Sunfawn has exactly Magmole's numbers (HP 179, Attack 34, Speed 39, growth 12 / 2 / 3), the common Support budget. Ohmouse has Thornbuck's HP and Speed (224, 22; growth 15 / 2 / 2) and Attack 34 (Thornbuck 30, Fogwyrm 38): the common Guard budget. Summed at level 30: Magmole and Sunfawn 745; Thornbuck 827, Ohmouse 831, Fogwyrm 864. Level cap 30, rarity common, never catchable (the loader already refuses a catchable stage-2 species and a place that lists one; a test now names both).
- **One price for the choice.** Both forms cost level 15 and 20 Essence, and the loader now says so (`checkCreatures`: both branches the same level and Essence), so the evolve screen shows one price and the sim and the server need no branch-by-branch cost. The creature view says `evolution: { toSpecies, level, essence, available, alt?: { toSpecies } }`; `toSpecies` stays the first form, so an older client still evolves it.
- **Abilities need no engine work.** Sunny Patch is Rally 6% for 2 rounds plus a 30% Shield Wall on its own row for 1 round; Static Wall is a 60% Shield Wall on its own row for 2 rounds, then re_mark (Charged). Both use the existing `rally`, `shield_wall` and `re_mark` effects (no new field, so every stored fight replays exactly; the replay fixture and the engine are untouched). Sunny Patch fires on its default round 5 (like Warm Glow, the other Support rally); Static Wall at the first zone hit like the other Guards. As a Grove Support, Sunfawn heals its neighbours 6% a round through the existing element rule (`supportFx`), by role and element, not by species.
- **The upgrade rule moved to the first form.** The loader asked every stage-2 ability to be at least as strong as its stage-1 one. That holds for Thornbuck and Voltaro (`evolvesTo`) and still does; the second form (`altEvolvesTo`) is another role, so its ability only has to exist (Sunny Patch is not "more" than Bark Guard, it is different). A test covers both sides.
- **Server.** `POST /api/creatures/:id/evolve` takes an optional `species` (a picklist of species ids, strict body). The server checks it is one of the creature's branches (`not_a_branch`, 409), then the level and the Essence as before; none means the first form (an older client). It is one keyed ledger spend like today: a retry with the same key and the same form returns the stored result and charges nothing; the same key for the other form is `key_conflict`. A creature that has evolved is `not_evolvable`, so the choice cannot be undone. The client's key now includes the form (`evolve:<creature>:<species>`), so a failed press of one form never reuses its key for the other.
- **A role-locked Charm comes off.** Sunfawn is a Support and Ohmouse a Guard, so a worn Guard Charm on a Sproutle (or a Setter Charm on a Zipmouse) would break the wear rule. The evolve takes off what the new form cannot wear (the same `wearBlock` as the level reset) inside the same transaction, and the answer says how many (`unequipped`); the app adds one short sentence to the notice. Not in the design; found while checking what a role change touches.
- **Keep is live, nothing new was needed in it.** The Creatures A work (two creatures per line, `line_slot`, `keepOffered`, the pending cards) only waited for a line with two branches. Offered while the line has a form the player does not own and fewer than two creatures; refused (`keep_not_offered`) otherwise. Worker tests run the whole path on the shipped content: a real trip brings a duplicate Sproutle, the card offers Keep, Keep makes the second creature, it evolves the other way, and Keep is gone with both forms owned or two creatures of the line. The Kept creature may also be evolved into the same form as the first (the player's choice; Keep is not blocked, only offered while it can lead to a form the player lacks). Recommended is Keep when nothing better (a talent the book already holds).
- **Talent pools are per line**, so Sunfawn and Ohmouse use their line's pool. A talent that does nothing for the new role (a Guard's armor on a Support) is flagged idle on its card, as for any creature.
- **The evolve screen is a Drawer** (`EvolveDrawer.tsx`, `evolve-view.ts`), opened by the evolve button only for a line with two forms (a line with one form evolves at once, as before). Two forms side by side at 390 px: picture, name, element and role chips, the ability (name and the content's sentence) and HP, Attack, Speed on the new form at this creature's level and fusions; nothing is picked at first (a permanent choice has no default); one button for the form that is picked ("Evolve into Sunfawn") over one line, "20 Essence. This choice is permanent." (permanent opens the new `evolution` glossary entry). The card and page buttons say "Choose an evolution" for these lines. Workbench page: `EvolveDrawer`.
- **Words.** New glossary entry `evolution`; `duplicate-choice` now explains Keep ("a second creature of a line with two forms, so you can evolve it the other way"); How to play's Creatures section says in two phrases that a few lines pick one of two forms and that a duplicate can sometimes be Kept. Lore and compendium entries for both forms (visual brief, traits); the quirk pool is the line's. Sprites: 16x16 in the chibi format with shiny palettes (Sunfawn: a fawn whose leaf ears have opened into pink blossoms; Ohmouse: a wide puffed mouse with giant ears, planted feet and sparks).
- **Content order.** The two species are appended at the end of `creatures.json` (and of the lore), not beside their line. Several tests build sample rosters from the first N species by index, and nothing reads the order.
- **Fixtures.** The creature view gained a field, so the four fixture sets that carry creature views (creatures, trips, dungeon, gear) were re-captured from real server output, as were the new `branches-*` ones (a Sproutle ready to evolve, a duplicate that offers Keep, Keep, both answers, the errors); `branches-fixtures.test.ts` checks their shapes, the client contract test reads them with the app's own parsers. The dungeon replay's `names.abilities` lists every ability, so it carries the two new ones.
- **Sim.** The journey takes the first (original) form of a line that grows two ways (`firstForm`, policy.ts), so every legacy digest and baseline stays as it was while another branch reworks the dungeon (lead's call). A role-aware pick, `pickBranch` (take the form whose role the three strongest other creatures lack, else the first), is built and tested for a later study; with it the first player's top-3 power moved 333 to 378, 361 to 349, 261 to 197 and 203 to 200, which is worth a look once the dungeon settles. Keep is not simulated (one creature per line). The balance scalars and the raid and dungeon curves are untouched.

## 2026-10-18: The last three weathers are live: Kind Rain, Morning Haze, Bramble Season

Finishes step 6 of `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` (section 14): all 10 Boons and all 10 Omens are in the valley. Technical decisions by the implementer, for the lead's review. No migration, no change to `RULES`, no raid or dungeon number tuned (another branch is reworking the dungeon).

- **Three new operations in the closed set** of `weather-schema.ts`, each with its words as a template: `roundHeal` (scaled, `bp` of max HP a round), `skipFirstTurn` (fixed, no number) and `bramble` (scaled, `bp` share of a reaction's bonus). Kind Rain's trip half uses the existing `tripParts` (25% more parts, `places` Tidepools and Verdant Hollow) and Morning Haze's the existing `tripTime` (+10%), so trips needed no new rule, only the data; the Verdant Hollow id is checked at load against the destinations as before. The `planned` status stays in the schema for later ideas (a planned entry is still never drawn); nothing ships as planned any more.
- **Engine hooks are optional fields**, so absent means exactly today's fight and the replay fixture and every stored fight are untouched (a test replays 24 random fights with the fields absent, empty and zero and compares byte for byte): `FightInput.rainHealBp`, `skipFirstTurn` (creature ids) and `brambleBp`; `DungeonRunInput.rainHealBp`, `hazeLeft` and `brambleBp`, copied into every room. They are built by `weatherSky(day)` (`@creatures/game`) and `skyFightFields` / `skyRunFields` (`packages/engine/src/sky.ts`) in the Worker's raid stage and dungeon run and in the sim, and stored with the fight input like the rest of the day. None of them draws a die, so no existing die moves.
- **Kind Rain (`rainHealBp`).** At round start, after the Supports' Mend and before finale abilities, every living creature heals `floor(max HP x bp / 10000)`, changed by its own `healReceivedBp` (so Long Shadows' "healing is 25% weaker" weakens the rain as well, as the Omen says), never above what is missing and never a revival. One `sky_heal` event per round lists who was really healed (a per-creature event would flood a 15-creature log); nothing is written when nobody needed it. It rains in enrage rounds too (the sky is not a boss move). Scaled by the Boon's strength: 2% at x1, 2.5% at rank 6 and 3% at rank 10.
- **Morning Haze (`skipFirstTurn`).** The named creatures skip the first turn they reach alive (a `skip` event with reason `haze`). A first turn that was silenced anyway is the lost turn (the silence counts down as usual and the haze is spent on it), so the haze never costs two turns; a creature that starts the fight knocked out never spends it. "First creature of each row" is the leftmost slot of each zone that has anyone (`firstOfEachRow`; a row of one or two is all Middle, so one creature). In the dungeon the row of three has one slot per zone, so the engine's zones would skip all three: the dungeon names only the left creature (`team[0]`) and does it in every room (each room is a fresh fight, as Fair Winds' first hit is per fight). The skip is fixed, not scaled (a count of turns). It draws no die: the skipped creature's dice are drawn and unused, so every other creature rolls what it always did.
- **Bramble Season (`brambleBp`), the rule chosen.** The first reaction each round (by a creature still standing) sends `bp` of its bonus damage back as a reflected hit on the creature that set it off, meaning the payoff creature as for Backdraft (`combat-rules.md` 1.17, 5.4.5); the boss takes the rest of the bonus, and the setup credit is worked out from what the boss took. The returning part goes through `hurt(..., 'reflect', 'reflect')`, the same path as Backdraft's, so a Guard that can steps in, then Shield Wall, armor, `reflectedBp`, the ward and `takenBp` all count: the gear line "Backdraft ward" works against it with no new code. It is a share, not the whole bonus (Backdraft's boss move still reflects 100% of its reaction): when Backdraft reflected the round's first reaction, Bramble does nothing more that round. Scaled by the Omen's strength like every Omen number. **The share is 12% at x1 (lead's call after the sim):** it appears from rank 6, where it is 18% (24% at rank 10, 30% at rank 14), round numbers at every strength. The design's 30% cost 5 points of finale wins at x1 and 17.8 at rank 14, against 0 to 2 for every other Omen; at 12% it costs 0.6 at x1, 1.3 at rank 6 and 5.0 at rank 14, still the hardest Omen at high ranks, as a late-game Omen should be. The number is `bp` in `weather.json`.
- **In the log and on the stage.** `describeEvent`: "Kind Rain mends A (+20 HP) and B (+20 HP).", "X is lost in the Morning Haze and misses its first turn.", and the reaction line gains "Bramble Season throws 95 of it (30%) back at X." (names as text). Playback counts `sky_heal` as a visible beat item; the stage shows green numbers on every creature healed and a small "Hazy" over a hazed creature (not the silence mark); the client's HP bars count the rain (`creatureHpAt`) and the client accepts the new event type. The wall calendar, forecast card and "Sky today" line read the data and needed no change. Workbench: the sky entries have presets for each of the three, and the stage page has a scenario for each.
- **Forecast.** `forecast.ts` plays the same fight input, so it predicts a day's rain, haze and brambles wherever the input carries them (no change to the file).
- **Tests.** The engine tests (`sky.test.ts`) cover each hook, "absent = identical", the dice, the dungeon's leftmost rule, Backdraft's interplay, the Guard and the Shield Wall; the game tests cover `weatherSky`, the schema and the draw (Haze never meets Fair Winds, Bramble Season never before rank 6); the Worker tests check the stored raid and dungeon inputs; the client tests check the cues and the HP.

## 2026-10-18: More gear and modifiers: position lines, one special line per item, and gear sets

Misa asked for more gear and modifiers. This builds section 6.1 (position lines) and 6.2 (set bonuses) of `gear-and-creatures-v2.md`; chance and trigger lines (6.3, 6.4) and the two position lines that need a per-round engine check (Warm burrow, Lane anchor) wait for their own work. Technical decisions by the lead agent and the implementer. No migration: set membership is the base's id in content, so stored items are untouched.

**Position lines (ten, group `position`).**

- **Which.** Shoulder to shoulder, Odd one out, Edge-walker, Heart of the row, Front-liner, Back-row patience, Lantern bearer, Four winds and Sheltered, plus **Beside the mark-maker**, which uses the existing `besideRoleBp` with `setter` (Guard's shadow's twin). Tier V is the design's number; tiers I to IV scale like Guard's shadow (about 25%, 40%, 60% and 80% of V). Slot weights lean each line where it makes sense (Front-liner never on a Hat, Back-row patience never on a Scarf, Lantern bearer and Four winds mostly on Charms); all roll from Common up. Beside the mark-maker differs from the table a little: `besideRoleBp` adds to every hit while a living Setter stands beside, not only to hits on a marked foe, so it has the shape of Guard's shadow and needed no engine work (tier V stays 10%).
- **How they are folded.** A creature's gear is folded again with its place when the fight input is built (`placeRow`, `packages/game/src/rules/gear-position.ts`; the engine's `placeSlots` applies it to a row of fight slots and `rowLayout` cuts the row). The engine sees only `FightSlot.stats`, `crit` and `mods`, so every stored fight replays as it was. **"Its row" is a zone in the raid** (Front, Middle or Back; neighbours stop at the zone's edge, as on the board) and **the whole line in the dungeon** and in the sim's reference fights, where each of the three zones is one slot: there the left slot is the Front row, the right slot the Back row, and Four winds can never fire (it needs a raid row of four or more). A creature whose numbers cannot change is not touched, so a row without position gear is exactly what it was. Guests (no gear) count as neighbours and as part of a row but are never changed: a Lantern bearer's HP goes to the creatures that have a fold kit (a player's creatures), not to guests.
- **Folds.** Shoulder to shoulder is Attack for each neighbour of its own element (at most two); Odd one out is Attack when no neighbour shares its element (also alone in a row); Edge-walker is Attack at either end of its row (also alone); Heart of the row is HP when it has a neighbour on both sides; Front-liner is `takenBp` in the Front zone; Back-row patience is crit chance in the Back zone; Lantern bearer is HP for the creatures beside it (added up, not for itself); Four winds is the reaction bonus of the wearer's own element when its row holds four different elements; Sheltered adds to the cover a Guard gives it (`SlotMods.shelterBp`, one engine field read in the cover step: no Guard beside it, no cover to add to). Attack and HP shares are global shares like an Attunement or Creature HP: they multiply the creature, never an item's own stat.
- **Equip best cannot see the row**, so a position line counts at its `worth`, which is the worth if it works times the share of places where it does (Edge-walker 18% of a hit's power, Front-liner 10%, Four winds 5%, and so on; Guard's shadow, which the engine checks, keeps its 40%). A first draft counted them at full worth and made Equip best prefer them over every utility Charm.

**At most one special line per item.** A line group in `SPECIAL_GROUPS` (`gear-schema.ts`: position now; chance and trigger lines join it) is special. `rollLines` takes the other special lines out of the pool once one is drawn (an Epic that rolls a position line first gets two ordinary lines more), and the content loader requires every position effect to be in the group and the other way round. Every way an item gets lines goes through `rollLines` (a drop, a craft, a Reach, the conversion, the dev tools) and nothing rerolls, so there is no other path to guard. Guard's shadow is in the group (it is the Guard's position line); stored items had at most one special line before, so none breaks the rule.

**Gear sets (ten, `gear-sets.json`).** Tidepool Finery, Forge-Warm Kit, Hollow Weave, Ridge Copper, Warden's Watch, Hearth Helpers, First Footprints, Pounce Set, Crossroads Wanderer and Lantern Night. Maker's Mark is not built (it needs who made each piece in the fold; later).

- **Pieces are bases.** 27 new bases (a Hat, a Scarf and a Charm for each of the nine plain sets) with `set` naming the set, plus a **Festival Lantern Cap** (a new Festival base, Grove tag, one fixed line, Lantern light: explorer XP) that completes Lantern Night with the two Festival pieces that were already there. A set piece has the base stats of the **fine (item level 10) Hat or Scarf** of its slot and tag (the neutral ones for the role and explorer sets) and the Charm line of the plain Charm of its tag: the bonus is the reward, no stat creep. It rolls lines like any base. The Hat and Scarf of a **role set are role-locked** like a role Charm (the loader allows a role tag on a Hat or Scarf only with `set`).
- **They unlock at item level 12** (`minItemLevel`; a set's Charm may now have one; the Lantern Night pieces are Festival pieces and stay at 1, they drop at the finale's level like all Festival gear). 12 is the first level after the fine makes (10) and inside the tier III band, which a committed player reaches around week 8 to 10: not an end-game wall.
- **How they drop.** A set piece is one more base of its slot and tag in the even draw (`dropBase`, `eligibleBases(..., withSets)`), so from item level 12 an element's drops are a quarter Hats and Scarves and half Charms a set piece: a third of all of them. The finale's role lock (1 in 20, finale only) now also covers the Hats and Scarves of the role sets from item level 12, and picks evenly among that role's pieces of the slot; no extra draw is made unless there is a choice, so below item level 12 every draw is what it was. The Old Crossroads gives the Compass until item level 12 and the Crossroads Wanderer set too from there (evenly among the explorer pieces). Misa kept this rate (2026-10-18: not too generous).
- **Blueprints.** Set pieces are craftable at Crafting level 6 (a craft makes item level 2 x level, and they unlock at 12) and their blueprints are learned like any other (finale reward slot, trips, dismantle luck). The **role sets' pieces are craftable too**, unlike the role Charms: the finale's role lock alone would make a role set a matter of hundreds of weeks, and a role player should be able to chase it (`craftable`: a role tag is fine when the base is in a set; Festival pieces still cannot be crafted). A blueprint roll picks a set piece with chance `RULES.gear.setBlueprintBp` (20%) and an ordinary base otherwise (`pickBlueprintBase`), so 27 more bases do not bury the ones a new player needs. A draw of 0 still picks the ordinary base, so scripted tests are as they were.
- **Counting.** `setCounts` counts one piece per slot of a set among what a creature wears (so 3 at most). Two pieces fold the set's plain lines through the same fold as the same line on an item (never reported as idle on an item card); three also fold one named effect, resolved when the sheet is built, so the engine sees numbers and flags.

**The three-piece effects: all built as specified, none replaced.** Each is an optional `SlotMods` field (absent: exactly today; the replay fixture is untouched) with a log line in `describe.ts`:

| Set                 | Field                                          | Reading                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Tidepool Finery     | `unreflected`                                  | The creature's first reaction of the fight cannot be reflected by the boss's trap: the trap is spent on it without effect (`proof` on the event).                                                                           |
| Forge-Warm Kit      | `critWindowBp` (2000)                          | After it sets off a reaction, its crits deal +20 pt crit damage through the end of the next round (`warm` on the hit).                                                                                                      |
| Hollow Weave        | `healOnReactionBp` (300)                       | It heals 3% of max HP whenever it cashes a reaction, never above what is missing (a `heal` event with the creature at both ends).                                                                                           |
| Ridge Copper        | `chainMark`                                    | After it cashes a reaction the boss keeps the creature's own mark, so the next creature can cash on it (Overload leaves Charged, a Grove creature can cash Grounding). Not when the reaction was reflected.                 |
| Warden's Watch      | `coverReach`                                   | A Guard's cover also reaches the creature two slots away in its row (the raid's zone, the dungeon's line).                                                                                                                  |
| Hearth Helpers      | `boostReach`                                   | A Support's boost also lands on the creature two slots away; only the boost (no Mend and no cleanse reach that far).                                                                                                        |
| First Footprints    | `sealMark`                                     | The first mark it leaves each fight cannot be replaced by another creature's hit (a Setter's replace). A finale ability's Re-mark can still replace it: that is a player's deliberate move.                                 |
| Pounce Set          | `firstReactionCrit`                            | Its first reaction each fight is a critical hit (when crits are on; it draws no extra die).                                                                                                                                 |
| Crossroads Wanderer | `GearRules.dayTripRolls`                       | One extra catch roll on a 24 hour trip, fixed when the trip is sent (the claim reads the stored rolls).                                                                                                                     |
| Lantern Night       | `lingerRounds` on the Guards of the wearer row | A gift of the row (`Placement.rowShieldWall`): every Guard in the row of a wearer of all three pieces has Shield Walls that last one more round (like Lingering, at most 3 together). Two wearers in a row do not stack it. |

Every effect that names a reaction works for a wearer of the element that cashes it (Steam is cashed by Tide wearers), and the reach effects only for the role that has a cover or a boost. Two pieces of a set worn by someone the effect does nothing for still give the set's plain lines.

**The client.** A set piece's art is its own sprite per slot (a trimmed variant of the plain one: a pointed cap with a star and a gold band, a striped scarf with a tassel, a gold-framed diamond), tinted by the set's tag; every tag has its own colour (the four elements, and one each for Guard, Support, Setter, Payoff, explorer and the Festival). The item card shows the set as a badge (tap: the set's glossary entry) and, under the lines, a block with the set's name, how many pieces the wearer has ("2 / 3") and the two bonuses, the ones reached in the gear colour with a marker and the word "active" for assistive technology. The creature page's gear section has one compact line of the active set bonuses under the slots. Glossary: one entry per set (generated from the set's own content, so the numbers cannot drift), `gear-set` and `gear-position` as fixed entries, and the generated line entries for the new lines. How to play's gear section keeps its five lines: two sentences ("Nothing re-rolls" and "Look for the pieces that fit your creature") came out to make room for one on position lines and sets; the tier entry still says nothing re-rolls.

**Sim (not tuned: Misa said no balancing now).** The fold and every policy use the same pure functions; the sim's raid weeks, dungeon runs and fight-scored power (`fight-power.ts`: one line of three, so position lines see their neighbours and ends) all fold the row. Gear share of fight-scored power, 20 servers of `leet:gear`, before (`87f8a30`) to after, at weeks 2, 8, 20 and 30: min-maxer 7.2, 23.2, 31.6, 31.6 to 6.9, 24.7, 30.6, 29.4; engaged 4.0, 12.4, 24.2, 26.9 to 4.7, 13.6, 24.3, 28.1; regular 3.3, 9.4, 16.7, 22.9 to 2.8, 8.7, 18.5, 24.2; drop-in 4.7, 5.0, 13.5, 20.1 to 5.4, 5.1, 14.2, 22.5. The moves are within about 2.5 points either way, which is the draws shifting (the pool of lines and bases is bigger, so every seeded drop is a different one); position lines are at most one line in five and fire only in the right place. The sim's pinned digests were re-pinned for the same reason. The raid and dungeon scalars were not touched.

**Fixtures.** The gear fixtures were captured again from real server output, because the scenario's seeded drops changed (the shapes are the server's, unchanged).

**Follows the board (merged with the fight-uses-the-board decision above).** Position lines, set reach and Morning Haze read the same board the fight does: a creature beside an empty board slot has no neighbour on that side (Lantern bearer, Same-element lines, Sheltered, Rally cry and the rest use `zoneNeighbours`), two places away (`coverReach`, `boostReach`) needs a creature in the slot between, Morning Haze skips the first creature standing in each board row, and the worker folds the gear with `sparseBoard`. A fight input without a `board` replays exactly as before.

## 2026-10-18: Weather numbers a player can read

Misa's forecast showed "31.2% more parts" and "Crit chance +3.7 pt": a Boon or Omen's number times the day's strength (x1.25, x1.5, x2.5) gave odd decimals. `scaleStrength` (`packages/game/src/rules/weather.ts`) now rounds the scaled number towards zero to whole percent or points from 10 up and to halves below (31.25% is 31%, 3.75 pt is 3.5 pt, 2.5% stays). It is the one function the rules and the text both use, so the number shown is still the number applied. The changes are at most half a point; the sim's legacy hashes moved and its headline numbers did not. Lead's call.

## 2026-10-18: Chance lines and trigger lines

Builds sections 6.3 and 6.4 of `gear-and-creatures-v2.md`. Technical decisions by the implementer, for the lead's review. No migration (a line is content and a stored item keeps its line ids and tiers), no change to `RULES`, no raid or dungeon number tuned (another branch reworks the dungeon). The engine reference is `combat-rules.md` sections 11 (chance) and 12 (trigger).

**What shipped.** 21 new lines, all special (an item has at most one special line: position, chance or trigger, through the same `rollLines` rule) and all **Rare and up**: nine **chance** lines (Second wind, Echo, Sidestep, Quick re-mark, Steady paws, Spill-over, Warm heart, Tinker's luck, Second look; Quick re-mark and Spill-over fell out of the mark code, so they are in) and twelve **trigger** lines (Payback, Kindling, Opening mark, Rally cry, Ember tail, Storm warning, Stubborn, Last stand, Overflow, Second breath, Patient, Shared warmth; the four optional ones fit cleanly, so all twelve are in). New line groups `chance` and `trigger` (both in `SPECIAL_GROUPS`), a new line unit `chance` (a card reads "Echo 8%", not "+8%"), and the loader checks that the group, the unit and the effect belong together (a chance above 100% is refused).

**Rarity, weights, worth.** "Rare and up" because a Common has one line and the design says these feel like Rare lines; a Rare or an Epic rolls one about a quarter of the time (a test checks it is between 10 and 45%). Stubborn is an Epic line from item level 12 with one value (like Lingering and Unbothered); Opening mark is a Rare line with one value. Weights lean the slots where the design says who wants it (Echo and Ember tail on Hats, Payback, Last stand and Sidestep on Scarves, Kindling and Warm heart on Charms) and sum to about 5 to 8% of a slot's pool for each of the chance and trigger kinds, so with the position lines (15 to 18%) a Rare or Epic's first line is special about a third of the time. The three Festival pieces each got one chance line in their narrow pool (Hearth Scarf: Second wind, Lantern Charm: Warm heart, Lantern Cap: Echo), so the +2 points of a Festival piece means something. `worth` is set so Equip best does not over-value them: a tier V line is worth under 4% of the wearer's power and never more than a 5 point crit chance (tier V chance lines 1.2 to 2%, triggers 0.5 to 2%, Stubborn 2%); a test checks both. The lines that fit a role carry `roles` (Payback, Last stand, Warm heart and Shared warmth: Guard; Kindling: Support; Opening mark: Setter; Echo, Ember tail, Overflow, Spill-over: Payoff), which only decides what a role-locked Charm rolls and who counts as "meant for" in Equip best; nothing is restricted.

**Values.** Chance is 4 / 6 / 8 / 10 / 12% at tiers I to V, **+2 points on an Epic or a Festival piece**. The bonus is worked out where the card and the fold read the line (`lineBonus` in `itemLines`, from the item's rarity and base), so a stored item needs no change and the card and the sheet agree. What a chance line does when it fires is not tiered and lives in code (`CHANCE_FX`: Second wind 5% of max HP, Echo 30% of the plain hit, Warm heart a 10% boost); a different size would be a new line, because it would change how a stored fight with that line replays. Trigger values are the design's number at tier V and about 25 / 40 / 60 / 80% of it below; Kindling is 40% at tier V (not the table's "a boost") because it works only on the first hit, where a Support's 15% works every round.

**The luck die.** A fight draws luck dice **only when some creature of it carries a chance line** (`hasChance`), and then `LUCK_DICE` = 10 per creature per round, uniform in [0, 10000), as the very last dice of the round (after crits, boss crits, the random target, the patterns, the Guard roll and the extra attacks' crits), so a fight without a chance line draws exactly what it always drew (the replay fixture is untouched, and a test replays 24 random fights with absent, empty and zero fields byte for byte and counts the draws). One die per kind of decision, numbered so one line never moves another's: 0 Second wind, 1 Echo, 2 Steady paws, 3 Warm heart, 4 Quick re-mark, 5 Spill-over, 6 to 9 Sidestep (the 1st to 4th area hit that creature meets in a round, the 4th for any after). All come from the fight's injected RNG (a seeded one in tests and the sim, the server's `crypto.getRandomValues` seed live); `Math.random` is not used. Round 1's other dice are identical with or without the line (a test); later rounds shift by the luck dice, as the Guard die has always shifted them.

**Chance lines in the fight.** Second wind: at round start, 5% of max HP (`healReceivedBp` applies, never above what is missing; nothing is written when nothing was missing), as a `heal` event with `cause: second_wind`. Echo: after a turn hit (not an ability's strike), if the boss still stands, the hit lands again for 30% of its **plain** damage (the reaction bonus is not repeated; no mark, reaction or crit), counted down to the phase floor like any hit (a `proc` event with the boss's HP; it can retreat the boss). Sidestep: an area hit that would reach the creature deals nothing, before Shield Wall, armor and cover (a `boss_hit` with `damage: 0` and `sidestep`); aimed and reflected hits are never sidestepped (so Bramble Season's reflect still lands). Steady paws: a silence the boss's move would put on it is shrugged off, after Unbothered. Warm heart: at round start, the living neighbour with the most Attack (ties to the lower slot) gets a 10% boost on its next hit. Quick re-mark: a non-Setter's hit on a boss with a mark of another element (not sealed) replaces it as a Setter's would. Spill-over: a reaction the creature sets off (not a reflected one) leaves its own mark, the chance version of Ridge Copper's `chainMark` (with both, Spill-over does nothing).

**Trigger lines in the fight.** All write a `proc` event; "next hit" is the creature's next **turn** hit (an ability's strike neither gets nor spends it, like `firstHitBp`). Payback: a creature that takes a hit meant for a neighbour (a Guard step-in, a reflected hit included, a Shield Wall that stops it included) and stands afterwards. Kindling: round 1, each living neighbour starts with a boost on its first hit (each dungeon room is a fresh round 1). Opening mark: round 1, the boss has no mark, the first creature in slot order with the line marks before anyone acts. Rally cry: a neighbour is knocked out (from any cause), all its hits deal more for the fight, once per fallen neighbour. Ember tail: a crit (turn or strike) gives the next turn hit a bonus. Storm warning: marked by a charge announcement, it takes less from that landing (splash included, not the plain attack of the same round). Stubborn: the first hit that would knock it out leaves it at 1 HP; the hit still shows its full damage and carries `stubborn`, the HP lost is what it really lost, and a Bramble Season or Backdraft reflect is a hit like any other (a test). Last stand: a hit leaves it standing at a quarter of its max HP or less (also when it started the fight below that), once, then it takes less from every hit. Overflow: it sets off a reaction, the next living creature in the round's turn order gets a boost on its next hit (nobody when it acts last; for an ability's strike at round start, the first in the order). Second breath: the first enrage round starts, it heals. Patient: a silence lands on it (not one it shrugged off or ignored), its next turn hit after the lost turn deals more. Shared warmth: a Support's Mend really heals it, that Support's next boost is bigger (it waits until the Support next boosts). The boosts the lines give go in the same list as a Support's, kept apart by a tag (a Support replaces its own boost each round, never one of these), are counted in `bonusBp` and credited as support credit to the creature whose line gave them.

**Tinker's luck and Second look (the two out-of-combat chance lines).** Both read the worn gear at the claim, like Apprentice and Lucky find, and use the server's live randomness. **Craft cost** means the plain craft's parts for the base and item level (`craftCost`: scrap by item level, plus one shard of the base's element for an element base; not a Reach's glimmer, shards or Essence, which the item does not remember). Tinker's luck: for each item of a dismantle job the wearer runs, with the chance, each kind of part is lifted to the craft cost when the ranges gave less (glimmer and Essence stay as rolled); one extra die per item, after that item's other draws and only when the worker has the line. The claim result says how many items it refunded (`tinkered`) and the job reveal says so. Second look: a **duplicate** the explorer's trip brings rolls its talent twice (one die for the chance, then a second roll) and keeps the better, where **better** is `talentScore` (the same worth Equip best and Recommended use) for the player's creature of that line (the first one, if they Kept two); a tie keeps the first roll. The trip result badges the catch "Second look". A Festival duplicate has no explorer, so it never has one. Both are stored with the claim's result, so a retry or a race returns the stored result and rolls nothing (worker tests through the real routes, with concurrent claims).

**The client.** The item card needed no change: it lists each line's server text with its number ("Echo 8%", "Payback +30%", "Stubborn") and tier pips, and every line opens its generated glossary entry (the line's `about` words, under 80 characters, section `gear`); two fixed entries, `gear-chance` and `gear-trigger`, explain the kinds (and `gear-position` now says the three kinds are special). The fight log reads every new event (`describeEvent`; a `proc` is a visible beat for the pacing); the stage floats a word over the creature whose line fired, a "+N%" over a creature that got a boost, the damage of an Echo over the boss, "SIDESTEP" (no flash, no shake) and green numbers for the heals; the replay moves the boss's HP for an Echo and the creatures' HP for a Stubborn hit and the heals, which a test checks against ten real engine fights with every line on (every creature ends with the engine's HP, the boss with its own). The client accepts the `proc` event type. Workbench: ItemCard presets "A Hat with a chance line (Echo, Epic: +2 pt)" (`trail-cap`, epic, item level 15, lines `echo:4,crit-damage:3,attack-local-pct:2`), "A Charm with a trigger line (Payback)" (`cinder-charm`, rare, 15, `payback:4,hp:3`), "A Scarf with a once-a-fight trigger line (Stubborn)" (`woolen-wrap`, epic, 15, `stubborn:1,area-ward:3,hp-local-pct:2`), "A Charm for the Workshop (Tinker's luck)" and "A Festival piece with a chance line (Second wind, +2 pt)"; Stage "Boss moves" scenarios "Stubborn (stays up at 1 HP)" and "Chance and trigger lines (Echo, Sidestep, Payback)".

**Sim (not tuned: Misa said no balancing now).** The sim plays the combat lines in its fights (the same engine; the luck dice come from the fight's seeded RNG) and does not play Tinker's luck or Second look. Gear share of fight-scored power, median at weeks 2, 8, 20 and 30, 20 servers of `leet:gear`, measured on this branch with the new lines off (their weights zeroed, which is the state before) and on: min-maxer 8.0, 23.3, 30.0, 30.3 to 7.5, 22.6, 28.6, 29.4; engaged 4.5, 13.4, 24.1, 26.2 to 4.4, 14.3, 24.9, 28.4; regular 3.0, 10.2, 17.7, 24.3 to 3.1, 9.3, 17.1, 22.5; drop-in 5.6, 4.5, 15.0, 21.4 to 5.3, 5.8, 14.7, 21.4. Every move is within about 2 points either way, the draws shifting (the pool is bigger, so every seeded drop of a Rare or an Epic is another item). The numbers of the position-lines entry above (for example min-maxer 6.9, 24.7, 30.6, 29.4) were taken before the weathers were merged in, so they are not the "before" of this change. The pinned digests were re-pinned (reason in `legacy-unchanged.test.ts`): the run without gear, Bait earned and raids won are the same; the first player's top-3 power moved 375, 248, 209 to 370, 246, 205. `pnpm scalars` was run (the new lines are knobs).

**Fixtures.** Of the real-server captures (gear, dungeon, trips, creatures, branches) only the two gear conversion notices changed in content (the converted items roll other lines); every other change was fresh ids and an unseeded quirk, so those files were left as they were. The shapes are unchanged (the dismantle result has an optional `tinkered`, a catch an optional `secondLook`).

**Not done.** Warm burrow and Lane anchor (position lines that need a per-round check of who stands beside a creature) and Maker's Mark stay later ideas.

## 2026-10-18: flipping between creatures and items

Misa: both detail views (the creature page, the item drawer) should let you flip to the next or previous thing, by swipe on a phone and by buttons and keys on a desktop, and the two should work the same. One shared building block, `ui/Flipper.tsx` (`FlipNav`, `FlipArea`, `FlipLive`) with its pure rules in `ui/flip-logic.ts` and the key handler in `ui/flip-keys.ts`; the owners of a list build the props with `flipOver`.

- **Where it is used.** The creature page flips through the creatures in the order of the Creatures tab's list as it is shown (the chips and search as they are; if a change under an open page hides the creature, through the whole roster). The Workshop's item drawer flips through the bag in the grid's order (`sortItems`, the same as `InventoryGrid`). The creature page's item picker flips through that slot's grid (the worn item first, then the rest). Nothing draws (no arrows, no swipe, no keys) for a single thing. The equip, take off, give to another and dismantle actions work on the item now shown.
- **No wrap-around.** The first thing has no previous and the last no next. The arrow at an end is dimmed and says `aria-disabled` (not `disabled`, so a keyboard user who reached the end keeps the focus where it was); a swipe at an end moves the content a little and it settles back (a rubber band, 30% of the drag, at most 28 px).
- **Swipe.** Pointer Events on the detail content, `touch-action: pan-y pinch-zoom`. A finger or a pen counts; the mouse does not (it would fight with selecting text), except in the workbench where a switch allows it. The first 10 px of a move decide: sideways wins only if it is at least 1.7 times the vertical move (about 30 degrees from the horizontal), anything else is a scroll and the gesture is left alone for good. Letting go flips if the move was 56 px (or a third of the area's width, if that is less), or a flick: 24 px at 0.5 px/ms. Left is next, right is previous. A swipe that starts in a text field, a list, a slider or a part marked `data-no-flip` is ignored. A swipe that starts on a plain button is not (swiping across a big button is natural) and the click that follows it is swallowed, so it never also presses the button; a tap with no sideways move stays a tap. The content follows the finger while it is held and the new thing slides in from the side over 160 ms; with `prefers-reduced-motion` nothing is eased or slid (the content still follows a held finger).
- **Keys.** Left and Right flip while the page or the drawer has the focus; they do nothing in a text field (Rename), with a modifier, or when something already handled the key. A drawer keeps the arrow keys from the page behind it, so the picker's arrows flip its items, not the creature behind it.
- **Look.** Two pixel arrow buttons with "3 / 12" between them in the quiet colour (a spaced slash, never "of"), 36 px high on the creature page and a small look in a drawer's heading row. On the creature page the arrows take a thin row of their own under Back and Lounge (a 390 px screen has no room for three controls in one row); in the Workshop's item drawer they sit at the top left of the handle's row with the close button at the right; in the picker they sit between the title and the close button and take the place of the "n in the bag" note. The buttons are named "Previous creature: Cindle", "Next item: Trail Cap".
- **Focus and speech.** A flip with an arrow button keeps the focus on that button, also on the creature page, which is drawn anew for each creature (so its scroll position, drawers and expansions start clean and it is at the top). A flip with a key or a swipe puts the focus on the page (the creature page) or leaves it where it is, and the drawer takes it if the control that had it went with the old item. A polite live region that stays drawn says "Cindle, 3 / 12" after a flip (it says nothing on opening). Back from a page that was flipped scrolls the list to the card the page ended on, instead of restoring the old scroll position.
- **Workbench.** `FlipNav` (Controls): the arrow looks, a swipe pad (mouse drag allowed with the switch), the real creature page over a sample roster, the Workshop item drawer and the item picker. `Workshop` has an optional `initialSelectedId` (like the page's `initialOpen`) so the workbench can show its drawer open.
- **Tests.** The gesture rules as pure functions, the arrows, keys, boundaries and swipes against the fake DOM (`test-dom.ts` learned pointer events, capture-phase listeners and `classList`), the three real views, and a check that each of them goes through the shared building block.

## 2026-10-18: Second forms for the six other evolving lines: every line grows two ways

Step 7 of `gear-and-creatures-v2.md` (section 8.4) finished: after Sproutle and Zipmouse, the other six evolving lines get a second form too, so every evolving line has two, each element has all four roles at stage 2 between its lines, and Keep is offered for every evolving line. Names are working names from the lead agent's brief (Misa may rename; they sit in `creatures.json`, the lore, the sprites and the compendium). Technical decisions by the implementer, for the lead's review. No migration (a stored creature keeps its species; the new forms are only reachable through the evolve request), no `RULES` change, no balance tuning.

| Line     | First form (as today) | Second form   | Element | Role    | Ability                                                                                                                   |
| -------- | --------------------- | ------------- | ------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Splashet | Tidewing (Setter)     | **Geyserpup** | Tide    | Payoff  | Geyser Burst: a strike for 320% of its Attack, round 5                                                                    |
| Cindle   | Pyrelynx (Payoff)     | **Wickat**    | Ember   | Setter  | Slow Burn: leaves the boss Scorched; all creatures hit 6% harder for 2 rounds; round 3                                    |
| Mistnewt | Fogwyrm (Guard)       | **Drizzlewt** | Tide    | Support | Soft Rain: all creatures hit 5% harder for 2 rounds; the boss's adaptation is off for 2 rounds; round 5                   |
| Ashmole  | Magmole (Support)     | **Kilnmole**  | Ember   | Guard   | Banked Embers: its zone takes 70% less damage for 2 rounds; all creatures hit 5% harder this round; at the first zone hit |
| Stormray | Tempestray (Payoff)   | **Halcyray**  | Spark   | Support | Afterglow: all creatures hit 8% harder for 2 rounds; its zone takes 30% less damage this round; round 5                   |
| Mossback | Ancientshell (Setter) | **Bloomback** | Grove   | Payoff  | Full Bloom: a strike for 300% of its Attack, round 5                                                                      |

- **Names, from the line's own words.** Geyserpup: Splashet's droplet tuft finally opens into a spout (quirk "Keeps its feet very dry and hates a surprise puddle"). Wickat: Cindle's ember steadies into a lit wick (quirk "Is a little scared of its own sparks": it stopped jumping). Drizzlewt: Mistnewt's mist cloud becomes a rain cloud (quirk "Fusses over its own mist cloud more than anyone"). Kilnmole: Ashmole's burrow and ash set into a shell of baked clay with a chimney, a burrowing armoured mole (quirk "Hates getting its paws dirty"). Halcyray: halcyon plus ray, the ray that comes after the storm (quirk "Prefers sunny days and says so often"). Bloomback: Mossback's garden opens into one enormous flower (quirk "Pretends to ignore its garden, but checks it twice a day"). All are short, one word and playful like Sunfawn and Ohmouse.
- **Stats follow the role budget** (finished forms within about 5% of each other inside a role; same rule as Sunfawn and Ohmouse). Each takes the existing stage 2 of its role and rarity as the reference, exactly: Geyserpup is Pyrelynx's numbers (HP 134, Attack 56, Speed 39; growth 9 / 3 / 3; 664 summed at level 30), Wickat is Voltaro's (157 / 39 / 45; 11 / 3 / 3; 734), Drizzlewt is Magmole's (179 / 34 / 39; 12 / 2 / 3; 745), Kilnmole is Ohmouse's (224 / 34 / 22; 15 / 2 / 2; 831, the common Guard budget), Bloomback is Tempestray's (168 / 70 / 49; 12 / 5 / 3; 867). There is no rare Support stage 2, so Halcyray is the common Support budget at the rare rate: every rare is 1.25 times the common of its role at stage 1 (Stormray 120 / 50 / 35 against Cindle 96 / 40 / 28; Mossback 140 / 35 / 40 against Zipmouse 112 / 28 / 32), so Magmole's 179 / 34 / 39 gives 224 / 42 / 49 (42.5 rounded down), growth 15 / 2 / 4 (12 / 2 / 3 times 1.25, rounded to keep the sum at 21 like Ancientshell's 14 / 3 / 4), 924 summed at level 30 (Tempestray 867, Ancientshell 910). A test (`content.test.ts`) pins each form to its reference. Level cap 30, rarity as the line, never catchable (the loader and a test, now over all eight branch forms, refuse a catchable form or a place that lists one). Both forms of a line cost the same level and Essence (the loader's rule from the first branches): 15 and 20 Essence, and 15 and 40 for Stormray and Mossback.
- **Abilities need no engine work**, only the existing `strike`, `rally`, `shield_wall`, `re_mark` and `purge` effects, in the strength band of the stage-2 abilities of their role. The two Payoffs copy their sibling's strike (Pyrelynx 320%, Tempestray 300%). Slow Burn is a Setter's mark plus a 6% rally for 2 rounds, about the 12% of Tidal Mark in total. Soft Rain is a common Support (5% for 2 rounds, below Warm Glow's 8%) with a short purge, a nod to the fog line. Banked Embers sits between Static Wall (60% for 2 rounds, then a mark) and Fog Veil (70% for 2 rounds, then a long purge). Afterglow is Magmole's rally (8% for 2 rounds) with Sunny Patch's 30% wall, the rare Support's pay for its rare price. No new field, so every stored fight still replays exactly and the replay fixture and the engine are untouched. A Guard fires at the first zone hit like every Guard; the rest on their default round.
- **The upgrade rule is unchanged**: only the first branch must out-do stage 1, the loader's rule from Sunfawn. All six abilities are new ids and each belongs to one species.
- **Sprites.** 16x16 chibi, each the same creature grown differently (like Thornbuck and Sunfawn): Geyserpup keeps Splashet's drop-shaped body and its tuft opens into a tall spout with spray; Wickat keeps Cindle's round kitten with a tall flame on a dark wick, floating sparks and a small lit tail; Drizzlewt keeps Mistnewt's low body under a wide grey-blue rain cloud with falling drops; Kilnmole is Ashmole's snout and tiny eyes under a dome of clay bricks with a glowing chimney; Halcyray keeps Stormray's diamond and violet wings, with a small rainbow arc and a mint ribbon in place of the bolt; Bloomback keeps Mossback's dome shell under one huge pink flower with a yellow heart. Each has a shiny palette in the line's hue (the pair matches). The sprite tests (distinct silhouette, 12 pixels apart from every other creature, bigger than stage 1, the shared face, 11 colours at most) pass for all.
- **The evolve screen needed no change.** Checked in the workbench at 390 px for all eight pairs, shiny and not: the longest names (Ancientshell, Tempestray, Drizzlewt) fit on one line in the half-width columns, the longest ability sentences (Ancient Bloom) wrap inside their card, and the stats row stays aligned. The workbench page `EvolveDrawer` has a preset for each line. Creature card, creature page, Keep, the pending card and the server read `evolution.alt` generically, so no client code changed; the server's `not_a_branch` check and the Charm-off rule (`wearBlock`) run on the content, and tests now run the evolve request for both forms of all eight lines.
- **Words.** The glossary entry `evolution` says every line that evolves grows one of two ways; `duplicate-choice` says Keep is for an evolving line; How to play says Many evolve at 15 for Essence, and each picks one of two forms. Lore and compendium entries for all six (visual brief, traits, notes), compendium counts (30 species) and the quirk pools unchanged (a Stage 2 form uses its line's pool).
- **Content order.** The six species are appended after Ohmouse in `creatures.json` and in the lore (order is not read anywhere), the abilities beside their line's.
- **Fixtures.** The creature view gained `evolution.alt` for the six lines, and the dungeon replay's `names.abilities` lists every ability, so the contract tests of the creatures, trips, dungeon, gear and branches fixtures showed a change. The 38 fixture files that carry a creature of those lines or the ability names were patched with exactly those fields (no new ids or timestamps, so the diff stays small) and the contract tests pass against real server output. The creatures scenario answers Splashet duplicates and shows Keep refused, which Splashet's new second form would have changed, so that scenario now runs with no branches (`branchesOf`, as when it was written); Keep on the shipped content has its own scenario (branches) and tests.
- **Sim.** The journey takes the first form (`firstForm`), so every legacy digest and baseline holds; `pickBranch` (the role-aware pick) is still not switched on.
- **Not done, for Misa's eye.** The talent pools of the six lines were written for the first form, so a talent that does nothing for the second form's role shows as idle on its card (as for Ohmouse); adding second-role talents to each pool is a later pass, like Sproutle's "Shares the Sun" and "Steady Under Pressure". Rare Support stage 2 has no other reference, so Halcyray's numbers are an extrapolation at the rare rate; the systems review should look at it when the dungeon settles.

## 2026-10-18: The flip arrows look the same everywhere

Misa: the arrows looked different on the creature page and in the item drawers. Now they are one full-width row in both: previous at the left, the position ("3 / 12") in the middle, next at the right, same button size. On the creature page the row sits under Back and Lounge; in a drawer it is its own row under the heading (`.drawer-flip`), no longer squeezed into the handle strip. The small variant of `FlipNav` is gone. Then (Misa, same day): the buttons say Prev and Next beside the arrows (the words are hidden from screen readers, which hear the button's label); the creature page's row has a little margin above and below; the drawer's row has a gap before the content; and the Workshop item drawer shows no X (a tap outside, the handle or Escape closes it; the close button still takes the keyboard focus and shows when focused). Later the same day Misa made it a rule: **no drawer shows an X** (the picker, the Talent Book, the evolve drawer, every one). A tap outside, the handle or Escape closes a drawer; the close button stays for the keyboard and screen readers and shows only while focused (`drawer.css`).

## 2026-10-18: Compare in the item picker (what Equip would change)

Selecting an item in a slot's picker that is not the worn one now shows what wearing it would change for this creature, in a row under the item card: the changes on the left, the Equip button on the right (so it reads as the answer to "what does Equip do?"; the button moved out of the card's top right corner for wearable items, Take off and the reason stay there).

- **One fold, no copy.** The rules are `compareGear` in `packages/game/src/rules/gear-compare.ts`: it runs `applyGear` twice (the worn items now, and with the item in place of its slot's) and takes the difference, so a set's plain lines, percent lines, the item's own stats and talents are counted as the server counts them. The client (`creature-page/compare-view.ts`) only builds the input from what it holds: effective stats from level and fusion, the species' crit, the worn items looked up in the bag for their lines, and the active talents (`activeTalents` over the creature's Talent Book). If a worn item is missing from the bag, or the creature cannot wear the item (the picker already says why), there is no block.
- **What shows.** Only stats that change: HP, Attack, Speed, then crit chance and crit damage in points ("Attack +12", "HP −30", "Crit damage +20 pt"), green for up and red for down, with the sign in the words and a spoken copy ("minus 30"), so colour is never the only signal. Set lines only when a set's bonus changes (0 to 1 piece and a swap inside one set say nothing): "Forge-Warm Kit 3 / 3: both bonuses", "Hollow Weave 2 / 3: 2 piece bonus", "Loses Hollow Weave 2 / 3". The item's own lines are not repeated (the card lists them). An empty slot compares to nothing, so every number is a gain. When nothing changes it says "No stat change".
- **Limits.** Position lines, a fight's neighbours and lines that only matter in a fight are not in a stat number, so they never show here (the fold the server uses for the creature page's numbers has no placement either). A set's three-piece effect that does nothing for this creature's element still says "both bonuses" (the card's set block says the same).
- **Workbench.** `CreaturePage` scenes K1 to K5 (better item, worse item, completes a set, breaks a set, empty slot). The sample bag now uses the game's own base stats and a real local Attack line on epics, so the card and the compare agree. `ItemPickerDrawer` got an optional `initialPicked`.

## 2026-10-18: Compare inline on the item card, with a switch

Misa saw the compare row and wanted the changes shown on the card itself, where they belong, with a switch. In the creature page's item picker (not the Workshop: it has no creature) the selected item's card now compares itself with the item worn in that slot.

- **Inherent stats.** Each base stat row (Attack, HP, crit) gets its difference to the worn item's same stat after the value: green with "+" when higher, red with "−" when lower, nothing when equal. A stat only the new item has counts all of it as a gain; a stat only the worn item has shows as a red row ("Crit damage −20 pt"). Numbers are the server's own stat values.
- **Mod lines.** Lines are matched by id; an element or reaction line has its own id per element (`ember-hunter`, `tide-hunter`), so those are different lines. On both items with another value: the difference after the value (from the game's resolved line values, which count the tier and the chance bonus), signed in the unit of the line; a reduction line (a ward) is written with the sign of the number it shows ("−4%", a bigger minus, green), so green and red always mean better and worse. Only on the new item: marked new (a green "+", "New:" for screen readers). Only on the worn item: listed at the end in red with a "−", struck through, "Lost:" for screen readers. Tier pips are as before. Colour is never alone: every mark has a sign and a spoken text ("plus 2 pt, better").
- **The switch.** "Compare", a small pixel switch (`role="switch"`, `aria-checked`) above the card, on by default, remembered in `localStorage` per browser (read and write in try/catch; without storage it starts on and still works). Off is the plain card. It is hidden when the slot is empty or the selected item is the worn one. The row beside Equip (stat totals and set changes, from `compareGear`) shows only while the switch is on, and always for an empty slot (where there is no switch).
- **Code.** `creature-page/compare-diff.ts` (pure diff), `CompareSwitch.tsx`, `compare-pref.ts`; `ItemCard` takes an optional `diff` and `ItemLines` an optional `diff` and a `DeltaMark`. The sample bag now uses real line ids and the game's own `itemLines`, so the workbench matches the server's shapes. Workbench presets K1 to K5 show the inline version, K6 the switch off.

**Follow-up (same day, after Misa's look).** The compare delta is written as an arrow in brackets, "Attack 28 +16 (▲42)" in green and "(▼16)" in red (arrow and colour mean better and worse; a spoken copy still says "plus 42, better"), the same on stat rows, crit rows and lines, so it is never read as part of the number or as the card's own local bonus. The row beside Equip only exists when it has the changes to show: with the switch off (or no numbers) Equip is back in the card's top right corner as before the compare, and in the row the text is centred against the button.

**Follow-up 2 (same day).** Misa dropped the stat change box beside Equip: the totals and set lines for the creature are gone, with their code, styles and tests, and so is the shared `compareGear` of `packages/game` (nothing else used it; the earlier paragraphs about the row and `compareGear` no longer apply). What is left is the inline compare on the card. The detail panel has one bottom row: the Compare switch at the left and the action at the right (Equip, Take off, or the reason an item cannot be worn), so actions always sit in the same place and Equip is no longer in the card's corner. The switch is hidden when there is nothing to compare (empty slot, worn item) and the action stays at the right. **Compare is off by default**, and a viewer who switches it on keeps it on (`localStorage` key `creatures:picker-compare`, read and written in try/catch; without storage it starts off). Workbench: K1 to K4 show it on, K5 an empty slot, K6 the default off.

**Follow-up 3 (same day).** Every lost line stays listed (no cap). Instead the picker's detail panel cannot grow without limit: the card sits in its own scroll box (`ip-detail-scroll`, `max-height: 40dvh`, with a `vh` fallback; 338 px on a 390 x 844 phone), and the bottom row (Compare switch left, Equip or Take off right) is outside it, so the actions are always visible. Why 40%: the drawer is at most 88% of the screen (743 px), its handle and heading take about 75 px and the action row about 56 px, which leaves the grid more than the 200 px its two rows need. A vertical drag scrolls the box (the flip area only takes a sideways swipe, `touch-action: pan-y`), `overscroll-behavior: contain` keeps it from dragging the page, and the box takes keyboard focus (`tabindex="0"`, a labelled group) so it can be scrolled with the keys. Workbench preset K7 shows a long Epic against a long worn Epic.

## 2026-10-18: The scroll hint (a reusable building block)

Misa: players may not notice that the picker's detail panel scrolls, because it has no scrollbar on purpose. One reusable piece in `apps/activity/src/ui/`: `use-scroll-hint.ts` (the hook `useScrollHint(ref, contentKey, options)`), `ScrollHint.tsx` (a box that uses it and draws the fade), `scroll-hint-logic.ts` (the rules as pure functions) and `styles/components/scroll-hint.css`.

- **Behaviour.** When the content overflows, the box rests `delayMs` (2500), then the hook moves it down at `speed` px a second (12), one `requestAnimationFrame` at a time with a fractional position of its own (the browser rounds `scrollTop`), until the bottom, and stops. It never scrolls up. It is over for that content at the first `pointerdown`, `touchstart`, `wheel`, `keydown`, focus inside, or a scroll it did not cause (the box is not where the hint last put it, with 1.5 px of room for rounding). A new `contentKey` (an item selected or flipped to, or the compare switched) starts it again from the top, every time, with no memory. A box whose content fits sets no timer and asks for no frame; timers, frames and listeners go on every content change and on unmount. A long gap between frames (a hidden tab) counts as at most 100 ms, so it never jumps.
- **Look.** The scrollbar stays hidden. While there is more below, a soft fade (1.25 rem, in `--scroll-hint-bg`, the panel's colour) shows at the bottom edge; it is on generally, not only under reduced motion. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (or `reducedMotion: true`) nothing is scrolled and the fade is the hint.
- **Use.** The item picker's detail panel (`ip-detail-scroll`, keyed by the item and the compare switch), and the workbench page `ScrollHint`. The picker's drawer footer no longer scrolls on its own (`max-height: none`), so the action row can never scroll away.
- **Tests.** The rules, and the hook through the component with fake timers, a stubbed `requestAnimationFrame` and a stubbed box size: nothing when the content fits, starts after the delay, the options, stops at the bottom, stops on each of the five interactions (during the rest and while moving), a foreign scroll, not stopped by its own scroll events (also with rounding), restarts on new content, cleanup, reduced motion, the fade.

## 2026-10-18: A second tap closes the item details in the picker

Misa asked how a player gets back to the grid once an item's details fill the bottom of the picker. Tapping the item shown again now closes its details (its cell's `aria-pressed` goes false), so the grid has the whole drawer and scrolls freely; tapping any item opens them again. With nothing shown, the Prev / Next row gives way to the "N in the bag" count. The picker still opens with an item shown (the worn one, else the first choice). Then (Misa): the item shown, or shown last after its details closed, keeps a selected look in the grid (lit from inside, with a light edge inside the rarity edge) and is scrolled into view when its details close; the creature list marks the card of the creature whose page was open last the same way (`current`), and Back makes sure that card is in view even if the list moved meanwhile.

## 2026-10-18: Item grid cells are square

Misa expected square item cells. The picker grid (`.ip-cell`) and the Workshop bag (`.inv-cell`) now take `aspect-ratio: 1` instead of a minimum height, so every cell is as tall as it is wide whatever the screen width (about 72 px and 78 px at 390 px).

## 2026-10-18: One item grid

Misa did not want two item grids. The item picker's look won (lead's call, Misa asked which): the rarity is the tile's single edge (the bag drew a dark tile and a framed image inside it, two edges, against the "one border" rule of 2026-10-17), the art fills the square, a gold "Worn" flag sits at the top right, and the selected tile is lit with a light edge. `ItemTile.tsx` is the one tile and `.item-grid` the one grid (`styles/components/item-tile.css`); the picker and the Workshop bag (`InventoryGrid`) both use them. A selected tile scrolls itself into view. The bag's own styles (`views/inventory.css`) are gone.

## 2026-10-18: Keeping the gear fold fast

PR #35's CI timed out on a nine-week sim run (`dungeon.test.ts`, 120 s): part 2 had made that run about 27% slower (32 s to 41 s locally). The profile put it in `wornEffects`, which every fold and every Equip best candidate runs: capping 19 possible sheet fields read each one off a mostly empty object (one fold went from 0.65 to 1.15 µs). The cap now walks only the fields the sheet holds, and the set fold skips counting when no worn item belongs to a set; one fold is 0.72 µs again and the run is about 13% over main (the rest is the new fight work). The nine-week test got the 300 s limit the other full runs (`run.test.ts`, `cli.test.ts`) already have. Nothing the fold returns changed; the legacy digest is untouched.

## 2026-10-18: Play-test round 2: one type scale, one pixel font per panel, Equip on, dismantle as badges

Misa's feedback on PR #35's drawers. Each point and what was decided:

- **Dismantle panel.** It opens scrolled to its own start (the title and "Who does the work?"), not to its buttons: the cause was the safe "Keep it" button taking focus at the panel's end, which scrolled it into view. The focus now goes there with `preventScroll`, and the panel scrolls itself to its start. What it gives is count badges (`ui/CountBadge`, now also taking a text amount): scrap, the element's shard (an any-kind shard has a sparkle and "any kind"), glimmer, Essence (the gem glyph), ranges written "3–4" and spoken "3 to 4"; the time is one more badge with the clock glyph ("51 min", `formatDuration`). The help line is "7% per piece to learn its blueprint or raise its quality." with "raise its quality" as a glossary tag. `dismantleLines` (the prose) is gone; `dismantleCountItems` and `durationCountItem` replace it.
- **Blueprint quality (Misa, same day).** Players read a blueprint's marks as its **quality**, not "well-thumbed": "Quality 3 / 10" wherever marks are counted (`qualityText`; the Blueprints list, the craft details, the loot and reveal lines), the glossary entry is "Blueprint quality" (id `blueprint-quality`, was `well-thumbed`): "Each point of a blueprint's quality makes its crafts 1% likelier to come out one rarity better, up to 10." Reach, Blueprint and Dismantle entries and the compendium say it the same way. Code names stay (`marks`, `marksText`, API fields, columns, `RULES.gear.craft.marks`). A test keeps "thumbed" out of every component.
- **Type scale.** Raw sizes were scattered (about 250 `font-size` declarations in 60 stylesheets, most of them 0.875, 0.8125 and 1 rem), and the item card's 1.125 rem rows were bigger than the creature page's 1 rem rows. Four tokens in `base/tokens.css`, for the pixel font (Jersey 10 draws about 28% bigger than its value, see `fonts.css`): `--fs-title` 1.5 rem (a drawer's or page's title, the creature's name), `--fs-heading` 1.25 rem (a card's name, a sheet's title), `--fs-row` 1 rem (a row: a stat, a modifier line, a picker option, a button in a panel), `--fs-small` 0.875 rem (a note under a row, a badge, a reason). Applied where this round touched: the item card (rows 1.125 to 1 rem, name 1.375 to 1.25 rem, line notes 1 to 0.875 rem), the picker's detail panel (`ip-detail`, its Equip button, the "On Pebble" note, the reason), the Switch, the Equip on row and the creature picker sheet (title 1.125 to 1.25 rem, name, level and notes). **Still raw (a follow-up):** the drawer title and the creature's name (1.5 rem, the value of `--fs-title`, but not using it yet), the creature page's other rows (`.cc-chip`, `.cp-sets` 1.125 rem, the head's XP text), the accordion titles, the Workshop's job and craft rows, `picker.css` (the creature radio list of the dismantle and craft panels: 0.9375 and 0.8125 rem), the Expeditions, Raid, Dungeon and How to play stylesheets, and the glossary popover.
- **"Needs level N."** The line under the card's modifiers is gone: the item's level is on its icon, and the picker says why a creature cannot wear it.
- **Equip on.** The Workshop item drawer's order is now: the card, the "Equip on" row, Dismantle, Details. The row is a trigger that looks like a dropdown (the wearer's sprite and name, or "Choose a creature", with a chevron) and opens the existing creature sheet (`ui/RosterPicker`, generalised without changing the raid and dungeon use: `status` is optional, an option can be a plain `action` such as "Take off", the sheet takes `pix-panel`). A pick equips at once (the usual busy state and error handling of `actions.equip`; no separate Equip button; the sheet closes). The options come from `equip-picker.ts`: for a worn item "Take off" first, then the wearer ("Wears it now"; picking it changes nothing), then the creatures that can wear it (highest level first), then those that cannot with a short reason: "Needs level 7" (the item's level or its slot's opening level, from `wearBlock`), "Guards only", "Away on a trip", "On a job" (the server refuses `creature_away` and `creature_on_job`, and checks them first).
