The server also refuses to move or take off an item whose wearer is away or on a job (`assertNotBusy` on the old wearer), so then Take off and every other option say "Cindle is away on a trip" instead of failing after the tap. The "Who cannot wear it" list, the native select and "Give to another" are gone. The sheet is opened inside a Drawer, so the Drawer's panel has a `transform` only while it is pulled now (a transform, even a zero one, makes the panel the containing block of `position: fixed`, which would have trapped the sheet inside the drawer); the sheet stops Escape, Tab and Left/Right from reaching the drawer behind it and is marked `data-no-flip`.
- **Item picker.** No Prev / Next row any more (and no swipe area): tapping in the grid is enough; the heading always says "N in the bag". Flipping stays on the creature page and the Workshop item drawer (this replaces the picker's flipping of the 2026-10-18 entries "flipping between creatures and items", "The flip arrows look the same everywhere" and "A second tap closes the item details in the picker", where the Prev / Next row was). "Needs Lv 12" is in the pixel font (see the root cause below). The scroll box of the detail panel has 0.375 rem of padding and the same negative margin, so the item frame's border at its edge is no longer clipped (the same fix as `.flip-area`). With the long Epic of workbench scene K7, at 390 x 844 the grid shows two full rows and a third started.
- **Switch.** `ui/Switch.tsx` (`role="switch"`, `aria-checked`, optional `disabled`) replaces `CompareSwitch`. The track's edge is an inset shadow, so the old knob touched the edge in both states and only looked fine off, where the edge is dim. The track now has the edge plus one more pixel of gap as padding (6 px, a 40 x 24 px track and a 12 px knob), so the knob is 3 px clear of the edge at the left and at the right.
- **Fonts, the root cause.** The pixel font was opt-in per element: `.pix` (or an `h1` to `h5`, or a button) set it, and everything else (a `<p>`, a `<span>`, a reason line, a select) fell back to the body font, Atkinson. Every new line in a pixel panel needed someone to remember `.pix`; "Needs Lv 12" (a `span` beside the Equip button), the Workshop's "Worn by ... Needs level ..." line and the native select all forgot. The fix is at the container: `.pix-panel` (in `components/pixel.css`) sets the pixel font once, and everything inside inherits it, controls included; long reading text opts out with `.prose` (the item's Details). It is on the item card, the item drawer's section, the picker's detail panel, the dismantle panel and the creature sheet. **The guard:** `apps/activity/scripts/font-audit.mjs` (`cd apps/activity && node scripts/font-audit.mjs`) opens a list of workbench presets in a 390 px window and checks that every piece of text inside a `.pix-panel` computes to Jersey 10 and every piece inside a `.prose` to Atkinson, and that each preset still has the number of panels it should (a panel that loses its class is noticed, not skipped). It exits 1 with the offending element and text, e.g. `<span class="ip-reason"> "Needs Lv 16" is Atkinson Hyperlegible, should be Jersey 10` (checked by putting the old font back on that class). Fake-DOM tests also check that each panel carries the class. Add a preset to the script when a view gets a pixel panel.
- **Workbench.** `Switch` (Controls: off, on, disabled), `CreaturePage` scenes W4 (the Equip on sheet for a Guards-only Charm: every reason), W5 (a worn item: Take off first), W6 (the dismantle panel open), W7 (the row closed) and K8 (an item that cannot be worn); the `FlipNav` page lost its item picker scene. (Lead, after the screenshots: an element the app focuses on purpose, `tabindex="-1"` like the item card's name, shows no focus box; real controls keep theirs.)

- **Workbench.** `Switch` (Controls: off, on, disabled), `CreaturePage` scenes W4 (the Equip on sheet for a Guards-only Charm: every reason), W5 (a worn item: Take off first), W6 (the dismantle panel open), W7 (the row closed) and K8 (an item that cannot be worn); the `FlipNav` page lost its item picker scene.

## 2026-10-18: Never stack drawers; one width inside a drawer; the item picker opens with nothing selected

Misa found a sheet opening on top of a drawer stressful, saw the sheet's Close button sit further right than the content below it, and did not want the item picker to choose an item for him.

- **The rule: one sheet or drawer at a time.** A choice inside a drawer opens inside the same drawer, as a drill-in view with "‹ Back" at the top left, in the place of the drawer's content. It never opens another sheet. A sheet (`RosterPicker`) is only for a choice that opens from a page (the raid roster, the dungeon team).
- **Equip on is a drill-in now.** This replaces the sheet of the entry above. Tapping the "Equip on" row replaces the item view with "‹ Back" and "Equip on" over the creature list (the options of `equip-picker.ts`: Take off first for a worn item, the wearer ("Wears it now"), who can wear it, then the rest with a reason). A pick equips and returns to the item (the focus goes back to the row); Back, or Escape (which does not close the drawer), returns with nothing changed. The list is `ui/RosterList.tsx`, extracted from `RosterPicker`, which the raid and dungeon sheet still uses (now a title, a Close button and the list; its status line is optional).
- **Flipping pauses while the list is shown.** `FlipProps.paused` hides the arrows and the announcement, ignores Left and Right and a swipe, and keeps the drawer's structure (so the item view's state is not thrown away: dropping the flip props made the swipe area appear and disappear, which redrew the content and closed the list at once). The Workshop sets it while `ItemDetail` tells it (`onDrill`) that the list is open; the list is also `data-no-flip` and keeps arrow keys to itself. The drawer panel's transform-only-while-pulled rule from the entry above stays.
- **Where else a sheet or drawer opens from inside a drawer.** None. The four drawers (the Workshop item drawer, the item picker, the Talent Book, the evolve drawer) hold no `RosterPicker` or `Drawer`; the one place was Equip on, converted above. What can still open over a drawer are the glossary popovers (the "?" and the dotted tags): small popovers on their own layer, not sheets, and they must work everywhere, so they stay. The dismantle panel is a panel inside the item view (its creature radio list is inline), not a sheet.
- **One width in a drawer.** `--drawer-pad` (0.75 rem, `base/tokens.css`) is the side padding of the heading, toolbar, body, footer and the flip row. A pixel ring is drawn outside its box, so a button (3 px) or an accordion (2 px) at the content edge poked out of it: Misa's Close button, the Prev / Next row, the item image, the Equip button, Dismantle, the Equip on row and the Details accordion all did. Each now sits in by its ring's width, so its ring ends at the same edge as the text and the cards. The creature sheet's Close button and its list no longer differ either (the list lost its right-hand scroll gutter, which was 0.25 rem of difference). Checked at 390 px by measuring every block of the Workshop item drawer (item view, Equip on view, dismantle panel) and the item picker (open, and with a long item) against the content edges, ring included: nothing that is at an edge is off by more than a pixel; what remains are indented parts inside a card (the item card's text beside its image, the modifier lines), which are meant to be.
- **The item picker opens with nothing selected.** No item is chosen for the player, not the worn one nor the first wearable: the grid has the whole drawer, the heading says "N in the bag", and no tile looks selected until one is tapped (then its details and the action row show; a second tap closes them as before). The workbench's `initialPicked` still starts on an item (K1 to K8). `firstPick` of the picker is gone with its test.
- **Workbench.** `CreaturePage` scenes W4 to W7 now draw the item content inside a real drawer (W4 and W5 start on the Equip on list), K9 is the picker on open, and `RosterList` has its own page (flat as the drawer draws it, and as the sheet draws it).

## 2026-10-18: Close for the item details, the job result as a picture with one XP component, reasons on the right, Dismantle last

Misa's next notes on PR #35.

- **An X on the item picker's details (and still none on drawers).** The selected item's detail panel in the creature page's item picker has a small pixel X at the right end of the item name's row (`ui/Drawer.tsx`'s own `CloseGlyph`, labelled "Close details"). It closes the details only, the same as a second tap on the tile: the tile stays marked and in view, the focus goes to it, the drawer stays up. The distinction: **the rule "no drawer shows an X" stays**: a drawer closes by a tap outside, its handle or Escape. The X belongs to a panel inside a drawer, which has no other visible way to close (tapping the tile again is not something a player finds).
- **The claimed job as a picture.** The Workshop job card (`JobReveal`) lost its sentences ("X taken apart.", "4 Scrap, 1 Ember Shard, 6 Essence", "+12 Crafting XP", "The worker: +8 experience, level 4 to 5"). A dismantle is two rows. Row 1: the item or items (the frames with their corners), a pixel arrow, and what they became as count badges, the dismantle panel's own look, with Essence, which was missing. Row 2: the creature that did the work (sprite and name) with a list of two gains: **Experience** (the creature's gold bar, "+8", "Lv 5 to Lv 6" only when the level changed, small tags for a treat, the Gold Ribbon and "Waiting at this level") and **Crafting** (its own colour, token `--xp-craft`, so it is never read as the creature's experience; "+12", "Level up!"). Each bar has the level it is at and the next, and says `Cindle's experience` / `Cindle's Crafting XP` to a screen reader. The orb that opens is kept for crafts and supplies (their item pops out of it) and dropped for a dismantle, whose first row is the picture; a craft keeps its item, name and lines and gets the same worker rows in place of the XP sentences. A job from before the worker was recorded shows the Crafting bar alone. The card finds the worker by the experience's `creatureId` (`JobReveal` takes the player's creatures now). "Got it" stays.
- **One XP gain component.** `ui/XpGain.tsx` (`XpGain` and its list `XpList`): a label, an optional level change, the gain in green, tags and a note, and a bar under it, as a group of a two-column description list. Its numbers come from `xp-gain.ts` (`experienceGain` from the server's `ExperienceView`, `craftingGain` from the job's `skillXp` with the game's own thresholds). Used by the job card, and as a drop-in by the trip result's explorer XP and level rows (same grid and look: its `tr-*` styles moved to `components/xp-gain.css` as `xp-*`). **Follow-ups, not redesigned here:** the dungeon results' per-creature line ("+120 experience, level 4 to 5", text from `experienceText`, `tm-exp`) is one muted sentence in a member row, so it needs a design of its own; the expedition claim's worker line is the trip result already; the creature card and page, the explorer picker and profile show XP bars but no gain; raids pay no XP.
- **Reasons on the right in the Equip on list.** `RosterList` takes `reasonSide`: the note ("Wears it now") or reason ("Needs level 12", "On a job", "Guards only", "Away on a trip") sits at the right of its row, right-aligned and centred vertically, in a column of at most 38% that wraps when the row is narrow. Only the Equip on list uses it. The raid and dungeon sheet keeps its notes under the name: its texts are long ("On the board · moves here", "3 / 3 placed, remove one first") and in a 38% column they would take three lines and make the rows taller than they are now.
- **Dismantle sits last.** The item drawer's order is the card, Equip on, Details, then Dismantle at the very bottom, centred, apart from the rest (the panel it opens takes the full width under it). **A destructive action sits last in its view, centred, apart from the rest.** (There is no written guide on button alignment; this is the one line we have.)

## 2026-10-18: "Level up!" is the shared XP line's job

Misa asked why the job result showed a level change and "Waiting at this level" for the creature but a green "Level up!" only for Crafting. `ui/XpGain` now adds the green "Level up!" itself whenever the level went up, for every kind of XP (creature experience, Crafting, the trip result's level), so no caller adds its own. "Waiting at this level" stays as its own tag: a creature waits only at a ribbon level (10, 20) or its evolution level, so it can level up and then wait in the same claim (9 to 10, then the Bronze Ribbon). The workbench preset now shows that real case (9 to 10) instead of 5 to 6 with waiting.

## 2026-10-18: UX patterns written down

Misa asked that the lessons from the part 2 play-tests be kept so future work starts from them. `docs/design/ux-patterns.md` holds the standing UX rules (one drawer at a time, no X on drawers, equal edges, badges over prose, the pixel font per panel and the type scale, pick lists with reasons, keeping the player anchored, a destructive action last) and the table of shared components to use. `CLAUDE.md` points UI work to it. New patterns are added there in a line or two, with the reasoning here. Then (Misa): each XP label stands on its own line, the level change, gain and tags flow on one row under it, and the bar takes the next row (one column in `xp-gain.css`, the same on the trip result). Every kind of XP has its own colour, defined once in `styles/base/tokens.css`: experience gold (`--xp-experience`), explorer XP orchid (`--xp-explorer`, #e7a6d6: a pastel pink with a lilac tint, softer than Epic's hot pink #ff6fbf and far from the warning red) and Crafting leather brown (`--xp-crafting`, #c08552). `XpBar` and `XpGain` take a `kind` (`experience` by default) and set `xp-kind-<kind>`, which sets `--xp-fill`; the bar fill and `XpGain`'s level tags read it. Explorer XP bars (the trip result, the explorer picker, the explorers list) pass `explorer`. Then (Misa): on explorer and Crafting rows the gain ("+300 XP") and the "Level up!" / "Rank up!" badge take the kind's colour too, so the whole row reads as that kind; a creature's experience keeps the green. CSS only (`xp-gain.css`, read from `--xp-fill`). "Rank up!" moved after the gain on the trip result, in the same order as "+240 Level up!".

## 2026-10-18: A fallen creature gets back up on the way down into the Undercroft (B-021)

**Decision.** When a run goes down into the Undercroft, standing creatures still recover `catchBreathBp` (20% of max HP) and a knocked-out creature gets back up at `RULES.dungeon.undercroft.reviveBp` (10% of max HP, at least 1 HP). The history entry of the descent carries `revived` (how many got up; absent on a run stored before this, and the client treats it as 0), and the descent card carries `reviveBp`. The simulator's policy goes down when the row's average HP after the breath is over 50% (it was over 60% with nobody down).

**Why.** Misa's call: try the catch-a-breath revive unless it makes going deeper too easy. The full sim says it does not (B-021): 53.6% of the offers go down (26.6% before), but a descent clears its first keeper 21.6% of the time (34.4% before), runs average 4.25 fights, and 500 gives the same numbers as 1000. So the revive makes the choice open to more players without making the Undercroft easier; most of the new descents are lost below, for half the pack. Kept at 1000. Setting `reviveBp` to 0 does not remove the revive (a creature still gets up at 1 HP); removing it is a code change.

**No guaranteed camp in the Undercroft** (Misa, same day): a camp below stays rare (only from a carved door, about 5% of descents), so a descent is close to all-in. Editing the layout and its odds is left to future gear lines, once the gear branch in progress has merged.

## 2026-10-18: Dungeon terminology: room vs floor

**Decision.** Misa fixed the words the dungeon uses in player text and docs:

- A **room** is one encounter on a map: a fight, elite, mystery room, cache, camp, door, epic fight or boss.
- A **floor** is the whole set of rooms at one depth. The day's map is one floor; each Undercroft level is another floor below it ("Undercroft floor 1"). Going down is "going down a floor".
- Each floor ends in its own **floor boss**. "Room boss" becomes "floor boss" everywhere (glossary, Essence and shard entries, the map, the stage header, How to play), and the Undercroft "keeper" becomes the Undercroft floor boss: the word "keeper" is gone from player text.
- Rows of the map are no longer called floors in player text. Progress counts rooms: "Floor 3 / 7" became "Room 3 / 7"; the stage header reads "Room 7 / 7, floor boss"; "Undercroft 1 · Floor 2 / 4" became "Undercroft floor 1 · Room 2 / 4"; "Undercroft 2 cleared" became "2 Undercroft floors cleared" ("1 Undercroft floor cleared"); "The keeper is down" became "The floor boss is down"; the descent card says "N rooms with a floor boss at the end". "Cracked floor" keeps its name. The depth track says "Undercroft +1 room" where a landmark lengthens the first Undercroft floor.
- The Undercroft band label from the server is now "Undercroft 1", "Undercroft 2" (it was "Undercroft · foes are fiercer here" and "Undercroft II · ..."; Misa asked for the short titles). The strip and stage banner use the same name ("Undercroft 1 · Room 2 / 4"). It is built when the map is shown, never stored.
- The glossary entry `room-boss` is now `floor-boss` (nothing stored or sent to the server refers to a glossary id).

**Internal names stay.** In code, API fields and stored run JSON, `floor` is still a row (step) of a map, `floors` the steps of a map, `level` an Undercroft floor (1 to 3, 0 is the day's map), `campFloor` and node ids (`f2l1`, `u1f0l0`) are unchanged, and `keeper` stays in function, file and workbench stage names (`isKeeper`, `openKeeperChest`, `dungeon-act-keeper.json`). Renaming them would change stored runs and the API for no player-visible gain. `docs/design/dungeon-paths.md` has a "Terminology" section that says so. Older dated entries above keep the old words.

## 2026-10-18: The dungeon map goes downward; the page follows the run

**Decision.** Misa's test feedback: players delve deeper, so the map starts at the top (entrance row) and ends in the floor boss at the bottom. The Undercroft floors are drawn below the day's floor, each under a dashed seam with its label at the top of its darker band. The green start triangles sit above the entrance rooms and point down into them. The same map draws on the run screen, the pre-run card, the results and the workbench. Keyboard order follows the picture (top to bottom). The depth track is unchanged.

**Page scroll.** The page itself scrolls (the Activity's iframe window; no inner container). A fight brings its arena to the top of the view when it starts (as before). A card that replaces the map (mystery room, camp, descent) brings itself into view. Back on the map, the room just played (or the entrance of the Undercroft floor just entered) goes into the top half of the view, unless it is already there, so the rooms below it are on screen: `dungeon-scroll.ts`. Smooth, or instant with reduced motion.

**Smaller changes.** An unvisited mystery room's drawer no longer names its scene; the "?" help stands right after the drawer title (a `titleHelp` slot in `Drawer`). The descent card drops its "What is the Undercroft?" line.

## 2026-10-18: The camp's row can be rearranged on the entry's board, and a recruit can be placed in the same act

**Problem.** Misa swapped a downed creature for another at the camp and could not set the team up around the new recruit. The server already took an `order` with the camp act, but it was applied before the choice, so the recruit (not yet in the row) could not be placed, and the client's only control was small Left/Right buttons that were easy to miss.

**Decision.** `order` is now a permutation of the row as the choice leaves it, applied after the choice. For Rest and Keepsake nothing changes (the row as it stands). For Swap it is the row after the swap (it holds `in`, not `out`), so the recruit can stand anywhere in one act. No new act type and no separate reorder step: the camp stays "pick one thing", and the order always goes with it. A reorder-only act would be one more state to make idempotent for no gain. Validation is unchanged (exactly the ids of the row, no repeats); a refused act changes nothing; retries return the stored answer.

**Client.** The camp card shows the row on the dungeon entry's board (`RaidBoard`, single large row; the same drag and drop and the same reaction cues), with the HP under each creature (the tile has an optional HP line: bar plus "140 / 203 HP", red under a third and when down, "0 / 215 HP"). A creature moves by dragging it onto another, or by tapping it and then another (tap-to-swap, for anyone who cannot drag; the keyboard uses the same). A Swap gets a third step after "who goes home": the row as it will stand, the recruit in the place of the one that left and shown at "Full HP", to rearrange, then "Swap and push on". The run strip above the card hides its own row at the camp so the row is shown once.

## 2026-10-18: The dungeon results screen, reworked

**Decision.** Misa's feedback on the end-of-run screen:

- The **title** is short and coloured with the existing tokens: "Cleared!" and "Home safe" in green (`--good`), "Defeated" and "Lost in the Undercroft" in red (`--bad`). Under it, one **summary line** sums the run up in plain words ("You defeated the first floor boss and advanced to depth 9. You were defeated in the Undercroft and kept half of your loot pack."; a defeat on the day's floor names the room it fell in, "room 3 / 7", with the spaced slash the count-wording rule asks for; a camp exit says "You stay at depth 8"). A defeat with an empty pack does not promise half of nothing. It replaces the old "Depth 8 to 9" sentence and the "Pack: half kept" line.
- **Rewards** show only the count badges (no loot-pile picture). **Keepsakes** moved under "Your team": they are run stats, not a reward.
- The route toggle is **Map | Rooms**, the map first. Rooms lists every room visited in order (fights, elites, the floor boss, mystery rooms, caches, camps, doors, Undercroft rooms) from the run's route log, with CLEARED or DEFEATED (the room the run fell in) and a short line of what happened in the rooms that are not fights. A run with no route keeps the old strip of fights.
- **Your team** shows each creature's level (a green "+1" when the run levelled it up), an experience bar with the part just gained drawn brighter, "+130 experience", one line saying what multiplied it ("Treat gave 2x experience", "Treat gave 3x experience and Hearty Breakfast gave +25%") and "Waiting at this level" when it waits.

**Server.** The experience entries of a dungeon run (`rewards.experience`) gained `treatTimes` (what the treat multiplied by: 2, or 3 on Hearty Breakfast; null without a treat) and `dayEffects` (the day's Boons and Omens that changed experience, by name, with basis points). Both are optional for the client, so runs stored before read as "no extra line". Nothing in the gear changes dungeon experience, so a treat and the day's weather are the only sources.

## 2026-10-18: Part 2 meets the dungeon paths (merging main #32 into #35)

The dungeon paths (PR #32) were merged to `main` first; this is how part 2's work was carried into them so nothing either side meant is lost.

- **Weather in every room.** The map run's base input carries Kind Rain, Morning Haze and Bramble Season (`skyRunFields`), so every room built from it (`buildRoomInput`) heals, hazes the leftmost creature and reflects as part 2 built them. A row reordered at the camp hazes its new leftmost creature.
- **Position lines and sets in the run's row, kept after the camp.** The run state gains `seats` (by creature id: the slot before its place and the weather are known, and its gear fold's kit, frozen when it joined). The row is folded with its places at the start (`seatedRow`: `placeSlots(..., 'line')`, then the weather), and again at the end of every camp act, after a swap or a reorder (`reseat`), so the position lines and gear sets see the new neighbours. Gear stays frozen from the start, as the paths decided: the kits are the gear the creature wore then. Only a creature whose max HP the new place changes (a Lantern bearer's gift, Heart of the row) gets a new max, and its carried HP is capped at it; the others keep the max of their last fight. A run stored before seats keeps every slot as it was (the camp only reorders). Tests: `apps/worker/test/dungeon-camp-seats.test.ts`.
- **XP bars.** Main's "just gained" highlight and part 2's kinds live together: the gained part is the kind's bright colour, the part before it the kind's shade (`--xp-<kind>-shade` in `tokens.css`).
- **Fixtures.** The dungeon fixtures were re-captured from the server (part 2's new abilities are in every reply's ability names); the capture's random creature ids were mapped back to the ones the client tests name.
- **Sim.** The legacy digest was re-pinned (see `legacy-unchanged.test.ts`): Bait earned and raids won are the same. The gear share study's test now measures three servers, not one: with one, a lone min-maxer's share swung with whichever creatures stood in its top three (61% at week 20, 20% at week 30 for the test's seed), which the merge's different dice exposed.

## 2026-10-18: Raid posts are embeds that sum up the raid

**Problem.** The raid posts were plain text lists of everything we knew (contribution numbers, Essence, the sky, every first with its catcher), so the news got lost.

**Decision.** Each post is one embed: a headline (the boss retreats, holds firm, is defeated or survives), an HP bar when the boss still stands, up to three columns (top three fighters by medal, the big hit or combo, and the next fight or the top reaction), the boss's portrait as the thumbnail and a "Play Leet Creatures" button that launches the game like the lounge's button (same custom id). The finale adds the rank line, when the next boss comes and the creatures new to the server. Removed: Essence amounts, contribution numbers, the weather (Misa: useful, but not raid news) and catcher names. The reveal keeps the moves and puts the three fight times in columns.

**How.** The formatters return a message (embeds and the button); the queue stores it as JSON in the same `content` column, so no migration is needed, and older rows (plain text) still go out as text. The sender makes the portrait path absolute for the environment (`publicOrigin`: the production or staging site, none locally) and sends embeds without the SUPPRESS_EMBEDS flag. Portraits are drawn from the boss sprites by `apps/activity/scripts/boss-portraits.mts` and committed under `apps/activity/public/discord/bosses/`; a test checks every boss has one. Discord does not render timestamps in footers, so times sit in fields and the description. The reveal's fight times are one line each, "Fri 13:37": the weekday is named in the guild's timezone and the time is a Discord timestamp (each reader's own timezone); Misa found the full date plus the relative time too long.

**Why.** Misa wants the Discord posts to be a pleasure to read: the status at a glance and a few highlights, with the details in the game.

## 2026-10-18: Engine and test cleanup from the audit (E2 to E7, E9, B13 step 1)

- **A second replay lock.** `packages/engine/test/replay-rules.test.ts` hashes the whole result of 200 fixed seeded fights that use what `replay.json` leaves out: the combat `rules`, every attack pattern and charge, sparse boards, the phase floor, enrage, extra boss attacks, luck of every kind, the day's sky, adaptations, creature abilities, partial HP and modifier sheets that stand for gear, sets, talents and weather. Everything the fights read from outside the engine is pinned in the test file, so a content retune never moves it; coverage tests fail if a new `SlotMods` field is not exercised. It is the gate for splitting `fight.ts` later (B13 step 2).
- **Backdraft is announced once a round (E3).** The duplicate `gimmick` event on the boss's turn is gone; the trap is announced when it is armed. 120 fights of `replay.json` and 20 of the new lock changed their event lists only: outcome, HP and credit are identical (checked by hashing each result with its events removed, before and after). Fights stored before this change still carry the second event, so the stage keeps its one-cue-a-round de-dup for them.
- **Hollow Weave heals through `healReceivedBp` (E4).** Like Mend, Kind Rain, Second wind and Second breath, so Well-fed makes it stronger and Long Shadows weaker. The event is unchanged (no `cause`). The six 3-piece set effects are now in `combat-rules.md` 10.10.
- **Two texts now say what the engine does (E2, E5).** Lantern Light: "Creature abilities last 1 more round (Rally, Shield Wall, Purge). Basic abilities do not." (basic abilities belong to no creature, so they never got longer). Lantern Night: "Guards in its row: their abilities last 1 more round." The behaviour is unchanged. Making the basic abilities longer too stays a proposal.
- **Tests do their work, not their bookkeeping (E6, E7, E9).** Hot loops collect problems and assert once (engine `property.test.ts` 18 s to 3 s; game `rng.test.ts` from 12 s to no longer among the slowest files). The game package is back on the default timeout (one test of a million rolls asks for 20 s). The 45-player perf test uses warm-ups and a median like the one above it. The simulator's tests share one run per file, and `RunSettings` gained optional horizons (`pityWeeks`, `longRunWeeks`, `tripLongWeeks`, `studyWeeks`) that default to the shipped constants, so `pnpm sim` is unchanged while the tiny test run plays 6 weeks, not 20 to 60.

## 2026-10-18: `pnpm fixtures:capture` and stable fixture ids (roadmap polish item 10)

- **One command, one manifest.** `apps/worker/test/fixture-sets.ts` lists the five scenario sets (branches, creatures, dungeon, expeditions, gear), the rngs each one is played with and the files it owns. `pnpm fixtures:capture` (`apps/worker/scripts/capture-fixtures.mjs`, Node only, no new dependency) runs `test/capture/capture-all.test.ts` with `vitest.capture.config.ts` (the normal config leaves `test/capture/**` out), reads the `FIXTURE ` lines, writes every file whole, formats it with Prettier and lists the files that changed. `--set <name>` captures one set; `--check` writes nothing and exits 1 on a difference. The five `*-capture.test.ts` files and their `it.skip` toggling are gone; the five `*-fixtures.test.ts` files take their runs from the manifest, and share `test/fixture-contract.ts` (`shape` with one folding rule for id-keyed and part-kind-keyed maps, `expectSameShape`, `createRecorder`).
- **Stable ids.** Server ids are random, so every capture used to rewrite every id (and the client tests had to name them by hand). The script now gives each UUID of a set, in order of first appearance across responses, errors and requests, the id `00000000-0000-4000-8<set>00-<12 digit counter>` (set digits 1 to 5), also in map keys and `:id` path parts. The scenarios are deterministic, so a capture right after a capture changes nothing (`--check` exits 0).
- **Every random source is seeded.** The sets seed all the guild's rngs (quirks and the lounger were live, which is why a capture used to churn `quirk` and `loungerId`); the rngs a scenario scripts win.
- **One-time tidy-up.** The all-at-once dungeon run's error rows and request bodies moved to `dungeon-legacy-errors.json` and `dungeon-legacy-request-bodies.json`, so `dungeon-errors.json` and `dungeon-request-bodies.json` are written whole. Client tests that need both read both. The four creature ids in `dungeon-fixture-screens.test.tsx` are the stable ids.
- **Not covered (phase 2).** The raid and live fixtures have no scenario; a `raid-scenario.ts` in the manifest would cover them.
- **What the first capture changed besides ids.** Quirks and `loungerId` (now seeded, not live); `dungeon-act-descend-pending` and `dungeon-act-keeper` lost the repeated Backdraft announcement (4 `gimmick` events each, the engine fix of the cleanup; nothing else in them moved); `expedition-claim-loot` shows `offered.keep: true` (the stale file predated Keep for two-form lines); `gear-request-bodies.json` follows the scenario's own request order.

## 2026-10-19: Overnight cleanup after #32 and #35 (the lead's calls)

Misa asked for a polish night after the two big merges: fix bugs, improve UX, streamline the code, update tooling and docs, plan next steps, and bring every call that is not a clear fix to him one by one. Six read-only audits ran first (server, combat and content, screens at 390 and 1024 px, frontend code, backend code, tooling and docs, then the tests and CI). Their findings live with the PR; this entry records the decisions taken without him.

- **No evolving while busy.** A creature on an expedition or with an open Workshop job cannot evolve (409 `creature_away` / `creature_on_job`). A branch form can change role, and a trip's and a job's perks are read at claim from the creature as it then is; evolving also took a role-locked Charm off a creature whose gear is frozen while busy. Refusing is consistent with that freeze rule; storing the role on the trip at send was the alternative and was not needed.
- **A chest entry in a run's history is only what the chest adds.** The Undercroft keeper's chest repeated the fight's Essence and scrap, and the client hid the main boss fight's own loot to avoid counting twice, so the main boss room under-reported. Now both chests log only their extras and the room drawer always shows the fight's loot.
- **A dungeon step's stored answer keeps its run view for 2 days** (`ACT_ANSWER_RETENTION_MS`), then only the act and `ended`; a later retry answers `stale_step`. The answer only serves a retry, and it grew with every step.
- **`DungeonToday.modifiers` is gone.** It existed for pre-map clients, which cannot play (the old run route answers 410).
- **UX rules applied to the new screens**, with before and after screenshots in the PR: badges instead of prose for rewards, costs and times; one wording for busy creatures ("On a job", "Away on a trip"); the Undercroft drawers do not repeat themselves; rooms ahead say "Further ahead." (the old "Not on your path." was wrong for the camp and the boss, which every path reaches).
- **CI runs in parallel jobs** (checks, worker in two shards, sim, the rest) behind one `ci` job, which keeps the name the `main` ruleset requires. With the faster sim tests, CI went from about 10 minutes to under 3. `docs/design/testing.md` is the testing plan: layers, speed budgets, and the rule to prove a rule once at the lowest layer.
- **Tooling:** vitest 5.0.3 with `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` 1.4.0 (replaces Dependabot #2 and #13), wrangler 4.149.0, vite 8.3.4; the worker's typecheck also checks its generated types; Dependabot groups vitest with its Workers plugin; the economy rules moved from the worker to the game package so the simulator no longer imports worker code.
- **Proposals for Misa** (game rules, look and feel, larger refactors, tests and deploys, what to build next) are on a decision page linked from the PR; nothing in them is built.

## 2026-10-19: The simulator picks second forms like players, and the legacy digest is retired

Two changes to the balance simulator, neither touching a game rule or content (the lead's calls from the cleanup night, approved by Misa).

- **Branch style.** A player profile has a `branch` dial: `first`, `role` or `mixed`; the default for every preset and archetype is `mixed`. `first` takes the original form of a line that grows two ways (`firstForm`); `role` takes the form whose role the team's other top three lack (`pickBranch`); `mixed` gives each player one or the other with a fair coin drawn from the run's seed and the player's id, so a seed gives the same players and about half of a server picks by role (`usesRoleBranch`, `SimPlayer.roleBranch`). The 2026-10-18 decisions kept `firstForm` so the baselines stayed put while the dungeon was reworked; that reason is gone. What moved is in `docs/balance/log.md`, B-022: a few points on the raid and gear targets, one more target failing than before on one seed (66 to 65 of 96 pass), no rule changed.
- **Keep is still not simulated, on purpose.** Keep adds a second creature of a line once both forms are wanted. The sim keys creatures (`<player>/<line>`), explorer XP, familiarity, the duplicate rules (`collect`) and the creatures and gear studies by line, with one creature per line, so a min-maxer with two creatures of a line would be a change to the player state and every study that reads it, not a policy. It would also need the pick of which duplicate to keep and which creature to train, with no live data yet on what players do. Revisit when live data shows Keep is common.
- **The legacy digest is gone** (`legacy-unchanged.test.ts`, `legacy-digest.ts`, `fixtures/legacy-digest.json`). It pinned hashes of four configs that all asked for the old leet-only income (`income: 'current'`), which players no longer have, and about 20 deliberate re-pins in three days showed that it fired on every rule change and was updated by hand each time, so it guarded nothing. Its cost was about 70 s of CI. In its place `run.test.ts` asserts eleven headline numbers of a tiny run on the shipped income (readable, not a hash), with the rule that a change to them is explained in the PR (`docs/design/testing.md`). Determinism, the shipped horizons and the targets stay covered by `run.test.ts`, the targets tests and the `sim` workflow.

## 2026-10-19: Look and feel pass, six approved changes (type scale everywhere, pixel cards, camp Back, one XP row, rooms recap, reset line)

Misa approved six items from the overnight audit (U2, U8, U11, U14, F11, F17, F18). All are visible; before and after screenshots are in the PR.

- **The type scale is applied everywhere (F11, U2).** Every text size in `styles/` is now `--fs-title`, `--fs-heading`, `--fs-row` or `--fs-small`: 0.6875, 0.75 and 0.8125 rem became small; 0.9375 and 1.0625 became row; 1.2 and 1.375 became heading; 1.125 went by role (card and section headings, `h3`, the accordion head and the trip title to heading; numbers on bars, option names and buttons to row). `h2` and `h3` are both heading size now (the headings differ by margin and colour). The drawer's close glyph and the creature card's arrow went from 2 rem to title. **Kept raw, on purpose** (the list is in `type-scale.test.ts`, per file): display and art (the page's base size and h1, the pixel heading, the glossary mark, the living room's emoji and labels, the How to play illustrations' labels, the stage's effects) and ten compact labels (the 9-slot boards' tile names, HP and "auto" tags, the lane head, the zone label, the stage's unit names and "you" tag, the narrowest phone's tab bar), which are 9 to 10 px because the tiles are 5.5 rem wide; at 14 px the species names and HP lines would not fit. **The ratchet had a bug:** its pattern counted `font-size: var(--fs-row)` as raw (the backtracking `\s*` matched before the space), so its counts were the number of declarations, not of raw sizes. It now counts only real raw sizes and the allowlist is the short exempt list.
- **Pixel font per panel on the dungeon cards (F17).** `RevealShell` has a `pix` option that adds `.pix-panel`; the camp, the mystery room and the descent card use it. Their sentences (camp intro, the arranging line, the scene, the descent's intro and the lines under its two buttons, each choice's line) are `.prose`; the `.pix` on each choice name is gone (they inherit). The font audit has presets for the three cards and each camp step.
- **Camp steps have "‹ Back" at the top (F18).** `ui/DrillHead({ title, onBack, backLabel })` is the head of every drill-in; the item drawer's Equip on view and the camp's three steps use it, and the bottom Back buttons are gone. Escape goes back one step (`ui/drill-keys.ts`, `onDrillKey`, not while an act is running), and a camp step opens with the focus on Back so Escape has something to hear it. The head's CSS moved from `item-detail.css` to `drill-head.css`.
- **One XP row everywhere (U8).** `ExperienceGainRow` (over `ui/XpGain`) is the creature's experience on a Workshop job, a trip and each dungeon team member: label "Experience" (the trip said "Level"), the level change and a green "Level up!" when it levelled, the green "+N", tags (Treat, Gold Ribbon, Waiting at this level) and the bar. The trip result row now has its bar. The dungeon team keeps three columns; each member's row sits under its HP in a narrow form (the level always shown, no bar caps, no words on the bar). Dropped from the dungeon row: the sentence "Treat gave 2x experience" (a "Treat" tag says it; a 3x treat is no longer spelled out), "+260 experience" (the green "+260" says it) and the brighter "just gained" part of the bar (the other two screens never had it). The day's Boons and Omens that changed the amount stay as one small line under the row ("Cold Snap gave -20% experience").
- **The Rooms recap stamps only fights (U11).** A camp, cache, mystery room and door have no CLEARED stamp; a room with nothing to say reads "Visited" in the card's own text. The stamp is as wide as its picture but at most 7 rem, so it stays a stamp at 1024 px. A panel header is two short lines (`where` and `what`: "Room 4" over "Elite" or "Floor boss"; in the Undercroft, "Undercroft 1" over "Room 4 · Boss"), always two lines tall, so the pictures in a row line up and no header wraps to three at 390 px. A screen reader still hears the whole title.
- **The reset line drops the timezone name (U14).** "Resets at Wed 13:37, in 2 d 3 h." The countdown says when; an IANA name is not for players.

## 2026-10-19: `fight.ts` split into modules (B13 step 2); Lantern Light lengthens basic abilities too

- **`fight.ts` split.** `simulateFight` was one closure of about 1900 lines over about 30 mutable variables. They are now one explicit `FightState` object (`fight-state.ts`, with the creature and mark types and the small shared helpers: crediting, mutator hooks, who stands beside whom). `hurt` and its Guard helpers are in `fight-damage.ts`, `hitBoss` in `fight-strike.ts`, the boss's gimmicks, charges and attacks in `fight-boss.ts`, and the round-start work (Support buffs, Kind Rain, gear lines, finale abilities) in `fight-round.ts`. `fight.ts` keeps the input checks, the dice draws in their fixed order, the round loop and the totals. Behaviour-neutral: both replay locks (`replay.test.ts`, `replay-rules.test.ts`) pass byte for byte with no re-pin, every other engine test is unchanged, and the engine's public exports are unchanged.
- **Lantern Light lengthens basic abilities too (Misa: yes).** New optional `FightInput.lingerAllRounds` (0 to 3; absent: 0, so every stored fight replays as it was) adds rounds to the Rally, Shield Wall and timed Purge of every ability, basic ones included. One source only: `weatherSky` carries it (`lingerAllRounds`) and the raid, the dungeon run and the simulator pass it on with the other sky fields. `weatherSheet` no longer puts the day's linger on a creature's sheet, so a creature's own ability is not lengthened twice; gear and set linger (`lingerRounds`) stay per creature and add on top. The weather text is back to the full promise: "Shield Walls and other lasting abilities last 1 more round, basic abilities too."

## 2026-10-19: Lantern Cap can drop, mystery line goes away, worker files split (wave 2, worker)

- **A Festival win gives a Lantern Night piece the player lacks (Misa: yes; audit E1).** The Lantern Cap (Grove) could never drop: the piece was picked by the Festival creature's element, and there is no Grove Festival creature, so the Lantern Night set could not be completed. Now `festivalBaseFor(rng, owned)` draws evenly among the Festival bases the player does not own yet, and among all of them once they own every one. "Own" is any item in `gear_items` (in the bag or worn; `ownedBaseIds`). The element plays no part and the grant no longer takes it (`grantFestivalGear(deps, userId, weekId, rank, bossId)`; the sim's `grantFinaleLoot` takes a `festival` flag and reads the player's bag and worn items). The base is drawn first, one extra draw, so the drawn rarity and lines of a seeded Festival piece moved: the gear fixtures re-captured (the Hearth Scarf's lines in `gear-*` and `workshop-*` fixtures, nothing else). A game test proves every Festival base is reachable and that two of three owned always gives the third; a worker test proves it through the grant (one in the bag, one worn). Grant keys and idempotency are unchanged.
- **The mystery room's line is shown only until the next move (Misa: yes; audit U13).** `applyMove` clears `lastEvent` once a move is accepted, so the run strip shows "Sleeping Beetle: Take one coin. You find 1 scrap." right after the pick and not for the rest of the run. The step log keeps what the room did. Nine dungeon fixtures re-captured (`lastEvent` is `null` where a move followed an event).
- **Response types moved (audit B11, pure move).** Dungeon views to `dungeon-types.ts`, gear views to `gear-types.ts`, expedition views to `expedition-types.ts`, analytics events to `analytics-events.ts`; the old files re-export them, so no importer changed.
- **Behaviour split along seams (audit B12, B10).** `workshop.ts` (`Workshop`: previews, start and claim of jobs; `Gear` delegates, so routes are unchanged) with the shared reads in `gear-access.ts`; `expedition-claim.ts` (the claim pipeline as functions over a `ClaimEnv`); `dungeon-replay.ts`, `dungeon-finish.ts` and `dungeon-rows.ts` (stored rows and shared reads); `analytics-props.ts` (the sanitizers); `leet-sync.ts` (`LeetSync`, with one-line delegates on `GuildCore`). Every moved method still runs inside its caller's `atomically` or `transactionSync` (none opens one of its own, except the public methods that did before); `pnpm fixtures:capture --check` stayed clean through the pure moves and the existing worker tests are unchanged. `guild-core.ts` went from 1516 to about 1250 lines.

## 2026-10-19: Frontend splits with no visual change (audit F15, F16; Misa approved)

- **One single-row board.** `CampRow` and `DungeonTeam` shared about 70 lines each (drag and drop, keyboard walking, drop statuses, reaction cues, the hint, the screen-reader lines, the drag ghost). They now share `use-single-row-board.ts` (the state) and `SingleRowBoard.tsx` (the board with the hint, the live regions and the ghost). The camp keeps tap-to-swap; the dungeon keeps the picker and the "Remove from team" popover.
- **The one intended change, not visible:** where the two had drifted, the dungeon's behaviour won for both. An empty place in the camp row is read as "Left slot, empty" (it was "Empty slot"), and the camp row now also announces what the order sets off, as the dungeon team always did.
- **`Stage.tsx` split** along its seams: `stage/StageFx.tsx` (numbers, bursts, KO tags, round intro, marks, beams), `stage/StageUnit.tsx` (the creature, the foe's picture, the ability badge) and `use-stage-abilities.ts` (the ability hand and its popover state). `Game.tsx` split: `use-gear-state.ts`, `use-forecast.ts`, `use-tab-pips.ts`, `creature-actions.ts` (`createCreatureActions`, mirroring `createGearActions`) and `loaded-state.ts`.
- **Proof:** the 261 workbench presets of the dungeon, stage, collection, creature, raid board and sky pages were captured at 390 px before and after (260 identical pixel for pixel; one drawer preset moved 13 px in some runs on both sides, a timing flake) and their page HTML compared (identical except the camp's new screen-reader line). A Game-level run with a faked server (collection, ribbon, four tabs) gave identical pictures, text and server calls.

## 2026-10-19: The type scale one rung smaller (Misa: "smaller everywhere"); creature card variant E in the workbench

- **New token values** (`styles/base/tokens.css`): `--fs-title` 1.5 to 1.25 rem, `--fs-heading` 1.25 to 1 rem, `--fs-row` 1 to 0.875 rem, `--fs-small` 0.875 to 0.75 rem (24 / 20 / 16 / 14 px became 20 / 16 / 14 / 12 px). Misa tried it on his 360 px phone in the creature card round and asked to keep it everywhere. The proposed values held up in practice, so they shipped as proposed.
- **The tab bar loses its raw size.** At 360 px it had a 0.625 rem override (10 px); it now uses `--fs-small` (12 px) at every width, so it reads better than before on the narrowest phone. At 12 px "Expeditions" is 61 px wide and a fifth of the bar is 58 px, so a tab is no longer allowed to be narrower than its word (`min-width: min-content` instead of `0`): the tabs still share the width evenly, and Expeditions takes the 3 px it needs from the others. This also ends the spill at 390 px, where "Expeditions" (14 px) ran 12 px past its tab. `type-scale.test.ts` no longer allows a raw size in `tabs.css`.
- **Proof:** every workbench preset (629, at 360 and 390 px) was loaded and measured on the new scale and again with the old values put back on the same page: page overflow, and text (measured with a Range, so tap areas and pseudo elements do not count) that runs past the box that clips it or past its own box. No new case; 384 old ones went away (most are the 9-slot boards' tile labels and the tab bar). The ones there before and after are the compact labels that end in "…" by design (board tiles, the stage's unit names). No layout needed a fix.
- **Creature card, variant E (workbench only, for Misa's approval; the game's card is unchanged).** Misa liked A (the content under the bar takes the card's full width) and asked for: no level tag at the top right, no arrow, the creature's tags in words under the name (the current icon-only element chip becomes "Tide", the role "Guard", plus the species of a nickname, Festival, Shiny, First on server, Away, On a job, Lounging), then a full-width slim experience bar (variant D of the XP round, at rest), then the gear on the left and the talents on the right. `src/workbench/creature-card-e/CreatureCardE.tsx` draws it from the card's own helpers; styles in `styles/components/creature-card-variants.css` (`.cce-*`). Choices: the name stays on one line (title size up to 20 letters, which fits "Captain Puddlestomps" at 360 px; longer nicknames step down to heading size, past about 28 letters they end in "…"); the tags on the card are plain (no "?"), since the whole card opens the page, where each has its help; the level tag has one width from "Lv 1" to "Lv 30", so the bars of a list start at the same place; the bar's numbers sit in the larger part of the bar (in ink on the fill from half full, in bone on the trough below), so they are never cut by the fill's edge; empty and closed gear slots are the same 48 px square as a worn item with its ring, so "Lv 13" fits; every ringed thing (level tag, gear rings, the gold button) sits in by its ring so all edges line up with the sprite. The B and C variants (not chosen) were removed from the workbench. When the shared slim bar lands in `XpBar`, E's `StaticBar` is replaced by it.

## 2026-10-19: Every XP gain is the "Count-up" row, with a treat icon and one multiplier chip

Misa picked variant D, "Count-up", of the XP-gain designs, and asked: "Let's make an icon for treat and use that in place of the worded treat-badge. It could be a melon slice or other easily recognizable fruit. It would have a small footprint and be clickable for more info. Let's also make a conscious decision to not show extra stuff like 'treat gave 2x experience' so all representations of gaining exp are uniform. If we want, we could experiment with the treat icon badge including a modifier for the actual XP multiplier: 3x on some weather days, and modifiable by things like gear. Clicking on it would open a breakdown of the exp multiplier effects that applied, in a popover." He then changed one part: no number on the melon ("melon ×3" reads as three treats); the multiplier is its own small chip.

- **One row for every XP gain** (`ui/XpGain`, in `XpList`; creature experience is `ExperienceGainRow`): the Workshop job card, the trip result and the dungeon results team; creature experience, explorer XP and Crafting XP alike. The label and the level change with "Level up!" ("Rank up!" for the explorer), then the level tag, a slim bar, the "+N" and the multiplier. The kind colours stay (experience gold with a green "+N", explorer orchid, Crafting leather). In a list the rows share four columns (CSS subgrid), so the bars line up.
- **The count-up.** The bar fills from the old value to the new one in 12 pixel steps (0.6 s a bar, after 0.35 s); a level crossed fills the old bar, flashes, the level tag steps up, "Level up!" pops in and the new level's bar fills with the rest. The bars are worked out backwards from what the server sent and the game's level sizes (`xp-change.ts`), so the server sends nothing more for it. Reduced motion shows the end at once. The timing is pure (`xp-count.ts`).
- **Readable numbers.** The earlier variant drew the numbers twice, clipped at the fill's edge, so a number split half on the fill and half off. Now they are one colour (bone) with a one-pixel ink edge on all sides, legible over the trough, the old part and the fill.
- **The treat icon** is an 8x8 glyph, a watermelon slice (`treat` in `glyphs.ts`), shown only when a treat was packed and never with a number. **The multiplier chip** ("×2", "×1.25", "×3.15") in the kind's colour sits next to the "+N" whenever the total from all sources is not x1. Chip and melon are one button; it opens the shared popover with each source ("Treat ×3", "Hearty Breakfast +25%", "Cold Snap -20%", "Apprentice +20%", "Trail XP +17%", "Milestones +5%") and the total. The glossary's Treat entry shows the melon and says what it means.
- **No prose about a gain.** The "Treat" word tag, "Treat gave 2x experience" and the dungeon's day-effect line ("Cold Snap gave -20% experience") are gone; the breakdown says it.
- **The server sends the breakdown** with every gain something changed (`boost: { parts, totalBp }`; absent when nothing did): on `ExperienceView` (trips, jobs, dungeon runs), on a trip result's `xp` (explorer XP) and as `xpBoost` on a job result (Crafting XP). It is built in the same place as the amount, from the same numbers (`@creatures/game` `xp-boost.ts`): the treat's multiplier (2, or 3 on Hearty Breakfast), the day's Boons and Omens as applied (scaled by the day's strength), the gear rule's line (Trail XP, Apprentice; talents that add to the same rule are counted under the line's name), and a trip's own shares (Milestones, the week's twist, a push-on goal). The total is the treat's multiplier times one plus the shares, the order every gain is worked out in. Trips and jobs now store each Boon's and Omen's experience change with the day's weather (`xpEffects`); one started before this names the stored total by the Boon or Omen that changes experience (`legacyDayParts`). The dungeon's older `treatTimes` and `dayEffects` stay for one release for clients that read them, and the client reads them for runs stored before `boost`, as it reads an older trip's or job's treat flag as the usual doubling.
- **Not changed:** raids pay experience but show no gain row; the thick `XpBar` of the creature page, the explorer picker and profile stays as it is.
- **Follow-up, 2026-10-19 (Misa, after seeing it):** the melon's colours did not fit, so the treat icon is redrawn in the game's palette: four 8x8 designs (cherries, cookie, bone biscuit, wrapped candy) live in `glyphs.ts` as `TREAT_ICONS`, and one constant, `TREAT_ICON`, picks the one shown as `treat`; cherries ship (a fruit, as first asked, and the clearest at 16 px). The workbench's `XpGain` preset "Treat icons" shows all four in a real breakdown for Misa to pick. The icon is no longer next to the chip, and no longer a big picture before the breakdown's "Experience" title: it is only the prefix of the breakdown's "Treat" line (and the glossary entry's picture). The chip is the game's small tag (`ui/Tag`: plain text on ink, no gold ring), its text in the kind's colour, which reads on ink for all three kinds. Every multiplier is one format, chip and breakdown alike: a decimal with an "x", up to two decimals, trailing zeros trimmed ("2x", "1.25x", "0.8x", "3.51x"); a percent source is shown as its multiplier ("+25%" is "1.25x", "-20%" is "0.8x"), and "Total" is gold like its value. Note: the shares still add before they multiply the treat (3x with 1.25x and 0.8x is 3 x (1 + 0.25 - 0.2) = 3.15x, not 3 x 1.25 x 0.8 = 3x), so the lines no longer multiply out to the total when two shares meet; that was the rule then, only the wording changed (superseded the same day: see "Every XP multiplier multiplies" below). The breakdown's lines already used the popovers' body font (Atkinson Hyperlegible, 12 px); they looked different because the page's `dt` rule made the names haze and tabular figures swapped the body font's digits for its other set (no slashed zero). They are now bone with the font's usual figures, the same as a glossary popover's lines. "Level up!" and "Rank up!" now rise into place from below the line when the count crosses the level (320 ms, three 3 px steps up, one step past its place, then back; `step-end`, so it moves in pixel steps); with reduced motion they are there from the first frame.

## 2026-10-19: The creature card is variant E, with the talents one to a row

Misa approved variant E of the creature card (the workbench mock of the same day) with one change: "I'd only want the talents to always display on new rows instead of on the same row (see Festive and [20 - 25] displaying inline since they're short)." It is now the game's card (`CreatureCard`, `CreatureHead`, `creature-focus.css`); the workbench mock, its `variant` switch and `creature-card-variants.css` are gone.

- **Layout.** The sprite at the top left; beside it the name on one line and the tags in words under it (the species of a nickname, the element with its icon, the role, Festival, Shiny, First on server, Away, On a job, Lounging); then the slim experience bar across the card; then the gear slots on the left and the talents on the right; then the gold button when something is ready. No level tag at the top right and no arrow. The whole card is still one tap target with the same accessible name ("Open Cindle, common" on the name button, the creature's name on the card) and the same keyboard path.
- **Talents one to a row.** Each talent, each empty slot and the closed slots' levels are rows of their own (`button.cc-talents` is a column). The closed slots stay one chip ("20 · 25"), the thing Misa pointed at: a row per closed slot would make a new creature's card a row taller for no new information.
- **The bar is a shared component, `ui/XpTrack`.** There was no slim bar to reuse: the "Count-up" row draws its bar inline in `ui/XpGain`, which another change was editing. `XpTrack` draws the same bar at rest (the level tag, the bar, the fill and the numbers) with the Count-up row's own classes from `xp-gain.css`, so the two cannot drift apart in look; only the line they sit on is new (`xp-track.css`). E's own `StaticBar` (numbers in ink or bone on whichever side of the fill was larger) is gone with the mock: the Count-up row's bone numbers with an ink edge read over the fill's edge, and one look for a bar everywhere wins. `XpGain` could draw its track with `XpTrack` too (a later tidy-up, not done here). On the card the level tag has one width from "Lv 1" to "Lv 30", so the bars of a list start together, and it keeps its tap-for-help (no "?" drawn).
- **Tags.** Away and On a job keep their glossary help as before (with the "?"), as on the page; the element, role, species, Festival, Shiny and First on server are plain, as in E (the whole card opens the page, where they have their help). The old icon-only element chip is now the word with its icon.
- **The name** never wraps: a nickname is at most 20 letters ("Captain Puddlestomps" fits at 360 px at title size), and one too wide ends in "…" (clipped across only, so the letters' tails show). E's step down to heading size past 20 letters is dropped, since no nickname can be longer.
- **The creature page is unchanged.** Its head is `creature-page/PageHeader`, which shares no markup with the card (only the `.cc-sub` badge line and the `.portrait` box, both kept).

Misa, after round 5: "the 2x (or similar) modifier in exp could be the default tag color instead of the varying color it is now. It's a modifier that can appear on any type of exp"; "The lounging tag has a bit too much space between the armchair icon and text. Let's also change the evolution button text to just "Evolve". I guess we can keep the price but let's bring it to the same row (saves vertical space)." He also kept the cherries as the treat icon.

- **The multiplier tag** ("3.15x") is bone on ink like every other small tag, whatever the XP kind (Experience gold, Explorer orchid, Crafting leather). Open, it turns ink on bone, so it still shows its popover is open.
- **"Evolve".** The card's gold button and the creature page's button say "Evolve" for every line (before: "Evolve into Pyrelynx" or "Choose an evolution"). A line with two forms still opens the evolve screen, which names both. A line with one form still evolves on the tap, and the button no longer names the form. _Follow-up, the owner's call (later the same day): this changed. Every evolve opens the evolve screen, also for a line with one form; see "Every evolve goes through the evolve screen" below._
- **The card's gold button is one row** when it fits: the words, then the cost or the reason it is off ("Evolve 20 Essence", "Earn Bronze Ribbon 3 Scrap", "Evolve Away on a trip"), wrapping to two rows only on a long one. Its height goes from 3 rem to 2.75 rem (still a full touch target). Every need uses this button, so all of them move to one row.
- **The armchair** keeps its wide gap in the Lounge button; in the "Lounging" badge (the card and the page) the gap is a tag's own (0.25 rem).

## 2026-10-19: Every XP multiplier multiplies, and one rule in `xp-boost.ts` pays and explains every gain

Misa decided that every XP multiplier multiplies: a gain's total is the product of all its parts (the treat, each Boon and Omen, each gear line, each trip bonus), not the treat times one plus the shares added together. This replaces the additive note in the follow-up of "Every XP gain is the Count-up row" above (3x with +25% and -20% is 3 x 1.25 x 0.8 = 3x, no longer 3.15x). Reasoning: players read the breakdown as a list of multipliers, so the Total should be the lines multiplied out; the old rule made the lines not multiply out to the total when two shares met.

- **One rule, one parts array.** `xpBoostTotalBp(parts)` starts at the product of the treats' multipliers times 10000, then applies each share in order as `total = floor(total * max(0, 10000 + bp) / 10000)`; it is never negative. `applyXpBoost(base, parts)` is `floor(base * total / 10000)`, so a gain is always exactly its base times the Total the player sees, rounded down. The values stay far below 2^53 (a base of at most thousands, a total of at most a few hundred thousand), so the integer maths is safe.
- **The amount and the breakdown come from the same parts.** `tripExperience`, `dungeonExperience`, `raidExperience`, `workshopExperience`, `tripXp` (explorer XP) and the Workshop's Crafting XP all take the parts and call `applyXpBoost`; the worker passes the same array to `xpBoost` for the result's breakdown. `creatureXpParts` (treat, then the day's parts) and `dayCreatureXpParts` build the creature-experience parts in one place, for the worker and the simulator. The old summed path (`ExpWeather`, `withDay`, `plusBp`, summed explorer shares) is gone.
- **An effect whose content left stays in the amount.** `dayParts` names it "Weather" instead of dropping it, so a stored trip or job pays the same as its breakdown says. Stored data is unchanged (`xpBp`, `treatMultiplier`, `xpEffects` on trips and jobs); a trip or job stored with only a legacy total still gives one day part. The dungeon view keeps its old `treatTimes` and `dayEffects` fields for one more release.
- **Rounding.** Rounded down after every share, in the order the parts are listed (the treat first, then the day, then gear and trip bonuses as the worker lists them). The order can move the Total by one basis point at most, and the Total shown is the one that pays.
- **Balance.** No scalar changed. Where two or more shares meet (explorer XP from milestones, a twist, a push-on goal and gear; a Boon with an Omen on experience) the gain is now a little bigger when the shares are positive. The numbers moved by the rule are in the PR description; the simulator's tiny headline test moved with them (explained in `packages/sim/test/run.test.ts`).

## 2026-10-19: The level tag and the XP bar share one height; the creature page starts like the card; one shared action button

Misa: "Could we ensure the level's container is always the same height as the exp bar? In the creature preview it's larger. Show me versions where it's shrunken to the xp bar's height. Then show me a version where the exp bar is enlarged to the badge's height. I hope you can make this 'size lock' in the exp bar component itself." Also: "On the creature page, let's mimic the order of elements so that it looks like the creature preview (in the creature list) ... Also make the evolve button span the entire width of a mobile screen, and pretty big on a desktop." And: "The evolve button in the creature list has too much bottom padding below its text. Probably a leftover from removing the second row of text. Make sure the button component that does this stops doing it if the bottom text is empty, so we don't replicate it elsewhere in the app by accident."

- **Why the tag was taller.** The tag had its own `height` and padding, and its 2 px ink ring was drawn outside the box, so it measured 21 px and showed 25 px next to a 20 px bar (whose ring is inside). Now `ui/XpTrack`'s line is a grid of one row, `--xp-h` high, and the tag and the bar both just fill it (border-box, `height: auto`, `min-height: 0`), the tag's ring inside its box. Nothing outside `xp-track.css` sets a height on them (a test reads every stylesheet to prove it), so no screen can break the lock. The old card-only width rule moved into the component as `--xp-tag-w`.
- **Looks to compare (`size` prop; `DEFAULT_XP_SIZE` in `ui/XpTrack.tsx` is the one place to switch).** `shrunk`: tag shrunk to the bar's height (1.25 rem, ring inside), tag text and bar text the same small size. `snug`: the same with a narrower tag. `flat`: the tag drawn like the bar (dark, ringed, the level in the kind's colour). `grown`: the bar enlarged to the tag's old visible height (1.5 rem), numbers one step up (`--fs-row`). Default for now: `shrunk` (keeps the gold tag Misa knows, the smallest card, readable at 360 px). All keep text at `--fs-small` or larger. Workbench presets: XpTrack and CreatureCard, one per look.
- **The page mirrors the card.** `PageHeader` is now `CreatureHead` (sprite top left, name, tags in words) plus `XpTrack` (level tag and bar), then the action buttons, then Stats and the rest as before. The tags line is the card's own `NameTags`, one order for both (species of a nickname, element, role, Festival, Shiny, First on server, Away / On a job / Lounging); on the page they open their glossary entries. The page's thick `XpBar`, its own level badge and the separate tag row are gone; Back, Flip and Lounge stay in the top row. A nickname equal to the species name (any case) adds no species tag on either screen.
- **Why the button left space under its text.** The old card button was `display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; align-items: baseline` with `min-height: 2.75rem`. `align-items` places items inside a flex line, and a wrapping flex box keeps its line at the top, so the spare height all ended up below the text. `align-content: center` centres the line (one row or two), and the new `ui/ActionButton` draws no detail element at all when the detail is empty. It replaces the inline markup on the card (`wide`) and on the page for ribbon, evolve and answer-a-catch (`wide` and `large`: full width of the content on a phone, 3.5 rem tall, the full page column on a computer). The ribbon button now reads like the card's ("Earn Bronze Ribbon" with the cost beside it); what is missing is a small note under it with the help tag.

## 2026-10-19: Every evolve goes through the evolve screen; a data table panel (wave 7)

Misa: "Even with one evolution, let's use the same flow where they get to see that only one form is available." (and, once he saw it would need words: "Show, don't tell.") And: "On the creature card, give the stats 'table' a somewhat darker background and some padding. I'd like to use that kind of a reusable element when showing what is essentially a table of data. The same component could go into the exp multiplier breakdown popover."

- **Every evolve opens the evolve screen.** This supersedes two earlier lines: the 2026-10-18 evolve-screen entry ("opened by the evolve button only for a line with two forms; a line with one form evolves at once") and Round 5's "A line with one form still evolves on the tap". `Collection` no longer decides by `hasBranches` (removed): the card's and the page's Evolve button always open `EvolveDrawer`. No shipped line has one form (all eight have two), so this is the code path for a line that has one later, and for a form the content does not know.
- **A line with one form** shows that form on one card, the same facts as a card of two (picture, name, element and role, ability, HP, Attack and Speed on the new form). The card is full width at 360 px with the picture at the left (a half-width card beside an empty half would look unfinished) and is already picked: there is no choice, so the button ("Evolve into Pyrelynx") works at once, with the cost and "This choice is permanent." as before. The card is not a button (nothing to press). **No sentence says there is one form**: the single card shows it. A line with two forms is unchanged (nothing picked at first). The server request is unchanged (it always gets the species now).
- **`ui/DataTable`** is the shared table of data: rows of a label and a value, an optional second value column (the gear's "+204"; the columns line up across rows) and an optional gold total row, as a `dl`, on `--night` (darker than a `--dusk` panel or a `--dusk-2` popover) with padding and square corners. The creature page's stats and its About facts use it; so does the experience multiplier's breakdown popover (`bright` labels, the popover's body font and size, the treat's icon before its label, "Total 3.15x" in gold). The old `.sheet-stats`, `.sheet-fact` and `.xp-boost-line` rules are gone. The table is not for lists of things you can press, and not for a card's own layout (the evolve card's three numbers stay as they are).

## 2026-10-19: The XP bar's look is "grown"; the action button centres its detail

- **Misa picked "grown"** of the four XpTrack looks ("the best IMO because the text in the bar is properly centered"): the bar is enlarged to the level tag's height (1.5 rem) and its numbers are one step up. `DEFAULT_XP_SIZE` is `grown`; the other looks stay as workbench presets.
- **The detail of `ui/ActionButton` is centred on the label** (`align-items: center`, was `baseline`). Misa: "the 20 essence text on it isn't vertically aligned with the Evolve text". The pixel label and the body-font detail have different letter heights, so lining up their baselines put the small words low; centred, the ink of "Evolve" and "20 Essence" sits within half a pixel of the same middle.
- **No pips on the XP bar.** Dividers like the HP bars were mocked up (ten, five, notches); Misa: "Let's forget about pips". The HP bar keeps segments as its own look.

## 2026-10-19: Serpentine, dodge, status effects and Mire

**Problem.** Speed only sorted the turn order on a boss with `speed` order (only Cinder Wyrm), so the stat and the Zoomies talent did nothing three weeks in four, and an order by Speed is not fun to plan around (Misa). The four-element wheel cannot stretch to the many elements Misa plans.

**Decision.** Plan in `speed-and-dodge.md`, built in one PR. Cinder Wyrm gets **Serpentine** order (front row first, rows alternate direction, every row flips each round). Speed becomes the base of a **dodge** chance for creatures and foes (Speed x 10 bp to start, hard cap 50%; full chance on aimed hits, charges and enrage hits, half on area hits, never on reflected hits or a Guard's step-in; a Setter's dodged hit still leaves its mark). **Status effects** arrive as a general system: stacks up to a cap, a duration in the target's own turns, application power in basis points (140% is one stack plus a 40% chance of another), every application refreshes the timer, all stacks fall off together, purge moves and Support cleanse remove them. Matchups become an **element chart** of attacker-to-defender pairs. **Mire** joins: Mire and Ember deal 150% to each other, Tide deals 75% to Mire; its mark Muddied, its debuff Mired, the reactions Smother (Mire on Scorched, applies Mired) and Flare (Ember on Muddied, burns the Mired stacks for damage); a Guard line, a Support line, the raid boss Old Bogback, dungeon foes, the Mud room and the Sunken Fen.

**Why.** Speed should matter every day and reward commitment; durations in the target's own turns keep "3 turns" the same length in a raid of any size and in the dungeon; a chart of pairs lets each new element interact with the others without forcing a cycle (Misa: "we have to rip the bandaid off"). The engine keeps `speed` order and new dice sit behind stored flags, so old fights replay.

## 2026-10-19: The element chart is content, and a mark can have several triggers

**Decision.** `element-chart.json` is a list of `{ attacker, defender, bp }` pairs (valibot, strict); a pair not listed deals 10000. The old `strongAgainst` field and `RULES.combat.strongBp` / `weakBp` are gone; the same numbers (15000, 7500) live in the chart, and "strong" means exactly 15000 (`CHART_STRONG_BP`) for reactions, Hunter and Ward lines. `reactions.json` keeps one entry per (mark, trigger) pair, each with its own id and name; entries of one mark repeat its mark id and name. The engine looks a reaction up by (mark element, trigger element). Content tests check every reaction's trigger is strong against its mark and every strong pair has a reaction. Hunter folds onto every element the wearer's element is strong against, Ward onto every element strong against it, and Reaction lines onto every reaction the element sets off. No player-visible change yet: the four-element chart is the old wheel.

**Why.** Slice 2 of `speed-and-dodge.md`: the chart lets Mire and later elements pair up freely. Numbers moved to content so the balance simulator can tune any pair; they are listed in `docs/balance/scalars.md`.

## 2026-10-20: Mire content: Old Bogback, the bog foes, Muddy and the Sunken Fen (slice 5b)

**Decision.** Old Bogback joins the raid rotation as the fifth boss (appended, so the weeks now cycle through five: the old order is not kept, Misa). Left to right. Sinking Mud (a row sweep that applies Mired), Belly Flop (a front slam), Shake Off (a purge every fifth round), and two rank-gated moves to keep the "one move from rank 4, one from rank 7" rule every boss follows: Bog Lob (rank 4, a slam on the strongest Payoff) and Rising Bubbles (rank 7, a charged barrage). The draft had four moves; Bog Lob is the fifth. Its adaptation is Element Resistance (the bog learns the element that hurt it most). Every boss and dungeon foe now has a low `baseSpd` (2 to 5% dodge at 10 bp a point; Old Bogback 2%, Storm Sovereign 5%): inert while dodge is off. The dungeon gets Bog Toad, Tar Leech and the floor boss Fen Lurker (their moves apply Mired) and the Muddy room (`mud`: the team's Speed -30%). The Sunken Fen (Mire, explorer rank 1) holds Mudlump and Squelch, which are now catchable, with a fourth journal page (`fen-patient-ones`) because every place needs four.

**Old finds.** A place has no knob of its own for gear finds, journal pages or the vault, and the schema forbids rare odds where no rare species lives. So the fen's "the bog keeps old things" rests on what already follows a place: gear a trip finds there is Mire gear, shards are Mire shards, and it has the largest rarer-catch push (15%). A real per-place find bonus is a rules change for Misa to ask for.

**Why.** Slice 5b of `speed-and-dodge.md`. The dungeon fixture scenario now plays with the shipped foes and rooms minus the Mire ones (`pinPool`), because the seeded map draws from the whole pool and the client tests name the captured rooms; without it every new foe re-rolled the Undercroft fixtures and about 20 client tests. The dungeon test now allows a quiet mystery room on the route of the gamble test, and the chance-trigger test compares only fights that last as long as before (a trigger line that cuts damage can add a round, which draws its dice).

## 2026-10-20: Riposte stops the boss's turn; Mire passives in every fight

**Decision.** A boss that falls or retreats during its own turn (a Riposte on a dodged hit) stops there: no later hit, gimmick, plain attack or enrage hit, and no tick of its effects. `on_hit_taken` passives no longer fire on a hit that deals 0 damage. Passives (the Mire Guard and Support signatures) now work in Phase 1 and Phase 2 of a raid too, through a new `FightInput.passives` field, so those phases do not get the fireable finale ability.

**Why.** Review findings: a Riposte could end the fight while the boss kept hitting (and the dungeon carried that damage to the next room); the passives were built only for the finale though speed-and-dodge.md 5.3 calls them always-on. Stored fights replay as before (new field optional, no dice moved).

## 2026-10-19: Raid summary mock-ups for Misa to pick, workbench only

- **Why.** Misa on the after-action card: "lists and lists of bullet points that are incredibly cursed in their alignment"; wanted "a scoreboard or stuff like that, maybe a few highlights" (roadmap item 5). The live screens (the finale reveal and the Raid tab's result cards) are **not changed**; the mock-ups are one workbench page, `RaidSummaryMock` (Loot & reveals), fed by the data the server already sends (`AfterActionStats`, the stage's Essence, gear grant, Festival creature and rank change).
- **Four variants, plus today's card for comparison:** A scoreboard first (ranked table, three highlight cards); B one big moment (boss, how it ended, then a short scoreboard); C your raid first (your place, your numbers, your creatures; the server folded away); D a podium of the top three and a tight list. Rewards show in the same place in all four ("Your rewards": Essence and parts as count badges, gear, blueprints, the Festival creature, rank and season). Raids pay no experience today, so there is no `XpGain` row. The old lists (reactions, abilities, knockouts, counter-move, boss, top creatures) became folded `DataTable`s under one "More numbers".
