- **Shared pieces.** `ui/DataTable` rows gain `mine` (the viewer's own row: a lighter band and gold type). `raid-summary-view.ts` works out the scoreboard and the highlights from the stats alone (Hardest hitter, Star creature, The backbone, Favourite reaction, Quick hands, Nobody fell, Final push, Counter-move broken); a highlight only exists when the stats hold what it says. Only the variant Misa picks will be built into the game; the others and their CSS (`workbench/raid-summary-mock.css`) are then removed.
- **Would need new server data:** the biggest single hit, healing or shield absorbed (a real "Clutch save"), who dealt the killing blow, damage taken or saved per player, reactions set up per player, the boss's name and id in the stats (today they come from the raid view), a comparison with last week or a personal best, and per-player (not summed) setup, support and guard credit.

## 2026-10-19: WebSocket push for the lounge, as a prototype behind a flag

Misa: "a prototype of websockets, but not in the raid yet. Try it in the lounge! Easier to test and opens up more cozy options." Misa is on the Cloudflare Workers Free plan, and nobody deploys staging or production overnight. Design in `docs/design/lounge-live.md`.

- **What.** The guild's `GuildWorld` object accepts lounge sockets with the **Hibernation API** (`acceptWebSocket`, per-socket identity in the attachment, `setWebSocketAutoResponse('ping', 'pong')` as the heartbeat). After a successful change request it pushes the actor's room entry (the same shape as one `POST /api/room` resident) to the guild's other sockets. No new Durable Object class: the push belongs to the same serialised path as the write that causes it.
- **Server to client only.** A client sends only `ping`. Intents stay on their HTTP routes, so no new schema or rate limit is needed and incoming socket messages (billed) stay near zero. Any other incoming text closes the socket (4002).
- **Auth by one-time ticket.** `POST /api/lounge/ticket` (normal session auth) makes a 30 s, single-use ticket with its own MAC; the socket opens with `?ticket=`. A browser cannot set `Authorization`; Discord documents nothing about `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol`; a URL may be logged, so the ticket must be worthless after use. The Worker checks the MAC before waking any object. No origin check (Discord does not say what origin its proxy forwards; the ticket already blocks cross-site use).
- **Limits.** 3 sockets per player (oldest closed, the client does not fight), 64 per guild (503, the client polls), stale after 150 s without a ping (checked on connect and on push, no timers), 12 h maximum age.
- **Cheap on Free.** Hibernation, a 60 s client ping that the runtime answers without waking the object, no timers or outbound fetches for the sockets, no push when nobody else listens, no push for a change of nothing, no alarm writes (`ensureAlarm` already only sets an earlier alarm). The client opens the socket only while the page is visible and somebody else is in the room.
- **Fallback is the old poll.** While the socket is live the 45 s poll waits; on any failure (unsupported, flag off, refused, no hello in 15 s, silent for 150 s, drop) the poll comes back. Reconnects use backoff 1 s to 30 s with jitter, give up after 6 failures in a row, and fetch the whole room once after each connect (a push is not replayed).
- **Flag.** `LOUNGE_LIVE`: on locally and on staging, **off in production** (pinned by a test). Off means both routes answer 404 and `/api/config` omits `loungeLive`. `wrangler.jsonc` gained this var, `worker-configuration.d.ts` was regenerated, and the activity's CSP `connect-src` lists `wss://` hosts explicitly.
- **Not done.** Presence is not pushed (Discord's participant events stay the source). The raid finale keeps polling; the design doc says how it could reuse the pieces.

## 2026-10-19: The footer and the dev clock are data tables

- **The app footer** (Environment, Server version, Branch, Guild ID, API health) and **the dev panel's clock** are now `ui/DataTable`s (rows built by `footerRows` in `footer-rows.ts` and `clockRows` in `dev/dev-api.ts`, both tested). The footer's own `.row` style and the `.dev-clock` grid are gone. The clock's raid line, which was one long sentence that squeezed the labels at 360 px, is three short rows (Raid week, Raid status, Raid boss) plus Simulated players when there are any.
- **Looked at and left alone:** the explorer profile's stat tiles (two columns of bordered cards, the value larger under the label, in the same card style as the milestone and explorer rows below them: a card grid, not a table of rows); the evolve card's HP / Attack / Speed line (three small figures side by side inside a button); `ItemLines` (glossary-tag lines with tier pips and compare marks, not label and value); `WornItemLines` and `ItemCard` (item rows with pictures; there is no label and value table in them). Converting any of these would change a deliberate look or drop the colours and compare marks.

## 2026-10-19: Preact 11.0.1; the Workers test plugin stays at 1.4.0

- **Preact 10.29.8 to 11.0.1** (released 2026-10-08, 11 days before the bump; 11.0.0 came out 2026-09-30). It is the only runtime dependency that moved: `preact` in the catalog (`pnpm-workspace.yaml`) and the lockfile; `@preact/preset-vite` 2.10.6 and `@prefresh/*` already accept it. No new packages, no install scripts. Read the upgrade guide for the breaking changes and checked each against the code: no numeric `style` values (the automatic `px` is gone from core), no `defaultProps`, no `replaceNode`, no `Component.base`, no `forwardRef` or `ref` on components, no `preact/compat`. What did change: `RefObject<T>` now describes a ref whose `current` is exactly `T`, so every ref we create with `useRef<T | null>(null)` and pass on is typed `RefObject<T | null>` (a type-only change in 12 files), and the pointer event types moved from the `JSX` namespace to `preact` itself (`TargetedPointerEvent`).
- **One test-helper fix:** Preact 11 removes DOM nodes with `node.remove()`, text nodes included; the test DOM's `FakeText` had no `remove()`, so `remove()` moved up to the common `FakeNode`. Behaviour of the app is unchanged. The full activity suite (2759 tests) passes, the production build works, and a screenshot sweep of every workbench preset at 360 and 390 px shows no new overflow, clipping or blank states compared with Preact 10 (the full run flagged 68 rows, most of them timing noise of a busy machine, so those rows and every blank one were run again on both versions with a longer wait: no differences, and the same 150 blank rows on both, which are components without text such as bars and maps).
- **Dependabot's major-version cooldown is 14 days** (`.github/dependabot.yml`); 11.0.1 is 11 days old, 11.0.0 is 19. The repo rule that matters here (`minimumReleaseAge`, 7 days) is met; this PR does not touch the Dependabot file.
- **vitest and `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` unchanged** (vitest 5.0.3, plugin 1.4.0, released 2026-10-08). The newer plugin releases are 1.4.1 (2026-10-13), 1.4.2 (10-14), 1.4.3 (10-15), 1.4.4 (10-16) and 1.4.5 (2026-10-19), all younger than 7 days today, so `minimumReleaseAgeStrict` refuses them; the first one to qualify is 1.4.1 on 2026-10-20. vitest 5.0.4 (2026-10-12) already qualifies but broke the worker tests with plugin 1.4.0 (`fsModuleCache`), so the two stay together. Check again from 2026-10-20.

## 2026-10-20: The raid summary ships: scoreboard first, with the boss header (A plus B)

Misa picked the mock-ups of 2026-10-19: "A plus B's boss header". The live finale reveal and the Raid tab's result cards now draw it (`RaidSummary` fetches, `RaidSummaryCard` draws; the workbench page `RaidSummaryCard` shows the five samples). His changes to the mock-up, in his words:

1. "Show all the players in the scoreboard, removing the need for the accordion with all players." Every player is a row, the caller's row marked, and the raid total last. The "All N players" fold is gone.
2. "Give the table component proper headings and turn them on for this view." `ui/DataTable` takes `headings` (and a `label`). With them it is a real `table` (a header row of `th scope="col"`, each row's label a `th scope="row"`), because a `dl` cannot name its columns; without them it is the `dl` it was, so no other table changes. The scoreboard's columns are Player, Damage and "%" ("The last column can be titled just "%"").
3. "Remove the loot pile graphic from the rewards section and just keep the tags for each resource." `RewardSummary` already had `pile={false}`; the summary uses it. Essence and each kind of part are count badges, gear and blueprints are listed under "Finale reward" and "Festival gear", then the Festival creature, the rank sentence and track, and the season pips.
4. "Show the boss attack order and its special moves as clickable icons next to the boss (or under it)." The boss card's icon buttons (`BossIcon`, tapping opens a popover) moved to `BossIcons.tsx` as `BossIconList`; the boss card and the summary both draw it, under the boss's HP bar. The boss comes from the raid view the client already has (`summaryBoss`), **only when the view is of the week the summary is about**: a reveal that outlives the week (read on Monday) shows no icons rather than another boss's moves, and says "The boss falls!" if not even the name is known (the live finale still knows id and name from its own snapshot). Nothing in `packages/game/src/content` or the engine was touched, and the server sends nothing new. If the stats carried the boss id and name, the fallback would not be needed (the same "would need" item as before).
5. "Align the raid report title vertically with the cleared or defeated badge." The title and its badges (Cleared or Not cleared, and "Close one!") sit in one flex line, centred on each other (`.reveal-card > h3`, so every reveal card gets it, and the Results list's `h4`).

- **Dropped on purpose:** the orb animation and the Essence count-up of the old finale reveal, and the "pop in" of the gear (the summary is a calm result card; `RevealStage` and the sequence hook stay for the Workshop job reveal). Variants B, C and D, the podium and "Now" (today's card) and their code are gone; the record of what was considered is the 2026-10-19 entry above. `AfterAction.tsx`, `FinaleGearLoot.tsx` and the old after-action CSS went with them.
- **When the fight stats cannot be had** the card still shows the result badge, the rewards and the buttons, with a quiet line saying so, so a player is never stuck behind a failed request.
- **A card in the Results list** has no "Got it" (it is not dismissable), a plain panel instead of the gold frame, and an `h4` title.

## 2026-10-20: The raid summary, round two, and the Find components

Misa on the raid summary (quotes are his): "Drop 'damage, share of the raid' from beside the Scoreboard heading." The table has headings now, so the note is gone.

- **Boss moves.** "The 'new at rank 4' ability makes the abilities look ugly since they're different size… drop the info that it's new entirely… If possible, reduce the size of all of these elements." `BossIconList` takes `compact`: small tiles of one size (3.75 rem wide, 4 rem high, label on the type scale, at most two lines), no "New at rank" line, and the popover does not mention it either. **Only the raid summary is compact.** The boss card keeps the line (and its larger tiles): planning a team is where a new move matters, and the tiles there sit in a wide row of their own.
- **One list of rewards.** "Drop the 'finale rewards' and 'festival gear' subheadings. Lump them all into the 'Your rewards' section." The finale and Festival grants are merged (`rewardLoot`) into one list under the resource badges.
- **A blueprint looks like an item.** "I'd like the blueprint reward to have the same look as an item reward. The image could be the 'blueprint style' image of the item itself, the title could be 'Blueprint: Item Name' and it could have the item's tags… and a 'blueprint' help tag." The Workshop already drew a blueprint as the item's own sprite in a plain (common) frame; that image is reused with a dashed outline (`.item-frame.blueprint`). Tags are what the base defines (slot, element or role, set), never a rarity, plus the existing `blueprint` glossary entry (no change to `glossary.json`, no content touched).
- **A creature is the expedition's card.** "If a creature is given as a reward, use the same component as we use when finding creatures on expeditions. No need for the prose that exists now." The picture, name, element, badges and talent of `FindCard` moved to `ui/FindCreature`; `FindCard` is now that card plus the duplicate choice. The Festival creature uses it (`festivalFind` builds it from the grant); the sentence `festivalGrantText` is no longer shown (it stays in `live.ts` for the live loot text). A duplicate Festival creature keeps one short line, "It waits on the Collection tab.", because that is where the player answers it.
- **"Watch Replay" was narrower than it could be.** `wide` insets the button by its ring, "Got it" does not. Both now run the full width of the card (`large`, no inset).

**The bigger ask:** "I'd like to establish these as patterns when finding anything: gear, resources, creatures… The components should live in a place where they're accessible everywhere… and they should be reusable." The Find components live in `src/ui/` (see the table and rule in `ux-patterns.md`): `FindRow` and `FindList` (the box and its list), `FindItem` / `FindItems`, `FindBlueprint`, `FindCreature`, and resources as `CountBadge` with `bare`. They were not written new: `ItemRow` and `GearRows` became `FindItem` and `FindItems`, `FindCard`'s head became `FindCreature`, and `LootList`'s blueprint notes became `FindBlueprint`. **Screens converted:** the raid summary and finale reveal, the Raid tab's results, the trip result (creatures, gear, blueprints, resources), the waiting catches on the Collection tab, the dungeon results, the descend card's banked gear, the map drawer's chest gear and found loot, the Workshop job reveal (a learned blueprint, a known one's quality, what a dismantle gave), and the worn-gear list of the living room's popover (same row, not a find). **Left alone, and why:** the Workshop's crafted-item result (it also shows the item's lines and an upgrade note, so it is an item card, not a compact find: converting it would change what it says); the dismantle's "taken apart" row (items going out, not found); the cost lines and the pack at risk (costs, not rewards, they keep the "?"); the Workshop's blueprint list (the blueprints you own with a Craft button: a list of what you have, not of finds).

Misa on the dungeon results (the picture is a run with one gear piece and a day 7 streak bonus):

- "Gear rewards are centered, not left-aligned." / "If multiple pieces of gear are found, display them side by side and let them wrap. Leave a reasonable gap between gear." / "Consider adding a background color to each gear piece with a small padding so that they read as 'boxes'. Remember that touching a shared component like this should change it everywhere in the app." Done in the shared `FindList` and `FindRow`: a darker box (`--night`) with a line round it and padding, side by side, centred, wrapping with a gap. Everywhere a find shows (trip result, chest, raid, Workshop, a creature's worn list) it now looks like this; the creature card got the same box colour.
- "The random 1 scrap that is listed as a reward under the gear piece should be merged with the rewards above…" **What it was:** the streak bonus's parts. The server pays the bonus apart from the run's own rewards (`rewards.streak` is "not in the amounts above"), and the page drew its parts as a loot list under the gear, with the Bait and Essence only in a sentence. It is not merged into the totals (that would hide that a streak paid it, and the server's two amounts are different sources); it is the next point's section instead.
- "The text says 'Day 7 streak bonus: 1 Essence, 1 Scrap'… tell the user what they got as a reward. Let's make this its own section titled 'N Day Streak Bonus' and show the rewards under it. Prefix the title with the gift icon we use on the streak counter." A section "7 Day Streak Bonus" with the gift glyph before it and the Bait, Essence and parts as count badges (`streakBonus` in `dungeon-playback.ts`); the sentence and the lone parts row are gone.
- "Remove the question mark icons from the resources (scrap, essence, bait)." `CountBadge` has `bare` (tappable for its help, no "?"), on in every reward or find list (`RewardSummary`, the trip result, the descend card, the map drawer's found loot, the job reveal, the streak bonus). The "?" stays on cost and price lines and on the pack at risk. This is the same `bare` the glossary tag already had for item badges and the level tag.
- **Depth track.** "The Gear Drop, bonus essence and Undercroft Opens texts are displayed in the middle of the tracker… they should be on the left, left aligned under 'Depth 7'." The right end's lines are mirrored (icon at the edge) by `.ptrack-unlock`; the rule also hit the current step, which put its lines at the right of its own half, in the middle. Now only the next step mirrors. It is the same `ProgressTrack` as the raid summary's Boss Rank, fixed once.
- **Streak.** "The streak element could also be reworked to just have a 'Streak' title with a question mark title after it with a popover explaining what a streak is. Below the title we'd show the streak tracker and when it resets. Rework the fonts in it too." The row is "Streak ?" (the existing `streak` entry, the section-title size in the pixel font), the days with their gifts, then the reset line in the body font at the small size. The "N days in a row" line is gone (the lit days say it; the screen-reader label still gives the count of the cycle).

## 2026-10-20: No "?" on tags, anywhere

Misa: "drop the question mark icons on the tags here too. I think that could be done globally. I constantly find myself not liking question mark icons on tags."

- **Every tappable chip or tag** (`GlossaryTag look="chip"`, so also `ui/Tag` with an entry and `ui/CountBadge` with an entry) draws no "?" now. It still opens its explanation, and it brightens on hover and while its popover is open. Words keep the dotted underline. The only "?" left is the small help button (`look="help"`) beside a heading or a control ("Streak ?", "Keepsakes ?", the title row's How to play).
- **The `bare` switch is gone** (from `GlossaryTag`, `CountBadge`, `CountBadgeList` and `PartCount`): it only turned the "?" off, and now nothing draws one. This replaces the earlier "costs and prices keep the '?'" rule (2026-10-20, Find components).
- **One test proves it** (`ui/find.test.tsx`): no stylesheet draws a "?" as CSS `content`.

## 2026-10-20: Lounging with company earns XP; the live lounge goes to production

Misa: "Let's enable the websocket based lounge for production… I'd also like to include a passive EXP tick for a creature that is lounging IF there is another creature present. An empty lounge earns no experience, but lounging with a friend does. It needs to be quite small." On the cap: "If we cap it at 60 EXP per day, then that means that players will have the incentive to stop lounging at some point. If we can give each creature 60 daily EXP for lounging, we'll encourage leveling up those lower leveled creatures." He chose option C of `living-room.md`, "Lounging with company".

- **The rule:** 1 XP every 2 minutes with company, at most 60 a game day **per creature** (then Rested), no cap per player, no treats or weather. `RULES.experience.lounge` (`msPerXp`, `dailyCap`); the glossary entry `lounging` says the same numbers and a test keeps them in step.
- **Presence came from the live sockets, not the client** (replaced the same day by Discord's participant list, see "Lounging XP fixes after the first production test"). The participant list is the client's word; a socket's owner is proven by a ticket of an authenticated request and its lifetime is the server's own record. So lounging pays only through the socket, and `LOUNGE_LIVE` is now on in production too (`wrangler.jsonc`, pinned by a test). Polling clients see the room but earn nothing. A script holding a socket open with a friend's earns at most 60 per creature a day and cannot pass a ribbon: not worth more defence.
- **Paid lazily, without timers or alarms** (`lounge-company.ts`): on every socket join and leave (the leaver still counts until then) and at the start of a player's own request (so a swap pays the old lounger first). Per player, `lounge_company` keeps up to when they were paid; per creature, `lounge_rest` keeps today's XP. Time before the current game day's start is never paid, so a session across the reset loses its last few minutes of the old day rather than paying them to the new one. Spans use real time; the game day follows the dev clock where it runs. Migration 35.
- **The state** has `loungeXp` (creature id to today's XP); it refreshes with any request of the player (not pushed).
- **UI.** The lounger's tag: "Lounging 34 / 60 XP", then "Lounging · Rested" (filled green); another creature at the cap: "Rested" (outlined green). Both open the `lounging` glossary entry. Colour token `--lounge` (today `--good`), so lounging can change colour in one place. **Choosing:** a quiet icon-only armchair at the end of a free creature's name line on the Creatures list (Misa wanted a Lounge button there without noise or bloat: no button face, no words, the page's colour until hovered or focused; the lounger and busy creatures have none, a Rested one keeps it); the page's Lounge button; and "Swap" beside "Move" in the popover of your own creature in the room, opening the "Who lounges?" sheet (the raid picker's `RosterPicker`, with each creature's experience bar (as on the card) and Rested; free ones that still earn first, lowest level first; reasons on the right). The popover takes several actions now (`action` may be a list).
- **The creature popover, brought up to date** (Misa: "change the name color to match the rarity and add a tag for the rarity too… consider using the new data table"). Everywhere a small creature opens its popover (the room, the raid board, the dungeon team, the stage): the name in its rarity's colour at the heading size, as on the card; the tags under it are the rarity, the element and role, and Shiny, each a chip that opens its explanation inside the popover; the facts (Level, Owner, Species of a nickname, Crafting level, Explorer rank) are a `DataTable` in the pixel font, as the creature page's tables (the body font looked wrong to Misa); a board's effect stays one sentence under it; worn items and the buttons as before. `creatureFactRows` replaces the bullet lines of `creatureFacts`.
- **Your own lounger's popover in the room** has its card's Lounging tag too ("Lounging 34 / 60 XP", then "Lounging · Rested"; Misa: "Just for my own creature though"). Other players' creatures show none: their XP is not sent to you.
- **A long name and the card's armchair:** the armchair never moves or wraps; the name gives way. At 390 px a 20-letter name still fits; at 360 px the longest names end in "…" a few letters earlier than before (the full name is the button's title and on the page).

## 2026-10-20: Push-on extra time starts at the decision

Misa's call. The extra hours of a push-on used to count from the end of the trip, so a player who opened the app late found it already over and the creature acted as if it had pushed on all along. Now `push_started_at` is the moment of the choice and `push_ends_at` is that plus the goal's extra hours: pushing on carries its price in time, and the creature stays busy until then. Claiming earlier still means coming home (the push-on is skipped). Trips already pushing on keep their stored times (no migration). This replaces the "loses no time" line of the push-on entry above.

## 2026-10-20: Workshop conversions, Refine and Recycle

Misa: players can turn expedition supplies into other things in the Workshop, as timed jobs like dismantling.

- **Recipes are data.** `packages/game/src/content/data/conversions.json`, validated by `content/conversion-schema.ts` at module load (like supplies and destinations). A recipe is `{ id, kind, inputs, outputs, hours, craftingLevel }`; an input or output is `{ type: 'supply' | 'part', id, amount }`. Recipes name supplies by id only, never by effect, so supply numbers can change freely, and a later currency adds recipes (and, if needed, a new ingredient `type`) without engine changes. The file is explicit (16 recipes) rather than generated at load: it is easy to read and tune, and the loader checks the rules below so a typo cannot ship. Amounts are fixed integers (no ranges): a recycle is deterministic, so the screen can show exactly what comes back.
- **Refine** (8 recipes): 3 supplies of one kind and tier into 1 of the same kind, next tier (basic to good, good to great), lures included (a lure uses no Bait to refine). 2 hours for basic to good, 4 hours for good to great. It needs the Crafting level of the supply it makes (3 for good, 5 for great, from the supply's blueprint). The loader rejects a refine that does not make exactly one supply of the same kind one tier up, or that takes fewer than 2.
- **Recycle** (8 recipes: every good and great supply, none for basic): 1 supply gives back parts only, never Bait (a lure recycles to scrap only). Each part is under half of what crafting the supply cost, part by part (cost 4 gives 1, cost 7 gives 3, cost 3 gives 1, cost 1 gives 0), so crafting, recycling and refining can never make parts from nothing. Basic supplies cost 2 scrap, so half is already 1: they have no recycle recipe (it would be 50%, or nothing). 1 hour for good, 2 hours for great, Crafting level 1. The loader enforces "less than half, parts only, same supply".
- **Pure rules** in `rules/conversions.ts`: `conversionBlock` (level, then supplies, then parts), `conversionJobMs` (the same Crafting level reduction as `supplyJobMs`, 3% a level; Busy paws and the day's weather are applied by the server on top, as for supplies), `conversionXp` (nominal hours times `RULES.gear.skill.xpPerHour`; creature experience follows the nominal hours too).
- **Server.** A new Workshop job kind `convert` (`JOB_KINDS`), one for both refine and recycle: the recipe id in the job says which. Migration 36 (`workshop-convert-migration.ts`) rebuilds `workshop_jobs` to widen the kind CHECK (same pattern as 29: every row, column and index kept). The start request is strict (`{ kind: 'convert', creatureId, recipeId, treat?, idempotencyKey }`, unknown keys and recipe ids rejected); the request names only the recipe, never amounts. Starting takes the supplies and parts in the same transaction as the job under the client's idempotency key (the usual `runKeyed` of Workshop starts), uses a Workshop slot and a free creature, and refuses with `skill_too_low`, `insufficient_supplies` or `insufficient_parts` (nothing is taken). The job stores the recipe's resolved inputs and outputs, so the claim pays exactly what was shown even if the data changes meanwhile; claiming again returns the stored result and grants nothing. No randomness is involved. Analytics: the usual `job_start` and `job_claim` events with `job: 'convert'`, the first input supply as `base` and its tier as `rarity`, hours and Crafting level (ids and numbers only).
- **Client.** `ui/RecipeCard` is the general "combine things" component (inputs with have / need as count badges, arrow, outputs, a time badge, a reason and one button; it knows nothing of supplies); `ConversionCrafting` uses it in the Workshop's supply section for Refine and Recycle, listing only recipes whose supply the player holds. Start opens the same creature picker as other Workshop jobs. Job slots read "Refine Plain Lure" and "Recycle Sweet Lure"; the claimed job shows what went in, an arrow and what came out as count badges.
- **Words:** "Refine" for the upgrade and "Recycle" for parts back (compendium `items.md`, glossary entry `job-convert`).
- **Open:** the numbers (3 to 1, the hours, "less than half") are a first pass for Misa to tune in the data file; basic supplies cannot be recycled.

## 2026-10-20: Expedition trips pay in ticks

Player feedback: a 12 h trip was no better than six 2 h ones, rewards were tiny, supplies felt useless, catches were rare and nobody could craft. Misa's answer, built the same day (`docs/design/expeditions-v2.md`, "Ticks"):

- **A finished trip has ticks by length:** 2 h is 2, 6 h is 7, 12 h is 15, 24 h is 32 (`RULES.expeditions.lengths.<l>.ticks`). They run ahead of the hours, so long trips win; players are only told "longer trips are better". Ticks are nominal: explorer rank shortens the time, not the ticks.
- **Each tick** gives 20 explorer XP (`RULES.expeditions.tick.xp`, boosts multiply the total) and rolls every table on its own, in basis points per tick: creature 1250, scrap 5000, a shard 600, a gear piece 513 (rolled like a push-on drop, rarity 8500 / 1350 / 150), glimmer 30, blueprint 40, supply 200, journal page 70 (about 0.1 pages for a 12 h trip, as before). No caps: lucky streaks are fine. All modifiers (gear lines, talents, weather, twists, stars, milestones, Guard, Setter) scale these scalars.
- **Shards follow the place**, never the explorer; a place without an element (Old Crossroads, Festival Grounds) draws each shard's element evenly of the five. "Parts" means every crafting ingredient: scrap, shards and glimmer.
- **The catch pity is gone** (`newAfter`, `creatureEvery`; the two counters stay in `player_progress`, unused, no migration). The dupe shield and the first-expedition Zipmouse stay. The `catch_pity` analytics event keeps its id but is no longer recorded.
- **Recall** pays the ticks of the whole nominal hours already spent (never the bonus ticks), each rolled normally, and no fixed parts, XP, journal or stats. **Push-on** hours are ticks, one per hour, on top of the goal's reward; a goal's `catchRolls` are extra creature-only rolls at the tick's chance (the old EV, now fairer on short trips). Weekly twist `rolls` are extra ticks. Because the extra hours are worth ticks, the risky goals' odds were re-derived so each is a fair gamble again (about 1.00 in the simulator's worth table).
- **Supplies have a fixed part and a per-tick part**, all in `supplies.json`: Map (extra ticks: fixed plus a share of the ticks, fractions roll as a chance), Lure (a fixed chance of one bonus creature that surely comes, a little more chance per tick, `newBp` as before), Lantern (a deeper chance per catch plus a fixed chance that the first creature comes from the deeper area), Satchel (fixed scrap, shards and a chance of glimmer, plus a share more per tick and more supply finds). Lures cost less Bait (6 / 15 / 30). Blurbs say what each does (issue #47). A creature is worth 15 / 40 / 90 Bait in the simulator's value table.
- **Server:** the trip's `rolls` column stores its whole ticks (length, twist, day, gear); the map's ticks and the lure's and lantern's chances are rolled at the claim with live randomness. A trip in flight at deploy is paid by its length (`max(stored, length ticks)`; the old stored catch rolls were always below it). No migration (version 36 is another change's).
- **Simulator:** plays the tick rules; `node packages/sim/scripts/trip-yield.mjs` prints what a trip brings by length and supply. The sim's headline pins (`run.test.ts`) are not re-pinned here.

## 2026-10-20: The daily dungeon pays more, and its choices pay more again (B-024)

- **Why.** Misa: a full run paid 2 to 4 scrap, which felt like nothing. New target: a full clear of the main map pays, in expectation, about 1.5 crafts of parts (about 10 scrap and 1.5 shards) and one gear piece, and choices made inside the dungeon (elites, risky mystery options, the carved door, the Undercroft descent) raise that.
- **What.** Scalars, plus one new pack field: fight rooms can drop a shard of their foe's element into the pack (`RULES.dungeon.shardBp` by room kind: fight 15%, elite 40%, epic 60%, floor boss and Undercroft keeper a sure one), at risk like the rest of the pack; it replaces `bossShardBp`. Room loot, the boss chest (every full clear drops a gear piece), the mystery rooms' options and the Undercroft's chest and epic fight pay more; the numbers and the before and after are in `docs/balance/log.md`, B-024. Difficulty is unchanged.
- **Consequences.** The sim's headline pins and a few gear-share cells moved; the pins are not re-pinned in this change. A gear piece on every clear makes a landmark's certain drop ordinary: only its rarity bonus and extras set it apart.

## 2026-10-20: A recall pays explorer XP too

Misa's rule is that each tick gives a fixed XP. A recall already paid loot, creatures and parts for its whole hours, so it now also pays `RULES.expeditions.tick.xp` per tick of those hours (milestone, twist and Trail XP shares apply). It is still not a finished trip: no creature experience, familiarity trip, journal page or account stats, and the explorer's trip count (`explorer_progress.trips`) does not move. A recall can raise the rank, so rank gifts and slot unlocks work in the recall claim too (`payExplorerXp` in `apps/worker/src/expedition-claim.ts`). A recall before the first whole hour pays nothing, as before.

## 2026-10-20: Economy targets, tested exactly

Expedition and crafting balance is now checked with exact expected values (`packages/sim/src/trip-ev.ts`, `pnpm sim:economy`), and the bands live in `ECONOMY_TARGETS` (`packages/sim/src/economy-targets.ts`) with a fast test (`economy-targets.test.ts`). Every supply must return 1.1 to 4 times its crafting cost on every trip length, about 2 on a 12 hour trip, each tier must gain more than the one below, and a new part kind or supply kind without a worth fails a test. Known exception: the lantern on a 2 hour trip (ROI 0.4 to 0.5), because its fixed part only exists when a creature comes. A bonus deep creature roll like the lure's would fix it (follow-up, a rule change). The tuning is B-025.

## 2026-10-20: The lantern gets a bonus deep creature roll

Misa's rule is that every supply must be worth it on short trips too. The lantern's fixed part (`firstDeepBp`, the first creature of the trip comes from the deeper area) needed a creature to come, so it was worth 0.36 to 0.53 of its parts on a 2 h trip. It now works like the lure's bonus roll: `bonusBp` is the chance of one bonus creature roll that surely brings a creature drawn from the deeper area, on a finished trip only (a recall never rolls it), and only where the deeper area is open (a lantern opens it; a place with no deeper content gets no bonus and its chance is not drawn; a place with a rarity push but no deeper species still gives the bonus creature the deeper rarity shift). `firstDeepBp` is gone, `deepBp` (the per-creature chance) stays. Draw order: the lure's bonus chance, the lantern's bonus chance, the ticks, the lure's bonus roll, the lantern's bonus roll. The three lanterns were retuned (B-025 follow-up) and the `lanternShortRoi` exception is removed: every supply now meets the ROI targets on every length.

## 2026-10-20: The Workshop's supplies become one compact picker (version C)

Misa chose version C and asked to keep it compact throughout. The long supply list and the separate Refine and Recycle section (`ConversionCrafting`, removed) are now one picker (`SupplyCrafting`): a grid of the 12 supplies as pictures (4 kinds across, 3 tiers down, rarity edges grey, blue and pink, stock in a corner, supplies no creature can craft yet dimmed but selectable) and one card under it for the chosen supply with its effect text from content, cost, time, Crafting level and actions: a primary Craft and small Refine and Recycle text buttons, each opening the same creature picker and server jobs as before (no API change). `ui/RecipeCard` gained a `compact` mode (no button or reason) to show a recipe inputs and outputs above the picker. The section keeps the id `ws-supplies` that the trip screen scrolls to. Reason: one phone screen instead of two long lists.

## 2026-10-20: A deeper-area find is the deep species 20% of the time (was 35%)

Misa (owner) wanted the deep species to stay a rare find: `RULES.expeditions.familiarity.deepFindBp` 3500 to 2000, so a deeper-area roll is one of the place's deep species 1 time in 5. The 35% in the Familiarity entries above is now 20%. The three lanterns' sure deep creature (`bonusBp`) was raised to keep the economy targets (`docs/balance/log.md`, B-025, "Follow-up: deep species 20%").

## 2026-10-20: The send screen picks supplies from the Workshop grid

Misa asked for the Workshop's compact supply grid on the expedition send screen too. `SupplyPicker` now draws `ui/SupplyGrid` (4 kinds across, 3 tiers down, rarity edges, stock badges) instead of the per-kind rows, and the separate `LurePicker` row is removed (lures are the first column). Tapping packs a supply (a tick; `aria-pressed`), tapping it again removes it, another tier of a packed kind swaps it, and the kit holds at most `RULES.expeditions.maxSupplies` kinds. Supplies with no stock, and (once the kit is full) the kinds not in the kit, are muted and disabled, with the reason in the button label. One card under the grid shows the last tapped supply (blurb and choice mark) and a "Packed:" line lists the kit. The send request is unchanged. `SupplyGrid` gained optional `disabled` and `packed` cell fields; the Workshop use is unchanged. Reason: one phone screen, one picker to learn.

## 2026-10-20: Fusion goes up in points, slowly at the top

Expeditions now bring many more duplicates, and with one level of fusion per duplicate the raid team was fully fused (fusion 5) within two to four weeks for committed players (the creatures study, 8 weeks: a min-maxer's team averaged fusion 4.0 by week 2 and 2.9 of 3 creatures at 5 by week 4; fusion was 13% of fight-scored power and 7% of the Attack + HP score). Misa asked for fusion to be more incremental, with the first levels easy and the last ones much harder, and to stay fairly hidden.

A creature now has **fusion points** (`creatures.fusion_points`, migration 37). Fusing a duplicate adds points by the duplicate's rarity (`RULES.fusion.pointsByRarity`: common 1, rare 2, epic 4; shiny adds nothing extra). Fusion level N is reached at `RULES.fusion.thresholds` cumulative points: 1, 4, 9, 18, 34 (1, 3, 5, 9 and 16 points per level; first set at 1, 3, 6, 11, 19 and steepened the same day, before release, so migration 37 was updated in place). The stat bonus per level is unchanged (`cumulativeBonusBp`, +20% at level 5): the measurement did not show a level worth too much, the cost is what changes. Points past the maximum are not stored; at level 5 Fuse is no longer offered and Recommended goes on to Release, as before. `fusion_count` stays and is always the level those points have reached, so stats, raids and the rest of the game read it as before. Migration 37 gives each creature the points of the level it has (0, 1, 4, 9, 18, 34), so no level drops. A row whose level was set without points reads as the level it has (never lower). Recommended keeps its order (teach, keep, fuse until max, release).

The client shows it quietly: the creature page says "Fusion 2 · 4/6" (level and points toward the next), and the Fuse row says what it gives ("+2 fusion", with "level up" when it reaches the next level). The sim answers duplicates with Recommended as before, adding the duplicate's points. Before and after numbers are in the PR description.

## 2026-10-20: Raids harder for the tick economy; steeper fusion; early gear at a lower level

Misa: "We should make raids harder. Raids shouldn't be a pushover, but neither should they be impossible to win at the early stages." With expedition ticks, the dungeon payout and gradual fusion in, the sim's teams won every raid (typical finale 99.3%, four bosses at 100%, 1.9% of creatures knocked out). Decisions, all through scalars (balance log B-026):

- **Fusion is steeper** (the lead's call, inside Misa's "the last levels a lot harder"): `RULES.fusion.thresholds` cumulative 1, 4, 9, 18, 34 points (1, 3, 5, 9 and 16 per level) instead of 1, 3, 6, 11, 19. Migration 37 is not released, so its hard-coded backfill points changed in place to 0, 1, 4, 9, 18, 34 (and its test): a released migration would have needed a new one.
- **Bosses and the curve.** Boss HP up 15% to 40% per boss (Cinder Wyrm and Thornmother, which stayed at 100%, the most), Attack 33 to 35, rank 1's share of the pool 35% to 26% (so week 1 keeps its absolute HP and stays easy for casual servers), ranks 2 to 5 unchanged (they get tougher through the base HP), ranks 6 and up +9% HP. Result in the sim: typical finale 87%, 15 players 94%, Phase 1 84%, KO share 12.7%, no boss at 0 or 100%, long-run win 67 to 83%.
- **Gear (Misa, 2026-10-20 via the lead):** gear becoming a bigger share of power over time is intended, especially late as players pour resources into crafting. Gear rewards are not cut and gear-share bands should rise over the weeks (the proposal with measured values is in B-026). Early gear stays low-level: expedition finds are at item level 1 per explorer rank (was 2) and dungeon gear gains a level every 3 depths (was 2); crafted gear is unchanged. Week 2 gear share is now near the bands for engaged, regular and drop-in players.
- **Not changed, on purpose:** `creatureBp` stays 800 (about 46 catches a week for a min-maxer is acceptable, Misa 2026-10-20), and the ticks, dungeon payout, supply values and crafting volume are as merged. The targets that encode the old scarce economy (expedition slot days, casual discovery, duplicate counts, late gear shares) are listed as proposed new bands in B-026 for the lead to decide.

## 2026-10-20: Lounging XP fixes after the first production test

Misa and a friend lounged together in production and saw no XP ("My friend said they had to swap their lounging creature to another one in order to start gaining experience"). Then: "Let's say I never actually disconnect from the lounge, just switch tabs… When I return to the game, can we sync the missing intermediate time… Ideally other players who don't know that I'm in another browser tab would see their EXP tick up naturally."

- **The bug: the client skipped its ping while pushes arrived.** The server counted a socket as present only while it pinged (150 s), but the client pinged only after 60 s with nothing received. Two players moving creatures push to each other often, so neither pinged: the server saw both as gone (no company time), and the next push closed their sockets as stale. **Fix:** the client pings every 60 s whatever arrives (`lounge-socket.ts`), which keeps the live room healthy too.
- **Presence moves to Discord's participant list**, which the server already reads with the bot token about once a minute for the lounge message. A hidden tab closes the socket on purpose (to save requests), but Discord keeps the player listed until they leave the Activity, so now a tab switch, a sleeping phone or a dropped connection costs nobody XP, and the others' XP keeps going up. The sockets are only for instant room updates again (the socket presence code of the first version is gone).
- **Paid as it happens, not when you return:** each check credits the minute to everyone together at both ends of it (at most 3 minutes for a late check; never before the day's start); it is paid every 10 minutes, when someone leaves company, at the daily reset (to the old day), and at the start of the player's own request. If the guild's object is evicted, under 10 minutes of unpaid time can be lost. When Discord cannot be asked, the last list stands.
- **Showing it:** the client reloads the state every 2 minutes (one XP) while it earns (someone else here by Discord's list, page visible, not Rested) and when Swap opens. Misa asked why 4: it was to halve requests; 2 minutes shows every step.
- **Trade-off:** someone who leaves the Activity open while away counts as present, as before; the 60-per-creature daily cap bounds it.

## 2026-10-20: The dungeon card is where the team gets ready (the creature configurator), and one gear list

Misa: "I'd like to have the ability to see the gear equipped on my creatures in the dungeon team select… Plan a way for me to be able to do the gear switching and why not even talent switching etc. in a way that the dungeon team composition screen is my 'base of operations'." After four mock-ups (all three at once, the tapped one, the Creatures tab's cards, a tile that opens the page) Misa chose the tapped one: "a lightweight 'creature configurator' where the goal is to allow the user to understand what the creature is and does… and configure it for a task ahead."

- **`CreatureConfigurator`** shows one creature small: the card's top (`CreatureHead`), then the parts the screen switches on, then the screen's buttons. **It is never told where it is used**: the parts are props of their own (`experience`, `explorer`, `crafting`, `stats`, `ability`, `gear`, `gearLocked`, `talents`), so a future task (a crafting job, a raid) picks its own without touching the component. The parts are the page's own pieces (the experience row, the Stats `DataTable`, the gear list, the talent slots and their drawers); it adds no words of its own and few styles (a card the width of a drawer at most, 34rem, so it keeps its proportions on a wide screen). It is a `.pix-panel`; the font audit checks it.
- **On the dungeon card** a tap on a creature of the board shows its configurator under the board (experience, stats, gear, talents; another tap hides it; the creature counts as inspected for the board's cues). Its buttons: **Creature page** (the creature's own page in the card's place, with "‹ Dungeon", the arrows flipping through the team, Remove from team last; evolve opens the evolve screen there too) and **Remove from team**. The Dungeon tab now loads the bag and gets the same creature and gear actions as the Creatures tab (`equipOn`, `createCreatureActions`). Gear is still frozen when a run starts, so this is the pre-run card only.
- **Why the non-pixel font shows up:** the body font is Atkinson Hyperlegible; the pixel font is only on headings, `.pix` and inside a `.pix-panel`. Screens built before the panel rule (the creature page's Stats table, the dungeon card's count and treat lines) fall back to the body font. The new parts are panels; the older screens are a separate sweep.
- **One gear list (`GearList`), option 3 of the three shown** (Misa: "I like option 3 with the gear list"). A creature's gear in full is a row per slot in the find rows' look (`FindRow`: picture, name in the rarity colour, rarity and slot badges, the item's lines under them), on an ink panel. An open, empty slot says "Empty"; a slot not open yet shows the level it opens at. With `onPick` each open slot is one button (its badges then do not open their help: a button cannot hold buttons); without it the list is locked. `emptySlots={false}` lists only what is worn. It replaces the creature page's three slots (`GearSlots`, removed) and the living room popover's worn list (`WornItemLines`, removed); the creature card keeps its small gear icons (a summary), and finds and rewards keep `FindItems`. `FindRow` gained an optional `button`.
- **After Misa saw it:** "the slot showing twice is something that doesn't need to happen on the gear list… But when a dungeon drops an item, or an item is gained in another way, then I think we do want to show what the slot is for it with the tag." So the gear list has no slot badge (the row is the slot; the picture's corner still shows its icon), and finds (`FindItem`: loot, chests, trip finds) gained one. "The tappable and locked rows don't really have to be visually different… it's clear from context", so a tappable row looks like a locked one (no raised edge). A slot not open yet shows a **padlock** in its frame (and its level); an open one shows no padlock, so a row that cannot be tapped says why. The small picture's crowded corners (the slot icon and "Lv" over the item at 3x) went to Misa as a close-up to judge.

## 2026-10-20: The item picker does not move gear off another creature

Misa: "Items in the item picker… give no indication that it's worn on some other creature. This easily leads to players accidentally unequipping items from some creatures when they equip them on others. Maybe disable those items now and somehow indicate someone is wearing them."

- **In the item picker** (the creature page, the configurator), an item another creature wears is **taken**: greyed out like an item the creature cannot wear, with the wearer's small picture at the top right of its tile (bright, not greyed), listed after the free items and before the ones it cannot wear (`equipChoices`). Its details have no gold Equip; they have a plain **"Take from Pebble"** button (Misa: "Let's build it with the 'take from' wording"), which moves it with the same equip call. A mis-tap in the grid only opens the details, and the button names who loses the item. If the creature cannot wear it anyway, only the reason shows.
- **The Workshop bag** shows the wearer's picture instead of the word "Worn" (it may be any creature there); its items stay tappable.
- **The server is unchanged:** an equip still moves an item from its wearer (the Workshop needs it). This is a client guard against a mis-tap, not a rule.

## 2026-10-20: Configurator buttons in one row; Compare against an empty slot

From Misa's staging test on a phone: "The buttons at the bottom are ugly in their positioning and alignment. The remove button could be red." And in the item picker, for an empty slot: "the equip button looks wonky on its own row. Normally there'd be the compare toggle in the bottom left corner… we could 'compare against no gear', resulting in a bunch of green lines."

- **The configurator's buttons** share one row in equal columns, sat in by their ring; a button can be `danger` (the red `button.danger`), and Remove from team is red, on the configurator and at the end of the creature page opened from the dungeon.
- **The item picker always has Compare** beside Equip (not for the worn item itself). For an empty slot it compares against nothing (`diffItems(next, null)`): every stat is a gain and every line is new, so they all show green.

## 2026-10-20: Lounging XP, the evening check

Misa: "People report not getting as much exp as they expect, and even losing some if the game disconnects… I want to make sure our end of the deal is solid." The production report could not help yet (it covers days up to two days back; lounging went live today), so the code was audited for every way our end loses time.

- **Deploys lost up to 10 minutes a player.** Collected time was paid every 10 minutes and lived in the guild object's memory; every deploy restarts the object (and closes every socket, which players see as a disconnect). There were three deploys on the first evening. **Fix:** each XP is paid as soon as it is earned (`LOUNGE_FLUSH_MS` is one XP, 2 minutes), so a restart loses under one XP. Writes stay bounded by the cap (60 payments a creature a day), and a Rested lounger now collects nothing and writes nothing.
- **A tag that seemed to go back a step.** Two background state reloads at once (the 2-minute one and a tab switch) could answer out of order, and the older one landed last. Only the newest reload is applied now (`refreshState` in `Game.tsx`).
- **Not ours, but worth knowing:** "Lounging 34 / 60" starts over at the daily reset (13:37); the XP bar drops at a level-up; a creature waiting at a ribbon or its evolution keeps only the next level's XP, as from any activity; the first minute after someone joins and the last before they leave count for nobody (company is measured at two checks a minute apart); and when Discord drops a player from the Activity (a crash, a reload into a new session), they stop counting until it lists them again.

## 2026-10-21: The configurator on the Raid Roster, gear during a dungeon run, and a font sweep

Misa picked three follow-ups of the configurator work: the raid's team screen, gear during a run, and the screens still in the body font.

- **Raid Roster.** A tap on one of your creatures on the board shows its configurator under the board (experience, stats, gear, talents), with Creature page and a red Remove from board, like the dungeon. Creature page opens the creature's own page in the Raid tab, flipping through your creatures on the board. Once the board is locked for the finale the fight is frozen, so the configurator shows only what the creature wears, locked (no talents, no Remove). Other players' creatures and your own auto tiles keep their popovers. The page-in-a-tab of both screens is one component (`TeamCreaturePage`, with the shared `TeamConfigure`). The raid reads gear when the finale freezes the fight, so a change before then counts.
- **Gear during a run.** The run's view sends each creature's gear as the run froze it (its seat's kit: base, slot, rarity, level and the text of its lines; `runGearView`), so a change on the Creatures tab mid-run never shows as if it counted. A run tile's popover lists it as the locked gear list. A run stored before seats sends an empty list. The popover now shows gear whenever its info carries some (a living room creature, a run creature), not only for a creature with an owner. The client fixtures are captured again.
- **Font sweep.** The creature page, the creature card, the depth track, the dungeon team block and the Raid Roster were built before the pixel-panel rule, so their plain text fell back to the body font. Each is now a `.pix-panel`, and its reading text (the species blurb, personality, finale ability, the roster's help line) is `.prose`. The action button's detail and the evolve stats drop their body-font overrides. The font audit covers all of these screens, the raid configurator included.

## 2026-10-21: Send screen polish (supplies step and the expected-haul summary)

Misa tested the send screen on a phone. The Supplies step title row now has the "?" help tag (the `supplies` glossary entry) and, at the right, a compact "Craft" button with the Workshop (anvil) icon; the paragraph under the title and the "Packed: ..." line are gone (the expected haul lists the kit). A packed supply is shown by the tick only: a new 8x8 `tick` glyph (gold tick on an ink badge) replaces the "✓" character; the card under the grid and the selection ring show only for a packed supply, so taking one out hides both. The item card is now `SupplyCard`, shared with the summary. The expected haul's "A 12 h trip with ..." line became `TripSummary` in the body font: duration, place, explorer, each supply as a dotted button that opens its card in a popover, a treat (a plain read of the send state), when the trip is back in the guild's local time ("Back at 20:15", or with the weekday on another day; never the timezone ID), and "Also in play": today's Boon and Omen when they have trip lines, and the weekly twist when the place is that week's special. Each opens a small popover with its trip lines. Other talent-drawer and raid-timeline ticks are text in other components and were left as they are. Reason: one glance shows everything that affects the trip.

## 2026-10-21: Richer rank gifts, and "Set out with" on the claim screen

Misa asked for two changes on the claim screen.

**Rank gifts** (`RULES.expeditions.rank.gifts`) were tiny (rank 2: 2 Bait and 1 scrap). They are now 10 Bait and 5 scrap at rank 2 and grow from there; from rank 4 a gift adds shards, from rank 5 one gear piece, with rising rarity odds (rank 6 and up rare or better, ranks 9 and 10 with a real epic chance). The full table is in `expeditions-v2.md`. Decisions:

- Shards and the gear piece are of the **explorer's element**, not the destination's. Ranking up is the creature's achievement, and it gives a reason to explore with several creatures. The gear piece uses `rollExpeditionItem` with `{ element: explorer's element, crossroads: false }` at the item level of the rank reached; the rarity is picked from the gift's own odds (`gearOddsBp`, scalars that sum to 10000).
- The gift is still granted once per (creature, rank) through the grant ledger (`rank:<creature>:<rank>`), inside the claim's transaction, with the live gear RNG. A replayed claim returns the stored result and rolls nothing. A full bag turns the piece into its dismantle yield (shown as "did not fit"), like every other grant. The gift appears in analytics as a `gear_drop` of source `rank_gift`.
- The rank track (the ladder in the Expeditions screen) says "3 shards of its element" and "a rare gear piece", since it is for every creature.
- The numbers were set by Misa's brief, not a balance pass. The sim grants the same shards and gear (`rollRankGiftItem`) so the economy targets still see them. See B-027 in `docs/balance/log.md`.

**Set out with.** The claim card shows what the trip started with, before "Brought home": the length, each supply packed (its name opens the supply's glossary card), a treat, the day's Boon and Omen that changed the trip, the weekly twist and the push-on goal. The trip view (`ExpeditionView`) gained `weather` (`{ boon, omen }`, id and name; `null` when no weather was stored: the trip had none, was sent before the weather, or the stored value is damaged). Everything else was already in the view. A trip that was out at deploy shows what it has; absent fields just do not show.

## 2026-10-21: Supplies are explained by tier words and a boost bracket, never by counts

Misa wants supplies explained honestly without exact numbers. Two parts:

- **Tier blurbs** follow one ladder per kind (lures: "Slightly more creatures, and likelier new species" / "More ..." / "Many more ..."; maps: "A better route: slightly more of everything" up to "much more of everything"; lanterns: slight / fair / strong chance of a deeper creature; satchels: "Slightly more parts" up to "Many more parts, glimmer too"). A map adds ticks to every table of a trip (creatures, parts, gear, glimmer, blueprints, supplies, journal, XP), so "everything" is true; the text never says the trip lasts longer. The glossary entries for the four kinds say the same.
- **Boost bracket per category, for the trip being sent.** `expectedTrip` (packages/game `rules/trip-ev.ts`) is now the ONE exact expected-value implementation; the balance simulator (`packages/sim/src/trip-ev.ts`) calls it with its reference trip, so both agree. `tripBoost` (`rules/trip-boost.ts`) returns, per category (creatures, new species, deeper creatures, parts by worth, gear, XP), the relative boost of the kit over the same trip with no supplies. Brackets: below +3% nothing; to +25% "slightly more"; to +60% "more"; to +100% "a lot more"; from +100% "at least double". Deeper creatures a trip would not bring at all without a lantern are measured against the plain trip creatures. The send screen shows the lines on a packed supply's card ("Parts: at least double on this trip") and the Expected haul strip's "+" marks repeat one to four times by bracket. Not modelled: the day's Boon on the strip, Keen nose gear, milestones (small, and they cancel in a ratio). A Small Satchel is "at least double" parts on 2 h and "more" on 12 h: short trips gain most from supplies, which is true to the maths.
- **The Workshop card shows only the blurb** (no trip is chosen there; an assumed trip would mislead).

## 2026-10-21: One line per supply, its strength word anchored to a reference trip

Misa did not like a supply's effect split over several lines on the send screen, and did like blurbs that refer to a reference trip.

- **One line per supply.** The supply card (send screen and Workshop alike) shows only its blurb. The per-category "on this trip" lines are gone. The Expected haul strip's "+" marks stay: they describe the whole kit on the trip being sent, and "at least double with your supplies" there can differ from a blurb word because the blurb speaks for the reference trip.
- **Reference trip** (`REFERENCE_TRIP` next to `tripBoost`): 12 hours at Ember Caves (the economy study's reference place), a payoff explorer, no stars, no twist. A supply's main category is lure creatures, map everything (measured on creatures), lantern deeper-area creatures against the plain trip's creatures, satchel parts (`referenceBoost`).
- **The rule:** a blurb's strength word is the bracket of that boost on the reference trip (slightly more, more, many/much more, double; lanterns: slight, fair, strong, very strong chance). A test in `trip-boost.test.ts` holds every blurb to it, so tuning cannot make the words lie.
- **Rewording, no tuning.** The map and satchel tiers did not spread over the brackets (Inked Map +20%, Trail Atlas +46%; Sturdy Satchel and Explorer's Pack both at least double). Raising the map scalars to separate them broke the economy targets (the 12 hour and day pay bands), so the blurbs were reworded instead: Inked Map "slightly more of everything, and then some", Trail Atlas "more of everything", Small Satchel "More parts", Sturdy "Double parts, sometimes glimmer", Explorer's Pack "Double parts, glimmer too". `MAX_SUPPLY_BLURB` rose from 50 to 60 for the Inked Map.
- **Final wording (Misa).** Maps are worded relative to each other, not to the brackets (the one documented exception, tested as a rising ladder): Map Scrap "A good route: a bit more of everything", Inked Map "A great route: more of everything", Trail Atlas "A fantastic route: much more of everything". Satchels: Sturdy "Double parts with a tiny chance of glimmer", Explorer's Pack "Triple parts and a small chance of glimmer" (glimmer is a chance, never promised: tested); "triple" is allowed only from +200% on the reference trip (`TRIPLE_FROM_BP`). "And then some" is gone.

## 2026-10-21: "Pack a treat" is a pixel switch row

Misa asked for a better treat box, then reviewed the screenshots: no gold edge when packed, keep the "2x", and no "Not enough Bait" line outside the row.

- **One row, one switch.** `TreatBox` is now a `ui/Switch` (the whole row is the tap target, the knob slides): the switch, the treat icon (grey until packed), "Pack a treat" with "for Cindle" under it (one line, a long name ends in "…"), then the multiplier chip ("2x") and the Bait cost. Packed shows as the lit switch and icon and the words going from haze to bone; the row keeps its quiet edge. The "?" stays beside the row. `Switch` gained `spoken`, `class` and `children` for this.
- **Short of Bait.** The cost's number is red. The switch is not disabled: a tap the player cannot afford shakes the row's second line and puts "Not enough Bait" there (red) for 3 seconds (`SHORT_NOTE_MS`); a second tap restarts it. Screen readers hear "not enough Bait" in the switch's name and the note through a status line. A real `disabled` (an action running) still disables it.
- **The dungeon team** lists its three boxes under one "Treats" heading with the only "?", and the Bait the packed treats leave ("12 left", counting down as treats are packed, since the three share one purse). Each row then says only the creature's name (`listed`), and the rows span the card like the Enter button.
- **Multiplier.** Each forecast day now carries `treatTimes` (`dayView`: the usual 2, or the 3 or 4 a Boon such as Hearty Breakfast sets). Trips, Workshop jobs and the dungeon all freeze the day's weather when they start, so today's number is the one that will be paid. `Game` provides it through `TreatTimesContext`, and every `TreatBox` shows it (a `times` prop still overrides it, for the workbench). Without a forecast yet the box says the usual 2x.
- **One box for the dungeon's treats** (Misa, same day): the three rows share one ink box split by thin lines, each a little shorter, with room before the Enter button.
- **The send screen** (Misa, 2026-10-21): the sentence under the treat box ("This trip costs 7 Bait for the treat, and takes … from your stock" / "Free · This trip costs nothing") is gone: the treat row shows its cost and the haul summary names the supplies and the treat. A packed treat's Bait is a badge on the Send button instead, the one clear button with the cost. "Choice likely / guaranteed" moved from under the treat box into the Expected haul icons (see the next line). The supply's own card keeps its mark. More room above Send.
- **The Expected haul** (Misa, same day; he picked the bar from two versions): each icon sits in a box of one height so the grid lines up; under its word is a four-segment bar in the boss HP bar's look, gold for what the trip gives on its own and green for what the supplies add (any help at all shows at least one green step, since a basic supply often moves less than a quarter). The "+" marks are gone. "Choice" is the last icon with the same bar: the chance of an end-of-trip choice (`offerChanceBp`: base, rank, supplies; the milestone bonus is not known on the send screen) in quarters, rounded up. 16 px from the treat row to the heading (was 30).

## 2026-10-21: The bag may overflow; a full bag warns where you set off

Misa found a dungeon find turned into parts because his bag was full, with no warning beforehand. It felt like a loss the game never explained. The new rule:

- **A reward that arrives while the bag is under the cap puts every item in**, even past the cap (58 + 4 = 62 / 60). The choice is made once per grant, before any of its items go in (`grantOnce`, `fits`).
- **A reward that arrives while the bag is full** (items plus crafts still to claim at or over the cap) turns its gear into the dismantle yield, as before (`converted`).
- **A dungeon run decides at its start** (`RunState.bagFull`): the boss chest on the way and the run's end follow the bag as it was when the run began, so a chest that fills the bag never costs the end of the same run. A state stored before has no flag and decides at each grant.
- **A finished craft always goes in.** It held its place when it started, and a grant may have filled the bag past the cap meanwhile. The old "cannot happen" refusal at the claim is gone, so a paid craft is never stuck.
- Starting a craft and the dev tools' item grant still refuse when the bag is full: those are deliberate actions.
- **The warning** (`ui/BagFullNote`): where the player sets off (the dungeon's Enter, the send screen, the raid board while it can change) a gold-edged line says "Your bag is full (62 / 60). Gear you find becomes parts." with a Workshop button. It never blocks. The Workshop's bag count turns red at or over the cap, with one line under it. The results line now names the reason: "Your bag was full, so Bog Charm became parts."
- Expeditions and the raid decide at the grant (their claim or finale), since there is no single start state to stamp. The warning is still right: while the bag is full, gear found becomes parts.

## 2026-10-21: At the camp a tap shows the creature; only a drag moves it

Misa, play-testing the camp: tapping a creature to see it (what it wears) and then tapping another swapped the two, and he felt he lost control. Tap-to-swap is gone.

- **A tap on a camp tile opens the creature's popover**: role, element, what it does in this order, and the gear it wears in this run (locked, frozen when it joined). A recruit that has not joined yet shows no gear. A second tap closes it, and a drag starting closes it too.
- **Only a drag moves a creature** (a finger needs the short hold, so a swipe still scrolls). The copy reads "Pick one. Drag a creature onto another to swap places."
- **Keyboard reordering at the camp is gone with it** (Enter on two creatures was the same tap). Misa accepts this: accessibility is nice where it is cheap, and the UI is heading towards a more game-like look.
- The same rule now holds on every board: a tap shows (the dungeon team's configurator, the raid board's configurator or popover, the camp's popover), a drag moves.
- Tests drag for real in the fake DOM: `test-drag.ts` gives tiles a layout and a window for the move and release listeners. The fake DOM gained `closest` and `dataset`.

## 2026-10-21: The run strip's pack is a backpack, and the keepsakes get their own panel

Misa: the "In your pack" line said what you carry, but not visually that it can be lost; the fonts were wrong, "At risk" sat out of line, and "Keepsakes ? Chalk Mark" read as one jumbled line.

- **Two ink panels under the row** (`.run-ledger`): **Pack** and **Keepsakes**, side by side when both fit (`minmax(15rem, 1fr)`), one under the other on a phone. Each has a small title in the pixel font.
- **The pack is a pixel backpack** (`sprites/pack-art.ts`, 16 x 16 at scale 3) with the red "At risk" tag hung under it (it still opens the pack's glossary entry), and the loot as count badges beside it. An empty pack shows the backpack without the tag and "Empty".
- **Keepsakes**: the title with its "?", the chips under it. The results screen uses the same block.
- **The whole strip is a pixel panel** (`.pix-panel`); the last mystery room's sentence stays in the body font (`.prose`), as reading text does.
- An alternative look (the whole pack panel drawn as leather, the loot in a pocket, the tag at the corner) was mocked for Misa to choose from; the ink panel is the default because it matches every other panel.

## 2026-10-21: One Alert component for notices, warnings and errors; the pack rolls out of the backpack

**Alerts** (`ui/Alert`, Misa's spec). Four levels, each with its colour and its own 8 x 8 pixel icon: info (blue "i"), success (green tick), warning (gold triangle), alert (red stop sign). An optional bold title in the level's colour, then the message in the body font, inline after the title or on its own line (`layout`). `onDismiss` adds an X on the right.

- **Where the X sits.** The icon and the X are in boxes exactly one line of text tall at the top of the alert, so with one line both are centred on it and with several they stay in the top corners. CSS does it; nothing is measured.
- **Dismissing** slides the alert 1.5 rem to the right while it fades (140 ms, 4 steps), then folds its height shut (`grid-template-rows` 1fr to 0fr) by 220 ms, and only then calls `onDismiss`. Under reduced motion it goes at once.
- `role="alert"` only at the alert level; the rest are `role="status"`, so a notice never interrupts a screen reader.
- **The overhaul** (Misa: the existing alert-like messages go too): the game's notice line (daily Bait, renames, talents, catch results) is a dismissible success alert, the game's error line a dismissible alert, and every `p.error role="alert"` across the screens (loading failures, refused actions) is an alert without an X. The full bag warning is a warning alert with its Workshop button. The Workshop's one-off "Gear has changed" card, the `.reason` refusal lines and the gold Callout for good news stay as they are: they are part of their screens, not alerts.
- The notice and error alerts take their text as `key`, so a new message shows again after the last one was dismissed.

**The pack** (Misa chose the leather look and asked for more): the backpack (now at scale 4) sits on the left and rises above its box, and the leather canvas (`--leather` tokens) rolls out from under it to the right, holding the "Pack" title with "At risk" beside it and the loot, so the loot reads as the pack's contents.

## 2026-10-21: The keepsakes get a locket and a velvet canvas; the canvases reach further under the picture

Misa's next round on the run strip: the leather should start at least from the backpack's middle, and the keepsakes should get the same treatment with a picture of their own.

- **One layout for both** (`.run-stash`): a 16 x 16 picture at scale 4 breaking out of the top of its box, and a canvas that reaches 3.25 rem in under the 4 rem picture, so it starts well left of the picture's middle. The colours come from three variables per canvas: leather for the pack, velvet (`--velvet` tokens, a deep plum) for the keepsakes.
- **The keepsakes' picture is a gold locket** on a ring with a cut red gem (`LOCKET_ART` in `sprites/pack-art.ts`): small treasures kept for this run only. The results screen's keepsakes use the same block.
- **The success icon's tick** was redrawn: the long arm one pixel longer and the whole tick a row higher, so it sits in the middle of the disc instead of on its bottom edge.

- **Follow-up the same day (Misa):** the pictures no longer rise above their boxes. Each is centred on its canvas's height, the canvases stretch to the taller one's height when they sit side by side (the grid row), and neither is shorter than its picture. The drop shadow under the pictures is gone, and the locket is a narrower, taller oval (10 x 11 pixels), since the old one read as stretched.
- **Second follow-up (Misa):** 10 pixels was too narrow; the locket is now 11 x 11, drawn whole (an odd width cannot be mirrored from a half). The keepsake chips are as tall as the pack's resource badges (1.375 rem, with a finger-sized target around them), and their edge is the keepsakes' own purple (`--velvet-glow`) instead of gold, to keep the velvet identity.
- **Third follow-up (Misa):** the pack's red "At risk" tag is gone. The pack has the same "?" as the keepsakes, which opens the pack's glossary entry (what goes in it, and that a defeat keeps half). The camp's "Head home" card and the descent card keep their own "at risk" wording.
- **Fourth follow-up (Misa):** the canvases are full width again, so their left edge lines up with everything else in the strip, and the pictures spill out past that edge instead (`--spill`: 0.75 rem for the backpack, 1.25 rem for the locket, whose drawing starts two pixels further in). Both canvases keep the same text edge. When the pack and the keepsakes sit side by side (a container query on `.run-ledger`, two columns from 30.5 rem), the locket stays inside its canvas so it never reaches over the pack; on its own row it spills like the backpack.
- **Final (Misa):** after the spill-out experiments, the pictures sit fully inside their full-width canvases everywhere, as the locket did beside the pack. No spill, no container query. Simpler, and it keeps every edge on the grid.

## 2026-10-21: The Trips view is a world map

Misa approved the world-map design for the Expeditions tab (mockups MapPhone / MapDesktop). The Trips view now shows a pixel map with a marker per place instead of the catalogue and the send screen; Journal, Profile and every other tab are unchanged.

- **All places are open on the map.** Every marker can be tapped. The explorer rank a place needs stays a per-creature limit, shown in the explorer picker (greyed with the reason). Time rules stay: Moonlit Reef by day and the Festival Grounds outside Festival weeks show their rule as a line on the marker and in the place's sheet or panel, and Send is disabled with "Opens tonight at 20:00".
- **Phone (under 60 rem): sheet and wizard.** Map, then a strip of chips for the trips that are out. A marker or a chip opens a `ui/Drawer` place sheet (banner, what the place says, the explorers there as rows, a gold "Send an explorer here"). Sending is a drill-in wizard in the same sheet (Who goes, Pack up, Ready) with the pinned Next / Send button; Back and Escape go one step back, from Who back to the place. "Decide" opens the push-on choice as a drill-in. After a started send the sheet closes and the new trip is on the map. A claim closes the sheet and the reveal shows above the map as before.
- **Desktop (60 rem and up): panel.** Map and the "Out exploring" shelf on the left, a 24 rem side panel on the right: "Where to?" until a place is picked, then the place and the whole send form with one Send button. Picking a place does not move focus (a live region says which place). A claim's reveal shows in the panel (the page only scrolls if the panel is out of view), and Decide shows the push-on choice in the panel with a Back.
- **The app card widens only for this view**: `main.card:has(.exp-world)` is up to 84 rem from 60 rem (`styles/views/expeditions/world.css`); Journal, Profile and every other tab keep 48 rem. An app-wide wide frame (top bar with tabs and currencies) is later work (roadmap).
- **The full bag warning** (`ui/BagFullNote`, from the bag overflow change) sits beside Send in both layouts: on the wizard's Ready step and at the panel's bottom.
- **Map positions are client-only**, like the room's `ROOM_SPOTS`: the server knows nothing about where a place sits on the map.
- **Selection is state in `ExpeditionWorld`**; the wide or narrow layout comes from the same media query as the CSS (`WIDE_QUERY`).
- Removed: `SendTrip`, `Catalogue`, `RunningTrip` (the per-trip cards), `turnCatalogue` and the catalogue styles. `PlaceInfo` stays, its styles are in `place.css`.

## 2026-10-21: the step-by-step wizard is its own component, not part of the Drawer

Misa likes the phone send flow (Who goes, Pack up, Ready, with Back / Next / Send), so it is now one reusable piece, `ui/Wizard` with `ui/use-wizard`, that any later drawer can import. It is separate from `ui/Drawer` on purpose: today it is always shown inside a drawer, but the drawer is only where it is placed. The wizard has three parts that work apart: `useWizard` (the position and the moves: next, back, goTo, reset, and `onExit` for Back on the first step), `WizardView` (the head with Back and the title, and the step's content; Escape goes one step back) and `WizardFooter` (the always-visible reason and the gold button, with slots for a rule line, a warning and a badge). A drawer puts head and body in its content and the footer in its `footer` slot, so the button stays pinned. The caller keeps the validation (the footer takes `blocked`, a reason or null), so the wizard knows nothing about trips. The send flow was moved onto it with no change to its look, wording, focus or request; later improvements to the wizard apply everywhere. The desktop panel (no steps) reuses `WizardFooter` without a wizard, as a finish button alone.

## 2026-10-21: "Equip on" shows what is already worn

Misa: the Workshop item drawer's "Equip on" list did not show which creatures already have something in the slot.

- **A small item tile at the right end of the row** (`ui/RosterList`, `.ro-worn`): the creature card's own `ItemThumb` (rarity frame, item level in the corner) at scale 2, after any reason ("Away on a trip" stays beside it). It reads at a glance as "slot taken", and shows which item.
- **An empty slot draws nothing.** The tile is the signal; a dashed "empty" box on most rows would make the list noisier and say less. The creature that wears this very item shows it too, next to "Wears it now".
- **Client only.** `PickerOption.worn` is filled by `equipOptions` from `Creature.gear` (the state view already carries base, rarity and item level of each worn item), matched on the item's slot. No server change.
- The tile has `role="img"` and the label "Wearing <item>, item level N, <rarity>".
- **Round 2 (Misa, 2026-10-21):** busy reasons are the creature card's status badges, not text ("On a job" `badge busy`, "Away" `badge away` with "on a trip" kept for screen readers; `PickerOption.busy` is set where the reason is made, other reasons such as "Needs level 12" stay text). The list is a `pix-panel` (pixel font for names, levels, notes and reasons; the effect line stays `.prose`), names truncate with an ellipsis.
