The Equip on list has no Recommended tag and no "On the board" note (those are the raid picker's); the workbench "slot taken" demo now matches the real drawer.

## 2026-10-21: A found item opens its item card (tap, or hover with a mouse)

Misa asked to inspect an item where it is found, ideally on hover on a computer.

- **`FindItem` does it itself**, so every screen that pays gear gets it (dungeon end screen, trip result, raid summary, other grants via `LootList`). The row becomes one button that opens the full `ItemCard` in the shared page popover (`host.openCustom`). Tap and Enter/Space open it, a second tap or Escape closes it. Hover (a mouse or pen, and only when `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` matches) opens it and leaving closes it; a click while it is open from hover pins it. A hover never replaces another open popover.
- **Where the data comes from.** A grant's gear carries only base, rarity and level; the card's stats and lines are the server's and are not worked out in the client. `Game` therefore provides the gear view's items as `GearItemsContext`, and `FindItem` takes an optional `id` and looks the item up. No id (a boss chest's banked gear on the descent card and node drawer), the gear view not loaded yet, or the item since dismantled: the row stays plain, as before, with its badges tappable.
- **Badges inside the button are plain** (not tappable), as in the gear list, so no button sits inside a button. The card's own badges are plain too.
- **An Equip button** (Misa, 2026-10-21) sits centred under the card in that popover, as wide as its label. It closes the popover and opens the creature sheet (`RosterPicker`, opened from a page, which the rule allows) with the Workshop's "Equip on" rows (`equipOptions`), and a pick sends the Workshop's equip action (`gearActions.equip`, server decides). `Game` provides it as `EquipContext` through `EquipHost` (`equip-from-card.tsx`), which also draws the sheet; without creatures or outside a host there is no button. The Workshop drawer does not show it (it has its own drill-in). So a mouse can reach the button, a hover-opened popover now closes 200 ms after the mouse leaves the row, unless it has entered the popover.

## 2026-10-21: The Blue Chasm, a rare mystery room with a treasure shown first

Misa asked for a rare mystery room: a plank over a chasm, a valuable treasure on the other side, 50/50 to cross or the whole team falls and the run ends. He wanted it kept at 50/50 with a full defeat, and made tempting by the prize. It is `blue-chasm` in `dungeon-events.json`, lore in `docs/compendium/dungeon.md`.

- **The room.** "Cross the plank" is a gamble (`chanceBp: 5000`, in the content, so it can be tuned): win takes the treasure, lose is a `fall`. "Don't risk it" does nothing. Two new event effects: `treasure` (take the treasure the room rolled) and `fall` (every creature at 0 HP, the run ends as a defeat: half the pack kept, `RULES.dungeon.defeatKeepBp`). A `fall` counts as a cost for the "every event has a free option" rule.
- **How rare.** An event may carry `rare: { chanceBp, fromDepth }`. A rare event is never drawn onto a map (the day's map is shared, the rare room is the player's own dice). Each time a run steps into a mystery room it may turn into a rare event: 15% (`chanceBp: 1500`), from depth 3, at most once a run (`rareSeen` in the run state). A run walks about one mystery room, so about one run in six to seven at depth 3 or more meets it (the first depths never do). Drawn live in `applyMove`, not at the map, so it costs nothing in the map code and the day's maps and the simulator's maps are unchanged. The simulator does not play it (no rare room on its maps).
- **The treasure** is rolled when the room opens (`rollTreasure` in `rules/dungeon-treasure.ts`, the live gear rng), stored in the run's pending state and shown in the room before the choice (the actual gear piece, the blueprint base, the supplies, the shards). Reloading cannot reroll it. One kind is drawn evenly among those that can apply to this player:
  - an **Epic gear piece** at the item level of the run's depth (`rollDungeonItem`), tagged with the element of one team creature;
  - a **blueprint** the player does not know, one tier above their best. A base's tier is its `minItemLevel` (1, 5, 10, and the gear sets' 12). The pool is the unknown craftable bases of the nearest tier above the best known one; with nothing unknown above, the highest unknown tier. Not a candidate when they know every blueprint;
  - **two great supplies** of one kind (never a lure);
  - **6 shards** (two per team creature of a team of three, of its element) and **4 glimmer** (`RULES.dungeon.treasure`; round 2 raised it from 3 and 2, Misa, 2026-10-21).
  - Not in the table yet: **a creature of a species the player lacks.** Granting a creature from a run needs its own path (collection rules, first talent, server firsts, duplicate card, a creature find on the end screen), so it is a follow-up.
- **Delivery.** A won treasure is stored in the run (`treasures`) and paid at the run's end by the run's own grant (`dungeon:<day>:<attempt>`), so it is paid once and a retry returns the stored result. A run that ends in a defeat wins none of it: an item cannot be halved, so unlike the pack it is all or nothing. The end screen shows a won treasure as finds (gear, blueprint, supplies, shards) and a lost one as "Treasure lost". The fall itself takes no treasure.
- **Round 2 (Misa, 2026-10-21): inspect the prize first.** The Epic piece is fully rolled when the room opens (base, element tag, level, lines) and kept in the run's state; the room's event view carries its `card` (`previewItemView` in `gear-access.ts`, built by the same code as the Workshop's cards, so the client computes nothing), validated by the client parse. Tapping or hovering the treasure row opens the full `ItemCard` with no Equip (`FindItem` takes `card`; a `card` is never equippable, only an owned `id` is). The grant at the end pays that stored item, not a re-roll. The card is built when the view is made, so a waiting room saved before this change shows the card too; if its base is gone from the content the row is simply plain. Shards prize raised to 6 shards and 4 glimmer. The 'creature you don't have' prize is deferred, very low priority (`docs/roadmap.md`).
- **Old runs.** A saved run without `treasures` and `rareSeen` loads as having none. A mystery room waiting from before the change has no treasure and answers as before.
- **Server side checks.** The event is picked by the server, the odds and the treasure are rolled by the server with live randomness, and the client sends only the option (0 or 1) as for every event.

## 2026-10-21: The dungeon pays experience per room a creature stood in, and the end screen recaps it

Misa: "experience should be paid per fight that a creature was in, and the dungeon end screen should show a recap and exp gain for each creature that earned experience... higher for harder fights, a bit for cache rooms but not as much as fights, and as an effect in mystery rooms."

- **The rule** (`RULES.experience.dungeon`, B-028): every creature that stood in the row for a room earns its amount, by kind: fight 32, elite 48, epic (Undercroft) 58, keeper (Undercroft) 58, floor boss 105, cache 10. A lost fight pays nothing, as before. Depth adds nothing. The treat and the day's Boons and Omens multiply the creature's total once at the end (`dungeonExperience(earned, parts)`), so the xp breakdown chip still reads the same.
- **No separate full-clear bonus.** It folds into the boss's amount (35 + 60 = 95 before, 105 now). It only went to the row that beat the boss, which is the row that fought the boss, so the same creatures are paid; the recap shows one "boss" entry instead of a bonus nobody can see. A creature sent home at a camp can never be paid for a boss it did not fight.
- **Numbers** came from the balance sim over 840 runs (B-028): a typical full clear pays +6% (the shortest +15%), a defeat -1% for the fights it played, against today. The first try (30 / 45 / 55 / 55 / 95 / 10) paid 2% and 7% less, so every number was raised a little.
- **Run state.** `RunState.tally` (fights cleared per creature) became `RunState.earned` (per creature: the base total plus counts of fights, elites, epics, keepers, bosses, caches and events that paid). Counts are for the recap; the total is stored as paid, so a rules change mid-run never changes what a creature already earned. A run stored with the old `tally` is read by `parseState`, which prices each fight as a plain fight and one as the boss for a creature in `fullClear` (`earnedFromTally`). Runs are per day and stale ones are closed by the sweeps, so only a run open during the deploy ever takes that path.
- **Mystery rooms** have a new plain effect `xp` (`{ op: 'xp', amount }`, 1 to 60, also allowed inside a gamble branch): every standing creature (not a knocked-out one) earns that amount before the treat and weather. Old Shrine's "Bow and walk on" gives 20 and Mushroom Ring's dance, when it wins, 25. The event description says the exact amount (a content test holds every description to its numbers).
- **The recap.** `DungeonRewards.recap` (`DungeonExperienceRecap`: creature id, name and species id; the counts and base were dropped from the payload the same day) sits next to `experience` (what was paid after boosts and its level changes). It names the creature, so one sent home at a camp, who is not in `team`, can be listed. Only creatures that earned something are paid and listed. The end screen's "Experience" list is one row for each (the shared `XpGain` row) with a short line under the bar ("4 fights, 1 cache, boss"; kinds that did not pay are left out) and a "Went home" tag on a creature not in the row at the end. A run stored before the recap shows its team's rows without the line.
- **Round 2 (Misa): no reason line, just the experience.** The "4 fights, 1 cache, boss" line under each bar is gone (`recapLine`, the `note` prop of `XpGain` / `ExperienceGainRow` and `.xp-note` were removed). The recap in the result keeps only who earned (id, name, species, so a creature sent home at a camp can be listed); run state still counts rooms per creature.
- **Not changed:** treats cost, the level-up and ribbon rules, the lost-fight rule, who stands in the row (a creature knocked out still stood in it for a cache and a won fight, as before).

## 2026-10-21: the Workshop's supply jobs are one wizard in a drawer; level limits may fold in job pickers

Craft, Refine and Recycle on the supply card now open one drawer, `JobDrawer`, built on the shared Wizard (`ui/Wizard`). The recipe (in, arrow, out, time) sits once at the top, pinned in the drawer's toolbar slot, and the "?" help is beside the title. Step 1, Who crafts: creatures who can do the job first (best crafter first, each with its time), busy ones at the level greyed with "On a job" / "Away on a trip", and the creatures under the job's Crafting level folded into one greyed row ("4 more creatures need Crafting level 3") that expands on a tap. Step 2, Ready: who and their time, and the treat box. The footer is the gold start ("Craft", "Start refine", "Start recycle", with the Bait badge for a packed treat) and always says why it is off. Back on Who and Escape on Who close the drawer; Escape elsewhere goes one step back. The start call is the same as before (same request, same idempotency key). A failed start keeps the drawer open and shows the server's error in the footer: the game's own error line sits behind the drawer, so `Game` passes its `error` down through `Workshop` and `SupplyCrafting`, and the drawer shows it only after its own start failed.

This is an exception to "pick lists show everyone" (ux-patterns.md): under-level creatures are folded, not listed one by one, because a Workshop roster is long and most of it is under level 3 for a good while. They are still one tap away, and busy ones are never hidden. The old silent grey Start (everyone at the level busy, a lower one free) is gone: with nobody who can do it the footer says "Nobody free has Crafting level 3.", or the slot and stock reasons the start check already had. `JobStarter` (the old inline panel) had no other user and was removed; gear crafting followed on 2026-10-22 (below).

- **The fold row is a quiet divider, `ui/FoldRow`.** It is not a boxed pick row and has no chevron (the first version's was oversized): the label is small, muted and centred, with long pixel dashes (4 units, a gap of 2) running out to both edges, so it reads as a divider. Open or closed is `aria-expanded`, and the revealed rows are the other cue. It is still a real button with a 44 px tall hit area and the app's focus ring. Generic on purpose (label, open, toggle, the id of what it folds), so other lists can use it.

The Wizard itself needed no change: the recipe fits the Drawer's existing toolbar slot, the error fits the footer's `extra` slot, and `initial` and `onExit` covered the start step and "Back closes". `PickRow` was split out of `CreaturePicker` so the job step and the gear pickers share one row.

- **Gear crafting uses the same drawer (2026-10-22).** Craft on a blueprint card opens `JobDrawer` (`craft-job.ts` builds the job); `CraftStarter` is gone. The pinned recipe is the plain craft's parts for the best crafter, as the card lists them, and the Kelp Cap as the output. Steps: Who crafts (the blueprint's own `needsCraftingLevel`, under-level creatures folded), **How hard do you reach?** (plain, Rare, Epic with the server's odds, time and cost for the picked creature via `useJobPreview`; Details has item level and step-up chance), Ready (who, the final time, the chosen reach, the treat). `useJobWizard` gained an optional `spec.craft`: it adds the Reach step, loads the preview, takes the cost, Essence and time from the chosen reach and puts `reach` on the request; supply jobs are unchanged (their request carries `reach: 'none'`, ignored). Past Who the footer says "Loading the odds..." or "The odds did not load. Go back and pick again." instead of a silent grey button; a reach that costs more Essence or parts than held is selectable and the footer says what is short. Decision: no `BagFullNote` on Ready. A craft with a full bag cannot start, so the footer's existing "The bag is full." blocks from the first step (the wizard cannot reach Ready), and the note's "gear you find becomes parts" would be misleading there.
- **Dismantling is a wizard too (2026-10-22).** The Workshop item drawer's Dismantle opens `DismantleWizard` (replaces `DismantlePanel`) as a drill-in in the place of the item, never a second drawer. Step 1, Who dismantles, is the job wizard's `JobWhoStep` (free creatures first with their time, busy greyed with the reason; it got `legend` and `groupName` props, defaults unchanged). Dismantling has no Crafting level rule, so `jobWho` runs with minimum 0 and nothing is folded; a dismantle job takes no treat. Step 2 is the confirmation: the worker with the server's time, the yields as badges, the blueprint chance, "Comes off X first", and "Cannot be undone." above the gold "Dismantle". The server preview is asked only when step 2 opens. Back and Escape are the safe exit (from step 1 back to the item, with the Dismantle button focused). The old "Keep it" button is gone; instead the focus moves to Back when the confirmation opens, so a second Enter on the button that said "Next" cannot dismantle. The request and idempotency key are unchanged and success behaves as before (the item leaves, the job appears). A refused start now keeps the confirmation up and shows the server's error there (before, the panel just closed): `Game` passes `error` through `Workshop` to `ItemDetail`. Flipping pauses while the wizard is shown, like Equip on. The gold button is sticky at the bottom of the drawer body, because the item drawer has no footer slot.

### 2026-10-22: The Wizard branches, and the dungeon camp runs on it

Misa wants every step-by-step flow on the one shared Wizard, so improving it improves them all. The camp was the odd one out: its menu leads to different step sequences (keepsake, or the Swap chain: who comes in, who goes home, arrange). The Wizard stayed a straight line, so the camp hand-built its steps with `DrillHead`.

**API (backward compatible; the send flow and the Workshop job wizard are untouched):** one list holds every step of every branch, and a `WizardStep` may carry `back` (where Back goes; default the step before), `forward` (where `next` goes; default the step after) and `final` (whether the forward button here is the finish; default the last in the list). A pick opens a branch with `wizard.goTo(id)`. We chose static per-step links over a list the caller swaps, because `goTo` then works in the same render as the branch choice (a swapped list only exists on the next render). A changing list also works: the current step is kept by id, and when its id is gone the wizard shows the first step. `WizardView` and `WizardHead` gained `disabled` (Back and Escape off while a request is out). `canBack` is now "has a back target or an `onExit`".

**Camp:** `DungeonCamp` holds its position in `useWizard` (menu, keepsake, out with `back: 'menu'`, arrange). The menu stays drawn by the card with no head, as before; the other steps are a `WizardView` (level 4, keyed by step so the focus goes to Back on every step, as the hand-built steps did). Decided without Misa: (1) the camp keeps its own "Swap and push on" button inside the card instead of `WizardFooter`, because the footer is the pinned gold bar of drawers and would change what the player sees; (2) going back from "who goes home" no longer clears the chosen creature, since the only way in is the picker, which sets it again. Screenshots of every camp step, the send wizard and the job drawer at 390 px match the old ones.

## 2026-10-21: One creature list for every creature pick

- **One component, `ui/CreatureList`** (the old `RosterList`, renamed and widened). The fixed row is sprite, name and level in the pixel font; the caller turns on the rest: element and role chips (`chips`, default on), the XP track, Rested, Recommended, the worn item tile, a quiet `note`, the effect preview, and a reason for a row that cannot be picked (the status badge for a busy creature, text otherwise; under the name or, with `reasonSide`, at the right). Two typed slots cover what only one screen has: `side` (right-end detail of a pickable row, a job's clock badge) and `more` (a block under the name, the explorer's rank, XP bar and familiarity). Misa asked for one list that is configurable by props, so the presentation and the keyboard behaviour stay the same everywhere.
- **Two selection modes.** `pick` is the listbox of before (roving tabindex, Up, Down, Home, End, Enter or a tap picks and the view closes): the raid and dungeon sheet, the camp swap, the lounge swap and the Equip on drill-in. `choose` is native radios in a fieldset: the choice stays, disabled radios for rows that cannot be chosen. It is the job worker and the expedition explorer. Native radios stay because they give arrow-key and screen reader behaviour for free.
- **One container rule** (also in `ux-patterns.md`): from a page or a small control, the shared sheet `RosterPicker`; inside a drawer, a drill-in view of the same drawer; a big page-level decision (send a trip, start a job) may show the list inline.
- **Migrated.** `CreaturePicker` (job worker) and `ExplorerPicker` (Who goes) are now thin mappers from their data to list rows; their own row markup and the `label.pick` creature rows are gone. The explorer's rank track stays under the list as its own piece. The lounge swap already used the sheet. What shows changed only a little: the worker rows now also show the creature level, a busy worker's reason is the status badge ("On a job", "Away") like everywhere else, and the explorer's "Needs explorer rank N" reason moved from the rank line to under the name. The job and explorer rows pass `chips={false}` to keep what they showed; turning chips on is one prop.
- **Workbench.** The `CreatureList` page has a switch for every part and both modes, with presets that match the lounge swap, the job worker and the explorer.
- **Round 2 (Misa, same day).** The explorer rows lose the "Explorer rank N" line (the rank bar row says it; the "Needs explorer rank N" reason for a blocked row stays under the name) and turn the element and role chips on, because places will have element bonuses; job worker rows keep chips off. Rows have more vertical padding (0.75 rem top and bottom, every mode). Every name in `CreatureList` is in its species rarity colour (`rarity-name rarity-<rarity>`, as on the creature card, with the rarity said aloud by a visually hidden word; an action row like Take off has none), so the lounge swap rows have it too; "Lv N" stays the quiet haze text, so the rarity name is the one coloured thing in the row (gold is the selection ring and the XP track's level box). The "?" of `TreatBox` is centred between the switch row and the container edge (0.5 rem each side) with the row itself the same width and place; the send screen, which uses the same box with a "?", gets the same centring, and the dungeon setup (no "?") is unchanged.
- **Standing rule: what a creature list shows (Misa, 2026-10-21).** Every row always shows the picture, the name in its rarity colour and the level (quiet grey). Beyond that, only what the player needs to choose well for the activity the creature is picked for, the bare minimum (crafting level and job time for a job; explorer rank, familiarity, element and role for an expedition; the worn item for Equip on). It differs per activity; new uses decide case by case and raise it with Misa when unsure. Not set in stone. Also in `ux-patterns.md`.
- **Merged with the wizards (#58, 2026-10-22).** Who crafts (`JobWhoStep` of `JobWizard`) and Who dismantles (`DismantleWizard`) draw their rows through `CreatureList` too; `PickRow` and its CSS are gone. `chips={false}`, `note` "Crafting level N", `side` the job's time, `reasonSide`, busy rows get the status badge, under-level rows keep "Needs level N" as text. The fold of #58 is a `fold` prop of `CreatureList` (`choose` mode): after the list it draws the `FoldRow` and, when open, a second list of the folded rows inside the same fieldset with the same radio name, so the radios stay one group and the arrow keys and screen readers work across the split. The camp swap already used `RosterPicker` (a `CreatureList`). The Ready step's single creature line is a summary, not a pick, and keeps its own small markup.
- **Every crafting pick shows the same row** (Misa, 2026-10-22): Who dismantles keeps "Crafting level N" and the job time even though every dismantle takes the same time today. Dismantling raises Crafting, so a player may pick a low-level creature on purpose, and the time can come to scale with level or effects; the whole crafting screen stays consistent. The `FoldRow` label uses the pixel font (`var(--display)`), like the rows around it.

## 2026-10-22: The Sealed Vault, a rare mystery room that marks the fights ahead, and a generic room badge on the map

Misa asked for a mystery room whose bad-ish answer makes the next fight epic with double rewards, and for a generic way to show such an effect on the map ("a generic modifier icon that can be attached to a room ... it could show 'x2'"). It is `sealed-vault` in `dungeon-events.json`; full rules in `docs/design/dungeon-paths.md` ("The Sealed Vault"), lore in `docs/compendium/dungeon.md`.

- **Rarity (Misa's rule: the more drastic, the rarer; a drastic room at most once a run).** Medium-drastic, so `rare: { chanceBp: 2500, fromDepth: 1 }` on the existing rare machinery (`rareSeen` already holds the ids, each rare event has its own chance, tried in content order after the chasm). One mystery room in four becomes the vault, once per run, never on the day's map. No schema option for "once per run" was added: `rareSeen` already does that. The vault is left out of the roll when no fight lies ahead on any path.
- **Which rooms.** Every fight or elite among the rooms the vault leads to; else the nearest fight or elite on each path through caches, camps and mysteries; never a floor boss or an Undercroft epic fight. The first marked room the run enters uses the mark up and the others go back to normal; a descent clears it. Stored as `RunState.sealed` (node ids); old runs load as none.
- **Epic on the floor.** The epic tier is a normal foe with the Undercroft's epic boost (160% HP, 130% Attack) at the run's depth, so the day's floor can use it as is (no stat fold): a marked room fights its own foe boosted as epic, replacing an elite's boost. It pays epic experience (58, `RULES.experience.dungeon.epic`).
- **Doubling.** `RULES.dungeon.seal.rewardMultiplier` = 2: the room's Bait, Essence and scrap in the pack times two, and its shard chance rolled twice (live gear rng). This is cleaner than rolling the whole loot twice (the pack has no dice but the shard) and keeps the grant idempotent: everything lands in the pack and is paid once by the run's own grant. Gear, the supply, keepsakes and the boss chest are not doubled.
- **Cost rule.** `{ op: 'seal' }` counts as a cost for the "every event has a free option" rule, so "Leave it sealed" must stay free.
- **The badge.** The node view carries optional `marks: [{ kind, text, label }]` (server data, validated by the client parse, `ui/RoomMark`), so a later effect reuses it. A marked room is called an epic fight everywhere (label, drawer title, Epic tag, foe HP); the fight's log entry carries `sealed: true` so the end screen's Rooms recap and the drawer keep saying so after the mark is gone. The retry of an act rebuilds the map view with the current marks.
- **Simulator and economy.** Unchanged by construction (the sim plays no rare room): see B-029 in `docs/balance/log.md` for the sim run before and after and an estimate of what the vault pays.
- **Compendium rule changed.** The compendium said no mystery room is ever a fight; the vault is the second exception (after the chasm's fall), on the player's own choice.

## 2026-10-22: Four more mystery rooms (Training Post, Crystal Hollow, Cursed Idol, Hungry Door), curses, and one rule for rare rooms

Misa approved four rooms. Lore is in `docs/compendium/dungeon.md` (and `places.md` for the Hollow); design in `docs/design/dungeon-paths.md`.

- **Rarity rule (Misa): the more drastic a room, the rarer it is, and each rare room appears at most once a run.** Several different rare rooms in a run are fine, never two of the same. `rollRareEvent` now draws for every rare event the run has not met (each with its own `chanceBp`, from its own `fromDepth`), one live draw each in content order, and when more than one comes up it picks evenly with one more draw. Before, it tried them in content order and took the first hit, which would have favoured the first rare room in the file. `rareSeen` (ids) already existed and is the "met" list. Hungry Door: `rare: {chanceBp: 2000, fromDepth: 2}`; the Blue Chasm stays 1500 from depth 3. A test checks each room keeps its own rate over 20000 rolls. Another room can be made once-per-run the same way (add `rare`), so the mechanism is generic.
- **Training Post, Crystal Hollow and Cursed Idol are normal map events.** The Idol is at most once a run without any flag: a map draws each event once and the Undercroft's maps draw no events (the `rare` machinery would also have taken it off the map, which Misa did not ask for).
- **Curse (new effect `curse`).** A keepsake-shaped def with `foe_stat` effects only, stored in `RunState.curses` (old runs: none), folded into every later fight with the keepsakes (`heldFolds`), so the engine is unchanged. The Idol's: foe Attack +2000 bp ("Foes deal 20% more damage."). Counts as a cost for the free-option rule. Shown as a warning note (`Alert`, a "Curse" chip with its glossary entry `curse`) under the pack and keepsakes, on the end screen, and as a line in the room's step.
- **`send_home` with `who: 'highest_level'`.** Sends the highest-level standing creature (ties: leftmost) out of the row like a camp swap-out, nobody in. It keeps its earned experience and is paid and listed as "Went home". It is not a cost for the free-option rule (no HP, no run), so Feed it is the door's free option.
- **One creature standing at the Hungry Door (decision).** Feed it is disabled with the reason "Needs two creatures standing." (the event view's option has `blocked`; the server also refuses it). Considered: ending the run home safe with the treasure. Rejected: it would make the door a way to leave a bad run with a prize and would surprise a player who tapped it hoping to keep going. Pry it open stays available.
- **Treasure table.** `rollTreasure` takes a table (`RULES.dungeon.treasure` for the chasm, the new `RULES.dungeon.doorTreasure` for the door): Rare gear at the run's depth, two **good** supplies (one below great) of one kind, **3 shards and 2 glimmer**, no blueprints (`blueprints: false`; the draw is even among the three kinds). The chasm's 6 shards and 4 glimmer are twice the door's. The `treasure` effect names its table (`table: 'door'`; default chasm), an option holds at most one treasure and an event's treasures share one table (the old "one treasure per event" rule was per event, but the door has one in each option).
- **Balance checks.** The value test (risky options pay a little more than safe ones) now counts experience at 1 point per 20, and skips rare rooms (their prize is its own table). Two equal-cost options may differ by up to 6 points instead of 3.5, for Crystal Hollow (3 scrap and 1 Essence is worth 8.5 against a 30% heal's 3): Misa's numbers, kept, because a heal is worth more to a hurt team than the rough value says. The sim plays the three common rooms (a curse is folded in; the Idol's "Take it" counts as risky for its policy) and not the door. Numbers in `docs/balance/log.md`, B-030.
- **Old runs.** A run saved before curses has none; a waiting room from before has no `blocked` on its options.

## 2026-10-22: Sealed Vault and the other rooms merged: one rare roll for all three rare rooms

The Sealed Vault (25% from depth 1), the Hungry Door (20% from depth 2) and the Blue Chasm (15% from depth 3) all go through the one generalised `rollRareEvent` (one draw per unmet rare room that has reached its depth, one more to pick evenly among several hits). The vault rule "never with no fight ahead" stays: the worker passes `rareEventsAt(map, node)`, which leaves the vault out of the list, so it takes no draw. Each rare room appears at most once a run; the marginals are about 11.9% (chasm), 16.3% (door) and 20.9% (vault). The vault balance entry stays B-029 and the four-rooms entry is B-030.

## 2026-10-22: A curse is a keepsake with a curse effect, and the Sealed Vault doubles everything material

Misa, after testing the new rooms: "a curse is a keepsake with a curse effect" (not its own warning note and tag), and "the vault doubles everything" (not only the pack).

- **Curses live in the keepsakes.** The keepsake schema got an optional `curse: true`; a curse def (the Idol's `idol-curse`) carries it. The client has no curse UI any more: the server lists curses after the real keepsakes in the run's `keepsakes` view and in the end summary's `keepsakes` (`heldViews`), each as `{id, name, description, curse: true}`. The `curses` field was removed from the run view and the summary (an end summary saved before this loses the old `curses`, so a finished run from before shows no curse chip; nothing else changes). `RunState.curses` stays as the server's data (old runs load unchanged), so the engine and `heldFolds` are untouched. The client chip is the keepsake chip with a new pixel glyph `curse` (a cracked skull in dark blood red, ink crack, glowing eyes) and the `--bad` red edge; the tap opens the same sheet; the spoken label starts "Curse,". Future curses only need a def with `curse: true`; `dungeonGifts` (every keepsake that is not a curse) is the pool for elites, camps and the sim, so a curse is never handed out as a gift. The `curse` glossary entry stays (the route recap chip uses it) and the `keepsake` entry says a curse is one.
- **Replay fix found on the way.** A fight's replay listed its folds by looking the ids up in the keepsake list, which dropped a curse; it now maps the folds it held (`keepsakeViewOf`).
- **The Sealed Vault doubles all material rewards.** Marked fight: Bait, Essence, scrap and shards as before. New: **gear and supplies**, which no fight drops itself (the run's gear and supply come with its end grant), so each marked fight cleared adds one more gear roll and one more supply to the end grant (`rollSealedBonus`; the run then holds two of each instead of one). The gear roll uses the same chance as the full clear's (certain today, so one piece), then rarity, base and rolls at the run's depth, from the live gear rng and drawn after the run's own dice (nothing is drawn without a marked fight). Counted in `RunState.sealedWins` (old runs: 0), paid by the same grant under the same key (idempotent on retry), a full bag turns the gear into parts like any dungeon gear. Like the run's own supply it is earned when the fight is cleared and paid whatever the outcome. **Keepsakes:** a marked elite gives two (distinct, none already held; as many as are left), the second as `bonusKeepsake` on the fight's log entry (a marked plain fight gives none, as a plain fight gives none). **Experience is not doubled** (still the epic amount). The option text says only "The next fight is epic, with double rewards." (Misa, 2026-10-22: name the promise, not the exact effects, which may change).
- **A marked room wears the epic skull on the map** (`nodeGlyphOf` uses the shown kind) while the mark lasts, with the x2 badge; the ring and the element tint stay the room's own.

## 2026-10-22: Rare rooms: one roll, one pool

Misa found three separate chances (25%, 20%, 15%) and a pick among the hits too fiddly, and asked for two pools. When a run steps into a mystery room, **one roll** (`RULES.dungeon.rareRoomChanceBp`, 4000 = 40%; the three chances gave about 49% together from depth 3 on and less at the shallow depths, so this is about the same share, a little lower) decides whether it is a rare room. If it is, the room is picked **evenly among the eligible rare rooms**: each keeps its `fromDepth` (vault 1, door 2, chasm 3), the vault's "a fight must lie ahead" filter (`rareEventsAt`), and never one the run already met (`rareSeen`). If none is eligible the room stays the normal one and no dice are drawn.

- `chanceBp` is removed from the `rare` field of an event (`rare: { fromDepth }`, the schema is still strict, so an old `chanceBp` is refused). `rollRareEvent` is now two draws at most (the roll, then the pick), live from the server's rng only. Adding a rare room is one `rare: { fromDepth }`; the odds of the whole pool are the one number in the rules.
- At depth 1 only the vault can come up, so a rare room there is always the vault (before it was 25%); at depth 2 the pool is the door and the vault; from depth 3 each is a third of the rare rooms (about 13% of mystery rooms each). Tests: each room's gate, once per run, the filter, the draw counts (none when nothing is eligible, one on a miss, two on a hit), the 40% share and the even split over 20000 seeded rolls.
- Worker tests that script the dice now push two values for a rare room (the roll, then the index of the pick in content order: chasm 0, door 1, vault 2).

## 2026-10-22: The dungeon's reflect is split in two: spiky armour (Thorns) and a reflective shield (Molten Shell). The raid is unchanged

Misa's call, after the balance work in B-002: reflect knocked out a creature in 97 to 100% of the runs that met it (the Thorns room modifier, or the Slag Tyrant's Molten Shell). The first reaction of each round came back whole, uncapped and undodgeable, so players learnt to avoid reactions, which is the opposite of what the game wants. The raid is untouched, because many players fight it together and its balance (the Cinder Wyrm's Backdraft, Bramble Season) was tuned as it is (B-007). Dungeon only:

- **Thorns is spiky armour (`spikes`, `bp`).** Every creature hit that lands on the foe (a turn hit or an ability strike, not an Echo) sends `bp` of that hit's **plain** damage back at the hitter, so a reaction costs the same as a plain hit. The returning hit: no Guard step-in, no dodge, no crit, no cover; Shield Wall, armor and `reflectedBp` apply as for a reflected hit; capped by `maxHitBp`. It can knock a creature out. Starting value 1500, shipped 1000 (10%) after the sim.
- **Molten Shell is a reflective shield (`reflect_chance`, `chanceBp`).** Each reaction a living creature sets off has that chance to be reflected exactly like Backdraft's reflect (combat-rules.md 5.4.1 to 5.4.3), with no once-a-round limit, and the returning hit is capped like a boss hit. One die from the fight's rng per reaction, drawn only while the shield is up, so no other fight draws differently (every stored fight and fixture replays as before). Tidepool Finery (`unreflected`) works against it. Starting value 1000, shipped 500 (5%) after the sim.
- **Why two ops, not one rule.** Misa wants reflect to have its own tag, distinct from Thorns: a glossary entry "Reflective shield" next to "Spikes", and the Thorns room modifier now reads "Every hit on the foe sends some damage back at the hitter."
- **Standing effects write no `gimmick` event.** They do nothing on the boss's turn; the "Next round" forecast names them (Thorns: "every hit pricks the hitter"; Molten Shell: "may reflect a reaction") when there is room after the foe's moves, and the spike hit shows as a number on the hitter, like any boss hit, without a wind-up.
- **The single-hit cap is off today** (`RULES.combat.maxHitBp` 0, Misa 2026-10-15), so "capped like a boss hit" is inert until it is switched on; the engine already applies it to both new hits. With a 50% cap a reflect would no longer be a sure knock-out (B-031 has the numbers for both).
- Old runs and fixtures: the ops are new and the engine's draws are unchanged for fights without them. Stored dungeon runs that hold the old Thorns reflect (embedded in the room) replay exactly as they were fought; only newly built runs get the new content.

## 2026-10-22: Molten Shell is a frequent partial Backlash, not a rare full reflect (replaces the reflective shield above)

Misa found a 5% full reflect too swingy: a lucky roll ruins a good run, and most runs never see it. The `reflect_chance` op is replaced by `backlash` (`chanceBp`, `shareBp`; the old op was never in a release, so no stored run holds it, and the raid's `reflect_first_reaction` stays so stored runs with the old Thorns reflect still replay).

- **What it does.** Each reaction a living creature sets off has `chanceBp` chance (5000: half the time) to backfire. The foe still takes the whole reaction (plain and bonus), setup is credited and the mark used up as usual, and `shareBp` of the bonus (2500: a quarter) comes back as a reflected hit on the payoff: the shape of Bramble Season's partial reflect (aimed, a Guard beside may take it, Shield Wall, armor and sheet apply, no crit, dodge or cover). Fairer than a rare whole reflect: it is nearly every fight, it is a cost and never a lost reaction, and no single roll decides the run.
- **Tuned with the same single-room study** (B-031): the share is the knob, chance stays 5000. Knock-out share climbs in steps with the share (a burn is lethal only when it is bigger than what the payoff has left), so there is no smooth ramp. 2500 puts the Slag Tyrant a few points under the other room bosses and level with Thorns.
- **Tidepool Finery (`unreflected`)** turns a backlash aside for the creature's first reaction of the fight, as it turns Backdraft aside: its set text ("cannot be reflected") still reads true because the burn is a reflected hit. The die is still drawn, so the rest of the fight draws the same either way.
- **Name and tag.** The glossary entry is "Backlash" (`gimmick-backlash`), distinct from Thorns' "Spikes". Not "Riposte" (a gear line already) and no longer "Reflective shield".
- `RULES.combat.maxHitBp` stays 0 and no dungeon cap was added; the burn takes the cap if it is ever switched on (combat-rules.md 5.5.3).

## 2026-10-22: Foe move lines say what a move hits; "Charged:" replaces "announced a round ahead"

- **Wording** (`gimmickShort`, the pre-fight drawer, the stage's room banner and the raid's icon labels). A line says what the move does, then how often, with no comma: "Slams your strongest creature and the ones beside it every 4 rounds". A charged move starts with "Charged:" and then says what it hits ("Charged: slams your strongest creature and the ones beside it every 4 rounds"); that it is announced a round ahead is what "charged" means (Misa: implied by "charged", which may later become a keyword players can act on, for example to interrupt). A slam names its aim (strongest, best Payoff, random, left); on the dungeon's row the left creature has one neighbour, so "the one beside it".
- **Layout.** Each drawer move row is a bullet: the glyph in its own column, centred on the first line of text (`height: 1lh`), the pixel-font name then the description inline, wrapped lines under the name.
- **Fonts stay two.** Misa asked whether the description could use the pixel font. Jersey 10 has one weight, so the name and its description would look the same; Misa's rule was to keep the two fonts (pixel name, body-font description) in that case.

## 2026-10-22: Desktop Expeditions map: full width until a place is chosen, the panel runs the wizard

Misa's feedback on the desktop world map, after testing it.

- **No "Where to?" panel.** With nothing chosen the map takes the whole width, with the "Out exploring" shelf under it. Choosing a place (or claiming a trip, whose result shows there) opens the side panel. An X in its heading (and Escape on the place) closes it.
- **The panel runs the phone's flow.** It was the whole send form as one long scrolling list. Now `PlaceFlow` holds the place, the send wizard (Who goes, Pack up, Ready) and the push-on choice, and draws them into a frame: `PlaceSheet` puts it in a `ui/Drawer`, `PlacePanel` in the panel, with the gold button pinned at the bottom in both. `SendForm` lost its all-at-once "panel" layout. After a send that started, both close.
- **The map keeps its scale.** On desktop the scale is picked from the full width, so opening the panel narrows the map's window instead of shrinking the art. When the panel opens, closes or switches place, the map glides (smooth unless reduced motion is on) so the chosen place, or the last one, is as near the middle as it can scroll. Closing gives the place's marker the focus.
- **Drag to pan.** A mouse can drag a map that pans (`ui/use-drag-scroll`). A press that moves 4 px or less stays a click on a marker; a real drag swallows the click that ends it. Touch keeps the browser's own scrolling. The cursor is a hand only when the map can pan.
- **Removed:** the "X is on a job in the Workshop. Claim it first." lines above the map. The explorer list already says "On a job" for each creature.

## 2026-10-22: Pixel scrollbars on desktop, the system's own on phones

Misa asked for scrollbars in the arcade look. `styles/base/scrollbar.css` styles every scrollbar, but only for a mouse (`hover: hover` and `pointer: fine`). The bar is 5 UI pixels wide: a night track in an ink frame, and a haze thumb with the panels' bevel that turns gold under the mouse.

- **Built with `::-webkit-scrollbar`.** Discord's desktop client is Chromium, and the standard `scrollbar-color` only sets two flat colours, which cannot make a pixel bevel. Chromium ignores `::-webkit-scrollbar` on any box that sets `scrollbar-width` or `scrollbar-color`. So the three `scrollbar-width: thin` lines were removed, and Firefox gets `scrollbar-color` only when it lacks the webkit selector.
- **Phones are left alone.** Discord's iOS and Android webviews draw a thin overlay indicator that shows only while scrolling and fades. Mobile games hide the scrollbar or rely on that indicator; a permanent bar on touch looks cramped and out of place. A themed bar is not worth it there. Where content is cut off with no other cue, a peek or an edge fade (`ui/ScrollHint`) tells the player there is more.
- **Boxes that hide their bar on purpose keep it hidden:** the world map, the living room and the scroll hint.
- **One scrollbar at a time.** While anything with `aria-modal` is open (a drawer, the roster sheet, How to play), the page behind it does not scroll and drops its scrollbar (`html:has([aria-modal='true']) { overflow: hidden }`). Only the drawer's own bar shows. The page keeps its scroll position and gets its bar back on close. Misa saw two bars side by side in a narrow desktop window.

## 2026-10-22: Valley Games engine: five kinds, stats against the field, a timeline the client only plays

The simulation core of the Valley Games (`docs/design/valley-games.md`). Code: `packages/engine/src/games/`, content `games-events.json`, rules `RULES.games` and `rules/games.ts`, study `pnpm sim:games`.

- **One pure engine, a presentation-ready timeline.** `runGamesEvent(def, entrants, rng)` resolves an event at the lock with the server's crypto RNG and returns beats (positions, effects, meters, scores, states, eliminations, props, commentary keys), the actors' start positions and the standings. The client positions sprites and plays effects; it decides nothing. Integer math only (basis points, a fixed integer sine table); deterministic for a seeded RNG, so a stored show replays exactly.
- **Five kinds, events are content.** `endure` (sauna), `chase` (melon), `feast` (peppers), `swim` (sharks), `shove` (floe). Each event is a kind with its own numbers (`params`), an element table, optional role and size multipliers, a scene and commentary overrides; a skin like the Frost Bath needs no code.
- **Stats count against the field, with diminishing returns.** Each kind reads its key stat as `sqrt(stat / median of the field)` (clamped to a quarter to four times), weighted per event by `statBp`, times the element, role and size multipliers. So a creature twice the median is not twice as good, and every event has a clear "this is the creature for it" (an element, a role, small size) that is not just level.
- **The end crowns the winner.** Every show ends with the standings' first place (every member of a winning team) cheering and the closing line naming it; a shared first place gets the draw line. In a feast the last one standing eats on until it bursts too, so outlasting everyone wins.
- **Team events cap at 48 creatures** (`RULES.games.maxTeamEntrants`, 16 teams of three) while solo events cap at 24: a 15-player server must fit a team event. A team's score is its best member's (`teamScore: best`) or the sum (`sum`).
- **The result carries each actor's own score and elimination tick** (`scores`, `outAt`), so the worker can keep personal records per creature (pins) and the simulator can measure when eliminations happen.
- **Nine events**, the spec's seven plus Berry Bonanza (a sour feast where Grove is hardy) and Shark Relay (a second team event). With a single team event of the endure kind and "no kind twice a day", the solo endure events could never be drawn.
- **Tuning targets** (`pnpm sim:games`, economy-sim rosters of 8 and 15 players at weeks 1, 4, 12 and 30, players' entry policies mixed): per event, the strongest entrant by power wins 15-45%, a bottom-half entrant reaches the podium in at least 15% of events, 20-50 s, at least one moment every 4 s, aptitude still predicts results (Spearman at least 0.6) and aptitude picks win at least 1.5x as often as random ones. Across solo events, creatures 4th or lower in their own roster take at least 15% of wins, level 1-9 creatures win at least 0.4x their share of entries and level 30+ at most 1.5x. First numbers pass all but Floe Sumo's Spearman (about 0.5: the floe is the luckiest event).

## 2026-10-22: Valley Games on the server: days, auto-entry, awards and the Prize Stall

The worker side of the Games (`apps/worker/src/games*.ts`, migration 38). Choices made while building it:

- **A show day is a guild-local date D.** Entries for D are open from D-1's show to D's lock (13:37, the daily reset); the show is at 13:40 (`games_days.show_at`, stored at the lock so the dev tools can move it). The "next" show day is the first day whose show has not started, so entries for D+1 open the moment D's show starts. D's lineup is drawn with the live Games RNG the first time anything needs it (`chooseLineup`, avoiding D-1's events).
- **The lock is one transaction** at D's reset: auto-entry, snapshots (the raid's fold through `gamesEntrantFrom`), one seed from the live RNG (`drawSeed`), each event run with `createSeededRng(<seed>:<event id>)`. The frozen entrants and each result are stored per event (`games_results`, one row per event so no row nears the storage's row limit), with the placings. A day locks only if somebody had joined before its lock, so a brand-new server shows nothing until the first lock after someone entered. A late lock (a missed alarm, a reset day) starts its show 15 s after the lock, or at 13:40 if later, rather than in the past.
- **Auto-entry.** A player joins with their first entry; auto-entry is on by default (`PUT /api/games/auto`). At the lock, every event a joined auto player left empty gets the creature(s) that ran that event id last time if still theirs and free, then their best free creatures by `gamesAptitude`, in lineup order (solo events first, the team event last, filled to three). A team the player set themselves is left as they set it, even if short. Bots (the staging dev guests) are always auto.
- **Entries** are a set (`PUT /api/games/entry`, no idempotency key). A creature already in another event that day is refused (`already_entered`) rather than moved, so the client always says what happens. Over the cap (24, or 48 creatures in a team event) players come before bots, each in entry order, and a team is kept or left out whole.
- **Awards at the show's end** (an alarm candidate; `awarded_at` makes them run once): medals by the real place (a bot ahead of a player does not move them up; bots themselves get no medal, record or experience), a pin per creature that beat its own record in that event id (its first entry only sets the record), `gamesXp` per creature, the pocket in medal points, then the podium post (`games:<day>:podium`). The post is queued at the award, never before, so it cannot spoil the show.
- **Results are never sent before `show_at`.** `GET /api/games` gives the last show that has started (with `serverNow`, the timeline and `endsAt`) until the next one starts; the player's own results (`you.lastResults`) only once a show is awarded. The show's length is `showTimeline` in `packages/game` (an intro card of 4 s per event, its play, a podium card of 5 s): the client must use the same function so the awards land as the podium ends.
- **The Prize Stall** (`games-stall.json`): five offers (a rare mystery gear piece at the player's dungeon item level 9 points, a good supply 4, 30 Bait 3, 3 shards 3, 1 glimmer 4), each with a weekly limit, all offered every Wednesday game day (no rotation yet: the content can change). Purchases go through `runKeyed` and the existing `payOnce` grant (source `games`, item origin `games`), with what they roll drawn from the live gear RNG at the purchase.
- **Analytics:** `games_entry`, `games_show` and `games_buy` events, and `games` as a creature-experience source.

## 2026-10-23: Valley Games polish: commentary by priority, paced exits, real finishes, stacks and a relay

Round 3 of the Games engine (the 2026-10-22 entry above).

- **Commentary is chosen when the show is finished, by priority:** exits and the race for the win first, then twists (Steam, a ghost round, a zap, a save), then filler (a gulp, a burp, "does not budge"). An important line waits up to 1.5 gaps for room, a twist half a gap, a filler not at all; a filler is used at most twice a show. Two exits close together share one line ("{a} and {b} ..."). A line never names a creature after it has left (its own exit line aside), and nothing but a last must-say line and the closing line comes after the crowning.
- **Exits are paced** (`ExitGate`): at most a quarter of the field within any 2 s; in a field of 12 or fewer, or once four or fewer are left, at least 1.5 s between two exits. A creature held back stays in at the very edge for a moment. So the commentary can name almost every exit and a crowd never leaves at once.
- **Size is a young creature's:** small means stage 1 of a line that still evolves. A single-stage line (Lanternfin, Hearthshell) is full grown and counts as big; before this, those creatures were "small" at any level and took most of the small bonus.
- **The stat curve has a floor:** a creature far below the field counts as half of it (the clamp was a quarter), so the weakest are never hopeless.
- **Melon Run has a finish line:** runners who cross are safe, ranked by finish order; the melon then smashes into the arch and the race is over. **Sauna Stack** teams sit stacked in one seat (Guards at the bottom shelter those above, higher is hotter, the ones above drop down when one leaves). **Shark Relay** is a real relay: one swimmer per team in the water at a time, tagging the next one in; a bitten-out swimmer ends its team's relay. Floe Sumo's floe grows with a field of more than 16 (up to the art's 84 px), stops shrinking at its smallest and the shoves get stronger instead. One or two entrants make a show of about 25 s.
- **New sim targets:** at least 70% of exits named (fields of 12 or fewer), at most 10% of shows with more than 8 s without a moment, at most 5% of shows with a quarter of the field out within 2 s, Floe Sumo's Spearman at least 0.45 (the chaos event). The strongest-by-power target misses in the HP events: their fit is a high-HP tank (a level 27 Thornbuck Guard) over the strongest glass cannon (a level 30 Tempestray Payoff), which is the feature's intent; the lead decides whether to keep that target for HP events.

## 2026-10-23: Valley Games: three events a day, a bonus event on Wednesdays

Misa: four events every day felt like a bit much. Now three a day (`RULES.games.eventsPerDay`, still exactly one team event), and on the bonus day (`RULES.games.bonus.weekday`, ISO 3: Wednesday, the show day's weekday in the guild's timezone) a fourth.

- **The bonus event is a solo event of a kind not already in the day** when the pool allows (`chooseLineup`'s `bonus` flag; it is drawn after the regular three with the same costs, so the regular draw is unchanged, and it sits before the team closer). With no solo event left it falls back to any event left.
- **It pays double medal points to the pocket** (`pocketBp` 20000, `pocketPoints`), medals and pins alike; lifetime medal counts take one each and experience is unchanged. Wednesday is also the Prize Stall's day, so the points land just in time to spend.
- **Stored in the lineup's JSON, no migration:** `games_days.lineup_json` is the ids in show order, or on a bonus day `{ "ids": [...], "bonus": "<event id>" }`. Days stored before (four events, a plain list) read as days with no bonus event, so staging data keeps playing and paying as it was.
- **The view marks it** (`bonus: true` on the lineup event in `next` and `show`, and on the player's result); the client draws a gold "Bonus" ribbon on the card and a plain "2x" tag on the result row, both opening the `games-bonus` glossary entry.
- **The simulator follows:** three events a day and the bonus event one day in seven; every target still passes.

## 2026-10-23: The alarm's cleanup runs once a game day; old dungeon steps are deleted

Cloudflare warned that the account had used 90% of the free tier's 5,000,000 Durable Object `rows_read` in a day. A local probe that counted the rows each query read (a month of play for 12 players) showed where they went.

- **The cause.** While any Activity instance is tracked, the lounge check runs the alarm every 60 s (`LOUNGE_TICK_MS`). Each alarm also ran the cleanup passes, and two of them read whole tables. The rollup's "last rolled day" (`MAX(day) WHERE metric = 'rolled'`) had no index, so it read all of `analytics_daily` (20,000 rows in the probe). The dungeon prune's `json_remove` and `act_json = '{}'` updates read every step older than 2 days, and old steps were emptied, never deleted (17,000 rows). That came to about 37,000 rows per alarm, about 2.2 million an hour while anyone plays, and it grew every day.
- **Once a game day.** `GuildCore.maintain` runs the analytics, gear action and dungeon prunes and `closeStale` on the first alarm of each game day. A new `maintenance` table (migration 38) remembers the day, because an evicted object starts again with nothing in memory. A long analytics backlog leaves the day unmarked, so the following alarms keep working it off as before. The rollup itself still runs on every alarm; it is cheap with the index.
- **Index.** Migration 38 adds `analytics_daily(metric, day)`.
- **Old steps go, after 3 days.** A player can only watch the current day's runs, so Misa cut the fights' retention (`RUN_RETENTION_MS`: inputs, events, steps and the state of a run that ended) from 90 days to 3. Past it a step of a run that ended is deleted, not emptied in place. Nothing reads it any more: the replay of such a run is gone too (its state is emptied), and a late retry of the step is still a `stale_step`. A run still open keeps its steps, because closing it reads its fights. The summary and rewards stay for good, and the last team is read from the summary. Day seeds and saved team drafts keep 90 days (`DAY_RETENTION_MS`): one small row each, and a draft is a player's choice.
- **Talent table check.** `booksByCreature` looked up `sqlite_master` (about 150 rows) on every call, once per player in a raid view. It now runs its query and answers empty on "no such table" (only an old migration, the raid board repair, reads it before migration 28).
- **Left as is:** the start-up passes in `migrate()` (gear row normalising, the gear and level conversions, missing talents) still read their tables each time the object starts. They cost a few hundred rows per start at today's sizes; gating them needs care because staging shares a database between branches.

## 2026-10-23: Games migration renumbered to 39

**Decision.** Main released the daily maintenance mark as migration 38 (#68) while the Valley Games branch, already deployed to staging, had its tables as 38. The Games now run as **migration 39** (`gamesMigrationV39` in `apps/worker/src/games-migration.ts`), which adds the Games tables when they are missing and the maintenance mark when a database recorded 38 for the Games instead. Neither released migration was edited.

**Why.** A migration that has run anywhere (staging counts) is frozen (2026-10-17 rule). Staging guilds can be at 38 with either set of tables, depending on which branch deployed last; this step makes every database end the same, the same order-independent approach as the dungeon and expeditions migrations (18 and 19). A worker test replays the staging shape.

## 2026-10-23: No Valley Games results post

**Decision (Misa).** The Games post nothing to Discord: the podium post that went out at the award is gone (`games-posts.ts` removed). **Why:** the show can be watched as a replay until the next one, and a results post spoils it for everyone who watches later. The results stay in the Games tab, for players who have watched or chose to look.

## 2026-10-23: Team events are real team sports: Log Carry and Pumpkin Push; Sauna Stack and Shark Relay retired

Misa: "Could we drop the sauna and ice room to just one per player? I don't think all the events work as team sports, but something like pushing a boulder or carrying a big item easily works." Then: a team event's length must not grow with the number of teammates or teams. One team event a day stays (three creatures means more bench experience), but only real team sports.

- **Retired: Sauna Stack and Shark Relay.** Sauna Stack sat each team as a stack in one seat; Shark Relay swam the legs one after another, so it grew longer per teammate. Both are out of the pool, and their stacked and relay code (stack seats, levels and heights; legs, tags and the shore) is gone from the engine (`heightBp` and `legLaps` left the schema). Endure and swim are solo kinds now. Stored days, results, records and replays may still name them: `GAMES_RETIRED` in `@creatures/game` keeps each one's name, kind, scene, team size, tick and line, `gamesEventInfo` and `gamesEventName` read it, the worker's shown lineup and creature records use it, and the client names, scores and replays them (the kinds' commentary templates stay, `swim.tag` included). The next day's lineup never offers one. Ids are never reused.
- **New kind `haul`, team only.** Two skins: **Log Carry** (`mode: carry`, scene `log-trail`): three creatures in a row under a birch log; the team's speed is its members' Speed against the field, the slowest counting most (`minWeightBp` 60%, the rest the mean), so a team must match; a team short of members is slower (`shortBp`). **Pumpkin Push** (`mode: push`, scene `pumpkin-hill`): three creatures one behind the other behind a giant pumpkin; Attack adds up with diminishing returns (the strongest whole, then 60% and 35%); big creatures and Mire push best. Both: the element, role and size multipliers as every kind; a Setter in the team leads (`leadBp`); now and then a load wobbles and one member keeps it steady (a base chance, its dodge, each Guard of the team); a Sidestep line steps clear, a Stubborn one catches the load once; else it drops (the log falls, the pumpkin rolls back) and the team stands a few ticks, one fewer per Support and for a Second Wind; a drop may bump a team in a nearby lane; a Spark member's zap jolts its team forward. All numbers are params, integer math.
- **Simultaneous and capped.** Every lane races at once; the race ends `graceTicks` (4 s) after the first team crosses, or at `maxTicks` (44 s). Ranking: finish order, then distance; every member shares its team's score. The new sim check (`teamLengths`) plays each team event with 4 and with 15 teams on the 15-player rosters: the means must be within 10% (now about 41 s against 39 s).
- **Readability.** One lane per team (feet y 48 to 100); the load is a prop per team (`log:<i>`, `pumpkin:<i>`, f: up, wobbling, down) and the members stand round it at fixed offsets. A field of the same speed bunched into one pile, so the lanes start staggered like a running track: up to four start columns (two teams each while that is enough), each 40 px further on, and each lane's finish moved as far (`finish:<i>` props, drawn as a short chequered strip per lane), so every lane is as long. The camera frames the whole field with the leader in view. One "you" marker per team, over its log or its pushers.
- **Commentary.** New keys `haul.start`, `drop`, `catch`, `steady`, `dodge`, `cheer`, `zap`, `bump`, `lead`, `finish`, `close` (a photo finish), `win`, `draw`. Lead changes and finishes are important; drops, catches, bumps, zaps and steadies are twists (capped at three a show, so a big field's many drops never crowd out the start and the finish).
- **Lineup.** The daily team event is drawn from Log Carry and Pumpkin Push; `chooseLineup` is unchanged.
- **Simulator.** `pnpm sim:games`: every target passes, the two new events included (Log Carry 38 s, strongest wins 35%, Spearman 0.85; Pumpkin Push 41 s, strongest wins 34%, Spearman 0.65).

## 2026-10-23: Valley Games: no level, a "Good fit" badge, event badges, no auto-entry

Misa's decisions for the v1 scope (`valley-games.md`, "v1 scope", items 2 to 4).

- **No level in the Games.** `gamesEntrantFrom` (`packages/engine/src/games/entrant.ts`) builds an entrant from the creature's current species only: HP, Attack and Speed are the species' `baseStats` (the level-1 stats of its current form: no growth, no fusion bonus, no gear stat line), dodge comes from that Speed alone, crit from `RULES.combat.luck` and the species' own crit bonus (no gear crit). Element, role and size (`gamesIsSmall`) as before. Of the gear sheet only the Games' chance and trigger lines are read (`GamesMods`: Sidestep, Stubborn, Second Wind, Echo, Payback, Last Stand, Momentum, Riposte, Warm Heart); flat dodge, stat lines and talents' stat effects are ignored. `level` left `GamesEntrant` (nothing showed it; stored days that carry it still replay). The worker's `gamesRoster` and the simulator pass the species and the sheet. The aptitude's reference stats are now the median species (HP 168, Attack 36, Speed 37). The field curve stays: it now compares species.
- **Retuned for it.** Without level the small-creature bonus outweighed the base stats a young creature lacks (level 1-9 creatures won 1.4x their fair share). Every `smallBp` above 1x is cut to 40% of its bonus (Sauna Showdown 2.1x to 1.44x, Shark Derby 1.55x to 1.22x, and so on), and Pepper Panic (`statBp` 8000, `jitterBp` 2500) and Pumpkin Push (`statBp` 7000, `jitterBp` 2000) read their stat more and their luck less, so a good fit still wins clearly more than a random pick.
- **"Good fit", one binary badge, replaces the 1 to 5 pips.** A creature is a good fit for an event when `gamesAptitude` reaches `gamesGoodFitMin(def)`: the aptitude of the species at the top `RULES.games.goodFit.topSpeciesBp` (25%) of all shipped species for that event (`teamTopSpeciesBp`, 35%, in a team event, which takes three), each species once and gearless, ties at the bar included. A Games gear line can lift a creature over it. **Why this method:** the bar comes from the content alone (no stored per-event number), so a new event or species needs no calibration step, and with no level two creatures of a species are equal, so the species is the right unit. **Calibration:** the shares were chosen with `pnpm sim:games` on the economy sim's rosters (8 and 15 players, weeks 1, 4, 12 and 30), counting each player's good fits for every event of every played day: per event the median is 2 to 4 (now 2 to 4 everywhere; Berry Bonanza and Pumpkin Push lowest at 2). 20% left Berry Bonanza at 1; 30% put four events at 5. The server sends only who has it, `next.goodFit: { [eventId]: creatureId[] }`; the picker lists the good fits first, then the rest, each by name, with no score anywhere. The glossary entry is `games-good-fit` (`games-fit` is gone).
- **Event badges.** A creature collects a badge per event id it has won (gold), with a win count: a new table `games_badges (creature_id, event_id, user_id, wins)` (**migration 40**, `games-badges-migration.ts`), written by the award for every member of a first place (a shared first place counts for each; bots never). It is back-filled from the awarded gold placings of real players. A table rather than a read of `games_placings` on every view: the tab is read often, and `games_placings` only grows (the rows_read lesson of the same day). The tab sends the next lineup's badges (`next.badges: { [eventId]: { [creatureId]: wins } }`), the creature route a list (`badges: { eventId, wins }[]`; a retired event's badge stays). Drawn as one generic medallion (`games/art/badge.ts`) tinted per event scene with a 5x5 glyph of the event, from the scene, else the kind, else gold with a star, so a new event gets one without new art. Kept as `{ eventId, wins }` so a larger badge system can grow from it. The creature page shows a shelf of the events won only (no faint outlines for the rest: fewer things to read); the picker shows the creature's badge for that event beside its name.
- **No auto-entry.** `PUT /api/games/auto` and its schema, the lock's auto-fill (`autoFill`, `byAptitude`, `lastRunner`), the `joined`/`auto` fields of the view, the client switch, its glossary entry and its fixture are gone. A player who does not enter that day is not in the show. The `joined` notion went too: a day locks when somebody entered it, and the alarm arms the next lock while a day not locked yet has entries. The `auto` columns of migration 39 stay, unused (released migrations are frozen). Staging bots are entered by the dev tools (`games-bots`, and `games-run-now` before it locks), never by the lock.
- **Simulator** (`packages/sim/src/games-study.ts`). Policies `fit` (at random among the free good fits, then among the rest), `best-level` (highest level first) and `random`. New targets: level does not predict a win (level 1-9 win share 0.8 to 1.2x their fair share of entries, where an entry's fair share is one over its field size, so the small week-1 fields do not count as level; level 30+ at most 1.2x), highest level first wins at most 1.2x as often as random, good-fit picks win at least 1.5x as often as random in every event, and a median of 2 to 4 good fits per roster in every event. "Strongest" is now by the species' base power, and its 5% floor is not applied to events that favour small creatures (the strongest by base stats is a grown one there, and the young are meant to beat it). Pepper Panic and Pumpkin Push join Floe Sumo with their own Spearman floor (0.5): luck-heavy, and every creature of one species ties on aptitude, which caps a rank correlation. Results (40 days): every target passes (129 of 129); level 1-9 1.18x, level 30+ 0.69x, best-level 1.11x random; good-fit picks win about 2x to 7x as often as random.

## 2026-10-23: Three more team events, all played at once: Bucket Brigade, Crate Tower, Kite Flight

Misa's rule for team events holds: a team event's length never grows with the number of teammates or teams. All five team events are now in the daily draw (`chooseLineup` is unchanged: it picks the day's team event from every live team event).

- **Three new kinds, team only, a fixed time.** `fill` (Bucket Brigade, scene `barrel-pond`), `stack` (Crate Tower, `crate-yard`) and `kite` (Kite Flight, `kite-hill`) run exactly `maxTicks` (100 ticks of 400 ms, 40 s) whatever happens, every team and every member at once. `loadGames` refuses them as solo events. The sim's team-length check (4 against 15 teams) covers them: about 42 s either way.
- **A column per team, a still camera.** Up to 16 teams side by side across the 192 px scene (`columnXs` in `packages/engine/src/games/columns.ts`: 11 px apart at 16, at most 40 px, shuffled so nobody is always at the edge). Each team has one object, a prop per team (`barrel:<i>` f = level in percent, `tower:<i>` f = crates, `kite:<i>` the kite's centre, f 1 tumbling), drawn in the team's colour (`teamRamp`, the secondary ramp of a code-drawn sprite in `art/drawn-team.ts`). The "you" marker sits over your team's object, as in the hauls.
- **Bucket Brigade.** Members shuttle on their own trips: dip (one tick fewer per Support teammate), run up (Speed against the field), pour, run back faster. The element multiplier is the pail's size (Tide 1.3x). A pour may slip unless steadied (a base chance and the dodge): only 30% goes in, and a slip may splash half of what spilled into a neighbouring barrel. An Ember member's pour may steam 1.5% off a neighbour. The actor's meter is its pail (the client draws it full or empty). Score: the barrel in percent; level barrels share.
- **Crate Tower.** The climber is the member that climbs best (Speed, `smallBp` for a young creature), the holder the steadiest, the fetcher the rest; the team's tempo is the members' mean climb and its steadiness their mean HP with the element and role multipliers (a Guard 1.25x, Grove 1.15x). A tower is safe up to `safeCrates` (7) times its steadiness, then each crate over adds 35% to the wobble chance; Grove members cut it (`rootBp`). An unheld wobble topples the top crate and may knock a neighbour's off. Score: crates; at the same height, the tower that got there first ranks first (the first numbers, a plain per-crate wobble, made the end a random walk round each tower's limit: Spearman 0.4).
- **Kite Flight.** Each tick a kite climbs by the team's lift (members' Speed with the element and role multipliers, the mean; Spark 1.25x) and sinks by 8% of its height, so it settles where its lift holds it. Gusts dip a kite unless an anchor holds (a base 20%, 30% per Guard or Mire member, at most two); lightning strikes the highest kite of a team with a Spark member; neighbouring kites within 8 px may tangle unless a member dodges (both drop and stop climbing for 3 ticks). Score: the height at the end, 4 px a metre (an average over the last seconds would have been smoother but would not match the live board at the end).
- **Aptitude.** Each kind's aptitude sits next to its rules (`fillAptitude`, `stackAptitude`, `kiteAptitude`) and `gamesAptitude` calls them. Crate Tower's weighs the hold three quarters and the climb a quarter, which is what decides a tower's height.
- **Commentary.** New keys `fill.*`, `stack.*`, `kite.*` (start, the chaos lines, the lead, "ten seconds left", win and draw). The lead and the last ten seconds are important; spills, splashes, steam, topples, knocks, gusts, zaps and tangles are twists (at most three of a line a show, so 16 teams never crowd out the rest).
- **Simulator.** `pnpm sim:games`: every target of the three new events passes (Bucket Brigade 42.8 s, Spearman 0.83; Crate Tower 42.8 s, 0.65; Kite Flight 42.2 s, 0.66). Records store `percent` and `crates` as units.

## 2026-10-23: Valley Games fit their parent, the Expeditions map's side panel for the leaderboard, one favour line per favour

Misa: "Make sure each game arena fits inside its parent container", and "make the game arena behave like the expedition map: same components please, because the side drawer is undoubtedly the same", and the favour popovers should say one generic line.

- **No breakouts.** The show no longer steps out of the card (full-bleed on a phone, 80 px each side from 900 px). The arena (stands and scene) fits its parent's content width with `gamesFit` (the largest whole scale, then stretched with pixelated canvases), at most 3x (576 px), centred. The workbench's Games scene pages lost their bleed too.
- **One wide layout for both screens.** `ui/WideLayout` (the main area, full width until there is a panel, then two columns with a 24 rem panel) and `ui/SidePanel` (gold heading, optional X, a body that scrolls, an optional pinned footer, or a bare box for a card with its own heading) were taken out of the Expeditions world map; `PlacePanel` and `ExpeditionWorld` use them with no visual change (screenshots identical, byte for byte). `WIDE_QUERY` (60 rem) moved to `ui/wide-layout.ts` with `useWide`; the app card widens to 84 rem while any `.side-layout.wide` shows (`components/side-panel.css`), and the workbench frame mirrors it.
- **The show.** Below 60 rem the leaderboard is a list under the arena; from 60 rem the arena is the main area and the leaderboard is in the side panel, titled with the event's name and with no X (it stays while the show does, live or replay). Before the show (the stands and the countdown) there is no board, so no side panel and no wider card.
- **Favour help.** The per-event `favourNotes` are gone (schema, content, the retired events, `gamesFavourNote`). The content has one `favourHelp` map instead, a plain line per favour tag, the same in every event ("Small creatures have an advantage here.", "Sturdy …" for HP, "Guards have an advantage here.", "Ember creatures have an advantage here."); the schema requires one for every favour and nothing else. The popover keeps the icon, the favour's name, that line and the link to the Valley Games section of How to play.
- **Overflow audit.** A Playwright audit (kept outside the repo) loaded every Games workbench page and state at 320, 360, 390, 412, 768 and 1100 px and checked for sideways scrolling, boxes past a clipping ancestor, the card's content box or the viewport, and text past its box; it is clean.

## 2026-10-24: Valley Games polish: crowding solved at display time, a calm leaderboard, one icon box

The "After v1" polish list in `valley-games.md`, Misa's items 1 to 4 and most of "Seen while building".

- **Crowded big fields are a client layout layer, not an engine change.** `games/show-layout.ts` spreads positions when `prepareEvent` reads the stored beats, so results, records and old shows stay as they were. Above 16 solo entrants creatures are drawn at 0.75 size (`is-small`, body only; the "you" marker follows); team events above 8 teams stand in ranks with gaps (Pumpkin Push and Log Carry: wider columns and crews further back; Crate Tower and Kite Flight: three ranks; Bucket Brigade: two, its pond is in front); Melon Run above 8 runners starts in staggered rows of three whose sideways offset fades out over the first 160 px, so the finish is the engine's. Boards rank by the engine's own x (`PosKey.rx`), never the drawn one.
- **A calm leaderboard.** `games/board-calm.ts`: two neighbouring rows swap only after the swap has held for 700 ms of show time (ticks are 250 to 1000 ms, so a one-tick swap never shows and a real overtake lands within about one commentary beat). It is driven by the show clock; a seek back, a jump over 1 s or a new event starts again from the true order, and the last tick and the podium show the exact order. Moves slide (FLIP), off with reduced motion. Board rows are one grid, so the score column never shrinks.
- **Commentary wraps instead of cutting.** The commentary box is always two lines high; a line that does not fit on one wraps to the second, so owners stay and the layout does not jump. Only a line that does not fit in two lines ends in "…".
- **One icon box and the app's spacing scale on the Games tab.** Favour icons and the picker's size marks share `--ge-icon` (1.5 rem); the size marks form one column whether or not the Good fit badge shows. Spacing snaps to the app's 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 rem steps. The Good fit tick sprite lost its opaque square.
- **No level in the Games picker.** `ui/CreatureList` takes `level={false}`; only the Games entry picker uses it.
- **Small items.** Team event cards carry a "Team" glossary tag (`games-team`). The rollup counts `games_entry` and `games_buy` as a `games` feature, adds `games.*` counters and the Prize Stall's medal spend as a flow (medal earnings are not tracked per player yet). `runCore` in the worker tests seeds every live dice (`readinessRng` stays live on purpose).

## 2026-10-22: The live finale plays about three times slower

Misa: the last finale "was over very quick"; "make it run much slower so people actually have time to react and see what's going on", about 8 minutes, with no pause between rounds.

- **One more pace, not a pause.** The engine's playback durations are now a `Pace` (`packages/engine/src/playback.ts`): `CLASSIC_PACE` is the old numbers, `SLOW_PACE` the live finale's. At the slow pace a plain beat is 2 s (busiest 2.6 s), a boss action is telegraphed for 1.5 s, the "Round N" intro is 1.5 s (at its own speed, Misa 2026-10-23; it was 2.5 s), the tail 4 s, and a round is 20 to 45 s. Effects stay on screen 50% longer (`fxBp`).
- **Measured, not guessed.** On the simulator's finales (the readiness sample: 432 fights) the classic pace gave a median finale of 2:13 and a median round of 12.5 s, which matches "over very quick".
