At the slow pace a 12-round finale takes 6.2 to 8.3 minutes (median 7.3), a round 28 to 45 s (median 40). A finale that runs into enrage rounds can take up to 3 rounds more.
- **Stored fights keep their pace.** `raid_live.pacing` gets a third value: 2 is content pacing at the slow pace, and every new fight (real and practice) stores it. 0 (fixed rounds) and 1 (classic content pacing) are unchanged, so a fight that is on while this deploys keeps its schedule. No migration: the column exists. The server schedules by `paceOfPacing(pacing)`, the snapshot carries `pacing`, and the client plays the round with the same pace, so the clock and the stage still agree. `round_ms` stores the slow pace's 45 s upper bound.
- **Replays and the dungeon stay at the classic pace.** They have their own speed controls, and nobody needs time to react in a replay.
- **The 5 minute cap of 2026-10-11 is replaced** by this: the longest 12 regular rounds are now 9 minutes.

## 2026-10-22: The live finale is pushed over the live socket

The finale polled `GET /api/raid/live` every 2 s. The lounge's WebSocket push has been in production since 2026-10-20, so the finale now rides the same machinery (`finale-live.md`).

- **One socket module with topics, one ticket mechanism.** A socket has one topic, chosen by the server: `t:lounge` or `t:finale:<week>:<party>` (tags `u:<userId>` and `t:<topic>`). The ticket string is unchanged; the topic sits next to the user id in the object's in-memory ticket book, so a client cannot choose it. `POST /api/finale/ticket { week, party }` (strict schema) needs the current week and membership of that party (403 otherwise). Each upgrade route (`/api/lounge/live`, `/api/finale/live`) opens only its own kind of ticket. Pushes go to one topic's sockets only; the lounge's messages, limits (64 per guild; 3 per player, now per topic kind, so a player can have a lounge and a finale socket) and tests are unchanged. `LiveMessage` stays `v: 1` and gains `{ type: 'finale', snapshot }`.
- **The alarm is the broadcaster.** Nothing ticks, rounds come from the clock. `desiredAlarmAt` gains one candidate, the next round start (or lobby opening, start, end) of any party with an open finale socket; `alarm()` pushes after `catchUpRaid()` when a party's stage moved. With no finale socket the schedule is exactly what it was, and the object still hibernates between rounds. Also pushed: to a party's other sockets after Fire, Hold, Unhold or a practice start (the actor gets the HTTP answer), and to the party when a socket opens or closes (the "watching" names; an open socket counts as watching).
- **Per-player snapshots, built by the poll route's code.** Each socket gets `raid.liveSnapshot(its own user)`: the seed and other players' private data never leave the server. A frame can be large (all events up to the round), so the client takes up to 1 MB for a finale frame.
- **Polling stays as the fallback and the safety net.** While the socket is live the client drops to a poll every 15 s (plus one look 2.5 s after the next boundary if no push came); a dropped or refused socket brings the 2 s poll back at once. "Newer snapshot wins" covers both paths.
- **Flag `FINALE_LIVE`:** on locally and in staging, **off in production**. Reason: the finale is the one moment a bug is costly, pacing is being changed in parallel, and nobody has watched a finale on the socket inside Discord yet. Turn it on after Misa has watched a practice finale on staging (one line in `wrangler.jsonc` and in the test that pins it).

## 2026-10-22: Guards have a reach

Misa found that one Guard per row was enough: a Guard stepped in for an aimed hit on any creature in its whole zone row, and the boss's plain attack went straight for the Guard of the front-most row. A Guard now **protects the creatures near it**, and where it stands matters.

- **One reach for both protections.** A Guard protects the creature directly to its left and directly to its right in its own row (1 each side). The same reach decides who it takes aimed hits for (`guardFor`) and whom it covers from area hits (`coverGuardFor`); the old "beside, plus `coverReach` one further" cover logic is replaced. Reach never crosses a zone edge or the end of the board, and an empty board slot breaks it (the walk of `zoneNeighbours`); a knocked-out creature still holds its slot, so it does not. The dungeon's line of three uses the same walk with no zones, so the default is exactly what it was.
- **Per side, and changeable.** `SlotMods.reachLeft` and `reachRight` are changes added to the base of 1; each side ends up between 0 and 3 (`guardReachOf`). Three sources add up: gear lines, a talent, and the Warden's Watch three-piece bonus (set effect `coverReach` renamed `guardReach`: +1 each side, "Protects one more creature on each side of it.").
  - Lines (Guard-only, Charm-only, rolled): **Wide stance** (+1 each side, rare, item level 10), **Long arm (left)** and **Long arm (right)** (+2 on that side, epic, item level 15), **Flank guard (left)** and **Flank guard (right)** (reach becomes 2 on that side and 0 on the other, rare, item level 10). A line names its side because a card has no room for a choice; two lines are clearer than one that rolls a side. They use a new line effect, `reach` (`left` and `right` changes), so no engine knowledge leaks into the data.
  - Talent: **Wide as a Puddle** (Wide stance) in the Mudlump pool, the Guard line's pool, which had room (7 of 8).
- **Reach only for the plain attack.** The boss's plain attack (and a slam aimed `front`) goes for the front-most standing creature, the rule it already used with no Guard, and a Guard steps in only if that creature is in its reach. A Lance still stops at the first Guard in its lane. With several Guards in reach, the existing `pickGuardWith` rule stands (left first without dice, the slot's die with rules).
- **Stored fights replay as they were.** The new rule is a field of the stored combat rules, `rules.guardRule: 'reach'` (not a field of `FightInput`: the rules are already what every builder copies, so the raid, the dungeon run input and every sim fight get it from `liveFightRules()` with no extra wiring, and a fight stored without it plays the whole-zone rule, byte for byte). The reach itself is frozen into each creature's sheet (`reachLeft`, `reachRight`) like `coverReach` was, so later gear changes cannot change a stored fight; the old `coverReach` is still read by old fights and ignored by new ones. `replay-rules.test.ts` is untouched except that one pinned sheet now carries the new fields, and its 200 hashes did not move, which proves an old-shaped input plays the same; `guard-reach.test.ts` proves the new rule.
- **Boards show it.** `guardReachSlots` (`zones.ts`) is the one pure function that walks a Guard's reach; the fight and `affects` (the boards' shading and the picker's line) both call it, so the shading is exactly the slots the fight protects. A slot with a Guard in it is never shaded (a hit aimed at a Guard stays on it). The server sends a Guard's reach on its board tiles and team creatures and on the collection's creatures (`reach`, `guardReach`; Guards only), so the shade and the lines ("Takes the boss's hits for the 2 creatures on its left") show the real gear.
- **Balance:** the simulator ran before and after (`pnpm sim:quick`, seed "leet") and nothing was retuned: the typical group's finale win rate went from 94.6% to 91.2%, and the Guard role's worth (win points lost with the Guard off, spread Guards) from 4.14 to 2.07. The numbers are in the PR.

## 2026-10-22: Lounging time is stored at every check

Misa: "the lounge experience counting is definitely flawed in some way". The cause: company time collected in memory until it made one XP (2 minutes), while the lounge check runs a minute apart. The live sockets (2026-10-19) use the Hibernation API, which lets the runtime evict the object while it sleeps between two alarms, and then the collected minute was lost every time: two players could sit together for an hour and earn nothing. A test that restarts the object between every check paid 0 XP for 20 minutes together before the fix and 10 after.

- **Every check stores what it credits.** Whole XP go to the lounger and the remainder goes to `lounge_company.carry_ms` in the same transaction, which already existed for the remainder. Nothing is held in memory, so the "flush at the player's own request" is gone too.
- **Cost:** one small transaction per player in company per minute (at most about 15 in a guild), plus the XP row every 2 minutes. That is fine for a Durable Object's SQLite.
- **Kept:** company is still needed at two checks in a row (a new arrival earns from their second check), and the 3 minute gap cap stays.

## 2026-10-22: The boss's move track, and fixed places for buffs and debuffs

Misa: boxes of moves that "come up from underneath", like notes in Guitar Hero, so players see a move coming; an empty box when the boss does nothing special; several moves side by side; tap (or hover with a mouse) for what a move does. Buffs and debuffs: no titles, always in the same place, with up and down triangles.

- **The track** (`move-track.ts`, `stage/MoveTrack.tsx`) sits left of the boss in the shared stage, so the raid replay, the live finale and the dungeon all have it. Five boxes: this round on top (taller, lit red with the move's name when one is due), the next four under it. Every special move comes on a fixed schedule (`every N` rounds), so the track is exact from the content and the round number alone, like the forecast.
- **Kinds:** a plain move is one box (it strikes at the boss's turn, after the creatures). A charged move is a long block over the round it marks and the round it lands (the engine announces it at the start of the round before). Backdraft's trap has a dashed edge (armed from the start of the round, for the whole round). A move that comes every round shows in the top box only: a copy in every box would hide the moves that do come.
- **Timing stays as it is:** the boss acts at the end of the round, after the creatures; only charges (marked at the start) and Backdraft (armed at the start) differ. No moves strike mid-round: the engine has no such timing and the track could not show it simply.
- **Buffs and debuffs:** the boss's effects stand in a column at its right, buffs at the top and debuffs at the bottom, with no titles; each chip has a ▲ (buff) or ▼ (debuff) in its corner, so colour is not the only cue. On a creature, buffs run along the top of the sprite and debuffs along its bottom.
- **Kept for now:** the "Next round: ..." line under the HP bar, which says which row or creatures a move aims at; Misa to decide whether it stays.

## 2026-10-22: Four looks for the move track; the boss's attacks fly

Misa, on the first move track: "the right idea but the UI looks clunky and rough"; a move used every round should show in every round's box; the lines under the boss that announce what happens are redundant with the track; the boss and the raid roster are the main focus, so the moves can be smaller and less pronounced. Four versions to choose from, and better attack animations: "a slight curved boomerang shaped arc of glowing light that glides at a creature", a white flash and big damage numbers, special and area moves with their own flavour, overlapping numbers are fine, reuse and tint rather than add files.

- **Four looks** (`TrackLook`, the workbench's Stage page has a `track` control): `whisper` (no frames, small glyphs fading with distance, a gold tick on this round; quietest), `glass` (faint see-through boxes fading with distance, a smooth glide, a soft red glow when a move is due; the default for now), `ladder` (crisp pixel boxes, a stepped rise, this round framed in red), `arcade` (bigger tiles, the due box pulses and the arriving move pops in; loudest). All are smaller than the first version. The markup is shared; each look is a set of custom properties and a few rules in `move-track.css`.
- **Every-round moves are in every box** (Lightning Rod, Backdraft).
- **Removed:** the "Next round: …" forecast line and the attack label under the boss's HP bar. The track names this round's moves, its moves explain themselves on tap or hover, and the board still outlines the row or creatures a move aims at.
- **Every number shows.** The rule that hid the small numbers of a move that hit more than eight creatures is gone: when a whole row is hit it is one big moment, and nobody reads each number.
- **Attacks fly** (`stage/BossShots.tsx`, `styles/stage/shots.css`). During the boss's wind-up something flies from the boss and lands exactly when the hit does (the white flash and the numbers were already there; the boss's numbers on creatures are now bigger: 1.5rem, crits 2rem). The plain attack, a slam and a strike on the strongest: a glowing crescent on a slight curve with two fading ghosts behind it; a chain jumps from creature to creature. A zone hit, a sweep and the enrage round: a tall crescent with a glowing wash behind it sweeps across the row. A barrage and a charge landing: orbs fall from above. A lance: a spear of light down the lane. A pincer: two crescents close in from both ends. A silence: violet crescents. Each ends in an impact ring. Everything is a CSS shape tinted with the boss's element (`--shot`); the motion is the Web Animations API (curves sampled into keyframes), so there are no new image files. Nothing flies with reduced motion. The old straight beams are gone.
- **Timing:** a shot flies for at most 900 ms at the end of the wind-up (the slow pace's wind-up is 1.5 s, so the reticles show first; the classic 600 ms wind-up of replays is all flight).
- **Workbench:** a new `BossShots` page plays a seeded raid fight round after round (any boss, party size and rounds, either pace, optionally the boss's turns only), to watch the attacks in motion.

## 2026-10-23: Themed boss attacks in pixel style, the countdown track, quieter marks

Misa, on the flying attacks: the projectiles were "all over the place", flying in from off screen; tint them by the boss's colour, element or the move's theme; the move track and the status effects were still "boxes in boxes on boxes"; the boss targets creatures "with just boxes"; be more imaginative ("vines coming in from below to tangle an entire row and shake them", "a meteor falling from off-screen to smash and shatter a creature and its neighbours"); the round intro should play at full speed. He asked for a few agents with different personalities to review the result and for the work to follow their feedback. Four reviewers (a raid leader, a casual phone player, an accessibility reviewer and a pixel-art director) looked at screenshots and stills of every boss's attacks.

- **Every boss move has its own animation** (`move-fx.ts` picks a recipe and a theme for each move; `stage/boss-fx.ts` plays it; `stage/BossFx.tsx` mounts it in the stage's strike layer; `stage/BossShots.tsx` and `shots.css` are gone). Recipes: a crescent (the plain attack, in the boss element's colours), vines bursting from the ground and coiling round each target (Bramble Bind), thorny vines growing in from both ends of a row (Briar Pincer), lightning from a dark cloud (Thunderclap) or jumping from creature to creature (Lightning Rod, Static Field), water, mud or lava surging up under a row (Undertow, Sinking Mud, a charged wave), a boulder falling from the sky and shattering (Bog Lob), the boss leaping and landing (Belly Flop), a ring of embers tightening (Coil Crush) or round the boss (Backdraft), embers or seeds raining, a lobbed blob, a water jet down a lane, a swarm, bubbles. Hit creatures shake. A move the table does not know falls back on its kind and the boss's element. A test checks that every boss and dungeon foe move in the content has a look.
- **Pixel style** (the reviewers' strongest point): hard edges, no soft glows or blurs, two or three colours per shape. The big things that fly (the crescent, the boulder, the blob) are small pixel sprites (`stage/fx-sprites.ts`) drawn on a canvas in the theme's colours, the same `parseSprite`/`swapPalette` the creatures use; the rest are CSS shapes tinted by a theme class. Surges are see-through with a stepped pixel crest, so the creatures and numbers show through. Lightning has a hard dark edge and flickers less. Impact rings grow in steps.
- **The countdown track is the default** (the reviewers' unanimous pick): the next four moves, each once, with the rounds left; this round's says "NOW" in a hard white-on-red badge, and this round's and the next round's moves carry their names. The other three looks stay in the workbench.
- **Status effects:** on a creature the debuffs now sit on the sprite's bottom-left (they covered the name), with a bare stack count (none for one stack); a buff's mark is an open △ in green, a debuff's a solid ▼ in red, so the shape differs and not only the colour.
- **Targeting without boxes:** while motion is allowed, a creature about to be hit gets a hard red bar on the ground and a hollow red chevron hopping over its head (not the solid ▼ of a debuff); a creature the splash will catch gets a dashed amber bar; a row about to be hit a red bar along its floor. With reduced motion nothing plays, so the old outlines stay.
- **Quieter roster:** your creatures keep a soft tint and the "you" tag, without the gold frame; the acting order under each creature is in a muted colour; a Shield Wall is a faint green tint with a solid floor line instead of a dashed box.
- **Full-speed round intro:** the slow pace's round intro is 1.5 s, the classic one's; the rest of the slow pace is unchanged.
- **Not done:** the target row in the track (a raid leader asked for "R4 Undertow: FRONT"): a move's popover says what it hits, and the board marks it when it winds up.

Misa, after trying the pixel attacks on staging: Backdraft is a lasting effect that "pops" on the first reaction, so show it on the boss, "a semi transparent shield that overlays the boss, like a magical barrier that breaks when a reaction hits", and make it "a more generic but configurable barrier" for every similar effect on every foe. On a phone the move list took "a ton of screen space that should belong to the boss": the boss should always sit in the middle of the stage, the track should lean on icons, with tiny text if any, and each round should be a row with its moves side by side.

- **One barrier for every lasting move** (`stage/Barrier.tsx`, `styles/stage/barrier.css`). The ops that raise one are `BARRIER_OPS` in `replay.ts` (today `reflect_first_reaction`: Backdraft, Molten Shell and the Thorns room). The replay state keeps it: it is raised when its move is armed, broken by the first reaction that meets it (reflected, or turned aside by a gear set), and gone when the round ends. Its look is configured per move in `move-fx.ts` (`BARRIER_LOOK`: a theme for the colours and a pattern, `hex` a magical lattice, `shell` plates, `thorns` a briar cage), and a new barrier move with no entry gets the foe's element and `hex`. It is a see-through pixel dome with a hard edge and a stepped shine; it forms in steps and, when it breaks, it flashes and is gone while its pieces fly off the foe and the reaction is thrown back at the creature as a bolt (the `backlash` recipe, played for the reflected hit). With reduced motion it simply shows and hides. Tests cover the state, that every barrier move in the content has a look, and the stage drawing it.
- **The track is a row per round** (the `rows` look, now the default): this round on top with a tiny "NOW" (white on red when a move comes), the next rounds below with only the rounds to go, each round's moves as icons side by side, an empty round as a dot, a charge faint in the round it marks and full in the round it lands. The other looks stay in the workbench.
- **The boss is always in the middle**: the boss row is three columns, the two sides equal, so the track and the effects float beside the picture without pushing it.
- **The track goes once the fight is over** (the foe or every creature is down, or the last round ended).
- **The boss's mark** is the element's icon and the mark's name with an ink outline: no "marked" ring glyph and no frame.
- **Empty board slots** are a faint dotted line on the floor instead of a dashed box.
- **Knocked-out creatures**: a small skull in the corner; your own lose most of their tint and the "you" tag fades.

## 2026-10-24: The barrier is shimmering triangles; the track points at this round

Misa, on the first barrier: its border was too pronounced; build it instead from small equilateral triangles whose opacity and saturation shift and shimmer, with no border, and on a shatter let the triangles fly out and fade. On the track, "NOW" was bulky and out of line with the other rows' numbers: a small right-pointing triangle that moves a little, like the Valley Games' marker over your own creatures.

- **The barrier is one small canvas** (`stage/Barrier.tsx`) of equilateral triangles cut to a dome (`stage/barrier-shards.ts`, pure and tested). Each triangle has its own slow pulse of opacity and drift of colour between the theme's main and light colours; a glint sweeps across now and then; the triangles at the dome's edge are a little brighter, so the shape reads with no border. The triangles are big (about 20 px a side, so about 6 to 7 across a boss and 20 across a phone: Misa found 9 px read as pixels). On a shatter it flashes, and every triangle flies outward, turns, falls a little and fades (750 ms); the canvas reaches 100 px past the dome on every side so the pieces fly free over the stage (only the stage's own edge clips them). Performance: a few dozen triangles on one canvas, redrawn only while a barrier shows, at most twice the CSS size on a high-density screen; with reduced motion it is drawn once and goes when broken. The patterns are now `glass` (the default), `plates` (bigger, slower, more solid: Molten Shell) and `thorns` (small and quick with a bright edge).
- **This round's pointer** on the track is the Valley Games' `YOU_MARKER` sprite at 1x, turned to point right, nudging a pixel back and forth; gold, red while a move comes this round. The other rows keep their round counts, in line with it.

Misa, on the triangle barrier: slightly smaller triangles, a shield big enough to spill around the boss (it sat on top of it and was hard to see), about 10% less opaque, a hexagon rather than the rough square, and a shimmer that starts at the top left and travels to the bottom right with a nicer timing.

- **A regular hexagon** (flat top and bottom) of whole triangles, its corners on the triangles' grid so the outline is clean (`barrier-shards.ts`). Its side is a whole number of triangles, sized from a box 2.5rem wider than the foe's picture on each side and centred on the sprite, so it spills well around the foe. The outer ring of triangles is a little brighter (two rings for the Thorns room's briar cage).
- **Triangles** are 16 px a side (21 for Molten Shell's plates, 13 for the Thorns room); their strength is 10% lower.
- **The shimmer** is a bright band that sweeps across the diagonal from the top left to the bottom right in 1.6 s with a quintic ease in and out (it gathers, rushes through the middle, settles), then rests 2.2 s. Each triangle keeps its own slow pulse of opacity and colour under it.

## 2026-10-24: Faster lint, a staging check, check:changed and other fixes from the retro

From the retro of the raid finale visuals (`docs/retros/2026-10-24-raid-finale-visuals.md`); Misa asked for smarter fixes than more memory.

- **Lint per package, with a cache.** Type-aware lint builds a TypeScript program per package; one `eslint .` held all of them at once (about 4 to 5 GB) and ran out of Node's default heap (2.2 GB here). Measured alone, the client peaks at 1.5 GB at the default heap, the worker 1.7, the shared packages 1.1. Each package now has its own `lint` (ESLint's content cache in its `node_modules/.cache`), the root runs them two at a time plus the few root scripts: 90 s cold with no memory flags, 5 s warm, 2 s with one file changed. CI starts without a cache, so it always lints everything. Not the files' fault: no generated or data files are linted (JSON, dist and node_modules are not), and the cost is the type-aware rules, which stay. Tests are about a third of the client's code and most of the worker's; their type-aware rules (floating promises, unsafe any) catch real test bugs, so they stay too.
- **What is on staging, on demand.** `apps/worker/scripts/deployed.mjs` reads the branch and commit from the public `/api/config` of the two fixed game hosts (read-only, no URL from the caller; the repo still denies curl and wget), checks them against a ref and can wait until a commit is live. The new `staging` skill says when to use it; CLAUDE.md's deploy step uses it instead of asking Misa to read the footer. The deploy workflow's last step runs the same check, so a green run means the commit is live.
- **`pnpm check` and `pnpm check:changed`.** `check` is every check in order; `check:changed` runs lint, typecheck and tests for the packages changed since `origin/main` and those that depend on them (pnpm's `...[origin/main]` filter, the root excluded), so a worker change runs the worker's API contract tests while iterating.
- **The workbench registry test** asks for a page only for PascalCase exports of `src/ui`; helpers and constants there no longer fail it.
- **`scripts/shoot.mjs`** shoots a workbench page at 390 and 1100 px with a labelled sheet; `ux-patterns.md` asks for both widths every round.
- **`docs/design/review-brief.md`**: Misa's standing priorities for every UX review agent; a suggestion that trades against one goes to Misa as a choice.
- **The test page keeps style values**, so components driven by CSS custom properties are tested directly.

## 2026-10-24: The main navigation is a dock on a phone and a rail on a desktop, with a HUD on top

Misa chose "Option 1: dock and rail" from three mock-ups ("let's build it now. The phone rail might need some work to make it a top class UX experience that scales with new features"), and wants the game to move "more into game territory", fully playable on a phone, and on a desktop toward the Expeditions screen's wide layout. This replaces the six-tab bar (roadmap item "Main menu redesign": with Games the bar barely fitted 360 px). The mock-up page (`MainNavMockups`, options 2 and 3 as well) is gone; its Option 1 is now the real thing and has a workbench page.

- **One list of places feeds everything.** `navPlaces(alerts)` (`nav-places.ts`) turns `TABS` and the pips of `tab-alerts.ts` into `{ id, label, glyph, pip }`; the dock, the rail and the More drawer read only that, and the logic never looks at what an id means. A new feature is one `TABS` entry (and its alert); a future map-based home can be one more place (a screen that is the map) or replace the dock's content, with no change to the dock rules. `Tab` and the current-tab state in `Game.tsx` (`useState<Tab>('collection')`, no URL) are unchanged, as are the pips and their timers (`use-tab-pips.ts`).
- **Phone: a bottom dock, 56 px buttons.** Pixel buttons fixed to the bottom edge, in thumb reach: 4 rem high (a 3.5 rem button, 0.25 rem round it) plus `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` (and Discord's `--discord-safe-area-inset-bottom`), every button at least 44 px wide and 56 px high. The card gets the dock's height as bottom padding (`main.card:has(.main-nav.dock)`), and `scroll-padding` keeps scrolled-to things clear of the HUD and the dock, so nothing hides behind either. The pip moved inside the button's top right corner (it used to sit over the edge): the dock touches the window's edges and the rail scrolls, so an overhanging pip would be cut off.
- **Labels: a word under every picture, always.** Misa's brief was a dock readable without guessing icons. Showing the word only on the active place would leave five unlabelled pictures; so every button is a picture over its word (the small type step, as the old tabs). To make that fit, widths are measured, not fixed: each button keeps the width of its word (never under 44 px) and the buttons share what is left, so six places fit a 360 px phone with "Expeditions" in full (measured 351 of 352 px; 320 px gets five and More). Fixed equal slots would have needed 62 px for "Expeditions" and pushed Games and Dungeon into More at 360 px.
- **More rule.** `useDockFit` draws every place at its natural size once, reads the widths and the room (before the first paint; again on resize, on a word change and when the pixel font has loaded), and `dockCapacity` picks the most buttons that fit with a More button. If they all fit there is no More. Otherwise the first places in list order fill the dock, and **the active place is always in the dock**: if it is not among them it takes the last button (the other buttons keep their places, so the dock does not reshuffle) and the place it displaced goes to More. More opens the shared `ui/Drawer` ("More places": one row per remaining place with its picture, its word and, when it has one, its pip with the reason in words); picking a row goes there and closes the drawer. More carries a **roll-up pip** when any place inside it has one, named for a screen reader ("More, Raid: creatures to place", or "More, 3 places need you"). Order stays the list's (rising danger), so the places a player uses least are the ones that end in More.
- **Desktop (from 60 rem, `WIDE_QUERY`): a left rail.** The same buttons as a column, picture and word side by side (8.5 rem), every place always listed (no More; the rail scrolls inside itself on a short window and sticks while the page scrolls). The HUD runs across the top of the card, the living room band under it, then the rail and the screen. The card is 58 rem (the old 48 rem plus the rail); a screen with a side panel (Expeditions, the Games show) still widens it to 84 rem as before. Known: switching between those two widths moves the rail sideways; it goes away when more screens get the wide layout (Misa's direction), so no side panels were invented in this change.
- **The HUD** (`Hud.tsx`) replaces the title row: the name of the place you are in (the page's `h1`), the currencies with their numbers (`CurrencyPile` now shows both amounts with their pictures, "1,240", and "12.3K" from 10,000 up; the exact numbers stay in the spoken label and the popover) and the "?". On a phone it sticks to the top edge; on a desktop it is plain. The "Leet Creatures sigma" title shows only before a game is on screen (loading, errors, choosing a starter). The living room band is unchanged (same code, now under the HUD; its `.room-head` overlay for the title row was removed).
- **Keyboard and screen readers.** A `nav` landmark ("Places") of plain buttons in the normal tab order, `aria-current="page"` on the place you are in, the pip's reason in the button's name, a focus ring drawn inside the button. The old `tablist` roles, the `tabpanel` wrapper and the arrow-key roving (`tabAfterKey`) went: this is navigation between screens, not tabs inside one, and a plain tab order is simpler and expected.
- **Removed:** `TabBar.tsx`, `tabs.css`, `tabAfterKey`, the TabBar workbench page, `LivingRoom`'s `children` and its title-row CSS, the combined currency icon. **Added:** `MainNav`, `Hud`, `nav-places.ts`, `use-dock-fit.ts`, `main-nav.css`, a `more` glyph (three dots), workbench pages `MainNav` (frame with the real HUD, dock or rail and the real Creatures, Games and Expeditions screens on fixture data; presets for each active place, no pips, nine places, a place from More active, ten places) and `Hud`.

## 2026-10-24: The phone dock is three group buttons with upward sub-menus

Misa tested the six-button dock on his phone (Discord on Android): "there's an awkward space below the bottom bar but that space is also rounded off by the phone corner bezels. I'm thinking six items is too much to fit here. Can we have three buttons instead? Something like the home / adventure split ... Having those buttons open cool sub menus (upward) could also be a thing." This changes the phone part of the entry above; the desktop rail, the HUD, `navPlaces`, the pips and their timers stay.

- **Groups are data.** `TABS` gives every place a `group`, and `NAV_GROUPS` (`tabs.ts`: id, label, glyph) lists the groups in order: **Home** (Creatures, Workshop), **Adventure** (Expeditions, Dungeon, Raid) and **Games** (Valley Games). `navGroups(places)` (`nav-places.ts`) turns the two lists into the groups the navigation draws; a group with one place is `direct`. A new place is one `TABS` entry with a group; a future village or home map is one more place in Home. New glyphs `home` (a house) and `peaks` (two peaks) are the pictures of Home and Adventure; Games reuses the trophy. The `more` glyph went.
- **Phone: three wide buttons, a picture over a word, the active group gold.** Three buttons always fit 320 px (equal thirds, "Adventure" is the longest word), so the measuring logic (`use-dock-fit.ts`, `splitDock`, `dockCapacity`), the More button and the More drawer are gone. A group's pip is the roll-up of its places' pips (`groupAlert`: one place named with its reason, several counted, a one-place group is that place's own pip), spoken in the button's name.
- **Sub-menus open upward as a tray.** Tapping a group with several places opens a tray of big buttons (picture, word, the pip's reason in words; 56 px high) above the dock, with a short stepped rise (off with reduced motion). The current place is gold and `aria-current="page"`. Picking a place goes there and closes the tray; a tap outside (a transparent scrim under the dock), Escape, or the group button again closes it. The group button has `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`; focus moves into the tray (the current place, else the first) and goes back to the group button when it closes by keyboard or pick (not after a tap outside, which has moved on). One tray at a time. Games, a group of one place, goes straight there. Own markup rather than the shared `ui/Drawer` (a modal sheet) or `ui/Popover` (a pointer arrow, a dialog): this is navigation, not a dialog, so the focus is not trapped and the dock stays usable.
- **The dead space.** The dock now says it reaches the bottom edge: the safe-area inset is padding inside the bar (`--dock-safe` is `max(env(safe-area-inset-bottom), --discord-safe-area-inset-bottom)`, never the sum), so the bar runs on into the rounded corners. The body's own bottom padding (Discord's inset again) is dropped while a dock is on screen, so the inset is not counted twice under the card either. The staging DEV button sat on the dock's buttons; with a dock on screen it now sits above the dock.
- **Desktop rail** still lists every place, now under small headings (Home, Adventure, Games), no sub-menus.
- **Workbench:** the MainNav page has presets for each active group and place, the Home and Adventure trays open, no pips, and an extra place in Home; the nine/ten places presets went with More.

## 2026-10-24: The dock reaches the bottom edge, and the lounge is a button in the middle

Misa tested the three-button dock on a real phone (Discord on Android, full screen): the dock still sat above an empty dark strip of about 45 px ("The menu could start from the way bottom of the screen"). He also said: "I'm also starting to think if we should get rid of the lounge on mobile and move it into a menu. ... the middle button in the menu could be a primary button type that says 'Lounge'. The left button could be something that refers to creatures, items, workshop. The right could be the current adventures button but it could include the games section too." This changes the phone part of the entries above; the desktop (room band, rail, HUD), `navPlaces`, the pips and their timers stay.

- **The strip.** The bar was already at `bottom: 0` with its own background, so the strip was the bar itself: it was padded by the whole safe-area inset (Discord's about 48 px), which is an empty, dark part of the bar under the buttons. `--dock-safe` is now `min(max(env(safe-area-inset-bottom), --discord-safe-area-inset-bottom), 0.5rem)`: the bar's background and border run to the last pixel and the buttons sit low. The body's bottom padding stays dropped while a dock is there. The staging DEV button sits above the dock and the Lounge's raised top, and hides while a tray is open.

The Games group is gone; its pip rolls into Adventure with the other three. The desktop rail now shows these two headings. `NAV_LOUNGE` is the Lounge entry; `dockItems` puts it in the middle of the groups, so it is data, not hand placement.
- **The Lounge is the primary button:** gold, larger, raised above the bar, with a sofa picture. To keep one gold button, the group you are in now shows a gold underline and gold word on its dark face in the dock (trays and the rail keep the gold face). The Lounge has a pip slot but no pip yet: no existing room signal means "needs you".
- **The room on a phone is a sheet.** The room band is no longer drawn on a phone (it saved the room from costing a button, but took the top of every screen). The Lounge button opens it in a bottom sheet, the shared `ui/Drawer` (`LoungeSheet`): the whole room with its loungers, swap, calendar and bubbles; closed by Escape, a tap outside, a pull on the handle or the keyboard close button. A wide window keeps the band.
- **Presence does not depend on the sheet.** The room's data (`useRoom`) and the once-per-XP state reload moved into `use-lounge-presence.ts`, called by `Game` whatever is drawn; the sheet and the band only draw. A test keeps the poll, the lookups and the reload running with the sheet shut, and shows opening and closing it starts and stops nothing.
- **Workbench:** the MainNav page has presets for each group and place, each tray open, the Lounge sheet open and a Lounge pip. `LivingRoom`'s deliberate band crop is marked `data-audit-clip`, so the audit no longer reports it.

## 2026-10-24: Home is a page: the Today board, with tilted notices and optional quiet ones

Misa chose option A, the "Today board", from two Home mock-ups: "I like the notice board style. Maybe we could go one step braver and tilt the notices a bit ... we could even animate the tilt. If there is nothing that is ongoing or needs the user's attention, let's consider dropping the entire notice to save space. A setup that allows either behavior is probably good if we ever let users control this. The coming up and valley news sections are a nice touch to bring the community together." He also asked that tapping a creature in the lounge shows its "first on server" tag. This replaces the Lounge button and sheet of the entry above the same day.

- **Home is a place** (`home`, `NAV_HOME` in `tabs.ts`, `Place` = `Home` or a `Tab`) and the default one (`DEFAULT_PLACE`): the app opens on it. The dock's middle button is **Home** (house picture, gold, raised, no pip, `aria-current` when you are there); the rail lists Home first. The groups stay (Creatures; Adventure). `LoungeSheet` and its CSS and tests are gone; `use-lounge-presence.ts` still runs from `Game` whether or not the room is drawn (its test moved with it).
- **The room:** on a phone the Home page draws the living room at the top (the shared `RoomBand`); on a wide window the band above every screen is unchanged and Home shows the board under it.
- **The board** (`home/NoticeBoard.tsx`, content from `home/board.ts` over `home-view.ts`): "Needs you" (a notice button per place with a pip, with the reason and a time that matters; none waiting: the calm line "All quiet in the valley..."), "Coming up" (the next five things on the clock: trips, jobs, the Games lock and show, the raid's next phase and finale, with real times), "The valley today" (sky and Games lineup; no news feed yet). Home refreshes the state, the Games view, the forecast and the raid view each time it is opened and re-reads the server clock every second.
- **Two options, one typed object** (`BoardSettings`; a future per-player setting, today `DEFAULT_BOARD_SETTINGS`). `tilt`: `none` flush, `still` (default) each paper leans -1.5 to 1.5 degrees (never under 0.5), the size from a hash of the notice's id (so it never changes between renders) and the side alternating with the notice's place (neighbours lean opposite ways); `sway` the same lean rocking 0.6 degrees either way over 7 s, staggered per paper, only under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` (else it is `still`). Papers hang from their pin, straighten under a finger or focus, and the board's gaps and padding leave room for the corners (audited at 320 to 1100 px). `idle`: `hide` (default) a place with nothing ongoing and nothing waiting gets no notice; `show` it gets a slim one-line notice under "Quiet places" ("Nobody is out", "No job running", "No run waiting", "This week's raid is over", "Results are in"). A place is quiet only when its data has loaded and nothing is running or waiting there. With everything quiet and hidden, the calm line and the valley line remain.
- **Raid times at start-up:** `use-raid-glance.ts` calls the existing `GET /api/raid` (no new endpoint) when the game opens and keeps only the week's schedule. A failure is silent: the raid rows are left out. The next phase is worked out from the schedule and the clock, so an old load never shows a past phase. It is a separate light fetch, not shared with the Raid tab.
- **"First on server" in the lounge:** `POST /api/room` now carries `firstOnServer` on each creature (worker: `lounger.ts`, read from the `server_firsts` records for its owner, species or stage one of its line, the jackpot record excluded as in the state view; nothing from the request). Your own lounger is worked out from your state with the same `isFirstOnServer`. The creature popover shows the same gold tag as a card.
- **Removed:** the Home mock-ups (place cards, variant B) and `placeCards`. **Workbench:** `HomePage` with presets (busy, nothing waiting, nothing going on with idle hidden or shown, tilt none/still/sway, crowded, no raid schedule); the MainNav page lost its Lounge sheet and pip presets.
- **Columns on a desktop** (Misa, on the first desktop shots: the leaning papers look "kind of wonky" when wide, better when narrow). Home takes the wide card from 60 rem (as Expeditions and the Games show), and the board keeps its papers about phone-wide: the "Needs you" and "Quiet places" notices stand in two columns from a 40 rem board and three from 56 rem (a container query on the board, so it follows the board's width, not the window's); Coming up and The valley today stand side by side (3 : 2), and only Coming up, still the widest paper, leans at half the angle. This replaces the first build's whole-board lean scale of 0.3.

## 2026-10-24: Shell polish: smaller navigation, scroll to top, Bait income in the currency popover, no footer

Misa, after testing on his phone: the dock and its sub-menus were too big; a menu item should open its page at the top, not scrolled down; bait income should sit behind the currency popover and "Recent Bait activity" go; and "remove the signed in as element and everything below it, including diagnostics".

- **Smaller navigation.** Place pictures are drawn at scale 2 (were 3) in the dock, the trays and the rail; Home keeps scale 3 and its larger word, so it stays the clear primary. Dock buttons are 3 rem high (were 3.5), Home 3.75 rem (4.25), the dock 3.5 rem (4). Tray rows are 44 px (2.75 rem, were 56 px) with the place's name in `--fs-row` (was `--fs-heading`) and the reason in `--fs-small`, the floor of the type scale; the tray is 15 rem wide (17). Touch targets stay at least 44 px.
- **Scroll to top on every change of place.** `useScrollTopOnChange(tab)` (`use-scroll-top.ts`, called in `Game`) jumps the window to the top, instant, whenever the place changes (dock, tray, rail, Home notice, a link from another screen); tapping the place you are already in also scrolls to the top. The window is the only scroller (`main.card` does not scroll), so `window.scrollTo` is the whole job.
- **Bait income in the currency popover.** The popover under the HUD's currency button now lists the newest five Bait credits (`baitIncome` in `ledger-text.ts`: Bait only, amount above zero, newest first; never a spend, never Essence), one compact line each ("+30 · leet on 2026-10-23 with 4 players", "+12 · daily allowance"). It asks `/api/ledger` (30 entries, so spends mixed in still leave five credits) every time the popover opens, as the old section did on each open. The `RecentActivity` accordion, its CSS, its test and the footer's `describeEntry` wording are gone.
- **The footer is gone.** It held: "Signed in as", the leet sync line, and a table (Environment, Server version, Branch, Guild ID, API health). Where each went: the **deployed branch, commit and environment** are `<meta name="build-branch">`, `build-commit` and `build-environment`, set from `/api/config` when the app starts (`build-info.ts`, values checked against the same shapes `deployed.mjs` accepts), and a **Build** section at the top of the staging DEV panel; `deployed.mjs` and its deploy-workflow check read `/api/config`, not the page, so they are unchanged. The **leet sync line**: a failing sync ("Leet points couldn't be loaded ... Tell the game admin.") is the one thing a player may need to report, so it shows as a warning inside the currency popover; the healthy "synced 5 minutes ago" line is dropped. Dropped with no replacement: "Signed in as", Guild ID, API health (the client no longer calls `/api/health`; the endpoint stays for uptime checks). `footer-rows.ts` and the `footer` CSS were removed; `Game` lost its `onLeetSync` prop (it reads `game.leetSync`, which its own polling keeps current).

The new stats page could include more stats like dungeons attempted, succeeded, if we have those."

## 2026-10-24: The new UX patterns, reviewed: one "frame" section in ux-patterns.md

Misa asked for an assessment of the patterns this day's navigation and Home work introduced: should they be written down, do they make old ones obsolete, are they reusable, clear in code, and will agents find them.

- **Written down, as rules, not history.** The dock and rail paragraph had grown to about 500 words of rules, measurements and history in one bullet. `ux-patterns.md` now has a section, "The frame: navigation, pages, the HUD and Home", with one short rule each: places come from one list; dock and rail; pages and Back (a place from the navigation has no Back, a page opened from another page is a drill-in with `DrillHead`, every change lands at the top); the HUD is the only bar (no footer; build facts in meta tags and the DEV panel); settings are display preferences on the device; Home is where a feature talks to the player (a notice row, a pin for a long-lived record); and a five-line "adding a feature" list. Measurements stay in the code and in the entries above.
- **Obsolete:** the tab bar, the Lounge sheet, the room band on a phone, the Expeditions tabs and explorer list, Recent Bait activity, the footer and the in-game title row (it stays only before a game starts). None of them is still described in `ux-patterns.md`; three stale code comments (the dev panel's order, the help ref, the room's wall band) were fixed.
- **Reusable:** `ui/DrillHead` now serves pages as well as drawers; `ui/Segmented` is new and shared (with a workbench page); `home/HomeBook` is the loader for a Home page. The notice board's paper look stays the board's own (the Shared components row says so), so it does not spread to ordinary panels.
- **Clear in code:** every new module opens with what it is, why and how to extend it (`MainNav`, `nav-places`, `tabs`, `use-scroll-top`, `build-info`, `home/*`, `settings/SettingsPage`, `stats-view`). The code still calls places "tabs" (`TABS`, `Tab`); `tabs.ts` says so at the top, and a rename is not worth its churn.
- **Found by agents:** `CLAUDE.md` already sends UI work to `ux-patterns.md`; the implementer brief (`.claude/agents/implementer.md`) and the reviewers' `review-brief.md` now name "The frame" in one line each, and the Shared components table has rows for a page opened from another page and for a setting.

## 2026-10-25: Home board: Journal and Stats off the board (Stats is a Collection place), a How to play pin, flush notices by default, no sway, smaller board text

Misa's feedback on the Today board: the Journal and Stats pins did not fit under "Needs you", and their teasers loaded late (they needed the profile), so the layout jumped.

- **Pins removed.** `BoardPins`, the teasers (`journalTeaser`, `statsTeaser`) and the profile load on Home are gone; Home loads nothing of its own. Stats is a place of the Collection group (Creatures, Workshop, Journal, Stats) with no Back, like the Journal. The Home sub-page machinery (`HOME_PAGES`, `HOME_PINS`, `Screen`, `homeScreen`, `homePageOf`, `navPlaceOf`) and `nav-state`'s `backTo` had no other user and were deleted; `Place` is the only screen id.
- **A "?" pin** left of the starter pin opens How to play (How to play stays in Settings too). Both corner pins share one `hb-badge` look. The board keeps its own focus ref so closing How to play returns focus to the pin.
- **No sway; flush by default.** `TiltMode` is `none` or `still`, default `none`. A saved `sway` loads as `still` (the player liked a lean).
- **Everything on the board that is about another screen opens it** (Misa): Coming up rows, Quiet places rows and the Games medals are buttons that call the same `onGo` as a notice; the valley's sky line opens the forecast card, the same popover as the room's calendar (the forecast has no screen of its own).

### 2026-10-25: A fixed top bar on phones, see-through pin shadows

- **The HUD is fixed, not sticky, on a phone** (Misa: "keep it at the top, full width of the viewport, always the same regardless of scroll position"). A sticky bar sat lower at the top of a page than once scrolled, and the strip above it (Discord's safe area) showed the page scrolling by. Now `main.card:has(.main-nav.dock) > .hud` is `position: fixed` across the window, padded down by `--discord-safe-area-inset-top` so its own colour fills the strip, and the card starts 0.75rem below it; the How to play page's head works the same way. The selector keeps workbench frames (no `main.card`) and wide windows (no dock) on the static bar.
- **Board pins:** 44 px, 0.5rem from the board's top, a shadow in see-through ink so the cork or a notice shows through it.
- **A slimmer HUD** (Misa): 2.75rem instead of 3.5rem. The currency pile, the cog and the "?" are drawn 2.25rem high; an invisible strip above and below (inside the bar's padding) keeps each a 44 px target. The cog is redrawn as an eight-toothed gear with a round hole (Misa: the old one read as a spaceship); the 8 x 8 grid drew it too rough, so it is a 10 x 10 sprite of its own (`sprites/data/hud-sprites.ts`, out of the glyph set), and its button is the currency pile's twin: the same 2.25rem height, dark box and one-line edge. The board pins' shadow is lighter (one pixel, 25% ink).
- **Taps during momentum scrolling** (Misa: "the scroll needs to stop before the menu button registers my input"). On a phone a tap on a gliding page only stops the glide and the browser sends no click. The dock's buttons, its trays and the HUD's cog now act on a touch's `pointerdown` (`ui/instant-press.ts`) and swallow the one click that may follow, wherever it lands (a closed tray, a new page), until the next press or 800 ms. Mouse, keyboard and assistive tech still use the click. Only for buttons fixed while the page scrolls: inside the page it would fire at the start of a scroll.

## 2026-10-25: Opt-in Discord DMs, derived from the game's tables

Misa chose four kinds of DM from the game's bot, all off by default: a trip comes home, a Workshop job is done, the raid finale starts in 10 minutes, the Prize Stall opens. Design in `dm-notifications.md`.

- **Derived, not hooked.** No hook goes into sending, starting a job or claiming. A scan reads the trips, jobs, raid week and stall day for events that came due for opted-in players (run in one transaction from the alarm and `ensureAlarm`, never throwing). It keeps the existing paths untouched and makes a late or repeated alarm harmless.
- **One DM per scan per player.** A player's new lines go into one message, so three trips ending together are one DM, not three pings.
- **Never twice, never old.** `dm_sent` holds one row per notified event. An event counts only if it came due after its kind was turned on (`<kind>_on_at`, so turning one on never floods old events) and within the last 12 hours (a long outage sends no stale pings). A trip that was claimed, recalled or cancelled first is never pinged; a chosen push-on pings again at its own end. The finale and stall keys include the player (`finale:<week>:<user>`), as `dm_sent` has one key space.
- **Server state, in the guild's Durable Object** (migration 41: `dm_prefs`, `dm_outbox`, `dm_sent`, and partial indexes on open trips and jobs so the alarm's "next due" lookup stays cheap). The queue, claims, backoff and give-up mirror the announcement posts; the sender is separate (`dm-send.ts`) so a DM can open a channel first, but reuses `createPostSender` for the message (no mentions, 429 handling).
- **Blocked players.** Discord answers 403 when a member does not accept DMs from this server. The player is marked blocked, nothing more is queued for them, and Settings explains how to allow DMs; changing any setting clears the block and tries again with a welcome DM. A 404 on a cached DM channel is forgotten and opened again once.
- **Welcome DM** when a player turns on their first kind (and when they try again after a block), at most once an hour. It tells them what they will get and proves DMs reach them; a block shows in the UI.
- **No sender, no DMs.** Without a configured sender (tests, local dev) nothing is queued or scheduled, and the API says `available: false`, which hides the section.
- **An exception to "settings stay on the device".** The Discord DMs section of Settings holds server settings (loaded when the page opens, saved on each change). The board's display options stay on the device. `ux-patterns.md` says so.
- **Privacy.** Logs and analytics hold kinds only (`dm_pref`, `dm_result`); no ids, tokens or text.

### 2026-10-25: One bottom bar between the phone and the wide window (prototype)

Misa asked whether the desktop's links, all in one bottom bar, could work on a screen that is neither a phone nor a desktop, and for the best breakpoint range per layout. Measured in a real window (Playwright, the real buttons, nine places, "Expeditions" the longest word): the words fit their equal share from about 600 px (no room left) and have about 8 px a side from 720 px; at 880 px it is roomy. Below 720 px the three-button dock stays (from about 600 px its buttons only get very wide). The rail is kept from 60 rem: at 720 px it would leave the page about 515 px, a phone page in desktop chrome; from 960 px the page gets about 760 px. So: under 45 rem the dock, 45 to 60 rem the bar (`BAR_QUERY`, `MainNav`'s `bar`), from 60 rem the rail. In the bar Home is first and gold but not raised (one place of nine), there are no trays, and its buttons answer a touch at once like the dock's (`instantPress`). The HUD stays fixed as on a phone (the bar is a dock). Each new place needs about 80 px more, so `BAR_QUERY` moves up with the list (Items splitting from the Workshop: about 50 rem). Misa may not merge it; it is a prototype to try.

### 2026-10-25: Evolution short of Essence shows on the creature page

A player's level 15 Cindle showed nothing about evolving: the server's `evolution.available` needs the level and the Essence, and the page drew the Evolve button only when it was true. Misa: show it greyed with the exact cost, as the ribbon does when it is short of parts.

- **The creature page** now draws the Evolve button greyed ("Evolve 20 Essence") when the creature is at its evolution level and the player has less Essence than the price, with a note under it: "Short of Essence: you have 12 / 20." and the Essence help tag (`evolveShortfall` in `creature-needs.ts`; the price is the server's).
- **The creature list's card** shows the same greyed button for a ribbon or evolution the player cannot pay yet: "Evolve 12 / 20 Essence", "Earn Bronze Ribbon 6 scrap and 2 Ember shards", or "1 more trip" when trips are what is missing. These are needs with a `blocked` reason (`creatureNeeds`), listed after what can be done now, so the card's button is never a greyed one while something else is ready. Before, a ribbon short of parts showed nothing on the card either.
- **The "Needs you" filter** counts them (Misa: it signals the player to go and get the parts). **The Home notice** does not: its words ("Cindle can evolve") promise something the player can do now, and it already left out blocked needs (away, on a job).
- **Below the level** nothing is shown, as before. A talent slot short of Bait is unchanged (no button, not in the filter). **Rule (Misa):** a greyed button and a place in "Needs you" are only for what blocks a creature's progress (a ribbon, an evolution), not for optional things like talents.
- Two card samples (talent to fill at 20, top level at 30) were a stage-1 Cindle past its line's cap of 15 still offering an evolution; they no longer carry one.

### 2026-10-25: The start-up passes run once per deployed build

Cloudflare stopped Durable Object requests for the day: the account went past the free tier's 5,000,000 `rows_read`, two days after the cleanup fix above. Production had one guild and about 8 players a day, so the reads came from work that repeats, not from big tables. A probe that counted the rows each statement read (12 players, three weeks of play) found it:

- **The cause.** `GuildCore.migrate` runs in the object's constructor, and after the numbered migrations it ran the start-up passes (gear row shapes, the gear and level conversions, missing talents, the `level_reset` lookup in `sqlite_master`). They read whole tables: 620 rows in the probe, more in production, growing with creatures, talents, gear and grants. The 2026-10-23 note left them as "a few hundred rows per start". But since the lounge's live sockets went on in production (2026-10-20) the object hibernates after a few idle seconds and starts again at the next alarm or request, and the lounge check runs the alarm every minute while anyone has the game open. So the passes ran about once a minute, all day.
- **Once per build.** The passes run on the first start of each deployed build and then a new `startup_passes` row remembers the build (`VERSION`, the git commit CI deploys). Later starts of the same build skip them: a start now reads about 43 rows. Each pass is idempotent and every deploy is a new build, so a branch switch on the shared staging database runs them again. Without a build (local dev, where `VERSION` is `dev`, and tests) they run on every start, as before. The table is made with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` where it is read, not as a numbered migration, so branches sharing staging never disagree about a number.
- **Level reset notice.** `pendingLevelReset` (every state load) asked `sqlite_master` whether its table exists (about 180 rows); it now reads the table and answers null on "no such table", as `booksByCreature` does.
- **The Games' day checks (migration 42).** Every request and alarm asked "a locked day not awarded yet?" and "entries on a day not locked yet?", and the Games tab "the latest shown day?" and "the latest awarded day?". Each read all of `games_days` (a row a day) or every entry of today's locked day. Three partial indexes on `games_days` (`games_days_unawarded`, `games_days_shown`, `games_days_awarded`) answer the first, third and fourth. `hasOpenEntries` now walks day by day (the first day with entries, is it locked, the next day), a few rows instead of a join over the entries. In the probe an alarm went from 130 rows to 30, the room check from 129 to 29 and the Games tab from 296 to 116.
- **Left as is:** the unresolved live finale lookup (about 14 rows, a row or two more each week) and the raid view's per-player talent reads (only when the raid page is open).
- **Staging counts too.** Staging runs on the same Cloudflare account, so its reads share the same daily limit.

### 2026-10-25: A read budget test and rows read in the ops report

The account went past the free tier's 5,000,000 Durable Object `rows_read` a day twice in a week. Both causes (the alarm's cleanup, the start-up passes) were whole-table reads on paths that run every minute, and both were found by a hand-made probe after the limit was hit. Nothing in the tests or the report could have shown them earlier, because no test counted rows and the report did not know about reads. Two guards now:

- **A read budget test** (`apps/worker/test/read-budget.test.ts`) seeds a guild a month old and checks the rows read by an object start, an alarm, and the six hot reads, each against a budget of about 1.5x what it read when the budget was set. A growth check adds a second month and requires the alarm and `/api/me` to read no more than a few rows more: that is what a new whole-table scan looks like, and it fails before any budget would. The budgets sit next to the measured values, so a PR that raises one has to say why. `CLAUDE.md` has the rule.
- **Rows read in production, by source and game day.** `GuildCore` wraps its `SqlStorage` in a `ReadMeter` (`read-meter.ts`) that keeps the cursors the statements return; at the end of every request and alarm it adds the sum of their `rowsRead` to `db_reads` (migration 43), one UPSERT through the unwrapped storage, so the meter reads nothing and does not count itself. Sources are `start`, `alarm`, `socket`, and `request:<area>` for a fixed list of route areas (`request:other` for anything else), never an id. The daily rollup copies a finished day into `analytics_daily` as `db.rows_read` (dim `source`), the daily cleanup drops counters older than the analytics retention, and the ops report shows rows per day against the free limit, the top sources and an alert from 80% of a day. The report workflow is unchanged: the JSON already carries every metric.
- **Accepted:** attribution is approximate when requests overlap (the cursors of two requests that interleave at an `await` go to whichever flushes first); the day's total is exact. The start's rows are added at the next flush, since the table may not exist until the migrations have run. A login (`createSession`, over RPC) is `request:session`, the ops report's own RPC `request:other`. The counters add one row written per request and alarm, far inside the 100,000 writes a day.
- **What the first measurements showed** (15 players, 30 creatures each, a month of history): the start reads 45 rows, 43 of them `MAX(version)` over `schema_version` (one per migration, no index); an alarm 19; `/api/me` 25; `/api/room` 33; `/api/dungeon/today` 78; `/api/games` 253; `/api/state` 610; `/api/raid` 3,268. The last two are not constant: `/api/state` reads a player's claimed trips and `/api/raid` every party member's equipped gear, both through an index on the player that still walks all of the player's rows, so they grow with history (+120 and +480 rows for a second month). `/api/raid` also reads every member's creatures and talents (16 players, 2,158 rows for talents alone). They are budgeted, not fixed here.

### 2026-10-25: Lounge reads: one-row lounger, XP over the socket

The lounge was the next biggest source of Durable Object rows read. While players are in the lounge the guild alarm runs the lounge check every 60 s, and for each player in the room it read the player's whole creature list twice (the lounger lookup and its idle filter). Separately, the client reloaded the whole state (`GET /api/state`, 200 to 300 rows) every 2 minutes only to move the lounge XP bar.

- **Stored lounger, one row.** `Lounge.resolve` first checks the stored `players.lounger_id` with a query that reads only that creature (exists, is the player's, not away, no open Workshop job: the same busy rule as before, now as SQL). If it passes, the list is never read.
- **Deterministic re-pick, one query.** When the stored lounger is gone or busy, one `LIMIT 1` query walks `creatures_user` from the newest-caught creature and stops at the first free one, preferring creatures that are not Rested today (a second query, run only when every free creature is Rested). The open-job check uses the existing `workshop_jobs_creature_open` index, so no migration was needed. Misa chose this: "Pick the first creature in the player's creature list that is free sorted by ... desc", replacing the random pick; `pickLounger`, the lounger rng and their tests are removed. `Lounge.choose` keeps its 404 and 409 answers and reads one creature.
- **XP over the socket.** When a lounge payment commits, the payer's own lounge sockets get `lounge-xp` (creature id, XP paid today, level, experience, waiting). The client patches `loungeXp` and the creature's experience; a level-up or a ribbon wait does one normal reload. The 2-minute reload stays only as the fallback while the socket is not live. Values are absolute, so a missed push corrects itself with the next one.

### 2026-10-25: Indexes for a player's recent trips and worn gear (migration 44)

The read budget test's growth check found two paths that read more every month: the state's recent claimed and cancelled trips (`expeditions_user_status` found the player's trips of that status, then sorted all of them for the newest few) and the raid view's worn gear per party member (`gear_items_user` walked every item of the player to find the worn ones). Migration 44 adds `expeditions_user_claimed (user_id, claimed_at) WHERE status = 'claimed'`, `expeditions_user_cancelled (user_id, started_at) WHERE status = 'cancelled'` and `gear_items_user_worn (user_id) WHERE equipped_on IS NOT NULL`. On the test's month-old guild the state went from 610 rows to 465 and the raid view from 3,268 to 2,773, and neither grows with another month any more; both are now in the growth check, and their budgets were lowered to match.

### 2026-10-25: The raid view stores its readiness bands and teammates' teams (migration 45)

_2026-10-26: the stored teammate teams (`raid_team_cache`, `shownRaidTeam`) are gone: a team is just the placed creatures, loaded by id. The table stays (a released migration), unused. The stored readiness bands stay._

The raid view read about 2,800 rows on a month-old guild of 15 players. Nearly all of it was the party board: for every member, `raidTeam` loaded the player's whole collection (every creature, talent and worn item) to find their strongest unplaced creatures, on every view. The readiness band did the same through its forecast; it was cached for 5 minutes, but in memory, and the object sleeps a few seconds after each request, so nearly every view computed it again.

- **Stored readiness bands** (`raid_readiness`): the band per week and party with the week state, the time and a stale flag. The rules are the same as the memory cache had (a change marks it stale, recomputed at most every 30 s; without a change kept 5 minutes). Rows of other weeks are deleted when a band is stored.
- **Stored teammate teams** (`raid_team_cache`, `shownRaidTeam` in raid-teams.ts): the ids of each member's team as the view last worked them out. A view loads only those creatures by id, with their talents and worn items (stats always fresh). The ids are worked out again after 5 minutes, after any raid change (placements, teams, abilities, the roster: the same calls that mark the readiness stale), or when a stored creature is gone. Your own team is always worked out fresh. Only the choice of a teammate's strongest unplaced creatures can lag, by at most 5 minutes (a teammate who trained another creature). Fights never use it: `buildPhaseInput` works every team out from scratch.
- **The away list for a swap** reads only id, species, nickname and level of an away teammate's creatures (it loaded them as fighters).
- On the read budget's guild the raid view went from 2,773 rows to 780.

### 2026-10-25: Old game data is deleted once nobody can use it

Misa: "just record the stats and delete the details when players can no longer interact with them." Also on trips: "literally never cared about as soon as the player claims the reward", and on raids: "Raids older than the last week's one also don't need to be kept." Done in `old-data.ts`, run from the once-a-game-day cleanup (`GuildCore.maintain`) after the analytics, gear and dungeon passes. Each pass reads through an index, deletes in batches of 500 (at most 20 batches an alarm; a longer backlog keeps the day unmarked so the next alarms continue), is idempotent and logs only an error name. The indexes it needs are made there with `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` (the first run builds them), not as a numbered migration, so branches sharing a database never disagree about a number.

| Data                                                                                                                                                                     | Deleted when                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Kept, and why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Trips (`expeditions`) and their `expedition_offers`                                                                                                                      | Claimed: before the start of today's game day (a claim retry within the day still finds its stored result). Cancelled: 30 days after they started.                                                                                                         | Active trips. The totals are their own rows: `explorer_progress`, `familiarity`, `account_stats`, `journal`, `places_visited`, `player_progress`, the ledger and the `expedition:<id>` loot receipts. Nothing is derived from old trips. The state's "recent trips" list now shows today's claims only. Cancelled trips wait 30 days because the one-time slot-wipe note reads them. |
| Workshop jobs (craft, refine, recycle, dismantle)                                                                                                                        | Claimed: before the start of today's game day.                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Open jobs. Crafting skill (`crafting_skill`) is its own table. Market Day counts jobs started today, so older ones are not read.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Valley Games `games_results`, `games_entries`, `games_placings`                                                                                                          | Days before the oldest of: the latest shown day (clients replay it), the latest awarded day (the player's "last results"), the earliest locked day not awarded yet (the award reads its results). Nothing is deleted until a show and an award both exist. | `games_days` (a row a day: the lineup, seed and times), `games_medals`, `games_creature_medals`, `games_records`, `games_badges`, `games_players`. The creature page reads only those.                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Prize Stall buys (`games_stall_buys`)                                                                                                                                    | Weeks before the current stall week.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | This week's rows (the weekly limit).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Raid details: `raid_parties`, `raid_party_members`, `raid_opt_outs`, `raid_teams`, `raid_placements`, `raid_abilities`, `raid_autofire`, `raid_reveal_seen`, `raid_live` | Weeks older than last week, and only when the week's finale is done and no real live finale of it is still unresolved (a practice fight goes regardless).                                                                                                  | This week and last week whole. `raid_weeks` (rank, ladder result, season, Festival), `raid_ladder`, `raid_events` (done markers: deleting one would run the event again), `raid_festival_grants` (small, one per won Festival), `server_firsts`.                                                                                                                                     |
| Raid results (`raid_results`)                                                                                                                                            | Older weeks (same rule) lose their `seed`, `input` and `result` (set to empty).                                                                                                                                                                            | The row and its `summary`, the stats of the fight. Their replay and after-action stats now answer not found.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Discord posts (`discord_posts`)                                                                                                                                          | Sent or failed, a week after.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Pending posts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| DM log (`dm_sent`, `dm_outbox`)                                                                                                                                          | Unchanged: 30 days and a week.                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

- **The loot reveal looks back to last week only** (`latestReveal`): it links to the replay and stats, which older weeks no longer have. A player away for more than a week no longer gets the card for a finale they missed (the loot was paid at the finale). It also makes the reveal's search a range of the table's key instead of all of it.
- **Left alone:** `gear_grants`. The newest 20 are the "recent finds" list, and the other receipts (`raid:<week>:...`, `festival:<week>`, `rank:<creature>:<n>`, `dungeon:<day>:...`, `welcome`) guard against a second grant; only the `expedition:<id>` ones could go and they are a row a trip. `weather_days`, `daily_allowance`, `lounge_rest` and `games_days` are a row a day or week and are read.
- **Staging only:** the dev tool that resets the latest Games day can leave the day before it without its stored show, since that one may be older than the latest awarded day.

### 2026-10-26: While you were away: missed rewards on the notice board

Misa: "accumulate missed rewards from older activities into a single place and show that to the user via the notice board after they log back on after a long period of inactivity ... efficiently, and compile loot from many sources."

- **Recorded at payout, never found by scanning history.** Migration 46 adds `away_rewards`: one row per player and source kind (`leet`, `raid`, `games`) with summed integer counts (Bait, Essence, finds, creatures, medals, XP) and the first and last payout time. Each automatic payout calls `recordAway` (`while-away.ts`) in the transaction that pays it: one UPSERT, gated in the same statement by `INSERT ... SELECT ... FROM players WHERE user_id = ? AND last_seen_at < ?`, so a player who is around records nothing and the gate is one primary-key lookup.
- **Away means unseen for more than 24 hours** at the moment of the payout (`AWAY_AFTER_MS`, a worker constant). The payouts run in the alarm, not in a visit, and a payout reads `last_seen_at` as it is then, so opening the game later (which moves `last_seen_at`) cannot hide what was paid before it.
- **Hooked payouts (everything the server pays without the player acting):** the daily leet Bait (`leet-sync.ts`); the raid's stage Essence, the stage experience, the finale's gear piece or blueprint, the jackpot Essence, and the Festival gear and creature (`raid-resolve.ts`); the Valley Games' medals and experience (`games-award.ts`).
