Not hooked, on purpose: the daily allowance (paid by the player's own first request, with its own notice), lounge XP (only paid while present), trips, Workshop jobs and rank gifts (the player claims them), and the one-time level reset refund (its own card). To add a source, see the header of `while-away.ts`.
- **Waiting, not recorded.** Finished trips and Workshop jobs are claimed by the player, so nothing was paid. The summary counts them at read time (`tripsWaiting`, `jobsWaiting`) with the user's trip and job indexes, and only when the player has something recorded.
- **Read once, cheaply.** `GET /api/state` (and every action answer that embeds the state) carries `awayRewards`, read by primary-key prefix: no rows, no cost. The counts for trips and jobs are two more indexed reads, only for a player with rows. `POST /api/away/dismiss` takes `{ until }` (the summary's last payout), is strict, uses the session's user and is idempotent. It deletes rows whose last payout is not after `until`, so a payout that landed after the card was drawn is shown next time. The daily maintenance drops rows older than 90 days.
- **The card** is a paper on the Today board above "Needs you" (`home/away-notice.ts`, `NoticeBoard`): a row per source ("Daily gifts", "Raid", "Valley Games") with count badges, one line for what waits ("2 trips and 1 job are waiting for you") and a gold "Got it". The card appears only when a source paid something; trips and jobs alone already have their "Needs you" rows. The game never says "leet", so the Bait source is called "Daily gifts".

### 2026-10-26: Raids: you join by placing; no auto teams

Misa reversed the 2026-10-10 rule: "Their occupied slots only hinder the active players. Therefore let's only add players to the raid if they legitimately actually assign their creatures to the board." It also removes the expensive part of the raid view and of every fight build, "find a player's best three" (every creature, talent and worn item of every member). He approved these seven rules exactly:

1. You join the week's raid by placing your first creature. Nothing is auto-filled: you bring exactly what you placed (1 to 3 creatures).
2. Only joined players count: boss HP scaling (`scaleBoss` by member count), loot, Essence, finale abilities and party size. Players who did not join get nothing and do not make the boss harder.
3. Away players' slots are simply free: nobody's creatures sit on the board unless they placed them. Slots stay first come, first served. The board's size rule keeps room for every active player to place three, capped at a party's maximum, and the guest rule for fewer than three joined players stays.
4. "Sit this week out" and the away-teammate assist are removed.
5. On servers over 15 players a player who first places a creature joins the party with the fewest joined players. No snake draft on power at week start.
6. Joining closes at Phase 1. After it the parties are fixed, but joined players can still move or swap their own creatures. Before Phase 1 a player who removes all their creatures leaves the raid again. _Amended 2026-10-26 ("join until the finale"): joining stays open until the finale starts._
7. The Raid tab's pip and the Home notice keep nudging an active player who has not placed yet, so nobody misses it. A joined player with fewer than three placed (and more creatures to place) is nudged the same way.

**What was built.**

- **Joining.** `Raid.place` joins the caller on their first placement (`joinSmallestParty`, which makes the party if it does not exist yet) while Phase 1 has not resolved (`raid_closed` after). `unplace` leaves the raid when the last placement goes before Phase 1 (`leaveParty`). The week opens with no members and no parties. A new server is placed (its first Boss Rank) when its first player joins.
- **Teams.** `raidTeam` and the new `teamOfPlacements` are the placed creatures only (at most three, `capPlacements`), loaded by id with their gear and talents. `autoTeam` is now only the strongest-first order of those. `buildPartyBoard` reads the party's placements once and builds every member's team from them; members with no creature left on the board do not fight and do not count for `scaleBoss`, the abilities or the payouts. A party where nobody has anything on the board has no fight (`buildPhaseInput` answers null; no result, no loot, no post). `updateLadder` waits only for parties that have a placement.
- **Board size.** `partyRowLength`: three per player for the larger of the party's joined players and its share of the active roster, at most 15 players, nine slots (guests) below three players, never shorter than the highest slot in use. The active roster is counted as of the week's creation (`activeWindowDays` before it), so the board does not shrink when someone stops playing and the zones of placed creatures do not move; it can grow when someone new becomes active. The party count and the party a newcomer would join come from the same number (`partyToJoin`).
- **View.** `GET /api/raid` shows a player who has not joined (and has a creature, while joining is open) the board of the party they would join, with `you.inRoster` false and no team. A joined player's `you.team` is the placed creatures. The roster, the parties and the finale only list joined players.
- **Posts and pings.** The Monday reveal names no head count (nobody has joined yet): its footer says "Place a creature on the raid board to join." It is queued when the server has any active player. The phase and finale posts are about the fighters of the party. The finale DM ping goes only to opted-in players who joined the week's raid.
- **Reads.** The raid view of the read budget's guild (15 players joined, three each) went from 780 rows to 633, with no collection read; its budget is 950. The stored teammate teams are not needed any more.
- **Dev tools (staging).** Simulated players place their three creatures (`placeGuest`); the caller's three strongest are placed for them by the roster, simulate and live tools (`joinCaller`), because those tools need the caller in the raid. "Resolve now" does not join anyone.

**Removed on purpose.** The opt-out (`PUT /api/raid/opt-out`, its schema, `Raid.setOptOut`, the `roster_optout` analytics event and its `raid.optout` rollup line), the away assist (`raid-assist.ts`, `placed_by` is no longer written or read, the away list, the "Swap for another" picker and the "moved by" lines in the client), `autoPlace` of unplaced creatures in the raid and the "auto" tile marker, `createParties`, `snakeDraft` and `playerPower`, the late-joiner sync (`syncRoster`), `shownRaidTeam` and `markShownTeamsStale`. The tables `raid_opt_outs`, `raid_teams` and `raid_team_cache` and the column `raid_placements.placed_by` stay as they are (released migrations are never edited) and are not used; nothing dropped in a new migration, since they hold no rows that matter and cost nothing. The retired `PUT /api/raid/team` route still answers (it changes nothing).

**Open for Misa.** A joined player who removes everything after Phase 1 stays in the party (rule 6) but does not fight, so they get no loot that week. A brand-new server shows no Boss Rank on the Monday post (it is placed at the first join); an older server shows its rank as before.

### 2026-10-26: The raid board is live over the sockets

Misa wanted teammates' placements to appear on the Raid tab as they happen. Until now the tab only synced when it was reopened, when you acted, or when a phase resolved. Details in `raid-live-board.md`.

1. A third socket topic, `raid` (`{ week, party }`), on the existing live machinery (tickets, caps, hibernation, the lounge's client controller). Its own ticket and upgrade routes, `POST /api/raid/board/ticket` and `GET /api/raid/board/live`, behind a new flag `RAID_LIVE` (on in local, staging and production, pinned by a test; `/api/config` announces `raidLive`). The ticket is made for the party the raid view shows the player: their own, or the one they would join.
2. **Push the change, not the view.** After `place` or `unplace` commits, the party's other board sockets get one small message: the changed cells (built by the view's own cell code, the actor's creatures as `mine: false` without ids) and the roster change if someone joined or left. Nothing is built when nobody listens (0 rows); a push reads about 10 rows (the placed creatures by id and one name), plus one row-length calculation on a leave. A push never throws.
3. Phase results and the readiness band are not pushed: the tab already reloads after each phase is due. The finale keeps its own socket.
4. Client: while the Raid tab shows a party's board and the flag is on, it keeps a board socket; a pushed cell is checked strictly and applied in place, and anything that cannot be applied (or a socket coming back after a drop) costs one normal `fetchRaid()` reload. No polling was added; with the socket down the tab behaves as before.
5. Not done: answering the player's own placement with the compact change. The HTTP routes still return the whole view (it would complicate the client's merge for the saving of one small response).

`wrangler.jsonc` changed (the `RAID_LIVE` var in all three places), which the PR calls out.

### 2026-10-26: Raids: join until the finale; new servers start at Rank 1

Amends rule 6 of "Raids: you join by placing; no auto teams" (joining closed at Phase 1). Misa asked for two changes.

1. **You can join the raid until the finale starts.** A player's first placement joins them at any time while the week's status is not `finale` (after Phase 1 and after Phase 2 too). The placement goes to the party with the fewest joined players (`partyToJoin`, lowest number on a tie), parties and all. Once the finale starts, the usual `locked` answer applies; `raid_closed` is gone.
   - The boss's HP pool is fixed when a party first fights (Phase 1 for a party that exists then) and a late joiner never rescales it. A party made after Phase 1 (every other party is full, or the active roster needs another one) scales its own pool, from its members, at its first fight.
   - Phase 2 and the finale are built from the current placements, so a late joiner's creatures fight from the next stage they are in. Essence, experience, gear and finale loot (jackpot, Festival creature) are paid per stage to the fighters of that stage only, so a late joiner gets nothing for Phase 1 or any stage before they joined. Their finale ability is the default by join order, like everyone else's.
   - The Raid tab's pip and the Home notice nudge an active player who has not joined until the finale starts; the live board's ticket and the view of the board they would join work the same after Phase 1.
   - **Leaving is unchanged:** removing all creatures before Phase 1 leaves the raid; from Phase 1 a member stays in the party and just does not fight with an empty board. (Allowing it later would mean moving a player out of a party with results, for no gain.)
   - The staging dev tool that fills the roster (`raid-roster`) still locks at Phase 1, as before; it is a setup tool, not a player rule.
2. **A new server starts at Rank 1 when its first week opens.** A server with no rank gets `placementRank` (always the start rank, 1) stored on its ladder at week creation, so the Monday post always shows "Rank 1" even before anyone has joined. Placement no longer waits for a member or reads the members' creatures: `placementFor` is removed and `placeServer` takes no week. Places that still place a rank without a member: an old open week with no rank gets it at its first join or at Phase 1 (`ensureWeekRank`), the legacy-week conversion still needs members (a legacy week where nobody joined stays as it was), and a re-declared first week uses the start rank.

### 2026-10-26: What the rows_read week taught us (standing principles)

Cloudflare stopped Durable Object requests twice in a week for passing the free tier's 5,000,000 rows read a day (#68, #85, #87, #88 fixed it: from several million a day to well under one million). The individual changes have their own entries above; these are the principles behind them, for every future feature.

- **The object sleeps after a few seconds; memory is not a cache.** With hibernatable sockets open, a guild object is evicted between the lounge's minute ticks and starts again on the next alarm or request. Anything kept in memory "for 5 minutes" (the readiness band, lounging time) is in practice recomputed or lost on almost every request. What must last between requests lives in a table (`raid_readiness`, `lounge_company`), and per-start work runs once per deployed build (`startup_passes`).
- **Measure, don't guess.** Every cut started from a probe that summed `cursor.rowsRead` per statement on a realistic month of data. Guesses were wrong twice: an index took the raid view only from 3,268 to 2,773 rows; the real cost was loading every teammate's whole collection. The probe is now the read budget test and the `db.rows_read` metric in the daily ops report.
- **Hot paths read a few rows** (CLAUDE.md, Conventions): every request, alarm tick and object start; an index for every lookup there; the read budget test with its growth check keeps it so.
- **Push the change, not the view.** A live socket is cheap (pings are answered by Cloudflare without waking the object) and a push can reuse the data the change already has: lounge XP (`lounge-xp`) and raid board cells cost a few rows or none, where a client reload of the whole state or view cost hundreds. A polling or reload loop on a timer is the last resort.
- **Record at the moment of change, never scan history.** The While you were away summary adds to one row per source at payout; the read is one indexed lookup.
- **Keep the stats, delete the details once nobody can use them** (Misa: "just record the stats and delete the details when players can no longer interact with them"). Old trips, Workshop jobs, Games shows, raid week details and sent posts go in the daily cleanup.
- **Don't guess for absent players** (Misa). Auto-picked teams for away players cost the most reads and only hindered the active players; a player now takes part by acting (placing), and what they did not do costs nothing.
- **Staging shares the account's quota**: its reads count against the same daily limit.

### 2026-10-26: A query plan audit and a day-in-the-life read budget

The rows_read week showed that a per-path read budget is not enough: it only measures the statements somebody thought of, and it prices one call, not how often it runs. Misa asked for a system that catches inefficient queries before they ship. Three checks were added to the worker tests, all on the seeded month-old guild of the read budget test (now `test/read-world.ts`, shared):

1. **Query plan audit** (`query-plan-audit.test.ts`). Every distinct statement of the hot paths (start, login, the main GETs, alarms with and without the lounge, a lounge payment, a raid placement with a board listener) gets an `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`; a full `SCAN` of a table that grows with play fails, naming the statement, the plan and the `src/` call site. Walking an index (`SCAN ... USING INDEX`) is not a failure, because the read budget counts the rows. Small tables are allowlisted with a reason (`sessions`, which live an hour; `players`, `dm_prefs`, `games_medals`, `raid_readiness` and the one-row tables); `ACCEPTED` holds single statements, empty today.
2. **A day in the life** (`read-day.test.ts`). One game day of 10 players: 4 opens each, raid looks, board placements, 5 players lounging for 3 hours with the object evicted between minute ticks, every alarm of the day. It budgets the total (about 5% of the free tier) and prints the rows by source on failure. This is where frequency is priced, which was the root cause of the week.
3. **The lounge tick and a live board placement** are in the read budget test, with their own budgets, and in the growth check (together with `/api/state` and `/api/raid`, which used to be left out).

The audit found no scan worth an index: every full scan on a hot path was of a table bounded by design. The readiness band recompute (about 600 extra rows for a raid view every 5 minutes) is the biggest single line of the day and is the next candidate if the day budget needs to come down. Reasoning for the checks over a lint rule: a static check cannot see data sizes or bindings, and the plans are the database's own answer.

### 2026-10-26: Raid results show real damage and a separate score

A player reported (#86) that the scoreboard's "Damage" added up to about half the boss's HP lost, and that a Payoff like Gaiadrake showed a third of what the fight log said it hit for. The column was the engine's `CreatureTotals.damage`: the hitter's share of the credit, after the setter's 60% of a reaction's bonus and the Supports' share of buffed damage had moved to other creatures. Misa: a "Damage" column must show damage; the credit is the score, and the results say what each one means.

1. **Damage** is what a creature really took off the boss: every `hit` in full (reaction bonus included) plus the Echo and Riposte `proc` amounts. The engine's `damageDealt(events)` sums it per creature from the stored events, so every stored fight (the stats route only serves last week and this week, whose events are kept) shows it without a migration. A property test checks that it adds up to the HP the boss lost.
2. **Score** is the engine's `contribution` (own hits after the shares, plus setup, support and guard credit), the weight of the Essence share. The stats send `damage`, `score` and, per player, `assist` (the part of the score from setting up, supporting and guarding, for the "backbone" highlight). The field `contribution` is gone from the stats; the stored summary is unchanged.
3. The scoreboard and the Top creatures table have the columns Damage and Score. One "?" by the Scoreboard heading (the `raid-scoreboard` glossary entry, "Damage and score") explains both, so the header row stays short (Misa). The share column ("%") is gone from the scoreboard (the "Hardest hitter" highlight still says the share); the hero shows the caller's damage and score.
4. **Sorting (Misa).** A tap on the Damage or Score heading orders that table by it, highest first (ties by the other number, then the server's order); the heading is a plain button, gold with a small pixel triangle while it is the order, and the table says it with `aria-sort`. Damage is the default. On the scoreboard the places follow the order; in Top creatures "#n" stays the place by damage among all creatures (the server picks the five best by damage). Each table keeps its own order while the card is open; nothing is stored.
5. The glossary entry keeps explaining lines although raid popups are one line (Misa, 2026-10-14): it sits in the How to play "Rewards" section, because the score is how the Essence share is decided, and Misa asked for an explanation here.
6. The replay's "Your recap" counts Echo and Riposte as damage too, and calls the setup share "score from setting up".

### 2026-10-26: A private note per creature

A player asked (#76) for a short note on a creature, next to Rename, to remember plans for it. Misa: a small text box, at most 256 characters.

1. **Storage.** Migration 47 adds `creatures.note` (TEXT, null for none). The note is a column of the creature row, so the owner's collection state, which already reads every row, carries it for no extra reads. `CreatureView` is only ever built for the owner's own collection, so nobody else sees a note; the room, raid and other views never read the column.
2. **Route.** `PUT /api/creatures/:id/note` `{ note: string | null }` (strict body, at most 2,048 UTF-16 units), owner only (404 for anyone else's creature, like a rename). `null` or empty removes it. Not keyed: saving the same text twice is harmless.
3. **Text rules** (`sanitizeCreatureNote`, `RULES.creatureNote`). Like a nickname, nothing is silently stripped: control, format (bidi, zero-width), unassigned, surrogate and line or paragraph separator characters are refused (`invalid_note`), but line breaks and tabs are allowed. NFKC, line breaks become `\n`, at most one empty line in a row, trailing spaces cut per line, ends trimmed, then at most 256 code points. Rendered as text only.
4. **Client.** A "Note" fold under Rename on the creature page, open when there is a note, with a text box (`maxlength` 256), a "12 / 256" count and Save (off until the text changed). The note stays through evolving and renaming; it goes only when the owner removes it.

## 2026-10-26: Combat v2 is designed in a lab before it touches the engine

**Decision (Misa, 2026-10-25 and 26).** The combat rework (species Knacks instead of finale abilities, primer and detonator marks, slot-based boss targeting, a boss turn after each row, armour with Blocked hits, raid attrition, healing as a pillar) is iterated as a proposal with a reviewer panel and a prototype simulator before any of it reaches `@creatures/engine`. Misa's calls so far: only Setters mark and only Payoffs cash marks (Anthem's primers and detonators); a hit that cashes a reaction leaves no mark by the base rules, but effects may add marks; no Swift or Last; targets are slots, and a slam is three slot targets that each roll dodge; dodge before intercept; armour is a flat stat and a hit at or below it is Blocked; the raid arena's row count varies by boss, and the boss acts after each row; in a raid weekend HP carries over, nobody heals between stages and knocked-out creatures are out; the dungeon keeps its structure; the same effect keeps the same name everywhere; counts shown as numbers (x3); strong combos may stand out (answer them with counter cards before nerfing); the live finale keeps Rally, Shield Wall and Purge for now.

**How it is built (lead's calls).**

- **Docs** in `docs/design/combat/`: `README.md` (North Star, which document wins, reading order, the loop), `principles.md` (P-01 to P-12, each with pass and fail examples and a review question; only Misa accepts one), `grammar.md` (families, limits, templates), `keywords.md`, `proposals/001-combat-v2.md` (the numbered rules R1 to R18, drawbacks, alternatives, open questions, testing plan), `reviews/` (panel results). `knacks.md` and `lab-report.md` are generated; never edited by hand. The structure follows research on rules references (one definition per keyword, a named authority), design pillars as filters with examples, ADR-style proposals and small always-loaded context.
- **`packages/lab`**: a pure prototype of the draft rules (no I/O, injected RNG, its own small rule set), separate from the shipped engine on purpose. It reads species stats from `@creatures/game`. Knacks are data with a small effect grammar (`KnackFx`) plus player text and design intent. `pnpm --filter @creatures/lab report` writes the report (scenarios per rule card, a best-team search per card for a one-row gauntlet and a three-row raid, Knack usage, a raid weekend) and the generated Knack catalogue; `test` checks the rules and the content rules (keywords exist, names unique, intent written). Pyrelynx and Bloomback carry shared family Knacks as placeholders so Grove and Ember marks have a Payoff.
- **The reviewer panel** is a skill, `.claude/skills/design-panel`: four reviewers (rules, player experience, systems and exploits, readability) judge against the principles; the lead writes one review file with a verdict per finding.

## 2026-10-26: Combat v2: Misa's answers to the first panel

**Decision (Misa).** (1) Raid attrition stays as written: a knocked-out creature is out for the weekend, with no "tired" return; players have spare creatures by week 2. (2) Silence stops the role's verb as well as the Knack, talents and gear effects: a silenced Setter does not mark, a Payoff does not cash, a Guard does not intercept or cover, a Support does not boost or heal; the creature still makes its plain hit. (3) Raid swaps across players are wanted: "we do it manually now; a system would help". Recorded in proposal 001 v0.5 (R12, R14, R19) and in the lab (`packages/lab/src/fight.ts`, test "silence stops the role"). Swap requests move up from "not a priority" on the roadmap.

## 2026-10-26: Combat v2: Knacks for every species, talents by role (panel 2)

Tagged combat v2. Lead agent, after the second panel (`docs/design/combat/reviews/2026-10-26-panel-2.md`); proposal v0.6.

- **The roadmap** (`docs/design/combat/roadmap.md`, agreed with Misa): eight gated phases from the lab to shipping, and the migration (creatures and levels stay; talents reroll; Knacks start as none; raid planning resets).
- **Every form has Knacks** in the lab. New: Guard's Shadow, Crown of Embers, Hide in Mist, Thick Fog, Drizzle, Living Shelter, Territorial, Carry Them, Starlight, Lantern Glow, Hearth. Families live in the data (`FAMILIES`), and a test fails on a family that is not defined.
- **Combat talents are by role, one name per effect** (R20): four per role from eleven, shared by every species; a talent never repeats a Knack and Seal is never a talent, so a roll never decides the Knack choice. Per-species flavour talents were tried and dropped: they copied Knacks and each other under other names (P-06) and each would need its own rule (P-09). **Misa (2026-10-26): use shared names everywhere**, so the shipped species-named talents (Splashet's "Playful" and the rest) take the shared name of their effect at the migration.
- **Ohmouse loses Double Mark** (a Guard that marks x2 was P-02's Fail example); **All at Once needs x3**; **Tidewing gets Double Mark**; Retaliate ignores armour; crit talents stay inside the reaction cap; reaction-fed Stores gain at most 1 per round.
- **Still open:** Bloomback in 10 of 14 best gauntlet lines, with Voltaro's Zigzag; the next slice.

## 2026-10-27: A generated wiki page of the game's configuration

**Decision (lead agent, for Misa).** `/wiki.html` is a reference page of what is in the game: every creature form (grouped by line, with stats, evolution relatives, its finale ability today, its current talents, and its combat v2 draft Knacks and talent pool), the current talents, and the combat v2 draft's Knacks, talents, keywords, Knack families, plus the elements. Each creature links to its talents and Knacks, which are entries further down the same page and link back to the creatures that have them (plain `#anchors`). The draft sections are labelled "Draft, not in the game yet". Design intent is shown, in a smaller style, because the page is for Misa too.

**Why build time, and why in production.** The page is generated from the content data (`@creatures/game`) and the lab's data (`@creatures/lab`) by a small Vite plugin (`apps/activity/src/wiki/`) on every build, so it can never be out of date and needs no hand-written text: a new creature, talent or Knack appears with no change to the wiki code (a test adds one to fake data and checks). It is one HTML file and one CSS file, with no JavaScript, so it fits the Content-Security-Policy (no inline style or script) and adds nothing to the app's bundle; the plugin loads the data modules with Vite's module runner outside the bundle, and the build test checks the production bundle still has no lab code. Unlike the combat lab and the workbench it ships to production and staging, because it is text that gives nothing away that a player could not read from the game's own screens, and Misa can open it from his phone on the live address. It is not linked from the game (the DEV panel does not link it either); it carries `noindex`, but it is not secret. Tests check every entry has an id, no link is dangling, every Knack of a form is linked, and text is escaped.

## 2026-10-27: Combat v2: Knacks grow on evolution, talents by line, golden stays golden

**Decision (Misa).** (1) A creature knows its form's Knacks from the catch, and evolving adds the new form's own Knacks (R11), so base forms work from day one and evolving brings something new. (2) Talent pools are per creature line, drawn from one shared library with one name per effect (R20), so two lines of the same role can roll different tools. (3) At the migration a golden talent rolls golden.

**How (lead).** The lab stores each form's own Knacks (`OWN_KNACKS`) and works out the full list from the evolution line (`knacksOf`); on a role change only the new form's own Knacks count, since the old role's would do nothing. The talent library grows from 11 to 18 talents (new effects: Max HP, crit damage, low-HP damage, an own area cut, Hold On, a reaction bonus, area dodge), each marked with the roles it works for; each line has a pool of seven with at least three that fit each of its roles (a test). The wiki shows the line pools and marks idle talents. In the lab report, Tidewing keeping Lead the Way moved Bloomback from 10 of 14 best gauntlet lines to 6.

## 2026-10-27: Combat v2: which talents can appear on which creatures

**Decision (Misa).** A line's talent pool says which talents can appear on its creatures. A creature starts with the talent it was found with (a starter with a set talent), and grows its own Talent Book through Teach: a found talent goes only into the book of the creature it was found for, and must be found again for another creature. When an evolution changes the role, slotted talents that become idle are rerolled from the pool among the talents that work for the new role, shown as unknown cards on the evolution screen (phase 4); until then idle talents after a role change are accepted.

**Notes (lead).** Teach already works per creature today (`catch-choices.ts`: a duplicate's talent goes into the book of the player's creature of that line), so only the starter's set talent and the evolution reroll are new; both are recorded in R20 and built in roadmap phase 4. Which talent each starter gets is content, chosen with the starter list.

## 2026-10-28: Content changes are not held back by existing rolls; modifiers, luck and fading debuffs

**Decision (Misa).**

- **Change freely.** Misa may change, remove or add any content at any time. A removed or changed modifier line on an existing item or creature is replaced by a fresh random roll; a new line just enters future rolls. Players expect changes; be fair, but don't let old rolls slow progress. (Also a standing rule in `CLAUDE.md`.)
- **Luck is welcome.** Chance-based modifiers stay, and most effects may be luck-based; deterministic rhythms are an option. P-04 still keeps a creature's main job certain.
- **Debuffs fade over time** (Mired loses stacks), and bosses may cleanse them, announced at round start, so a debuff creature has to keep working and its effect does not last the whole fight.
- **Same effect, same name** also between talents and gear lines (a gear line is one modifier on an item); a talent and a gear line of the same name add up.
- **Build a first batch** of the modifier and talent library (`docs/design/combat/modifiers.md`) into the lab and the wiki; Misa filters later.
- **New ideas:** Cull (a hit that leaves a foe below a small share of its HP defeats it) and killing-blow triggers (for example more loot for the raid). Cover gets a cap (an intercepted hit needs none, since the Guard still takes it).

## 2026-10-28: Armour works for every creature; the first talent batch is in the lab

**Decision (Misa).** Armour is a stat of every creature: Guards have the most, and talents, Knacks and gear may raise anyone's. No armour effect is limited to Guards (R13). The shipped gear line Armor still says "Guards only"; it changes with the engine port.

**The batch (lead).** 35 talents in the lab from `modifiers.md`, with the engine hooks they need (neighbour, position, round, boss-HP and mark conditions; triggers; Flank Guard and Long Shadow; shared armour; Thick Skull; Brace; Bright Boost, Kindred Boost, Triage, Overheal Spill; Heavy Hand, Clinging, Light Sleeper; Cull and the killing blow). Rules: Mired fades one stack per round, the Shake It Off card cleanses debuffs on an announced round, cover is capped at 75% (R8.6, R15, R21). Twelve old lab talents were folded in or dropped. The report: talents add +0% to +22% on their boards; Lone Wolf, Flank Guard (Left) and Shoulder to Shoulder are best on most cards of their role (measured on boards chosen to show them, so a ceiling); Share the Load, Long Shadow and Thick Skull are near zero on plain boards; Shake It Off has no working answer yet.

### 2026-10-27: Marked items, dismantled as a pile

A player asked for a quick way to clear items past the 60-item bag. Misa's rules: mark items, then one free creature dismantles the whole marked pile at once; worn items and items in an open job cannot be marked; equipping a marked item clears its mark; the request names the exact items the confirmation showed (409 and nothing taken if the marked set changed); the creature must be free (not away, not on a job); it gains Crafting XP but no creature experience; Market Day's bonus goes to the most valuable items; the old timed dismantle job goes.

1. **Marks.** Migration 48 adds `gear_items.dismantle_mark` (read with the bag, no index) and `dismantle_days (user_id, day_start, items)`. `POST /api/gear/mark { itemIds (1 to 60), marked }`: all or nothing, 409 `item_worn` / `item_in_job`, unmarking always works, no key (repeats are harmless). Equip and Equip best clear the mark in their own transaction. `GearItemView.markedForDismantle`; the item picker's tiles and detail card show it.
2. **The pile.** `POST /api/gear/dismantle { creatureId, itemIds, idempotencyKey }`, one keyed transaction (`gear_actions`, kind `dismantle_pile`). Marked set not exactly `itemIds` is 409 `marks_changed`; busy creature is 409 `creature_away` / `creature_on_job`. It takes no Workshop slot. The preview is `POST /api/workshop/preview { kind: 'dismantle', ... }` for a pile (range, Crafting XP, blueprint chance, `boosted`, `tinkersLuckBp`; no time).
3. **Rolls (rules unchanged, now per item).** Refund from `rollDismantle` (not by Crafting level), blueprint draw 6% + 1 point per Crafting level of the dismantler, the dismantler's Tinker's luck, Essence once through the ledger (`dismantle-pile:<key>`), all live server RNG, items rolled most valuable first.
4. **Crafting XP.** The total the old timed jobs paid: 6 per 5 Commons (a bundle), 12 per Rare, 24 per Epic (`pileDismantleXp`); Apprentice adds its share once on the total. No creature experience.
5. **Market Day** boosts the day's first 2 dismantled _items_ (not jobs): the pile is ordered by rarity, then item level (`pileOrder`) and the first `dismantleBoostLeft` items roll the top of their range. Old timed jobs started today count one each; piles count in `dismantle_days`.
6. **Replaced from 2026-10-16.** Starting a `dismantle` job is 410 `dismantle_job_removed`; running jobs finish and claim as before (their claim code is kept). The item drawer's per-item Dismantle wizard became "Mark for dismantling" / "Unmark"; the bundle rules (5 Commons, no mixing) no longer apply to new dismantles.
7. **UI.** "Select many": "Dismantle items" in the Gear section starts a selection mode (taps toggle, a small "i" on each tile opens the card, presets All Common / Below level 10 / Clear, a bar with the count and Dismantle), then the shared wizard in a drawer (Who dismantles, then "Dismantle 23 items (20 Common, 3 Rare)?"), then the existing job results card for the whole pile. Taps show at once and requests go out one at a time (`use-marks.ts`).

### 2026-10-27: Bulk selection replaces marking

Misa tested the marked-items build (above) on staging: he liked the interaction and the results screen, but testers read the "Dismantle items" button as "dismantle now". He asked for a general multi-select with bulk actions, started by gesture, plus a direct Dismantle on one item. This replaces the UI and the persisted marks of the entry above; the pile rules (rolls, XP, Market Day, ledger, no Workshop slot) stand.

1. **No marks.** Migration 49 drops `gear_items.dismantle_mark` (48 already ran on staging and is untouched; `dismantle_days` stays). `POST /api/gear/mark`, `markedForDismantle`, the "marks changed" 409 and the "equip clears the mark" logic are gone. `POST /api/gear/dismantle { creatureId, itemIds, idempotencyKey }` names the items; same key, same stored result.
2. **Worn items go too**, taken off by the same transaction (the row is deleted). The dismantler's own gear (Tinker's luck, Apprentice) is read before any item comes off, which is also what the preview showed. An item in an open job of a removed kind is still 409 `item_in_job`.
3. **Away creatures.** Equipping is frozen for a creature that is away or on a job (its claim reads gear live). A dismantle may still take an item off such a creature only if that changes nothing already started. Checked per activity (`dismantle-worn.ts`): a **trip** fixes its time (Quick feet) and ticks (Crossroads Wanderer) when sent but its claim reads Trail XP, Keen nose, Lucky find, Packer, Second look and the glimmer find live; a **Workshop job** fixes time (Busy paws), level, odds and mods at start but its claim reads Apprentice and, for an old dismantle job, Tinker's luck live; a **dungeon run** freezes each creature's folded gear into its stored input and `gear` ids (a creature that joins late is read live, excluding items the run carries), a **raid** phase or the live finale stores its fight inputs when built, and the **Games** snapshot entrants at the lock, so none of those read worn items again. The check folds the creature's rules with and without the item and compares only the fields its running activity reads live, so sets and caps are exact; items fine one by one but changing a capped rule together are all held. A held item is 409 `item_locked` (nothing taken, no detail). `POST /api/gear/dismantle-check { itemIds }` answers `{ locked: [{ itemId, creatureId, reason: trip | job }] }` read-only, so the warning step can say it before the creature is picked.
4. **Client.** No mode button, presets or "Done". Gesture, checkbox, Ctrl/Shift, right-click menu, keyboard and the live region as in `ux-patterns.md` "Bulk actions". Flow: worn warning (one creature row per wearer, "Dismantle anyway", Back; held items say why in their row and the button is off) when anything is worn, then a free creature, then the confirmation, then the results screen. The item card has a red Dismantle (last) running the same flow for one item. The one-time hint ("Press and hold an item to select several" on touch, "Click the box on an item to select several" with a mouse) is retired by dismissing it or selecting anything, per device in `localStorage` with in-session fallback (the same rule as `coach.ts`).
5. **Not testable here, needs the real Discord client:** the long press on iOS and Android (contextmenu and callout suppression, the buzz), the right click inside Discord desktop's iframe, and sticky bar placement above the real dock.

**UI revision after Misa's phone test of PR #94 (2026-10-28).** He liked the flow and asked for three changes, which replace the UI details of point 4 above:

1. **The worn step uses the established creature row** (`ui/CreatureList`, new `show` mode: a plain list nothing is picked from): one row per wearing creature (picture, name, level, element and role) with its item or items as small tiles on the right (`PickerOption.worn` may now be a list); a held item keeps its reason as the row's quiet line.
2. **Selection is a square checkbox.** The round circle became a square, square-cornered box (24 px drawn, in a 32 px button at the tile's top left: Misa found 44 px took too much of the tile on a phone, 2026-10-28) with the existing pixel `check` glyph (the one `PlacedCount` draws) on a chosen tile, which also keeps its gold ring. The small "i" is gone: a tap on the tile's body always opens the card (also while selecting, over the selection); the checkbox toggles. It is a button of its own with `role="checkbox"`, `aria-checked` and the label "Select Kelp Cap, item level 5", out of the tab order (Space on the tile still toggles, Enter opens). The slot icon of a bag tile moved to the bottom left so the box never covers it.
3. **One flow, one frame.** The card's Dismantle had its own `DismantleInline` (the same wizard inside the card's scrolling content: the gold button not pinned, a different header). It is deleted: the Workshop swaps the card's drawer for `DismantleDrawer` (the same component the bulk bar opens; Back from the first step returns to the card; the swap does not slide in again). Rule in `ux-patterns.md`; guards: `dismantle-frame.test.tsx` (both entry points draw the same drawer, head and pinned footer) and `wizard-footer-guard.test.tsx` (every `WizardFooter` in every workbench preset sits in a `Drawer` or `SidePanel` footer slot).

**Drawer: a pull on the handle is the page's gesture (2026-10-28).** On Misa's Android phone, closing a drawer by pulling its handle down left the page deaf to taps for about half a second; tapping outside, desktop and Chromium's phone emulation had no delay. Best explanation: Discord's Android app around the page also took the pull down as its own gesture and let it settle. The handle now marks its touch moves handled (`preventDefault` from a non-passive `touchmove` listener, only while a pull is under way and only when the browser allows it), which tells a WebView's host to keep out; a tap still closes. One fix in `ui/Drawer.tsx` covers every drawer. Misa confirmed on the phone that the delay is gone. A custom touch drag in a drawer or elsewhere should do the same.

## 2026-10-28: Selecting several is shared

Misa asked whether the next list that needs "select several and act on them" could use what the Workshop bag built, and whether agents would know it exists. Only the bulk bar and the long press were reusable; every gesture lived in the bag's `InventoryGrid`. Now:

- **Four shared parts in `ui/`:** `multi-select.ts` (`useMultiSelect`, the selection; was `bag-select.ts`), `use-select-grid.tsx` (`useSelectGrid`: the long press, the tile click with Ctrl, Command and Shift, the right click's menu, roving arrow keys, Space, Enter, Escape, the spoken count and the list attributes; moved out of `InventoryGrid`), `SelectCheck` (the corner checkbox; was inline in `ItemTile`) and `SelectMenu` (was `ItemMenu`), with `BulkBar` as before. `long-press.ts` moved to `ui/` too. The one-time hint is `ui/select-hint.ts`, keyed per list (`SelectHint`); the bag keeps its old key, so players who dismissed it do not see it again.
- **Shared CSS:** `select-grid.css` (the list, the checkbox, the menu; classes `select-grid`, `selecting`, `select-check`, `select-box`, `select-menu`). What stays per tile kind, on purpose: the chosen ring and the hold cue (`item-tile.css`), since every kind of tile has its own frame. That is a few lines to write again for a creature tile, and clearer than a shared ring that fits nothing well.
- **Nothing a player sees changed.** `InventoryGrid` only draws the items; all bag tests pass unchanged apart from class names.
- **How agents find it:** a row in the component table of `ux-patterns.md` ("Select several things in a list and act on them"), the "Bulk actions" rule pointing at it, workbench pages for `SelectCheck` and `SelectMenu`, and `select-grid-guard.test.ts`, which fails when a multi-select list, a long press or a selection checkbox is built outside the shared files (the same idea as the wizard footer guard).

## 2026-10-28: One spacing scale inside popovers

Misa saw the "More in How to play" button touching the text of a glossary popover. A measured pass over all 168 popovers the workbench can open found the cause and more: every popover part set its own margins, and a broad `.popover p` rule outweighed most of them, so gaps came out as 2, 4, 6, 8 or 10px with no rule (the link sat 2px under the text in the 25 glossary entries with no lines; the creature popover had a doubled gap at the bottom; a bold answer sat 2px over its list).

- **The scale** (tokens on `.popover`): 4px inside a group, 8px between groups, 12px above a button on its own line, the same padding on every side. Written up in `ux-patterns.md`, "Spacing inside a popover".
- **Stacks, not margins:** the popover, `pop-body` and `pop-stack` space their children (`:nth-child` gives the rules the weight of two classes, so a shared part's own margin never wins); `PopoverCard` puts the title and the short answer in one `pop-heading` group. Parts set no outer margins. Custom content (the item card's popover, the medal table) uses `pop-stack`; the forecast uses the tokens.
- **Prevention:** `ui/popover-spacing.test.ts` (CI) fails when any stylesheet gives a popover part (`.pop-*`) a vertical margin of its own; `scripts/popover-audit.mjs` taps every popover trigger in the workbench and measures the gaps in a real browser (it found the original bug when run on the old CSS). The audit is in the "Showing work to Misa" checklist next to the overflow audit; it is not in CI because CI has no browser.

## 2026-10-28: One spacing scale inside drawers and sheets

The popover pass (above) done for drawers and sheets. A measured pass over 155 drawers and sheets the workbench can open (1313 gaps between stacked blocks) found the same cause: gaps came in seven steps (2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16px) with no rule; about 110 vertical margins were set by the parts (item card, send form, evolve, dungeon rooms, pickers, place card), global `p`/`h*`/`label` margins leaked in, and grids with a `gap` plus margins on their children doubled it (the wizard added 8px under its Back row, the evolve footer padded itself inside the drawer's own footer padding, a choose-list row was 8px from the next while a pick-list row was 4px). A button sat 4px under the creature list (fold row) and 6px under the reason line of the send footer; the evolve stats floated up to 51px under the ability text; the sheet had its own padding and status margins.

- **The scale** (tokens on the root, `drawer.css`): `--dr-tight` 4px (in a group, between rows), `--dr-gap` 8px (between blocks), `--dr-action` 12px (above any button on its own line, the pinned footer's too: one rule for space above a button everywhere), `--dr-section` 16px (between sections and steps), `--dr-pad` 12px (sides, bottom, a sheet all round). 6px and 10px are gone. The popover's 4/8/12 are the same steps. The tokens are on `:root` (not on `.drawer-panel`) because the creature rows use them in pages too.
- **Structure:** the body, toolbar, footer and sheet are stacks, and `dr-stack` / `dr-group` / `dr-section` mark parts inside them (`:nth-child` weight, mirrored from `popover.css`). The body alone pads the top (8px under the heading); head and toolbar padding below went to 0 (the toolbar pads its own top). Parts that drew their own spacing lost it: item card and item drawer, wizard head and steps, send form (each step is a `dr-section dr-group`), job drawer, reach options, recipe card, haul strip, evolve (the ability grows to line the numbers up instead of an auto margin), dungeon rooms, place card, trip rows, the item picker's detail card, dismantle. Shared parts that a page also draws (`.actions`, `.reason`, `.drill-head`, `.line-list`) keep their margin for the page; inside a stack the stack's rule outweighs it. An empty live region adds no step.
- **Visible changes beyond spacing:** the item card's name and tags are one block and the stats sit 8px under it; the item drawer's Prev/Next row sits 8px (was 16) above the card; evolve cards' inner gaps are 8px (were 6) and their numbers still line up; choose-list rows are 4px apart (were 8); the sheet is padded 12px all round (was 8/12/12) and its title-to-list gap is 8px.
- **Prevention:** `ui/drawer-spacing.test.ts` (CI) fails when a drawer-only stylesheet (or a drawer-only class in a shared one) sets a vertical margin, and checks the tokens, the stack rules and the single body padding. `scripts/overlay-audit.mjs` (the popover audit generalised, `--kind popover|drawer|sheet|all`; `popover-audit.mjs` stays as a wrapper) opens every popover, drawer and sheet of the workbench in a browser and checks buttons, stack steps, edges, left and right alignment and own margins; it ends `AUDIT CLEAN` for all kinds. Not in CI (no browser); in the "Showing work to Misa" checklist. A new drawer or sheet entry goes in `OVERLAY_ENTRIES` in the script.

### 2026-10-28: Ribbons and medals on the creature card

Misa asked for a creature's ribbons and Valley Games medals on its card in the creature list: decoration only, no words, the card as small as before. He chose the look over several workbench rounds (four placements first, then this).

1. **Ribbons** hang as strips from the card's top edge, behind everything on the card (`CardRibbons` in `CreatureHonours.tsx`; the card gets `isolation: isolate` and the strips `z-index: -1`, so they sit above the panel and under all content). Gold is longest, then Silver, then Bronze; the highest is on the left, with one gap to the card's edge and between strips. A creature holds the lower ribbons too, so Gold shows all three. Colours are mixed 55% into the card's panel colour ("muted": in the background but noticeable), with a light outer edge and a dark line inside it in hard stops. Each strip starts 4 px above the card, as if hung over its edge, that part a little lighter, its corners cut. Misa plans to drop ribbons from the game later; the strips go with them.
2. **Medals** are small round pixel medals pinned over the card's top edge at the right (`CardMedals`), always three in a fixed order (gold, silver, bronze): a metal not won yet is a dark silhouette with no count, so the leftmost medal reads as the best at a glance (Misa, 2026-10-28; at first a metal with none was left out). A creature that has won no medal at all shows none. The count is on the face (at most 99 shown; Misa expects the card to be redesigned before any creature gets that far). Pins stay on the creature page only. The rows of the creature list are 16 px apart (was 12) so a medal never touches the card above.
3. **Both are hidden from screen readers** (`aria-hidden`): the creature page says the same in words (its Ribbons row and its Valley Games medals).
4. **Data.** `CreatureView.medals` (`{ gold, silver, bronze }`) comes from `games_creature_medals`, left-joined into the two creature reads of `collection.ts` (`CREATURE_SELECT`). The medal row is found by its primary key, so the state reads one row more only for each creature that has medals; no migration or index was needed. The read budget, query plan and day-in-the-life tests pass unchanged.

## 2026-10-28: Armour per species; talents go into the line's book automatically

**Decision (Misa).**

- **Armour is a species stat**, in the stat block like HP, Attack and Speed. Each line has its own base; lines that start as Guards lean higher; an evolution never lowers it. The Armor gear line works for every creature going forward (changed with the engine port, not now). Variety is the point: the species with the most armour is a choice to build around, not a default.
- **Found talents are recorded automatically** in that line's Talent Book; the duplicate choice is only about the creature (Fuse, Release, Keep). A creature learns a talent from the book for Bait the first time; afterwards swapping among its learned talents is free (before the raid locks). This answers panel 3's question on swap costs.
- **Idea for later:** if creatures ever "work" at places, a school where a creature learns a random talent from its line's book after some time.

**Notes (lead).** Both change the live game's systems (stats, the duplicate flow), so they are built with the game integration (roadmap phase 4) and the migration (phase 6). The lab gets per-species armour now, so the numbers reflect it.

## 2026-10-28: How strong a talent should be

**Decision (Misa).** Talent choice should feel like it has a big impact. Roughly **+20%** on a team's result is the upper target for a talent at its best (Misa's guess, to be checked in play). Power comes from many sources (Knacks, gear, placement and synergies, stats, levels), so what talents gain is taken from those. Too strong and players think each creature has only one valuable talent, so no pool may have a single must-pick.

**How the lab checks it (lead).** In the report's talent section (best legal board with against best legal board without, on a boss the classic line kills in about 8 rounds): a talent's gain on its best card is at most about +20%; the good talents of each role sit at about +8% to +15% where they fit; in each line's pool, every role has at least two talents within a few points of its best one. Raid-shaped talents (neighbours, row ends, long fights) are measured on a raid row as well.

## 2026-10-28: Talent strength is a share of the team's result

**Decision (Misa).** "About 20%" means the share of a team's success that comes from talents, on average: the same team with its talents taken away does about 20% worse (talents' share = 1 - without / with, the measure the journey sim's creatures study already uses). A single talent may spike above that on the week it fits best (option B), as long as no pool has a single must-pick. This replaces the earlier reading of +20% as a per-card ceiling.

**How the lab checks it (lead).** The report adds a team measure: for each card, the best gauntlet and raid teams with their creatures' best-fitting talents from their pools, against the same teams with no talents; the talents' share averages about 20% over the cards. The per-talent table stays to find duds and must-picks.

## 2026-10-28: Random talent rolls stay situational

**Decision (Misa).** A casual player with random talents gaining about 7% to 9% (against about 20% for the best fit) is fine: random rolls should stay well away from the best fit, so talents stay situational rather than flat. Finisher, picked by 9 of the 15 best teams, is fine for now; watch it. The lab's team check now asks that random rolls gain something but at most 60% of the best fit's share (the old 10% floor is gone).

## 2026-10-28: Combat v2 content direction: raids hyper-focused, dungeons all-round

**Decision (Misa).**

- **Existing foes are not sacred**: names, elements, gimmicks and numbers may all change for combat v2.
- **The raid** is announced in advance: one known boss, so players build a hyper-focused roster against it. Raid bosses get stronger and many more attacks, especially area attacks in varied patterns (rows, columns, several columns, chains, several random targets). Creatures should be knocked out in raids.
- **The dungeon** shows its encounters up front but sends many different foes, so it asks for an all-round team. Dungeons are tough, so the camp between rooms matters. The Undercroft stays a mystery: a push-your-luck choice players may skip.
- **Every mechanic matters somewhere**: each Knack family, talent kind, keyword, armour, silence, Mired, marks, Charged moves, Taunt, intercept and cover is the answer (or the problem) for at least one boss or foe.
- **Spiky is fine**: perfect balance is impossible; fun swings are welcome.

## 2026-10-28: A well-prepared raid team should usually win

**Decision (Misa).** A well-picked team built for the week's boss should win most weekends: finding the way to beat the boss and pulling it off is the reward, and that is the player agency combat v2 is for. Unprepared rosters (the same three creatures every week) should not stand much of a chance. The lab's raid targets follow: the best focused team wins most weekends (about 85% to 100%), the classic party rarely or never, and creatures are still knocked out along the way.

## 2026-10-28: Announced random attacks; dungeon clear target; the camp stays

**Decision (Misa).** Every random boss attack is shown at round start, with the attack's icon on each targeted creature (players cannot stop it, but watching it come is exciting). A dungeon clear is beating the day's floor boss; with the map's agency (route, skipped fights, safe mystery options, keepsakes, the camp) a good all-round team clears over 50% of runs at depths 1 to 8, and the Undercroft is where success drops. The camp keeps today's choices (rest, swap a creature, take a keepsake; push on or head home).
